## XX After Dinner (Continued) / 二十 餐后（续）

===Original===

The Curate stood in front of the looking-glass and solemnly divested himself of his collar.

“I never heard a more fantastic story,” said Mrs. Mendham from the basket chair. “The man must be mad. Are you sure﻿—.”

“Perfectly, my dear. I’ve told you every word, every incident﻿—.”

===Chinese===

副牧师（Curate）站在穿衣镜前，神情庄重地摘下了自己的领子。

“我从没听过比这更离奇的故事，”门德姆太太（Mrs. Mendham）坐在柳条椅上说，“这人准是疯了。你确定——”

“千真万确，亲爱的。我一字不漏、一桩不落地全告诉你了——”

===Original===

“*Well!*” said Mrs. Mendham, and spread her hands. “There’s no sense in it.”

“Precisely, my dear.”

“The Vicar,” said Mrs. Mendham, “must be mad.”

===Chinese===

“*嘿！*”门德姆太太说着摊开双手，“这里头毫无道理可言。”

“说得对，亲爱的。”

“教区牧师，”门德姆太太说，“准是疯了。”

===Original===

“This hunchback is certainly one of the strangest creatures I’ve seen for a long time. Foreign looking, with a big bright coloured face and long brown hair.﻿ ﻿… It can’t have been cut for months!” The Curate put his studs carefully upon the shelf of the dressing-table. “And a kind of staring look about his eyes, and a simpering smile. Quite a silly looking person. Effeminate.”

“But who *can* he be?” said Mrs. Mendham.

“I can’t imagine, my dear. Nor where he came from. He might be a chorister or something of that sort.”

===Chinese===

“这个驼背实在是我好久以来见过的最古怪的人物之一。一副外国人的模样，脸盘又大又红润，一头棕色的长发。……准有好几个月没剪过了！”副牧师把衬衫饰钮小心地搁到梳妆台的搁板上。“还有他那双眼睛，总带着一种直勾勾瞪人的神情，脸上挂着痴痴的笑。看上去十足是个蠢人。娘娘腔。”

“可他究竟*会*是谁呢？”门德姆太太说。

“我想象不出，亲爱的。也想不出他是打哪儿来的。没准是个唱诗班歌手，或者那一类的人。”

===Original===

“But *why* should he be about the shrubbery﻿ ﻿… in that dreadful costume?”

“I don’t know. The Vicar gave me no explanation. He simply said, ‘Mendham, this is an Angel.’ ”

“I wonder if he drinks.﻿ ﻿… They may have been bathing near the spring, of course,” reflected Mrs. Mendham. “But I noticed no other clothes on his arm.”

===Chinese===

“可他*为什么*要在灌木林里转悠……还穿着那么一身吓人的行头？”

“我不知道。教区牧师没给我任何解释。他只说了一句：‘门德姆，这是一位天使。’”

“真不知道他喝不喝酒。……当然了，他们也许是在泉水边上洗过澡，”门德姆太太寻思道，“可我留意到他胳膊上没搭着别的衣裳。”

===Original===

The Curate sat down on his bed and unlaced his boots.

“It’s a perfect mystery to me, my dear.” (Flick, flick of laces.) “Hallucination is the only charitable﻿—”

“You are sure, George, that it was *not* a woman.”

===Chinese===

副牧师在床沿坐下，解起靴子的鞋带来。

“这在我看来完完全全是个谜，亲爱的。”（鞋带甩得啪啪作响。）“往好里想，只能说是一场幻觉——”

“乔治，你确定那*不是*个女人。”

===Original===

“Perfectly,” said the Curate.

“I know what men are, of course.”

“It was a young man of nineteen or twenty,” said the Curate.

===Chinese===

“千真万确。”副牧师说。

“男人是什么德性，我当然清楚。”

“那是个十九二十岁的年轻人。”副牧师说。

===Original===

“I can’t understand it,” said Mrs. Mendham. “You say the creature is staying at the Vicarage?”

“Hilyer is simply mad,” said the Curate. He got up and went padding round the room to the door to put out his boots. “To judge by his manner you would really think he believed this cripple was an Angel.” (“Are your shoes out, dear?”)

(“They’re just by the wardrobe”), said Mrs. Mendham. “He always was a little queer, you know. There was always something childish about him.﻿ ﻿… An Angel!”

===Chinese===

“我实在想不明白，”门德姆太太说，“你是说那家伙眼下就住在牧师公馆里？”

“希利尔（Hilyer）简直是疯了。”副牧师说。他站起身，踢踢踏踏地绕着房间走到门口，把靴子放到门外去。“瞧他那副做派，你真会以为他相信这个瘸子是位天使呢。”（“你的鞋拿出去了吗，亲爱的？”）

（“就在衣柜旁边。”）门德姆太太说。“你知道，他一直就有点古怪。身上总带着股孩子气。……居然是天使！”

===Original===

The Curate came and stood by the fire, fumbling with his braces. Mrs. Mendham liked a fire even in the summer. “He shirks all the serious problems in life and is always trifling with some new foolishness,” said the Curate. “Angel indeed!” He laughed suddenly. “Hilyer *must* be mad,” he said.

Mrs. Mendham laughed too. “Even that doesn’t explain the hunchback,” she said.

“The hunchback must be mad too,” said the Curate.

===Chinese===

副牧师走过来站在炉火旁，摸索着摆弄背带。哪怕是在夏天，门德姆太太也喜欢生一炉火。“人生一切正经的问题他全都躲着，净在新奇的蠢事上消磨工夫，”副牧师说，“还天使呢！”他忽然笑出声来。“希利尔*一定*是疯了。”他说。

门德姆太太也笑了。“可就连这样，也解释不了那个驼子。”她说。

“那驼子也准是疯了。”副牧师说。

===Original===

“It’s the only way of explaining it in a sensible way,” said Mrs. Mendham. (*Pause.*)

“Angel or no angel,” said Mrs. Mendham, “I know what is due to me. Even supposing the man thought he *was* in the company of an angel, that is no reason why he should not behave like a gentleman.”

“That is perfectly true.”

===Chinese===

“这是唯一讲得通的解释了。”门德姆太太说。（*停顿。*）

“管他天使不天使，”门德姆太太说，“我知道我自己该受什么样的对待。就算这人当真以为自己是在同天使作伴，那也不能成为他举止不像个绅士的理由。”

“这话一点不假。”

===Original===

“You will write to the Bishop, of course?”

Mendham coughed. “No, I shan’t write to the Bishop,” said Mendham. “I think it seems a little disloyal.﻿ ﻿… And he took no notice of the last, you know.”

“But surely﻿—”

===Chinese===

“你当然要写信告诉主教啰？”

门德姆咳了一声。“不，我不打算写信给主教，”门德姆说，“我觉得那样有点不忠。……再说，上一回他压根儿就没理会，你是知道的。”

“可你总不能——”

===Original===

“I shall write to Austin. In confidence. He will be sure to tell the Bishop, you know. And you must remember, my dear﻿—”

“That Hilyer can dismiss you, you were going to say. My dear, the man’s much too weak! *I* should have a word to say about that. And besides, you do all his work for him. Practically, we manage the parish from end to end. I do not know what would become of the poor if it was not for me. They’d have free quarters in the Vicarage tomorrow. There is that Goody Ansell﻿—”

“I know, my dear,” said the Curate, turning away and proceeding with his undressing. “You were telling me about her only this afternoon.”

===Chinese===

“我会写信给奥斯汀（Austin）。私下里写。他准会去告诉主教，这你知道的。还有你千万得记住，亲爱的——”

“你是想说，希利尔能辞退你。亲爱的，那人软弱得很！这种事*我*可得说上一句话。再说了，他的活儿反正全是你替他干的。实际上，这个教区从头到尾都是咱们两口子在操持。要不是有我，真不知道那些穷人会落得个什么下场。明天他们就该白住进牧师公馆了。就说那个安塞尔老婆子（Goody Ansell）——”

“我知道，亲爱的，”副牧师说着转过身去，继续脱衣裳，“就在今天下午你还跟我念叨她来着。”

## XII At the Vicarage (Continued) / 十二 在牧师公馆（续）

===Original===

So the Angel was invested in a pair of nether garments of the Vicar’s, a shirt, ripped down the back (to accommodate the wings), socks, shoes﻿—the Vicar’s dress shoes﻿—collar, tie, and light overcoat. But putting on the latter was painful, and reminded the Vicar that the bandaging was temporary. “I will ring for tea at once, and send Grummet down for Crump,” said the Vicar. “And dinner shall be earlier.” While the Vicar shouted his orders on the landing rails, the Angel surveyed himself in the cheval glass with immense delight. If he was a stranger to pain, he was evidently no stranger﻿—thanks perhaps to dreaming﻿—to the pleasure of incongruity.

They had tea in the drawing-room. The Angel sat on the music stool (music stool because of his wings). At first he wanted to lie on the hearthrug. He looked much less radiant in the Vicar’s clothes, than he had done upon the moor when dressed in saffron. His face shone still, the colour of his hair and cheeks was strangely bright, and there was a superhuman light in his eyes, but his wings under the overcoat gave him the appearance of a hunchback. The garments, indeed, made quite a terrestrial thing of him, the trousers were puckered transversely, and the shoes a size or so too large.

He was charmingly affable and quite ignorant of the most elementary facts of civilization. Eating came without much difficulty, and the Vicar had an entertaining time teaching him how to take tea. “What a mess it is! What a dear grotesque ugly world you live in!” said the Angel. “Fancy stuffing things into your mouth! We use our mouths just to talk and sing with. Our world, you know, is almost incurably beautiful. We get so very little ugliness, that I find all this﻿ ﻿… delightful.”

===Chinese===

于是天使被穿上了一身行头：教区牧师的一条衬裤、一件后背撕开的衬衫（好安放那对翅膀）、袜子、鞋子——教区牧师的正装皮鞋——领子、领带，还有一件薄大衣。只是穿上最后这件时他吃了一番苦头，也提醒了教区牧师：那绷带终究只是权宜之计。“我这就摇铃上茶，再派格拉米特（Grummet）下山去请克伦普（Crump），”教区牧师说，“晚饭也得提前。”教区牧师在楼梯栏杆边大声吩咐的当儿，天使正对着一面穿衣镜上下打量自己，喜不自胜。若说疼痛于他是全然陌生之事，那么——兴许是多亏了做梦——对这种不伦不类里的妙趣，他显然绝不陌生。

他们在客厅里用茶。天使坐在琴凳上（因为有翅膀，只好坐琴凳）。起初他还想往炉前地毯上一躺。穿上教区牧师的衣裳，他看上去远不如在荒野上披着藏红花色袍子时那么容光焕发。他的脸依然发亮，头发和双颊的颜色出奇地鲜艳，眼里有一股超人的光，可大衣底下那对翅膀却叫他显出驼背的模样。这身衣裳确实把他变成了件地地道道的尘世俗物：裤子上横一道竖一道地打满褶子，鞋子也大了一号上下。

他和蔼可亲得讨人喜欢，却对文明世界最起码的常识一无所知。吃东西他学得不算费力，教他喝茶更是让教区牧师觉得趣味盎然。“这可真是乱七八糟！你们住的这个世界，多么可爱、多么古怪、又多么丑啊！”天使说，“想想看吧，居然把东西塞进嘴里！我们的嘴只用来说话和唱歌。要知道，我们的世界美得几乎无可救药。丑东西实在见得太少，所以这一切……反倒让我觉得妙不可言。”

===Original===

Mrs. Hinijer, the Vicar’s housekeeper, looked at the Angel suspiciously when she brought in the tea. She thought him rather a “queer customer.” What she would have thought had she seen him in saffron no one can tell.

The Angel shuffled about the room with his cup of tea in one hand, and the bread and butter in the other, and examined the Vicar’s furniture. Outside the French windows, the lawn with its array of dahlias and sunflowers glowed in the warm sunlight, and Mrs. Jehoram’s sunshade stood thereon like a triangle of fire. He thought the Vicar’s portrait over the mantel very curious indeed, could not understand what it was there for. “You have yourself round,” he said, apropos of the portrait, “Why want yourself flat?” and he was vastly amused at the glass fire screen. He found the oak chairs odd﻿—“You’re not square, are you?” he said, when the Vicar explained their use. “*We* never double ourselves up. We lie about on the asphodel when we want to rest.”

“The chair,” said the Vicar, “to tell you the truth, has always puzzled *me*. It dates, I think, from the days when the floors were cold and very dirty. I suppose we have kept up the habit. It’s become a kind of instinct with us to sit on chairs. Anyhow, if I went to see one of my parishioners, and suddenly spread myself out on the floor﻿—the natural way of it﻿—I don’t know what she would do. It would be all over the parish in no time. Yet it seems the natural method of reposing, to recline. The Greeks and Romans﻿—”

===Chinese===

教区牧师的女管家希尼杰太太（Mrs. Hinijer）端茶进来时，狐疑地打量了天使一番。她觉得此人着实是个“古怪的主顾”。倘若她见过他披藏红花色袍子的模样会作何感想，那就无人知晓了。

天使一手端着茶杯，一手拿着黄油面包，趿着步子在屋里踱来踱去，端详教区牧师的家具。落地长窗外面，草坪上成排的大丽花和向日葵在暖洋洋的日光里熠熠生辉，杰霍拉姆太太（Mrs. Jehoram）的阳伞立在那儿，活像一团三角形的火焰。他觉得壁炉上方教区牧师的画像实在稀奇得很，弄不明白摆在那里做什么用。“你本人明明是立着的，”他针对那幅画像发表议论，“干吗要个扁的自己？”那玻璃挡火屏又让他乐不可支。橡木椅子在他看来也很古怪——“你们身子又不是方方正正的，对吧？”教区牧师解释了椅子的用途之后，他这样说。“*我们*从来不把自己对折起来。想歇息的时候，我们就往日光兰花丛里一躺。”

“说起椅子，”教区牧师说，“老实讲，它也一直让*我*自己纳闷。我想，它该是地板又冷又脏的年代传下来的东西。后来我们大约就把这习惯保持了下来。坐在椅子上已经成了我们的一种本能。总而言之，要是我去探望哪位教民，忽然往地板上一摊——那才是最自然的方式——我真不知道她会怎么办。不出一刻工夫，这事儿就会传遍整个教区。可话说回来，论休息，躺着似乎才是最自然的姿势。希腊人和罗马人——”

===Original===

“What is this?” said the Angel abruptly.

“That’s a stuffed kingfisher. I killed it.”

“Killed it!”

===Chinese===

“这是什么？”天使冷不丁地问。

“那是一只制成标本的翠鸟。是我杀死的。”

“杀死的！”

===Original===

“Shot it,” said the Vicar, “with a gun.”

“Shot! As you did me?”

“I didn’t kill you, you see. Fortunately.”

===Chinese===

“用枪打死的，”教区牧师说，“用一支枪。”

“打死的！就像你打我那样？”

“可见我并没有把你打死。真是万幸。”

===Original===

“Is killing making like that?”

“In a way.”

“Dear me! And you wanted to make me like that﻿—wanted to put glass eyes in me and string me up in a glass case full of ugly green and brown stuff?”

===Chinese===

“杀人，就是把它弄成那副模样么？”

“在某种意义上是。”

“天哪！这么说，你方才还想把我弄成那副模样——想给我装上玻璃眼珠，拿线把我吊起来，塞进一个满是丑陋的绿棕两色玩意儿的玻璃匣子里？”

===Original===

“You see,” began the Vicar, “I scarcely understood﻿—”

“Is that ‘die’?” asked the Angel suddenly.

“That is dead; it died.”

“Poor little thing. I must eat a lot. But you say you killed it. *Why?*”

===Chinese===

“你要知道，”教区牧师开口道，“我当时实在不很明白——”

“那就是‘死’么？”天使忽然问。

“那就是死了；它死掉了。”

“可怜的小东西。我一定得大吃一顿才行。可你说它是你杀死的。*为什么？*”

===Original===

“You see,” said the Vicar, “I take an interest in birds, and I (*ahem*) collect them. I wanted the specimen﻿—”

The Angel stared at him for a moment with puzzled eyes. “A beautiful bird like that!” he said with a shiver. “Because the fancy took you. You wanted the specimen!”

He thought for a minute. “Do you often kill?” he asked the Vicar.

===Chinese===

“你要知道，”教区牧师说，“我对鸟类很感兴趣，而且我（*咳咳*）收藏标本。我想要那件标本——”

天使用困惑的目光盯着他看了片刻。“那么美的一只鸟！”他打了个寒战说，“就因为你一时兴起。就为了想要一件标本！”

他寻思了一分钟。“你们常常杀死东西吗？”他问教区牧师。

## XXVI The Angel Explores the Village (Continued) / 二十六 天使探访村庄（续）

===Original===

The Angel went on through the village, finding it all wonderful enough. “They begin, and just a little while and then they end,” he said to himself in a puzzled voice. “But what are they doing meanwhile?” Once he heard some invisible mouth chant inaudible words to the tune the man at the forge had hummed.

“That’s the poor creature the Vicar shot with that great gun of his,” said Sarah Glue (of 1, Church Cottages) peering over the blind.

“He looks Frenchified,” said Susan Hopper, peering through the interstices of that convenient veil on curiosity.

===Chinese===

天使继续往前穿过村庄，觉得这一切实在是够奇妙的。“他们开始了，一会儿工夫就结束了，”他困惑地自言自语道。“可这中间他们在做什么呢？”有一回，他听见一张看不见的嘴，正和着铁匠铺那人哼过的调子，唱着听不真切的词。

“那就是教区牧师用他那杆大枪打中的可怜家伙，”萨拉·格卢（Sarah Glue，教堂村舍一号）从遮帘上边张望着说。

“瞧着像法国人，”苏珊·霍珀（Susan Hopper）透过那面遮掩好奇心正合用的帘子缝隙说。

===Original===

“He has sweet eyes,” said Sarah Glue, who had met them for a moment.

The Angel sauntered on. The postman passed him and touched his hat to him; further down was a dog asleep in the sun. He went on and saw Mendham, who nodded distantly and hurried past. (The Curate did not care to be seen talking to an angel in the village, until more was known about him). There came from one of the houses the sound of a child screaming in a passion, that brought a puzzled look to the angelic face. Then the Angel reached the bridge below the last of the houses, and stood leaning over the parapet watching the glittering little cascade from the mill.

“They begin, and just a little while, and then they end,” said the weir from the mill. The water raced under the bridge, green and dark, and streaked with foam.

===Chinese===

“他有一双好看的眼睛，”萨拉·格卢说，她方才同那双眼睛对视过一瞬。

天使信步前行。邮差从他身边经过，朝他碰了碰帽檐；再往前，有一条狗在太阳地里酣睡。他又往前走，看见了门德姆；门德姆远远地点了点头，便匆匆而过。（副牧师可不愿意让人瞧见自己在村里同一位天使攀谈，起码在进一步弄清此人底细之前是这样。）有一座房子里传出小孩撒泼哭喊的声音，叫那张天使的脸庞露出困惑的神色。随后天使走到了村尾那座桥前，倚着桥栏站定，看磨坊那道波光闪闪的小瀑布。

“开始了，一会儿工夫，就结束了，”磨坊的堰这样说。桥下的水奔流而过，绿得发暗，一道道白沫划过水面。

===Original===

Beyond the mill rose the square tower of the church, with the churchyard behind it, a spray of tombstones and wooden headboards splashed up the hillside. A half dozen of beech trees framed the picture.

Then the Angel heard a shuffling of feet and the gride of wheels behind him, and turning his head saw a man dressed in dirty brown rags and a felt hat grey with dust, who was standing with a slight swaying motion and fixedly regarding the Angelic back. Beyond him was another almost equally dirty, pushing a knife grinder’s barrow over the bridge.

“Mornin’,” said the first person smiling weakly. “Goomorn’.” He arrested an escaping hiccup.

===Chinese===

磨坊后面耸起教堂的方塔，塔后是教堂墓地，一片墓碑和木牌像溅起的水花洒上山坡。五六棵山毛榉给这幅图景镶上了框。

接着，天使听见身后一阵趿拉的脚步声和车轮的摩擦声；回头一看，只见一个汉子穿着肮脏的棕色破衣裳，戴一顶蒙满灰尘、灰扑扑的毡帽，身子微微摇晃地站着，直勾勾地盯着天使的背影。他身后还有一个几乎同样脏的，正推着一辆磨刀车过桥。

“早啊，”第一个人有气无力地笑着说，“早——上好。”他拦住了一个要冒出来的酒嗝。

===Original===

The Angel stared at him. He had never seen a really fatuous smile before. “Who are you?” said the Angel.

The fatuous smile faded. “No your business whoaaam. Wishergoomorn.”

“Carm on,” said the man with the grindstone, passing on his way.

===Chinese===

天使盯着他瞧。他还从来没有见过真正痴愚的笑。“你是谁？”天使问。

那痴愚的笑淡了下去。“你管我是谁。祝你早上好。”

“走啦，”推磨刀车的人说着，自顾赶路去了。

===Original===

“Wishergoomorn,” said the dirty man, in a tone of extreme aggravation. “Carncher Answerme?”

“Carm *on* you fool!” said the man with the grindstone﻿—receding.

“I don’t understand,” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“祝——你早上好，”脏汉子说，语气恼怒到了极点，“你就不能搭理一句？”

“快走啦，*你这*蠢货！”推磨刀车的人说——声音渐渐远去。

“我不明白，”天使说。

===Original===

“Donunderstan’. Sim’l enough. Wishergoomorn’. Willyanswerme? Wontchr? gemwishergem goomorn. Cusom answer goomorn. No gem. Haverteachyer.”

The Angel was puzzled. The drunken man stood swaying for a moment, then he made an unsteady snatch at his hat and threw it down at the Angel’s feet. “Ver well,” he said, as one who decides great issues.

“*Carm* on!” said the voice of the man with the grindstone﻿—stopping perhaps twenty yards off.

===Chinese===

“不——明白？再简单不过了嘛。祝你早上好。你到底答不答话？嗯？咱们都是有身份的人，见了面互道早上好。你倒好，人家道了早上好不回礼。连这点规矩都不懂。看来得给你上一课。”

天使满心困惑。醉汉摇摇晃晃地站了片刻，然后一把抓下帽子——手也不稳——扔在天使脚下。“很好，”他说，那口气像是在裁决什么重大问题。

“快*走*啦！”推磨刀车的人的声音喊道——他大约在二十码开外停了下来。

===Original===

“You *wan* fight, you﻿—” the Angel failed to catch the word. “I’ll show yer, not answer gem’s goomorn.”

He began to struggle with his jacket. “Think I’m drun,” he said, “I show yer.” The man with the grindstone sat down on the shaft to watch. “Carm on,” he said. The jacket was intricate, and the drunken man began to struggle about the road, in his attempts to extricate himself, breathing threatenings and slaughter. Slowly the Angel began to suspect, remotely enough, that these demonstrations were hostile. “Mur wun know yer when I done wi’ yer,” said the drunken man, coat almost over his head.

At last the garment lay on the ground, and through the frequent interstices of his reminiscences of a waistcoat, the drunken tinker displayed a fine hairy and muscular body to the Angel’s observant eyes. He squared up in masterly fashion.

===Chinese===

“你是想*打*一架，你这个——”后头那个词天使没听清。“我倒要让你瞧瞧，人家道了早上好还敢不回。”

他开始同他的外衣搏斗起来。“当我醉了，”他说，“我就让你瞧瞧。”推磨刀车的人在车辕上坐下观战。“走啦，”他说。那件外衣构造复杂，醉汉为了把自己从里面解脱出来，在大路上挣扎得东倒西歪，嘴里威吓着要杀要剐。天使这才隐隐约约、慢慢地疑心起这些举动带着敌意。“等老子收拾完了你，看你认得不认得，”醉汉说，外衣差不多罩到了头顶。

那件衣裳终于落到了地上，透过他身上那件旧背心的处处破绽，醉汉那副毛茸茸、筋腱结实的好身板便在天使观察入微的眼睛前展露无遗。他摆出了一个行家里手的架势。

===Original===

“Take the paint off yer,” he remarked, advancing and receding, fists up and elbows out.

“Carm on,” floated down the road.

The Angel’s attention was concentrated on two huge hairy black fists, that swayed and advanced and retreated. “Come on d’yer say? I’ll show yer,” said the gentleman in rags, and then with extraordinary ferocity; “My crikey! I’ll show yer.”

===Chinese===

“把你脸上的颜料给你抹下来，”他念念有词，进进退退，双拳高举，两肘外张。

“走啦——”这话顺着大路飘了过来。

天使的注意力全落在那两只毛茸茸的黑色大拳头上，它们一晃一晃，忽进忽退。“你是说来吗？我这就给你瞧瞧，”那位衣衫褴褛的绅士说，随即又以一股非凡的凶狠劲儿嚷道：“我的天爷！我这就给你瞧瞧。”

===Original===

Suddenly he lurched forward, and with a newborn instinct and raising a defensive arm as he did so, the Angel stepped aside to avoid him. The fist missed the Angelic shoulder by a hairsbreadth, and the tinker collapsed in a heap with his face against the parapet of the bridge. The Angel hesitated over the writhing dusty heap of blasphemy for a moment, and then turned towards the man’s companion up the road. “Lemmeget up,” said the man on the bridge: “Lemmeget up, you swine. I’ll show yer.”

A strange disgust, a quivering repulsion came upon the Angel. He walked slowly away from the drunkard towards the man with the grindstone.

“What does it all mean?” said the Angel. “I don’t understand it.”

===Chinese===

忽然他一个踉跄扑将过来，天使凭着一种新生的本能，抬起一条胳膊护住自己，同时侧身一闪。那拳头离天使的肩膀只差一根头发丝，补锅匠却扑通一声瘫作一堆，脸抵在桥栏上。天使对着那团蠕动着、满口秽语的尘土堆迟疑了片刻，然后转身朝路上那人的同伴走去。“让我起来，”桥上那人说，“让我起来，你这猪猡。我这就给你瞧瞧。”

一种奇异的憎恶、一阵颤抖般的反感袭上天使心头。他缓步离开醉汉，朝推磨刀车的人走去。

“这一切到底是什么意思？”天使说，“我弄不明白。”

===Original===

“Dam fool!﻿ ﻿… say’s it’s ’is silver weddin’,” answered the man with the grindstone, evidently much annoyed; and then, in a tone of growing impatience, he called down the road once more; “Carm on!”

“Silver wedding!” said the Angel. “What is a silver wedding?”

“Jest is rot,” said the man on the barrow. “But ’e’s always avin’ some ’scuse like that. Fair sickenin it is. Lars week it wus ’is bloomin’ birthday, and *then* ’e ad’nt ardly got sober orf a comlimentary drunk to my noo barrer. (*Carm* on, you fool.)”

===Chinese===

“十足的蠢货！……说是他今儿个过银婚呢，”推磨刀车的人回答，显然十分恼火；接着又用越来越不耐烦的口气朝大路那头再喊一声：“走啦！”

“银婚！”天使说，“什么是银婚？”

“纯属胡扯，”车上那人说。“可他总有这么些个由头。真叫人恶心。上礼拜是他妈的过生日，再上礼拜，为庆贺我的新车，他喝了个礼节性的醉，到这会儿还没醒透呢。（*快走*啦，你这蠢货。）

===Original===

“But I don’t understand,” said the Angel. “Why does he sway about so? Why does he keep on trying to pick up his hat like that﻿—and missing it?”

“*Why!*” said the tinker. “Well this *is* a blasted innocent country! *Why!* Because ’e’s blind! Wot else? (Carm on﻿—*Dam* yer). Because ’e’s just as full as ’e can ’old. That’s *why*!”

The Angel noticing the tone of the second tinker’s voice, judged it wiser not to question him further. But he stood by the grindstone and continued to watch the mysterious evolutions on the bridge.

===Chinese===

“可我还是不明白，”天使说，“他为什么晃成那样？为什么老是那样去捡帽子——还总捡不着？”

“*为什么！*”磨刀匠说，“好一个天真透顶的地方！*为什么！*因为他瞎了呗！还能为什么？（走啦——*该死的*。）因为他肚子里灌得满满当当，再装不下一滴了。这就是*为什么*！”

天使听出了这第二个补锅匠话里的口气，觉得还是别再多问为妙。但他站在磨刀车旁，继续望着桥上那场神秘的演变。

===Original===

“Carm on! I shall ’ave to go and pick up that ’at I suppose.﻿ ﻿… ’E’s always at it. I ne’er ’ad such a blooming pard before. *Always* at it, ’e is.”

The man with the barrow meditated. “Taint as if ’e was a gentleman and ’adnt no livin’ to get. An’ ’e’s such a reckless fool when ’e gets a bit on. Goes offerin out everyone ’e meets. (*There* you go!) I’m blessed if ’e didn’t offer out a ’ole bloomin’ Salvation Army. No judgment in it. (Oh! *Carm* on! *Carm* on!). ’Ave to go and pick this bloomin’ ’at up now I s’pose. ’E don’t care, *wot* trouble ’e gives.”

The Angel watched the second tinker walk back, and, with affectionate blasphemy, assist the first to his hat and his coat. Then he turned, absolutely mystified, towards the village again.

===Chinese===

“走啦！看来我还得回去替他捡那顶帽子。……他老是这副德行。我从来没摊上过这么个活宝搭档。*老是*这副德行，他就是。”

推车的汉子沉吟起来。“他又不是什么绅士老爷，犯不着为生计发愁。可他只要沾上几口，就是个没轻没重的蠢货。见着一个就要跟一个叫阵。（*你*瞧瞧！）有一回他居然冲着一整支救世军叫阵，真是见了他的鬼。一点脑子都没有。（哦！*快走*啦！*快走*啦！）得，看来我这会儿只好回去捡这顶破帽子了。他呀，才不管给人家添多大麻烦呢。”

天使看着第二个补锅匠走回去，一面亲亲热热地骂着，一面扶第一个拾掇好帽子和外衣。随后他转过身，满心惶惑地又朝村庄走去。

## XXXVI The Angel’s Debut (Continued) / 三十六 天使的初次亮相（续）

===Original===

This section is (so far as my memory goes) the shortest in the book.

But the enormity of the offence necessitates the separation of this section from all other sections.

===Chinese===

这一节（就我记忆所及）是全书里最短的一节。

但此番罪过的严重性，决定了这一节必须同其余各节分开。

===Original===

The Vicar, you must understand, had done his best to inculcate the recognised differentiae of a gentleman. “Never allow a lady to carry anything,” said the Vicar. “Say, ‘permit me’ and relieve her.” “Always stand until every lady is seated.” “Always rise and open a door for a lady.﻿ ﻿…” and so forth. (All men who have elder sisters know that code.)

And the Angel (who had failed to relieve Lady Hammergallow of her teacup) danced forward with astonishing dexterity (leaving Mrs. Jehoram in the window seat) and with an elegant “permit me” rescued the tea-tray from Lady Hammergallow’s pretty parlourmaid and vanished officiously in front of her. The Vicar rose to his feet with an inarticulate cry.

===Chinese===

你们必须明白，教区牧师已经尽力把绅士的那些公认的行为准则灌输给了天使。“绝不可让一位女士拿任何东西，”教区牧师说，“要说‘请允许我’，然后替她接过来。”“在每位女士落座之前，务必一直站着。”“女士进门，务必起身替她开门。……”诸如此类。（凡是家有姐姐的男人，都晓得这套规矩。）

于是天使（他方才没能替哈默加洛夫人（Lady Hammergallow）接过茶杯）以惊人的灵巧舞步抢上前来（把杰霍拉姆太太撇在窗座上），又以一句优雅的“请允许我”，从哈默加洛夫人那位漂亮的女仆手里夺下了茶盘，殷勤得发狂似的一马当先在前头引路。教区牧师一声含混的惊呼，腾地站了起来。

## XVII The Curate (Continued) / 十七 副牧师（续）

===Original===

“Are *all* men so odd as this?” said the Angel.

“I’m in such a difficult position,” said the Vicar. “You see,” he said, and stopped, searching his chin for an idea.

“I’m beginning to see,” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“*所有*人都这么古怪吗？”天使说。

“我的处境实在太为难了，”教区牧师说。“你要知道，”他说，随即停住，摸着下巴搜寻主意。

“我开始明白了。”天使说。

===Original===

“They won’t believe it.”

“I see that.”

“They will think I tell lies.”

===Chinese===

“他们是不会相信的。”

“这我看得出。”

“他们会认为我在撒谎。”

===Original===

“And?”

“That will be extremely painful to me.”

“Painful!﻿ ﻿… Pain,” said the Angel. “I hope not.”

===Chinese===

“然后呢？”

“那会叫我极其痛苦。”

“痛苦！……痛苦，”天使说，“我希望不至于。”

===Original===

The Vicar shook his head. The good report of the village had been the breath of his life, so far. “You see,” he said, “it would look so much more plausible if you said you were just a man.”

“But I’m not,” said the Angel.

“No, you’re not,” said the Vicar. “So that’s no good.”

===Chinese===

教区牧师摇了摇头。直到如今，村子里的好口碑一直是他赖以呼吸的空气。“你要明白，”他说，“如果你只说自己是个普通人，事情看上去就要可信得多了。”

“可我不是。”天使说。

“是啊，你不是，”教区牧师说，“所以那行不通。”

===Original===

“Nobody here, you know, has ever seen an Angel, or heard of one﻿—except in church. If you had made your debut in the chancel﻿—on Sunday﻿—it might have been different. But that’s too late now.﻿ ﻿… (*Bother!*) Nobody, absolutely nobody, will believe in you.”

“I hope I am not inconveniencing you?”

“Not at all,” said the Vicar; “not at all. Only﻿—. Naturally it may be inconvenient if you tell a too incredible story. If I might suggest (*ahem*)﻿—.”

===Chinese===

“你要知道，这里从来没有人见过天使，也没有听说过什么天使——除了在教堂里。倘若你当初是在圣坛上——在礼拜天——初次露面，事情或许会不一样。可如今已经太迟了。……（*糟了！*）谁也不会，绝对没有任何人会相信你。”

“我希望没有给你添麻烦吧？”

“完全没有，”教区牧师说，“完全没有。只是——。当然，你要是讲一个太过难以置信的故事，事情兴许会不太好办。要是我可以提个建议的话（*咳咳*）——”

===Original===

“Well?”

“You see, people in the world, being men themselves, will almost certainly regard you as a man. If you say you are not, they will simply say you do not tell the truth. Only exceptional people appreciate the exceptional. When in Rome one must﻿—well, respect Roman prejudices a little﻿—talk Latin. You will find it better﻿—”

“You propose I should feign to become a man?”

===Chinese===

“什么建议？”

“你要知道，世上的人自己既然是人，几乎必定会把你当人看。你若说自己不是，他们只会说你不老实。唯有非凡之人才能赏识非凡之物。入乡随俗，就得——呃，多少尊重一点罗马人的成见——说拉丁话。你会发现这样更好——”

“你是提议我装作是一个人？”

===Original===

“You have my meaning at once.”

The Angel stared at the Vicar’s hollyhocks and thought.

“Possibly, after all,” he said slowly, “I *shall* become a man. I may have been too hasty in saying I was not. You say there are no angels in this world. Who am I to set myself up against your experience? A mere thing of a day﻿—so far as this world goes. If you say there are no angels﻿—clearly I must be something else. I eat﻿—angels do not eat. I *may* be a man already.”

===Chinese===

“你一下子就领会了我的意思。”

天使盯着教区牧师家的蜀葵出神，思量着。

“也许，归根结底，”他缓缓地说，“我*当真*会变成人。我方才说自己不是，未免太性急了。你说这世上没有天使。我算得了什么，竟敢同你的经验作对？不过是一日之物——就这个世界而言。既然你说没有天使——那我显然必定是别的什么。我要吃东西——天使是不吃东西的。我*说不定*已经是个人了。”

===Original===

“A convenient view, at any rate,” said the Vicar.

“If it is convenient to you﻿—”

“It is. And then to account for your presence here.”

===Chinese===

“不管怎么说，这是个方便的说法。”教区牧师说。

“只要于你方便——”

“确实方便。接下来还得解释你为何会在这里。”

===Original===

“*If*,” said the Vicar, after a hesitating moment of reflection, “if, for instance, you had been an ordinary man with a weakness for wading, and you had gone wading in the Sidder, and your clothes had been stolen, for instance, and I had come upon you in that position of inconvenience; the explanation I shall have to make to Mrs. Mendham﻿—would be shorn at least of the supernatural element. There is such a feeling against the supernatural element nowadays﻿—even in the pulpit. You would hardly believe﻿—”

“It’s a pity that was not the case,” said the Angel.

“Of course,” said the Vicar. “It is a great pity that was not the case. But at any rate you will oblige me if you do not obtrude your angelic nature. You will oblige everyone, in fact. There is a settled opinion that angels do not do this kind of thing. And nothing is more painful﻿—as I can testify﻿—than a decaying settled opinion.﻿ ﻿… Settled opinions are mental teeth in more ways than one. For my own part,”﻿—the Vicar’s hand passed over his eyes for a moment﻿—“I cannot but believe you are an angel.﻿ ﻿… Surely I can believe my own eyes.”

===Chinese===

“*倘若*，”教区牧师迟疑地寻思了片刻之后说，“倘若，比方说，你本是个有蹚水癖好的普通人，到锡德河（Sidder）里蹚水去了，比方说，你的衣裳又让人偷了，而我又恰好撞见你身处那种不便的境地；那么我不得不向门德姆太太作的那番解释——至少可以免去超自然的成分。如今人们对超自然的成分反感得很——就连讲道坛上也一样。你简直不会相信——”

“可惜实情不是那样。”天使说。

“当然，”教区牧师说。“实情不是那样，实在遗憾。但无论如何，只要你不硬亮出你的天使本性，就是帮了我的忙。事实上，也就是帮了所有人的忙。人们有一条定论：天使不做这种事。再没有比一条日渐崩坏的定论更痛苦的了——这一点我可以作证。……定论之于头脑，在不止一个方面都像牙齿。至于我自己，”——教区牧师的手在眼睛上掩了片刻——“我不能不信你是一位天使。……我总归信得过自己的眼睛吧。”

===Original===

“We always do ours,” said the Angel.

“And so do we, within limits.”

Then the clock upon the mantel chimed seven, and almost simultaneously Mrs. Hinijer announced dinner.

===Chinese===

“我们的眼睛，我们向来是信的。”天使说。

“我们也信，不过有个限度。”

这时壁炉架上的钟敲了七点，几乎在同一时刻，希尼杰太太进来通报晚饭已备好。

## V The Hunting of the Strange Bird (Continued) / 五 猎捕怪鸟（续）

===Original===

There is a place on the moor where the black water shines among the succulent moss, and the hairy sundew, eater of careless insects, spreads its red-stained hungry hands to the God who gives his creatures﻿—one to feed another. On a ridge thereby grow birches with a silvery bark, and the soft green of the larch mingles with the dark green fir. Thither through the honey humming heather came the Vicar, in the heat of the day, carrying a gun under his arm, a gun loaded with swanshot for the Strange Bird. And over his disengaged hand he carried a pocket handkerchief wherewith, ever and again, he wiped his beady face.

He went by and on past the big pond and the pool full of brown leaves where the Sidder arises, and so by the road (which is at first sandy and then chalky) to the little gate that goes into the park. There are seven steps up to the gate and on the further side six down again﻿—lest the deer escape﻿—so that when the Vicar stood in the gateway his head was ten feet or more above the ground. And looking where a tumult of bracken fronds filled the hollow between two groups of beech, his eye caught something parti-coloured that wavered and went. Suddenly his face gleamed and his muscles grew tense; he ducked his head, clutched his gun with both hands, and stood still. Then watching keenly, he came on down the steps into the park, and still holding his gun in both hands, crept rather than walked towards the jungle of bracken.

===Chinese===

荒野上有个地方，黑亮的水在肉质的苔藓间闪光，毛茸茸的茅膏菜——不小心的小虫的捕食者——向那位让自己的造物彼此为食的上帝，张开它染红的饥饿之手。旁边一道山脊上长着银皮的白桦，落叶松的嫩绿同冷杉的深绿交织在一处。就在一天最热的时分，教区牧师穿过蜂蜜般嗡嗡吟唱的石南花丛朝那里走去，腋下夹着一支枪——一支为怪鸟装好了大号霰弹的枪。空着的那只手上还搭着一条手帕，不时用来揩他那张汗珠直冒的脸。

他一路走了过去，经过大池塘和那个满是褐色落叶、锡德河发源的水洼，再顺着那条路（起初是沙路，往后是白垩路）走到通往园子的小门前。上到门前有七级台阶，门那头又是六级——生怕鹿跑出去——因此教区牧师站在门口时，脑袋离地足有十英尺开外。他朝两丛山毛榉之间翻腾着蕨叶的洼地望去，眼睛忽然捕捉到一样五彩斑斓的东西，晃了一晃便没了踪影。他脸上霎时一亮，肌肉绷紧；他缩起脑袋，双手攥住枪，站定不动。接着他敏锐地注视着前方，顺台阶下进园子，仍旧双手握枪，与其说是走，不如说是爬着朝那片蕨丛摸去。

===Original===

Nothing stirred, and he almost feared that his eyes had played him false, until he reached the ferns and had gone rustling breast high into them. Then suddenly rose something full of wavering colours, twenty yards or less in front of his face, and beating the air. In another moment it had fluttered above the bracken and spread its pinions wide. He saw what it was, his heart was in his mouth, and he fired out of pure surprise and habit.

There was a scream of superhuman agony, the wings beat the air twice, and the victim came slanting swiftly downward and struck the ground﻿—a struggling heap of writhing body, broken wing and flying bloodstained plumes﻿—upon the turfy slope behind.

===Chinese===

毫无动静，他几乎疑心是自己看走了眼，直到他走进蕨丛，半个胸脯没进沙沙作响的齐胸蕨叶里。忽然，一样满是变幻色彩的东西在他面前二十码开外腾起，扑打着空气。转眼间它已掠过蕨梢，张开双翼。他看清那是什么了，心一下子提到嗓子眼，出于纯粹的惊讶和习惯，他开了枪。

一声超越人间痛苦的尖叫，双翼扑打了两下空气，那受难者便斜着飞快栽落下来，摔在地上——成了一团挣扎扭动的东西：扭动的身躯、折断的翅膀、四散纷飞的带血羽毛——落在后面那片长满草皮的坡上。

===Original===

The Vicar stood aghast, with his smoking gun in his hand. It was no bird at all, but a youth with an extremely beautiful face, clad in a robe of saffron and with iridescent wings, across whose pinions great waves of colour, flushes of purple and crimson, golden green and intense blue, pursued one another as he writhed in his agony. Never had the Vicar seen such gorgeous floods of colour, not stained glass windows, not the wings of butterflies, not even the glories of crystals seen between prisms, no colours on earth could compare with them. Twice the Angel raised himself, only to fall over sideways again. Then the beating of the wings diminished, the terrified face grew pale, the floods of colour abated, and suddenly with a sob he lay prone, and the changing hues of the broken wings faded swiftly into one uniform dull grey hue.

“Oh! *what* has happened to me?” cried the Angel (for such it was), shuddering violently, hands outstretched and clutching the ground, and then lying still.

===Chinese===

教区牧师惊呆了，手里还攥着冒烟的枪。那根本不是什么鸟，而是一个少年，一张极其俊美的脸，身披藏红花色的袍子，背上一对虹彩的翅膀；他在痛苦中翻扭时，一道道色彩的巨浪——紫与绯红的晕、金绿与浓蓝的辉——在翼上你追我赶。教区牧师从没见过这样华美的色彩洪流：不是彩色玻璃窗，不是蝴蝶的翅膀，甚至不是棱镜间看到的晶体的光彩，地上一概的颜色都无法与之相比。天使两次撑起身子，都又侧身跌倒。随后翅膀的扑打渐弱，那张惊恐的脸渐渐发白，色彩的洪流渐渐平息，忽然他一声呜咽，俯身躺倒，折翼上变幻的色彩迅速褪成一片均匀的暗灰。

“哦！*我这是*怎么了？”天使（正是天使）大声喊道，浑身剧烈发抖，双手前伸抓住地面，随后便一动不动地躺着。

===Original===

“Dear me!” said the Vicar. “I had no idea.” He came forward cautiously. “Excuse me,” he said, “I am afraid I have shot you.”

It was the obvious remark.

===Chinese===

“天哪！”教区牧师说。“我完全没有想到。”他小心翼翼地走上前去。“恕我冒昧，”他说，“恐怕我把你打伤了。”

这话再自然不过了。

===Original===

The Angel seemed to become aware of his presence for the first time. He raised himself by one hand, his brown eyes stared into the Vicar’s. Then, with a gasp, and biting his nether lip, he struggled into a sitting position and surveyed the Vicar from top to toe.

“A man!” said the Angel, clasping his forehead; “a man in the maddest black clothes and without a feather upon him. Then I was not deceived. I am indeed in the Land of Dreams!”

===Chinese===

天使似乎头一回察觉到他的存在。他用一只手撑起身子，棕色的眼睛直望着教区牧师的眼睛。接着他倒吸一口气，咬住下唇，挣扎着坐起身来，把教区牧师从头到脚打量了一番。

“一个人！”天使双手抱住额头说，“一个穿着最疯癫的黑衣裳、身上连一根羽毛也没有的人。这么说，我没有受骗。我当真是在梦之国了！”

## XLIX The Last Day of the Visit (Continued) / 四十九 来访的最后一天（续）

===Original===

It was some minutes after the footsteps of the Angel had died away in the distance that Gotch raised himself on his hand. “By Jove!” he said. “Crump’s right.”

“Cut at the head, too!”

He put his hand to his face and felt the two weals running across it, hot and fat. “I’ll think twice before I lift my hand against a lunatic again,” said Sir John Gotch.

===Chinese===

天使的脚步声在远处消逝之后好几分钟，戈奇才用一只手撑起身子。“老天爷！”他说，“克伦普说得对。”

还照着脑袋抽了一下！”

他伸手摸脸，摸到两道横贯面孔的鞭痕，火辣辣地鼓了起来。“往后我要再对疯子动手，可得三思了，”约翰·戈奇爵士（Sir John Gotch）说。

===Original===

“He may be a person of weak intellect, but I’m damned if he hasn’t a pretty strong arm. *Phew!* He’s cut a bit clean off the top of my ear with that infernal lash.”

“That infernal horse will go galloping to the house in the approved dramatic style. Little Madam’ll be scared out of her wits. And I﻿ ﻿… I shall have to explain how it all happened. While she vivisects me with questions.

“I’m a jolly good mind to have spring guns and mantraps put in this preserve. Confound the Law!”

===Chinese===

“他兴许是个智力薄弱的人，可要说他的胳膊不厉害，我甘愿受罚。*嚯！*他那一记毒鞭，把我耳朵尖上的一小块都削了下来。”

“那匹该死的马准会照着戏剧里认可的路数，一路飞奔回宅子。小夫人会吓得魂不附体。而我……我还得把这一切是怎么发生的解释个明白。一边由她拿问题活活解剖我。”

“我真想干脆在这片猎苑里装上弹簧枪和捕人夹。该死的法律！”

## VIII The Vicar and the Angel (Continued) / 八 教区牧师与天使（续）

===Original===

“I have,” said the Angel, “a most unusual feeling﻿—*here*. Have had since sunrise. I don’t remember ever having any feeling﻿—*here* before.”

“Not pain, I hope,” said the Vicar.

“Oh no! It is quite different from that﻿—a kind of vacuous feeling.”

===Chinese===

“我有，”天使说，“一种极不寻常的感觉——就在*这儿*。打日出起就有了。我不记得从前在——*这儿*——可曾有过什么感觉。”

“但愿不是疼痛，”教区牧师说。

“哦，不是！同那个完全不同——是一种空落落的感觉。”

===Original===

“The atmospheric pressure, perhaps, is a little different,” the Vicar began, feeling his chin.

“And do you know, I have also the most curious sensations in my mouth﻿—almost as if﻿—it’s so absurd!﻿—as if I wanted to stuff things into it.”

“Bless me!” said the Vicar. “Of course! You’re hungry!”

===Chinese===

“也许是大气压有点不一样，”教区牧师摸着下巴说。

“还有你可知道，我嘴里也有最古怪的感觉——几乎像是——真荒唐！——像是想往里面塞东西。”

“我的天！”教区牧师说。“对了！你是饿了！”

===Original===

“Hungry!” said the Angel. “What’s that?”

“Don’t you eat?”

“Eat! The word’s quite new to me.”

===Chinese===

“饿了！”天使说。“那是什么？”

“你不吃东西吗？”

“吃！这个词儿对我太新鲜了。”

===Original===

“Put food into your mouth, you know. One has to here. You will soon learn. If you don’t, you get thin and miserable, and suffer a great deal﻿—*pain*, you know﻿—and finally you die.”

“Die!” said the Angel. “That’s another strange word!”

“It’s not strange here. It means leaving off, you know,” said the Vicar.

===Chinese===

“就是把食物放进嘴里，你晓得。在这儿非这样不可。你很快就会学会的。要是不吃，人就会消瘦、萎顿，吃好多苦头——就是*痛*，你懂——最后就死了。”

“死！”天使说。“又是一个新奇的词儿！”

“在这儿它可不新奇。意思就是停当、完了，”教区牧师说。

===Original===

“We never leave off,” said the Angel.

“You don’t know what may happen to you in this world,” said the Vicar, thinking him over. “Possibly if you are feeling hungry, and can feel pain and have your wings broken, you may even have to die before you get out of it again. At any rate you had better try eating. For my own part﻿—ahem!﻿—there are many more disagreeable things.”

“I suppose I *had* better Eat,” said the Angel. “If it’s not too difficult. I don’t like this ‘Pain’ of yours, and I don’t like this ‘Hungry.’ If your ‘Die’ is anything like it, I would prefer to Eat. What a very odd world this is!”

===Chinese===

“我们从来不停当，”天使说。

“在这个世界上你会碰上什么事，可说不定，”教区牧师说，一面把他细细打量。“倘若你真会觉得饿，又能觉出痛，翅膀还折了，那么没等你再脱身出去，说不定你还得死上一回。无论如何，你最好试试吃东西。至于我自己——咳咳！——比这难受的事儿还多着呢。”

“我看我*最好*还是*吃*一吃，”天使说。“只要不太难。我不喜欢你们这个『痛』，也不喜欢这个『饿』。要是你们的『死』同它们是一路货，我宁可*吃*。这个世界真是古怪得很！”

===Original===

“To die,” said the Vicar, “is generally considered worse than either pain or hunger.﻿ ﻿… It depends.”

“You must explain all that to me later,” said the Angel. “Unless I wake up. At present, please show me how to eat. If you will. I feel a kind of urgency.﻿ ﻿…”

“Pardon me,” said the Vicar, and offered an elbow. “If I may have the pleasure of entertaining you. My house lies yonder﻿—not a couple of miles from here.”

===Chinese===

“死，”教区牧师说，“人们通常认为比痛和饿都更糟糕。……不过也难说。”

“这些你往后都得讲给我听，”天使说，“除非我先醒了过来。眼下，请你教我怎么吃。劳驾了。我觉得有点等不及了。……”

“请，”教区牧师说着伸出一条臂弯。“若蒙赏光，就请让我尽一尽地主之谊。我家就在那边——离这儿不到两英里。”

===Original===

“*Your* House!” said the Angel a little puzzled; but he took the Vicar’s arm affectionately, and the two, conversing as they went, waded slowly through the luxuriant bracken, sun mottled under the trees, and on over the stile in the park palings, and so across the bee-swarming heather for a mile or more, down the hillside, home.

You would have been charmed at the couple could you have seen them. The Angel, slight of figure, scarcely five feet high, and with a beautiful, almost effeminate face, such as an Italian old Master might have painted. Indeed, there is one in the National Gallery (*Tobias and the Angel*, by some artist unknown) not at all unlike him so far as face and spirit go. He was robed simply in a purple-wrought saffron blouse, bare kneed and barefooted, with his wings (broken now, and a leaden grey) folded behind him. The Vicar was a short, rather stout figure, rubicund, red-haired, clean-shaven, and with bright ruddy brown eyes. He wore a piebald straw hat with a black ribbon, a very neat white tie, and a fine gold watch-chain. He was so greatly interested in his companion that it only occurred to him when he was in sight of the Vicarage that he had left his gun lying just where he had dropped it amongst the bracken.

He was rejoiced to hear that the pain of the bandaged wing fell rapidly in intensity.

===Chinese===

“*你的*家！”天使有点茫然地说；但他还是亲亲热热地挽住教区牧师的手臂，两人一边走一边谈，缓缓蹚过茂密的蕨丛——树下阳光斑驳——越过园子栅栏上的踏阶，又穿过蜜蜂成群嗡嗡的石南花丛走了一英里多，下了山坡，回家去了。

你要是瞧见他们俩，准会觉得可爱。天使身形纤细，身高不足五英尺，一张俊美得近乎柔媚的脸，就像意大利古代大师笔下画出来的一样。事实上，国家美术馆里就有一幅（《多比亚与天使》，作者已不可考），就面貌与神情而论，同他颇有几分相像。他身上只披着一件织有紫色花纹的藏红花色罩衫，赤着膝，光着脚，翅膀（如今折断了，铅灰色）收拢在背后。教区牧师是个矮胖的身材，面色红润，红头发，脸刮得干干净净，一双明亮的红棕色眼睛。他戴一顶缀黑缎带的杂色草帽，系一条十分整洁的白领带，挂着一条精致的金表链。他对这位同伴的兴趣实在太浓，直到牧师公馆已在望，才想起自己的枪还撂在蕨丛里，扔在原地没有拿。

听说上了绷带的那边翅膀痛得很快轻了下去，他很是欢喜。

## XXI After Dinner (Continued) / 二十一 餐后（续）

===Original===

And thus in the little bedroom over the gable we reach a first resting place in this story. And as we have been hard at it, getting our story spread out before you, it may be perhaps well to recapitulate a little.

Looking back you will see that much has been done; we began with a blaze of light “not uniform but broken all over by curving flashes like the waving of swords,” and the sound of a mighty harping, and the advent of an Angel with polychromatic wings.

Swiftly, dexterously, as the reader must admit, wings have been clipped, halo handled off, the glory clapped into coat and trousers, and the Angel made for all practical purposes a man, under a suspicion of being either a lunatic or an impostor. You have heard too, or at least been able to judge, what the Vicar and the Doctor and the Curate’s wife thought of the strange arrival. And further remarkable opinions are to follow.

===Chinese===

就这样，在山墙之上那间小小的卧室里，我们抵达了本故事的第一处歇脚地。我们一路忙着把这故事在你面前铺展开来，如今或许不妨稍稍回顾一番。

回头望去，你会看到已经发生了许多事；我们是从一道光的闪耀开始的——“并不均匀，而是满布着蜿蜒的闪光，宛如剑影翻飞”——还有洪亮的竖琴之声，以及一位背生五彩翅膀的天使的降临。

正如读者必定承认的那样，我们手脚麻利：翅膀剪了，光环摘了，那身荣光被塞进了上衣和长裤，天使就一切实际效用而言被造成了一个凡人，还背着要么是疯子、要么是骗子的嫌疑。你也已经听到——或者至少足以自行判断——教区牧师、医生和副牧师太太对这位古怪来客的看法。而更多的高见还在后头。

===Original===

The afterglow of the summer sunset in the northwest darkens into night and the Angel sleeps, dreaming himself back in the wonderful world where it is always light, and everyone is happy, where fire does not burn and ice does not chill; where rivulets of starlight go streaming through the amaranthine meadows, out to the seas of peace. He dreams, and it seems to him that once more his wings glow with a thousand colours and flash through the crystal air of the world from which he has come.

So he dreams. But the Vicar lies awake, too perplexed for dreaming. Chiefly he is troubled by the possibilities of Mrs. Mendham; but the evening’s talk has opened strange vistas in his mind, and he is stimulated by a sense as of something seen darkly by the indistinct vision of a hitherto unsuspected wonderland lying about his world. For twenty years now he has held his village living and lived his daily life, protected by his familiar creed, by the clamour of the details of life, from any mystical dreaming. But now interweaving with the familiar bother of his persecuting neighbour, is an altogether unfamiliar sense of strange new things.

===Chinese===

西北方向夏日落日的余晖暗成夜色，天使睡着了，梦中回到了那个奇妙的世界：那里永远光明，人人幸福，火烧不伤人，冰寒不了人；一道道星光的小溪流淌过不朽的花草原，汇入安宁之海。他做着梦，恍惚间自己的翅膀又焕发出千种色彩，闪过他来自的那个世界的澄澈天空。

他就这样做梦。教区牧师却醒着躺在床上，心事重重，全无睡意。最叫他烦心的是门德姆太太的种种可能；可晚间的谈话已在他头脑里打开一扇扇奇异的远景，刺激着他，仿佛借着模糊的目力幽幽瞥见：就在他的世界四周，躺着一个从未被疑心过的仙境。二十年来，他领着这个村子的圣俸，过着日常的日子，靠熟稔的信条、靠生活琐事的喧嚣护佑着，与一切神秘的梦想隔绝。可如今，同那位逼迫他的老对头的惯常烦扰交织在一起的，是一种全然陌生的、对新奇事物的感觉。

===Original===

There was something ominous in the feeling. Once, indeed, it rose above all other considerations, and in a kind of terror he blundered out of bed, bruised his shins very convincingly, found the matches at last, and lit a candle to assure himself of the reality of his own customary world again. But on the whole the more tangible trouble was the Mendham avalanche. Her tongue seemed to be hanging above him like the sword of Damocles. What might she not say of this business, before her indignant imagination came to rest?

And while the successful captor of the Strange Bird was sleeping thus uneasily, Gully of Sidderton was carefully unloading his gun after a wearisome blank day, and Sandy Bright was on his knees in prayer, with the window carefully fastened. Annie Durgan was sleeping hard with her mouth open, and Amory’s mother was dreaming of washing, and both of them had long since exhausted the topics of the Sound and the Glare. Lumpy Durgan was sitting up in his bed, now crooning the fragment of a tune and now listening intently for a sound he had heard once and longed to hear again. As for the solicitor’s clerk at Iping Hanger, he was trying to write poetry about a confectioner’s girl at Portburdock, and the Strange Bird was quite out of his head. But the ploughman who had seen it on the confines of Siddermorton Park had a black eye. That had been one of the more tangible consequences of a little argument about birds’ legs in the “Ship.” It is worthy of this passing mention, since it is probably the only known instance of an Angel causing anything of the kind.

===Chinese===

这感觉里有种不祥的意味。有一回，它当真压倒了一切别的念头，他怀着几分惊恐，跌跌撞撞地下了床，结结实实磕破了小腿，好不容易摸到火柴，点起一支蜡烛，好让自己重新确信这个习常世界的实在。但总的说来，更要紧、更具体的麻烦还是门德姆太太这场雪崩。她的舌头仿佛悬在他头顶，就像达摩克利斯之剑。在她那义愤的想象力平息下来之前，这桩事她什么样的话说不出来呢？

怪鸟的猎获者这般不安地睡着，与此同时，锡德顿的加利（Gully）在熬过乏味的一无所获的一天之后，正细心地给他的枪退膛；桑迪·布莱特（Sandy Bright）跪着祷告，窗子关得严严实实。安妮·德根（Annie Durgan）张着嘴酣睡，阿莫里的母亲梦见洗衣裳，她们俩早把那声响和那光亮的话题聊了个精光。“块头”德根（Lumpy Durgan）在床上坐起来，一会儿哼着几节曲子，一会儿凝神细听一个他听过一回、巴望再听一回的声音。至于伊平亨格（Iping Hanger）那位律师的书记员，他正琢磨着为波特伯多克（Portburdock）一位糖果店姑娘写诗，怪鸟早被他忘到九霄云外了。倒是那个在锡德莫顿园（Siddermorton Park）边界上见过怪鸟的庄稼汉挂了彩，眼睛乌青——那是他在“船”字号酒馆里为鸟腿问题小小争辩了一场留下的、比较实在的后果之一。此事值得一提带过，因为它多半是有史可考的唯一一例：一位天使竟惹出了这等事端。

## XXVII The Angel Explores the Village (Continued) / 二十七 天使探访村庄（续）

===Original===

After that incident the Angel walked along past the mill and round behind the church, to examine the tombstones.

“This seems to be the place where they put the broken pieces,” said the Angel﻿—reading the inscriptions. “Curious word﻿—relict! Resurgam! Then they are not done with quite. What a huge pile it requires to keep her down.﻿ ﻿… It is spirited of her.”

“Hawkins?” said the Angel softly,﻿ ﻿… “*Hawkins?* The name is strange to me.﻿ ﻿… He did not die then.﻿ ﻿… It is plain enough﻿—Joined the Angelic Hosts, May 17, 1863. He must have felt as much out of place as I do down here. But I wonder why they put that little pot thing on the top of this monument. Curious! There are several others about﻿—little stone pots with a rag of stiff stone drapery over them.”

===Chinese===

那场风波过后，天使一路走过磨坊，绕到教堂后面，去细看那些墓碑。

“这儿似乎就是他们安放破残零件的地方，”天使一面读着碑文说。“古怪的词儿——relict（遗孀）！Resurgam（我必复活）！这么说来，他们并没有彻底了结。要镇住她，得堆上好大一堆石头啊。……她倒真有股子劲儿。”

“霍金斯？”天使轻声念道，……“*霍金斯？*这名字我陌生得很。……这么说，他没有死。……明明白白写着呢——归入天使军，1863 年 5 月 17 日。他想必同我在这下界一样，觉得格格不入。只是我不明白，他们为什么在这碑顶上搁那么个小罐子似的东西。真古怪！四下里还有好几个——都是小石罐，上头还搭着一幅硬邦邦的石布。”

===Original===

Just then the boys came pouring out of the National School, and first one and then several stopped agape at the Angel’s crooked black figure among the white tombs. “Ent ’e gart a baak on en!” remarked one critic.

“ ’E’s got ’air like a girl!” said another.

The Angel turned towards them. He was struck by the queer little heads sticking up over the lichenous wall. He smiled faintly at their staring faces, and then turned to marvel at the iron railings that enclosed the Fitz-Jarvis tomb. “A queer air of uncertainty,” he said. “Slabs, piles of stone, these railings.﻿ ﻿… Are they afraid?﻿ ﻿… Do these Dead ever try and get up again? There’s an air of repression﻿—fortification﻿—”

===Chinese===

正在这时，男孩子们从国民学校里涌了出来，先是有一个、接着又有好几个，目瞪口呆地停在白色墓碑之间天使那黑黢黢的驼背身影前。“俺说，他背上还长着个包哩！”一位批评家发了话。

“他头发跟闺女家似的！”另一个说。

天使朝他们转过身去。一颗颗小脑袋从长满地衣的墙头上探出来，让他觉得稀奇。他对那些瞪视的小脸淡淡一笑，随即又转过去惊叹那圈围着菲茨-贾维斯（Fitz-Jarvis）家坟墓的铁栏杆。“一股古怪的、拿不准的气味，”他说，“石板，一堆堆石头，还有这些栏杆。……他们是在害怕吗？……这些死人有时还想再爬起来？有一股压制——设防——的气派”

===Original===

“Get yer *’air* cut, Get yer *’air* cut,” sang three little boys together.

“Curious these Human Beings are!” said the Angel. “That man yesterday wanted to cut off my wings, now these little creatures want me to cut off my hair! And the man on the bridge offered to take the ‘paint’ off me. They will leave nothing of me soon.”

“Where did you get that *’at*?” sang another little boy. “Where did you get them clo’es?”

===Chinese===

“去把*头发*铰铰，去把*头发*铰铰，”三个小男孩齐声唱道。

“这些人真是古怪！”天使说。“昨天那个人要剪掉我的翅膀，如今这些小东西又要我铰掉头发！桥上那个人还非要给我把‘漆’褪下来不可。要不了多久，他们就要把我身上弄得一件不剩了。”

“你那顶*帽子*哪儿弄来的？”另一个小男孩唱道。“你那身衣裳哪儿弄来的？”

===Original===

“They ask questions that they evidently do not want answered,” said the Angel. “I can tell from the tone.” He looked thoughtfully at the little boys. “I don’t understand the methods of Human intercourse. These are probably friendly advances, a kind of ritual. But I don’t know the responses. I think I will go back to the little fat man in black, with the gold chain across his stomach, and ask him to explain. It is difficult.”

He turned towards the lych gate. “*Oh!*” said one of the little boys, in a shrill falsetto, and threw a beechnut husk. It came bounding across the churchyard path. The Angel stopped in surprise.

This made all the little boys laugh. A second imitating the first, said “*Oh!*” and hit the Angel. His astonishment was really delicious. They all began crying “*Oh!*” and throwing beechnut husks. One hit the Angel’s hand, another stung him smartly by the ear. The Angel made ungainly movements towards them. He spluttered some expostulation and made for the roadway. The little boys were amazed and shocked at his discomfiture and cowardice. Such sawney behaviour could not be encouraged. The pelting grew vigorously. You may perhaps be able to imagine those vivid moments, daring small boys running in close and delivering shots, milder small boys rushing round behind with flying discharges. Milton Screever’s mongrel dog was roused to yelping ecstasy at the sight, and danced (full of wild imaginings) nearer and nearer to the angelic legs.

===Chinese===

“他们问的问题，分明并不想要回答，”天使说，“我从那口气里听得出来。”他若有所思地望着这群小男孩。“我不懂人类交往的法子。这些多半是友好的表示，一种仪式。可我不知道该怎样回礼。我想我还是回去找那个穿黑衣裳、肚子上横一条金链子的小胖子，请他解说解说。实在太难了。”

他朝墓园门楼走去。“*噢！*”一个小男孩用尖利的假嗓子喊了一声，扔过来一颗山毛榉果壳。果壳蹦蹦跳跳地滚过墓园小径。天使诧异地站住了。

这下子所有的小男孩都笑了。第二个学着第一个的样子，喊一声“*噢！*”，正好打中天使。他那副吃惊的模样实在太逗了。他们全都喊起“*噢！*”来，山毛榉果壳雨点般扔出。一颗打中天使的手，一颗狠狠掹在他耳朵边上。天使朝他们迈开笨拙的步子，气急败坏地咕哝了几句抗议，向大路退去。小男孩们见他这般狼狈、这般脓包，又是惊又是愤。这样的怂包行径断不可纵容。弹雨愈发猛烈。你也许想象得出那些活生生的时刻：胆大的孩子跑得贴近了投弹，斯文些的孩子绕到背后远远放箭。弥尔顿·斯克里弗（Milton Screever）家的那条杂种狗见了这光景，兴奋得狂吠不止，（满脑子狂想地）朝着那双天使的腿越舞越近。

===Original===

“Hi, hi!” said a vigorous voice. “I never did! Where’s Mr. Jarvis? Manners, manners! you young rascals.”

The youngsters scattered right and left, some over the wall into the playground, some down the street.

“Frightful pest these boys are getting!” said Crump, coming up. “I’m sorry they have been annoying you.”

===Chinese===

“喂，喂！”一个洪亮的声音说。“从没见过这种事！贾维斯先生上哪儿去了？讲点规矩，讲点规矩！你们这群小混蛋。”

孩子们四散奔逃，有的翻墙进了操场，有的顺着街道跑远了。

“这些男孩越闹越不像话，简直成了祸害！”克伦普走上来说。“他们搅扰了你，我很抱歉。”

===Original===

The Angel seemed quite upset. “I don’t understand,” he said. “These Human ways.﻿ ﻿…”

“Yes, of course. Unusual to you. How’s your excrescence?”

“My what?” said the Angel.

“Bifid limb, you know. How is it? Now you’re down this way, come in. Come in and let me have a look at it again. You young roughs! And meanwhile these little louts of ours will be getting off home. They’re all alike in these villages. *Can’t* understand anything abnormal. See an odd-looking stranger. Chuck a stone. No imagination beyond the parish.﻿ ﻿… (I’ll give you physic if I catch you annoying strangers again.)﻿ ﻿… I suppose it’s what one might expect.﻿ ﻿… Come along this way.”

So the Angel, horribly perplexed still, was hurried into the surgery to have his wound redressed.

===Chinese===

天使显得十分沮丧。“我不明白，”他说，“这些人类的做派。……”

“是啊，自然。于你是新鲜事。你那个赘生物怎么样了？”

“我的什么？”天使说。

“就是那个分叉的肢体，你晓得的。它怎么样了？既然你到了这边，进来吧。进来让我再瞧瞧它。你们这群小野孩！正好，咱们这些小浑蛋也该滚回家了。这些村子里的孩子全是一个样。凡是反常的东西一概*不能*理解。见着一个模样古怪的生人。扔块石头。想象力出不了教区一步。……（再让我撞见你们搅扰生人，就给你们开药吃。）……我想这也难怪。……打这边来。”

就这样，天使仍旧满心惶惑，被匆匆领进诊所，重新包扎伤口。

## XXXVII The Angel’s Debut (Continued) / 三十七 天使的初次亮相（续）

===Original===

“He’s drunk!” said Mr. Rathbone-Slater, breaking a terrific silence. “That’s the matter with *him*.”

Mrs. Jehoram laughed hysterically.

The Vicar stood up, motionless, staring. “Oh! I *forgot* to explain servants to him!” said the Vicar to himself in a swift outbreak of remorse. “I thought he *did* understand servants.”

===Chinese===

“他喝醉了！”拉思本-斯莱特先生（Mr. Rathbone-Slater）打破一片可怕的沉寂说。“*他*的毛病就在这儿。”

杰霍拉姆太太歇斯底里地笑了。

教区牧师站起身，一动不动，直勾勾地望着。“哦！我*忘了*给他解说仆人了！”教区牧师在一阵疾风骤雨般的懊悔中自言自语。“我原以为他*倒是*懂得仆人的。”

===Original===

“Really, Mr. Hilyer!” said Lady Hammergallow, evidently exercising enormous self-control and speaking in panting spasms. “Really, Mr. Hilyer!﻿—Your genius is *too* terrible. I must, I really *must*, ask you to take him home.”

So to the dialogue in the corridor of alarmed maidservant and well-meaning (but shockingly gauche) Angel﻿—appears the Vicar, his botryoidal little face crimson, gaunt despair in his eyes, and his necktie under his left ear.

“Come,” he said﻿—struggling with emotion. “Come away.﻿ ﻿… I.﻿ ﻿… I am disgraced forever.”

===Chinese===

“说真的，希利尔先生！”哈默加洛夫人说，显然正使出莫大的自制力，说话一阵一阵地喘。“说真的，希利尔先生！——您的天才*太*骇人了。我必须——我实在*必须*——请您送他回家。”

于是，在走廊里那场受惊的女仆同好心的（却笨拙得吓人的）天使之间的对话中间——出现了教区牧师：他那串葡萄似的小脸涨得通红，眼里是深切的绝望，领带歪到了左耳朵底下。

“来，”他说——强忍着情绪。“走吧。……我。……我算是永远丢尽了脸。”

===Original===

And the Angel stared for a second at him and obeyed﻿—meekly, perceiving himself in the presence of unknown but evidently terrible forces.

And so began and ended the Angel’s social career.

In the informal indignation meeting that followed, Lady Hammergallow took the (informal) chair. “I feel humiliated,” she said. “The Vicar assured me he was an exquisite player. I never imagined.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

天使盯着他看了一秒，便服从了——乖乖地服从，他察觉自己正置身于某种未知却显然可怕的力量面前。

天使的社交生涯就此开始，又就此结束。

在随后那场非正式的声讨会上，哈默加洛夫人担任了（非正式的）主席。“我觉得受了羞辱，”她说，“教区牧师向我担保他是位精致的乐手。我从没想到。……”

===Original===

“He was drunk,” said Mr. Rathbone-Slater. “You could tell it from the way he fumbled with his tea.”

“Such a *fiasco*!” said Mrs. Mergle.

“The Vicar assured me,” said Lady Hammergallow. “ ‘The man I have staying with me is a musical genius,’ he said. His very words.”

===Chinese===

“他是喝醉了，”拉思本-斯莱特先生说，“瞧他摆弄茶具的那副样子就看得出来。”

“真是*一场惨败*！”默格尔太太（Mrs. Mergle）说。

“教区牧师向我担保，”哈默加洛夫人说，“‘在我家做客的这位是个音乐天才，’他是这么说的。一字不差。”

===Original===

“His ears must be burning anyhow,” said Tommy Rathbone-Slater.

“I was trying to keep him quiet,” said Mrs. Jehoram. “By humouring him. And do you know the things he said to me﻿—there!”

“The thing he played,” said Mr. Wilmerdings, “﻿—I must confess I did not like to charge him to his face. But really! It was merely *drifting*.”

===Chinese===

“反正他的耳朵这会儿准在发烧，”汤米·拉思本-斯莱特说。

“我方才一直想让他安静下来，”杰霍拉姆太太说，“顺着哄着他。可你们知道他跟我说了些什么——就在那儿！”

“他拉的那个曲子，”威尔默丁斯先生说（Mr. Wilmerdings），“——我得承认，我不愿当面说他。可是说真的！那根本就是*信马由缰*。”

===Original===

“Just fooling with a fiddle, eigh?” said George Harringay. “Well I thought it was beyond me. So much of your fine music is﻿—”

“Oh, *George*!” said the younger Miss Pirbright.

“The Vicar was a bit on too﻿—to judge by his tie,” said Mr. Rathbone-Slater. “It’s a dashed rummy go. Did you notice how he fussed after the genius?”

===Chinese===

“不过是瞎摆弄一把提琴罢了，嗯？”乔治·哈林盖（George Harringay）说，“我原先还当是我听不懂呢。你们那些高雅音乐，好多不都是——”

“哦，*乔治*！”皮尔布赖特家的小姐说。

“照他那条领带看，教区牧师自己也有点上头了，”拉思本-斯莱特先生说，“这事真是邪门儿。你们注意到没有，他追在那位天才屁股后头手忙脚乱的那副样子？”

===Original===

“One has to be so very careful,” said the very eldest Miss Papaver.

“He told me he is in love with the Vicar’s housemaid!” said Mrs. Jehoram. “I almost laughed in his face.”

“The Vicar ought *never* to have brought him here,” said Mrs. Rathbone-Slater with decision.

===Chinese===

“这种事可得千万小心，”帕帕弗家最年长的小姐说（Miss Papaver）。

“他告诉我，他爱上了教区牧师家的女仆！”杰霍拉姆太太说，“我差点当着他的面笑出来。”

“教区牧师*根本*就不该带他上这儿来，”拉思本-斯莱特太太斩钉截铁地说。

## L The Last Day of the Visit (Continued) / 五十 来访的最后一天（续）

===Original===

But the Angel, thinking that Gotch was dead, went wandering off in a passion of remorse and fear through the brakes and copses along the Sidder. You can scarcely imagine how appalled he was at this last and overwhelming proof of his encroaching humanity. All the darkness, passion and pain of life seemed closing in upon him, inexorably, becoming part of him, chaining him to all that a week ago he had found strange and pitiful in men.

“Truly, this is no world for an Angel!” said the Angel. “It is a World of War, a World of Pain, a World of Death. Anger comes upon one﻿ ﻿… I who knew not pain and anger, stand here with blood stains on my hands. I have fallen. To come into this world is to fall. One must hunger and thirst and be tormented with a thousand desires. One must fight for foothold, be angry and strike﻿—”

===Chinese===

天使却以为戈奇死了，怀着狂烈的悔恨与恐惧，沿着锡德河岸的灌木丛和小树林一路游荡开去。你简直想象不出，这最后一桩、也最压倒一切的、证明他的人性正在步步进逼的事实，令他何等胆寒。生命里一切的黑暗、情欲和痛苦，似乎正无情地朝他围拢过来，化作他的一部分，把他锁在一周之前他还觉得又陌生又可怜的人间种种之上。

“真的，这绝不是天使待的世界！”天使说，“这是一个战争的世界，一个痛苦的世界，一个死亡的世界。怒火会袭上心头……我，一个从不识得痛苦与愤怒的人，如今站在这里，双手染着血污。我堕落了。落入这个世界，就是堕落。人必定要饥，要渴，要被千百种欲望煎熬。人必定要争立足之地，要动怒，要出手打——”

===Original===

He lifted up his hands to Heaven, the ultimate bitterness of helpless remorse in his face, and then flung them down with a gesture of despair. The prison walls of this narrow passionate life seemed creeping in upon him, certainly and steadily, to crush him presently altogether. He felt what all we poor mortals have to feel sooner or later﻿—the pitiless force of the Things that Must Be, not only without us but (where the real trouble lies) within, all the inevitable tormenting of one’s high resolves, those inevitable seasons when the better self is forgotten. But with us it is a gentle descent, made by imperceptible degrees over a long space of years; with him it was the horrible discovery of one short week. He felt he was being crippled, caked over, blinded, stupefied in the wrappings of this life, he felt as a man might feel who has taken some horrible poison, and feels destruction spreading within him.

He took no account of hunger or fatigue or the flight of time. On and on he went, avoiding houses and roads, turning away from the sight and sound of a human being in a wordless desperate argument with Fate. His thoughts did not flow but stood banked back in inarticulate remonstrance against his degradation. Chance directed his footsteps homeward and, at last, after nightfall, he found himself faint and weary and wretched, stumbling along over the moor at the back of Siddermorton. He heard the rats run and squeal in the heather, and once a noiseless big bird came out of the darkness, passed, and vanished again. And he saw without noticing it a dull red glow in the sky before him.

===Chinese===

他向天举起双手，脸上带着无可奈何的悔恨的至极苦楚，随后又绝望地一甩，垂了下来。这狭隘、狂烈的人生的狱墙，仿佛正一步一步、又稳又定地朝他逼来，眼看就要把他整个碾碎。他尝到了我们这些可怜凡人迟早都要尝到的滋味——那“必然之事”的无情力量，不但在我们身外，更（真正的苦楚就在这里）在我们心里；人高尚的决心注定要受尽折磨，那更好的自我注定要被一再忘却。只是在我们，这是一场缓缓的沉降，要在漫长的岁月里不知不觉地一分一寸落下去；而在他，这却是短短一周之内的骇人发现。他觉得自己正在残缺、板结、失明，被这一生的重重包裹闷得麻木；他觉得自己像个服下可怕毒药的人，感到毁灭正在体内蔓延。

他不曾理会饥饿、疲惫，也不曾理会时光的流逝。他只是走啊走，避开房舍和道路，一见到人影、一听到人声便掉头而去，同命运作着无言而绝望的抗辩。他的思绪并不流动，只是壅塞在那里，化作说不出口的、对自己沉沦的抗议。机缘引着他的脚步朝回家的方向去，最后，夜幕落下之后，他发觉自己又晕又乏又凄惶，正踉踉跄跄走在锡德莫顿后面的荒野上。他听见老鼠在石南丛里奔窜尖叫，有一回，一只无声的大鸟从黑暗里出来，掠过，又消失在黑暗里。他还看见前方天际有一团暗红的光——却并没有留意。

## LI The Last Day of the Visit (Continued) / 五十一 来访的最后一天（续）

===Original===

But when he came over the brow of the moor, a vivid light sprang up before him and refused to be ignored. He came on down the hill and speedily saw more distinctly what the glare was. It came from darting and trembling tongues of fire, golden and red, that shot from the windows and a hole in the roof of the Vicarage. A cluster of black heads, all the village in fact, except the fire-brigade﻿—who were down at Aylmer’s Cottage trying to find the key of the machine-house﻿—came out in silhouette against the blaze. There was a roaring sound, and a humming of voices, and presently a furious outcry. There was a shouting of “No! No!”﻿—“Come back!” and an inarticulate roar.

He began to run towards the burning house. He stumbled and almost fell, but he ran on. He found black figures running about him. The flaring fire blew gustily this way and that, and he smelt the smell of burning.

===Chinese===

可当他翻过荒野的山脊，一片刺目的光在眼前腾起，不容再置之不理。他顺着坡走下去，很快便看清了那火光的来历。它出自一条条飞窜颤动的火舌，金红两色，从牧师公馆的窗户和屋顶的窟窿里喷出来。一群黑压压的脑袋——其实是全村的人，只除了消防队——他们眼下正在艾尔默村舍（Aylmer's Cottage）那边忙着找机房门的钥匙——衬着熊熊烈焰显出一排剪影。一片咆哮声、一片人声嗡嗡，随即又是一阵狂怒的呼喊。有人高叫“不行！不行！”——“回来！”还有一声含混的吼叫。

他朝那燃烧的房子跑起来。他绊了一下，几乎跌倒，还是接着跑。他发觉四周都是奔来跑去的黑影。猎猎的火光一阵阵东歪西倒，他闻到了燃烧的气味。

===Original===

“She went in,” said one voice, “she went in.”

“The mad girl!” said another.

“Stand back! Stand back!” cried others.

===Chinese===

“她进去了，”一个声音说，“她进去了。”

“疯姑娘！”另一个说。

“退后！退后！”别的人喊道。

===Original===

He found himself thrusting through an excited, swaying crowd, all staring at the flames, and with the red reflection in their eyes.

“Stand back!” said a labourer, clutching him.

“What is it?” said the Angel. “What does this mean?”

===Chinese===

他发觉自己正从一群激动得摇来晃去的人里挤过去，人人都盯着火焰，眼睛里映着红光。

“退后！”一个庄稼汉一把抓住他说。

“出什么事了？”天使说。“这是怎么了？”

===Original===

“There’s a girl in the house, and she can’t get out!”

“Went in after a fiddle,” said another.

“ ’Tas hopeless,” he heard someone else say.

===Chinese===

“屋里有个姑娘，她出不来了！”

“为一把提琴进去的，”另一个人说。

“没指望了，”他又听见有人这么说。

===Original===

“I was standing near her. I heerd her. Says she: ‘I *can* get his fiddle.’ I heerd her﻿—Just like that! ‘I *can* get his fiddle.’ ”

For a moment the Angel stood staring. Then in a flash he saw it all, saw this grim little world of battle and cruelty, transfigured in a splendour that outshone the Angelic Land, suffused suddenly and insupportably glorious with the wonderful light of love and self-sacrifice. He gave a strange cry, and before anyone could stop him, was running towards the burning building. There were cries of “The Hunchback! The Fowener!”

The Vicar, whose scalded hand was being tied up, turned his head, and he and Crump saw the Angel, a black outline against the intense, red glare of the doorway. It was the sensation of the tenth of a second, yet both men could not have remembered that transitory attitude more vividly had it been a picture they had studied for hours together. Then the Angel was hidden by something massive (no one knew what) that fell, incandescent, across the doorway.

===Chinese===

“俺就站在她旁边。俺亲耳听见的。她说：‘俺*能*把他的提琴抢出来。’俺听见的——就是这么说的！‘俺*能*把他的提琴抢出来。’”

天使怔怔地站了片刻。忽然一闪念间，他全都明白了：他看见这个冷酷而小小的、争斗与残忍的世界，忽然披上一重胜过天使之国的光辉，霎时间充盈着爱人与自我牺牲的奇妙光华，灿烂得叫人承受不起。他发出一声奇异的喊叫，不等任何人拦住，已经朝那燃烧的房子奔去。四下响起喊声：“那驼子！那外乡佬！”

教区牧师正让人包扎烫伤的手，闻声转过头来，他和克伦普同时看见了天使——在门洞那片炽烈的红光映衬下的一个黑色剪影。那不过是一秒钟十分之一的瞬间印象，可这两个人的脑海里，纵然是对着一幅画一同端详几个钟头，也未必能把那转瞬即逝的姿态记得这般鲜明。随后，一样庞然大物（谁也不知道是什么）烧得通红，塌下来堵住了门洞，把天使遮没了。

## LII The Last Day of the Visit (Continued) / 五十二 来访的最后一天（续）

===Original===

There was a cry of “Delia” and no more. But suddenly the flames spurted out in a blinding glare that shot upward to an immense height, a blinding brilliance broken by a thousand flickering gleams like the waving of swords. And a gust of sparks, flashing in a thousand colours, whirled up and vanished. Just then, and for a moment by some strange accident, a rush of music, like the swell of an organ, wove into the roaring of the flames.

The whole village standing in black knots heard the sound, except Gaffer Siddons who is deaf﻿—strange and beautiful it was, and then gone again. Lumpy Durgan, the idiot boy from Sidderford, said it began and ended like the opening and shutting of a door.

===Chinese===

传来一声“迪莉娅（Delia）”的呼喊，再没有别的。可忽然间，火焰喷薄而出，化作一道刺目的强光，直冲上极高的高处，那炫目的光辉里闪动着千百点摇曳的光芒，宛如剑影翻飞。一阵火星，闪着千种颜色，打着旋腾起，随即消逝。就在那一刻，不知出于怎样奇异的机缘，一股乐声，像风琴的轰鸣，织进了烈火的咆哮之中。

黑压压成群站着的全村人都听见了这声音——只有耳背的西登斯老爹（Gaffer Siddons）除外——那声音又奇异又美丽，随即又消逝了。锡德福德（Sidderford）来的傻小子“块头”德根说，它开始和结束，就像一扇门开了又关上。

===Original===

But little Hetty Penzance had a pretty fancy of two figures with wings, that flashed up and vanished among the flames.

(And after that it was she began to pine for the things she saw in her dreams, and was abstracted and strange. It grieved her mother sorely at the time. She grew fragile, as though she was fading out of the world, and her eyes had a strange, faraway look. She talked of angels and rainbow colours and golden wings, and was forever singing an unmeaning fragment of an air that nobody knew. Until Crump took her in hand and cured her with fattening dietary, syrup of hypophosphites and cod liver oil.)

===Chinese===

只有小赫蒂·彭赞斯（Hetty Penzance）有一个美丽的幻想：她看见两个带翅膀的身影，在火焰中间一闪而起，随即消失了。

（自那以后，她便开始渴慕梦中所见的事物，人变得恍恍惚惚、古古怪怪。这让她母亲当时伤心透了。她日渐羸弱，仿佛正从这世界上渐渐消隐，眼睛里带着一种奇异的、遥远的眼神。她念叨着天使、虹彩和金色的翅膀，还总哼着一段谁也不晓得的无名曲子的残句。直到克伦普接手医治，用长胖的饭食、次磷酸盐合剂和鱼肝油把她治好。）

## XXXII A Trivial Incident / 三十二 一桩小事

===Original===

The Angel came thoughtfully by the hedge across the field towards the Vicarage. The rays of the setting sun shone on his shoulders, and touched the Vicarage with gold, and blazed like fire in all the windows. By the gate, bathed in the sunlight, stood little Delia, the waiting maid. She stood watching him under her hand. It suddenly came into the Angel’s mind that she, at least, was beautiful, and not only beautiful but alive and warm.

She opened the gate for him and stood aside. She was sorry for him, for her elder sister was a cripple. He bowed to her, as he would have done to any woman, and for just one moment looked into her face. She looked back at him and something leapt within her.

The Angel made an irresolute movement. “Your eyes are very beautiful,” he said quietly, with a remote wonder in his voice.

===Chinese===

天使一路若有所思，沿着田野间的树篱朝牧师公馆走来。落日的余晖照在他肩头，给牧师公馆镀上一层金色，又在所有的窗玻璃上烧得像火。大门口，沐浴在夕阳里站着小迪莉娅，那位侍女。她抬手遮着眉目，站在那里望着他。天使心里忽然一动：她，至少，是美丽的，不但美丽，而且鲜活、温暖。

她替他打开大门，退立一旁。她怜惜他，因为她的姐姐就是个残废。他向她鞠了一躬，如同他对任何女子都会做的那样，并且就那么一瞬，望进了她的脸。她回望着他，心里有什么东西跳了一下。

天使做了一个迟疑的动作。“你的眼睛真美，”他轻声说，声音里带着一种远远的惊叹。

===Original===

“Oh, sir!” she said, starting back. The Angel’s expression changed to perplexity. He went on up the pathway between the Vicar’s flowerbeds, and she stood with the gate held open in her hand, staring after him. Just under the rose-twined verandah he turned and looked at her.

She still stared at him for a moment, and then with a queer gesture turned round with her back to him, shutting the gate as she did so, and seemed to be looking down the valley towards the church tower.

===Chinese===

“哎呀，先生！”她说着惊退了一步。天使的神情转为困惑。他顺着教区牧师花圃间的小径继续往上走，她则手里扶着敞开的门，站在那里目送他的背影。就在玫瑰缠绕的游廊底下，他回过头来看了看她。

她还怔怔地望了他片刻，随后做了个古怪的手势，转过身去背对着他，随手把门带上了，仿佛在朝山谷那头的教堂钟楼眺望。

## XIX After Dinner (Continued) / 十九 餐后（续）

===Original===

So the Angel dined and talked to the Vicar, and presently the night came and he was overtaken by yawning.

“Yah﻿—oh!” said the Angel suddenly. “Dear me! A higher power seemed suddenly to stretch my mouth open and a great breath of air went rushing down my throat.”

“You yawned,” said the Vicar. “Do you never yawn in the angelic country?”

===Chinese===

就这样，天使用了晚饭，同教区牧师谈着话，不久夜幕降临，他被一阵哈欠袭了上来。

“呀——啊！”天使忽然叫了一声。“天哪！仿佛有一种更高的力量猛地撑开了我的嘴，一大口空气直灌进我的喉咙。”

“你打哈欠了，”教区牧师说，“在天使的国度里，你们从来不打哈欠吗？”

===Original===

“Never,” said the Angel.

“And yet you are immortal!﻿—I suppose you want to go to bed.”

“Bed!” said the Angel. “Where’s that?”

===Chinese===

“从来不。”天使说。

“可你们竟是不朽的！——我看你想睡觉了吧。”

“睡觉！”天使说。“那是什么？”

===Original===

So the Vicar explained darkness to him and the art of going to bed. (The Angels, it seems sleep only in order to dream, and dream, like primitive man, with their foreheads on their knees. And they sleep among the white poppy meadows in the heat of the day.) The Angel found the bedroom arrangements quaint enough.

“Why is everything raised up on big wooden legs?” he said. “You have the floor, and then you put everything you have upon a wooden quadruped. Why do you do it?” The Vicar explained with philosophical vagueness. The Angel burnt his finger in the candle-flame﻿—and displayed an absolute ignorance of the elementary principles of combustion. He was merely charmed when a line of fire ran up the curtains. The Vicar had to deliver a lecture on fire so soon as the flame was extinguished. He had all kinds of explanations to make﻿—even the soap needed explaining. It was an hour or more before the Angel was safely tucked in for the night.

===Chinese===

于是教区牧师给他讲解了黑暗的道理和上床睡觉的学问。（看来天使们睡觉只为做梦，而且像原始人那样，额头抵着膝盖睡。他们在白昼最热的时分睡在白罂粟的花田里。）天使觉得这卧室里的种种安排实在新奇得很。

“为什么样样东西都架在四条大木腿上？”他说，“明明有地板，你们却偏要把所有家什都架在一只木头四脚兽上。这是为什么？”教区牧师含含糊糊地作了一番哲理上的解释。天使把手指伸进了烛火里烫了一下——他对燃烧的基本原理全然无知。窗帘上窜起一线火苗，他反倒只觉得有趣。火焰扑灭之后，教区牧师只得马上发表了一篇关于火的讲演。他样样都得解说一番——连肥皂也需要解说。过了一个多钟头，天使才被安顿妥当，掖好被角过夜。

===Original===

“He’s very beautiful,” said the Vicar, descending the staircase, quite tired out; “and he’s a real angel no doubt. But I am afraid he will be a dreadful anxiety, all the same, before he gets into our earthly way with things.”

He seemed quite worried. He helped himself to an extra glass of sherry before he put away the wine in the cellaret.

===Chinese===

“他真是美丽，”教区牧师走下楼梯，已是疲惫不堪，“毫无疑问，他是一位真正的天使。可是，在他学会咱们人间这一套行事之前，我怕他还是一件叫人操心到极点的事。”

他显得相当发愁。把酒收进酒柜之前，他给自己多斟了一杯雪利酒。

## XVIII After Dinner / 十八 餐后

===Original===

The Angel and the Vicar sat at dinner. The Vicar, with his napkin tucked in at his neck, watched the Angel struggling with his soup. “You will soon get into the way of it,” said the Vicar. The knife and fork business was done awkwardly but with effect. The Angel looked furtively at Delia, the little waiting maid. When presently they sat cracking nuts﻿—which the Angel found congenial enough﻿—and the girl had gone, the Angel asked: “Was that a lady, too?”

“Well,” said the Vicar (*crack*). “No﻿—she is not a lady. She is a servant.”

“Yes,” said the Angel; “she *had* rather a nicer shape.”

===Chinese===

天使和教区牧师坐下来吃晚饭。教区牧师把餐巾掖在脖领下，看着天使同汤较劲。“你很快就会上道的，”教区牧师说。刀叉的功夫做得笨拙，倒也有效。天使偷偷打量着小侍女迪莉娅。待到两人坐下来磕坚果——天使觉得这营生相当对脾气——姑娘也退下之后，天使问道：“方才那位，也是一位女士吗？”

“这个嘛，”教区牧师说（*咔*）。“不是——她不是女士。她是个仆人。”

“是啊，”天使说，“她的身段*倒*更匀称些。”

===Original===

“You mustn’t tell Mrs. Mendham that,” said the Vicar, covertly satisfied.

“She didn’t stick out so much at the shoulders and hips, and there was more of her in between. And the colour of her robes was not discordant﻿—simply neutral. And her face﻿—”

“Mrs. Mendham and her daughters had been playing tennis,” said the Vicar, feeling he ought not to listen to detraction even of his mortal enemy. “Do you like these things﻿—these nuts?”

===Chinese===

“这话你可千万别告诉门德姆太太，”教区牧师说，心里暗暗得意。

“她肩膀和胯部没有鼓得那么厉害，中间倒更饱满些。她衣裳的颜色也不刺眼——就是素净。还有她的脸——”

“门德姆太太和她的女儿们方才打网球来着，”教区牧师说，觉得即便是死对头，也不该听人贬损。“你喜欢这些东西吗——这些坚果？”

===Original===

“Very much,” said the Angel. *Crack.*

“You see,” said the Vicar (*Chum, chum, chum*). “For my own part I entirely believe you are an angel.”

“Yes!” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“非常喜欢。”天使说。*咔。*

“你要知道，”教区牧师说（*嗑、嗑、嗑*）。“就我本人而言，我完全相信你是一位天使。”

“是啊！”天使说。

===Original===

“I shot you﻿—I saw you flutter. It’s beyond dispute. In my own mind. I admit it’s curious and against my preconceptions, but﻿—practically﻿—I’m assured, perfectly assured in fact, that I saw what I certainly did see. But after the behaviour of these people. (*Crack*). I really don’t see how we are to persuade people. Nowadays people are so very particular about evidence. So that I think there is a great deal to be said for the attitude you assume. Temporarily at least I think it would be best of you to do as you propose to do, and behave as a man as far as possible. Of course there is no knowing how or when you may go back. After what has happened (*Gluck, gluck, gluck*﻿—as the Vicar refills his glass)﻿—after what has happened I should not be surprised to see the side of the room fall away, and the hosts of heaven appear to take you away again﻿—take us both away even. You have so far enlarged my imagination. All these years I have been forgetting Wonderland. But still﻿—. It will certainly be wiser to break the thing gently to them.”

“This life of yours,” said the Angel. “I’m still in the dark about it. How do you begin?”

“Dear me!” said the Vicar. “Fancy having to explain that! We begin existence here, you know, as babies, silly pink helpless things wrapped in white, with goggling eyes, that yelp dismally at the font. Then these babies grow larger and become even beautiful﻿—when their faces are washed. And they continue to grow to a certain size. They become children, boys and girls, youths and maidens (*Crack*), young men and young women. That is the finest time in life, according to many﻿—certainly the most beautiful. Full of great hopes and dreams, vague emotions and unexpected dangers.”

===Chinese===

“是我开的枪——我亲眼见你扑腾。这无可争辩。在我心里。我承认这事稀奇，同我原先的成见相悖，可是——实际上——我确信，千真万确地确信，我看见了我必定看见的东西。可是瞧瞧这些人的做派。（*咔*。）我实在看不出咱们怎样才能说服众人。如今的人对证据讲究得很。所以我认为，你摆出的那个姿态大有道理。至少暂时如此，我想你最好还是照你自己说的办，尽可能像个凡人那样行事。当然，谁也说不上你会在何时、以何种方式回去。经历了这些之后（*咕噜、咕噜、咕噜*——教区牧师正在给自己斟酒）——经历了这些之后，哪怕我看见这面墙向旁边退去，天军显现，把你接走——甚至把咱们俩一齐接走，我也不会惊讶。你把我的想象力拓展到了这个地步。这么多年，我一直把仙境忘了。不过——。无论如何，把这事儿和风细雨地透露给他们，总归更稳妥。”

“你们的这个生命，”天使说，“我还是摸不着头脑。是怎么开头的？”

“天哪！”教区牧师说。“居然得解说这个！你要知道，我们在这世上开始存在时，是些婴孩——粉嘟嘟、傻乎乎、无依无靠的小东西，裹在白布里，瞪着一双圆眼睛，在洗礼盆边可怜巴巴地啼哭。随后这些婴孩长大些，甚至变好看了——洗过脸之后。他们继续长到一个尺寸。他们成了儿童，男孩和女孩，少年和少女（*咔*），青年男子和年轻女子。许多人说，那是一生中最好的时光——无疑也是最美好的时光。满怀着远大的希望和梦想、朦胧的情感和意外丛生的危险。”

===Original===

“*That* was a maiden?” said the Angel, indicating the door through which Delia had disappeared.

“Yes,” said the Vicar, “that was a maiden.” And paused thoughtfully.

“And then?”

===Chinese===

“*那位*就是少女？”天使指着迪莉娅消失的那扇门说。

“是啊，”教区牧师说，“那就是一位少女。”他若有所思地停了停。

“再往后呢？”

===Original===

“Then,” said the Vicar, “the glamour fades and life begins in earnest. The young men and young women pair off﻿—most of them. They come to me shy and bashful, in smart ugly dresses, and I marry them. And then little pink babies come to them, and some of the youths and maidens that were, grow fat and vulgar, and some grow thin and shrewish, and their pretty complexions go, and they get a queer delusion of superiority over the younger people, and all the delight and glory goes out of their lives. So they call the delight and glory of the younger ones, Illusion. And then they begin to drop to pieces.”

“Drop to pieces!” said the Angel. “How grotesque!”

“Their hair comes off and gets dull coloured or ashen grey,” said the Vicar. “*I*, for instance.” He bowed his head forward to show a circular shining patch the size of a florin. “And their teeth come out. Their faces collapse and become as wrinkled and dry as a shrivelled apple. ‘Corrugated’ you called mine. They care more and more for what they have to eat and to drink, and less and less for any of the other delights of life. Their limbs get loose in the joints, and their hearts slack, or little pieces from their lungs come coughing up. Pain.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“往后，”教区牧师说，“光华褪去，人生才算真刀真枪地开始。青年男女配成对——他们大多数。他们穿着漂亮而难看的衣裳，羞羞答答、扭扭捏捏地来找我，由我给他们主持婚礼。随后粉红的小婴孩降临到他们那里，从前的少年少女，有的长得臃肿粗俗，有的变得干瘦泼辣，姣好的面色消退了，还生出一种自以为高年轻人一等的古怪错觉，生活中的欢乐和光彩从此和他们无缘。于是他们把年轻人的欢乐和光彩唤作：幻觉。再往后，他们就开始散架了。”

“散架！”天使说。“多古怪！”

“他们的头发脱落，颜色变得晦暗，或者灰白，”教区牧师说，“*我*就是个例子。”他低头前倾，露出一块弗罗林硬币大小的圆形秃斑。“他们的牙齿掉出来。脸塌陷下去，皱缩干瘪得像一只蔫了的苹果。你方才不就管我的脸叫‘起瓦楞’么。他们对吃喝越来越上心，对人生其余的乐趣越来越淡漠。他们四肢的关节松了，心脏懈怠了，要不就是一小块一小块的肺给咳出来。痛。……”

===Original===

“Ah!” said the Angel.

“Pain comes into their lives more and more. And then they go. They do not like to go, but they have to﻿—out of this world, very reluctantly, clutching its pain at last in their eagerness to stop.﻿ ﻿…”

“Where do they go?”

===Chinese===

“啊！”天使说。

“痛越来越多地进入他们的生活。然后他们就走了。他们不愿意走，却不得不走——离开这个世界，万般不舍，到了末了，在他们巴望停下来的急切里，连那痛也紧紧攥住不放。……”

“他们到哪儿去？”

===Original===

“Once I thought I knew. But now I am older I know I do not know. We have a legend﻿—perhaps it is not a legend. One may be a churchman and disbelieve. Stokes says there is nothing in it.﻿ ﻿…” The Vicar shook his head at the bananas.

“And you?” said the Angel. “Were you a little pink baby?”

“A little while ago I was a little pink baby.”

===Chinese===

“从前我以为自己知道。可如今我老了，我知道我并不知道。我们有个传说——也许不止是传说。身为教中人，也可以不信。斯托克斯（Stokes）就说那里面空无一物。……”教区牧师对着香蕉摇了摇头。

“那你呢？”天使说。“你也当过粉红的小婴孩吗？”

“不久以前，我还是个粉红的小婴孩。”

===Original===

“Were you robed then as you are now?”

“Oh no! Dear me! What a queer idea! Had long white clothes, I suppose, like the rest of them.”

“And then you were a little boy?”

===Chinese===

“你那时也穿现在这身衣裳吗？”

“哦不！天哪！多古怪的念头！想必同大家一样，包着长长的白布吧。”

“后来你就是个小男孩？”

===Original===

“A little boy.”

“And then a glorious youth?”

“I was not a very glorious youth, I am afraid. I was sickly, and too poor to be radiant, and with a timid heart. I studied hard and pored over the dying thoughts of men long dead. So I lost the glory, and no maiden came to me, and the dullness of life began too soon.”

===Chinese===

“一个小男孩。”

“再后来就是个神采飞扬的少年？”

“我怕自己算不上怎么神采飞扬的少年。我体弱多病，穷得发不出光彩，心又怯。我用功苦读，埋首于早已作古之人的临终思绪。就这样，那光彩失掉了，也没有少女来到我身边，人生的黯淡开始得太早。”

===Original===

“And you have your little pink babies?”

“None,” said the Vicar with a scarce perceptible pause. “Yet all the same, as you see, I am beginning to drop to pieces. Presently my back will droop like a wilting flowerstalk. And then, in a few thousand days more I shall be done with, and I shall go out of this world of mine.﻿ ﻿… Whither I do not know.”

“And you have to eat like this every day?”

===Chinese===

“那你自己的粉红小婴孩呢？”

“一个也没有，”教区牧师说，停顿几乎难以察觉。“尽管如此，你也瞧见了，我已经开始散架。用不了多久，我的背就会像蔫了的花茎一样弯下去。再往后，几千个日子一过，我就完事了，就要走出我的这个世界了。……去往何方，我不知道。”

“你就得天天这样吃？”

===Original===

“Eat, and get clothes and keep this roof above me. There are some very disagreeable things in this world called cold and rain. And the other people here﻿—how and why is too long a story﻿—have made me a kind of chorus to their lives. They bring their little pink babies to me and I have to say a name and some other things over each new pink baby. And when the children have grown to be youths and maidens, they come again and are confirmed. You will understand that better later. Then before they may join in couples and have pink babies of their own, they must come again and hear me read out of a book. They would be outcast, and no other maiden would speak to the maiden who had a little pink baby without I had read over her for twenty minutes out of my book. It’s a necessary thing, as you will see. Odd as it may seem to you. And afterwards when they are falling to pieces, I try and persuade them of a strange world in which I scarcely believe myself, where life is altogether different from what they have had﻿—or desire. And in the end, I bury them, and read out of my book to those who will presently follow into the unknown land. I stand at the beginning, and at the zenith, and at the setting of their lives. And on every seventh day, I who am a man myself, I who see no further than they do, talk to them of the Life to Come﻿—the life of which we know nothing. If such a life there be. And slowly I drop to pieces amidst my prophesying.”

“What a strange life!” said the Angel.

“Yes,” said the Vicar. “What a strange life! But the thing that makes it strange to me is new. I had taken it as a matter of course until you came into my life.”

===Chinese===

“要吃，还要添衣裳，保住头顶这片屋顶。这世上有几样很讨人嫌的东西，叫作寒冷和雨水。这里的其他人——怎么回事、为什么，说来话长——让我成了他们人生的一种伴奏。他们把粉红的小婴孩抱到我这里，我得给每个新的粉红小婴孩起个名字，再念诵些别的。等孩子长成少年少女，他们又来，受坚信礼。这个你往后会更明白。然后，在他们结成一对、生养自己的粉红小婴孩之前，他们还得再来，听我诵读一册书。不然就要被众人弃绝，哪个少女也不肯同没有经我捧着书为她念过二十分钟就有了粉红小婴孩的少女说话。你会明白，这是必不可少的事。尽管在你看来未免古怪。再往后，等他们开始散架，我还要劝他们信一个连我自己都几乎不信的奇异世界——那里的生活同他们经历过的、盼望过的全然不同。最后，我埋葬他们，又对着那些不久也要跟去未知之地的人诵读我的书。我站在他们人生的开端、顶点和落照之处。每逢第七天，我这个人——看得并不比他们更远的人——对他们讲论来世——那我们一无所知的人生。倘若真有那么一种人生的话。我就这样在讲道声中慢慢地散了架。”

“多奇怪的人生啊！”天使说。

“是啊，”教区牧师说。“多奇怪的人生！可叫我觉出这奇怪的，倒是件新鲜事。在你走进我的生活之前，我一直把这当成理所当然。”

===Original===

“This life of ours is so insistent,” said the Vicar. “It, and its petty needs, its temporary pleasures (*Crack*) swathe our souls about. While I am preaching to these people of mine of another life, some are ministering to one appetite and eating sweets, others﻿—the old men﻿—are slumbering, the youths glance at the maidens, the grown men protrude white waistcoats and gold chains, pomp and vanity on a substratum of carnal substance, their wives flaunt garish bonnets at one another. And I go on droning away of the things unseen and unrealised﻿—‘Eye hath not seen,’ I read, ‘nor ear heard, nor hath it entered into the imagination of man to conceive,’ and I look up to catch an adult male immortal admiring the fit of a pair of three and sixpenny gloves. It is damping year after year. When I was ailing in my youth I felt almost the assurance of vision that beneath this temporary phantasm world was the real world﻿—the enduring world of the Life Everlasting. But now﻿—”

He glanced at his chubby white hand, fingering the stem of his glass. “I have put on flesh since then,” he said. (*Pause*).

“I have changed and developed very much. The battle of the Flesh and Spirit does not trouble me as it did. Every day I feel less confidence in my beliefs, and more in God. I live, I am afraid, a quiescent life, duties fairly done, a little ornithology and a little chess, a trifle of mathematical trifling. My times are in His hands﻿—”

The Vicar sighed and became pensive. The Angel watched him, and the Angel’s eyes were troubled with the puzzle of him. “Gluck, gluck, gluck,” went the decanter as the Vicar refilled his glass.

===Chinese===

“我们的这个人生，缠人的本事真大，”教区牧师说。“它，连同它琐碎的需索、一时的欢愉（*咔*），把我们的灵魂层层裹住。当我向我的这些人们讲说另一种人生时，有的正在满足口腹之欲吃糖果，别的——那些老头儿——在打盹，少年们瞟着少女们，成年男人们挺出白背心和金表链，浮华与虚荣底下垫着一层血肉的基质，他们的太太们互相炫着扎眼的帽子。而我还在絮絮叨叨讲那些看不见、未实现的东西——‘眼睛未曾看见’，我念道，‘耳朵未曾听见，也未曾进入人心的想象’，我一抬头，却撞见一位长生不老的成年男子，正在欣赏一双三先令六便士手套的合手。这年复一年，着实叫人心灰。年轻时我卧病在床，几乎有一种幻视般的笃定：这昙花一现的幻象世界底下，才是真实的世界——那永生不朽的世界。可是如今——”

他瞧了瞧自己白胖的手，手指摩挲着酒杯的杯脚。“从那以后我发福了，”他说。（*停顿。*）

“我变了很多，也长进了很多。肉身与灵魂之战，已不像当年那样搅扰我。我一天天对自己的信条信心渐少，对上帝信心渐多。我过的，恐怕是一种清静无为的日子：本分尽得还算周全，搞一点鸟类学，下一点棋，再消遣一点数学上的小玩意儿。我的时日握在他手里——”

教区牧师叹了口气，沉思起来。天使望着他，眼光里满是对此人的困惑。“咕噜、咕噜、咕噜，”酒瓶响着，教区牧师又给自己斟上了一杯。

## XI At the Vicarage (Continued) / 十一 在牧师公馆（续）

===Original===

“Those,” said the Vicar, “were ladies.”

“How grotesque,” said the Angel, smiling and smelling the sweet briar. “And such quaint shapes!”

“Possibly,” said the Vicar. “Did you, *ahem*, notice how they behaved?”

===Chinese===

“方才那些，”教区牧师说，“是女士们。”

“多别致啊，”天使微笑着，嗅着甜蔷薇说。“还有那么些古怪的形状！”

“也许吧，”教区牧师说。“你可曾，*咳咳*，留意到她们的举止？”

===Original===

“They went away. Seemed, indeed, to run away. Frightened? I, of course, was frightened at things without wings. I hope﻿—they were not frightened at my wings?”
