“At your appearance generally,” said the Vicar, glancing involuntarily at the pink feet.

“Dear me! It never occurred to me. I suppose I seemed as odd to them as you did to me.” He glanced down. “And my feet. *You* have hoofs like a hippogriff.”

===Chinese===

“她们走开了。瞧那样子，简直是逃开的。是受惊了？当然，我见了没翅膀的东西也受惊。我希望——她们总不是被我的翅膀吓着的吧？”

“是被你这整个的模样，”教区牧师说，眼睛不由自主地瞟了瞟那双粉红的赤脚。

“天哪！我压根儿没想到。想必我在她们眼里，就同你在我眼里一样古怪。”他朝下瞥了一眼。“还有我的脚。*你们*的蹄子倒像鹰马。”

===Original===

“Boots,” corrected the Vicar.

“Boots, you call them! But anyhow, I am sorry I alarmed﻿—”

“You see,” said the Vicar, stroking his chin, “our ladies, *ahem*, have peculiar views﻿—rather inartistic views﻿—about, *ahem*, clothing. Dressed as you are, I am afraid, I am really afraid that﻿—beautiful as your costume certainly is﻿—you will find yourself somewhat, *ahem*, somewhat isolated in society. We have a little proverb, ‘When in Rome, *ahem*, one must do as the Romans do.’ I can assure you that, assuming you are desirous to, *ahem*, associate with us﻿—during your involuntary stay﻿—”

===Chinese===

“是靴子，”教区牧师纠正道。

“靴子，你们管这叫靴子！可不管怎么说，很抱歉我惊动了——”

“你要知道，”教区牧师摸着下巴说，“我们的女士们，*咳咳*，对于，*咳咳*，衣着，有些特别的见解——相当不艺术的见解。照你眼下这身打扮，恐怕，我实在恐怕——尽管你这套行头无疑很美——你会发觉自己在社交场上多少有点，*咳咳*，多少有点孤立。我们有句小小的谚语：‘身在罗马，*咳咳*，就得照罗马人的规矩办。’我可以向你担保，倘若你愿意，*咳咳*，同我们交往——在你这不情愿的盘桓期间——”

===Original===

The Angel retreated a step or so as the Vicar came nearer and nearer in his attempt to be diplomatic and confidential. The beautiful face grew perplexed. “I don’t quite understand. Why do you keep making these noises in your throat? Is it Die or Eat, or any of those.﻿ ﻿…”

“As your host,” interrupted the Vicar, and stopped.

“As my host,” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

教区牧师为了显得委婉而推心置腹，一步一步凑近，天使退开了一步光景。那张俊美的脸露出困惑。“我不大明白。你为什么老在喉咙里发出这些声音？是‘死’还是‘吃’，还是那一类里的什么。……”

“作为你的主人，”教区牧师插话说，随即打住了。

“作为我的主人，”天使说。

===Original===

“*Would* you object, pending more permanent arrangements, to invest yourself, *ahem*, in a suit, an entirely new suit I may say, like this I have on?”

“Oh!” said the Angel. He retreated so as to take in the Vicar from top to toe. “Wear clothes like yours!” he said. He was puzzled but amused. His eyes grew round and bright, his mouth puckered at the corners.

“Delightful!” he said, clapping his hands together. “What a mad, quaint dream this is! Where are they?” He caught at the neck of the saffron robe.

“Indoors!” said the Vicar. “This way. We will change﻿—indoors!”

===Chinese===

“你可愿意——在更长久的安排定妥之前——给自己穿上，*咳咳*，一套衣裳，一套全新的衣裳，就像我身上这一套？”

“哦！”天使说。他退开去，好把教区牧师从头到脚打量个全。“穿你那样的衣裳！”他说。他既困惑又觉得有趣。眼睛瞪得圆圆的发亮，嘴角也抿了起来。

“太有趣了！”他说着，两只手掌一合。“这是多疯癫、多古怪的一场梦啊！衣裳在哪儿？”他一把抓住藏红花色袍子的领口。

“屋里！”教区牧师说。“这边来。咱们到屋里——去换！”

## X At the Vicarage / 十 在牧师公馆

===Original===

The Curate’s wife and her two daughters and Mrs. Jehoram were still playing at tennis on the lawn behind the Vicar’s study, playing keenly and talking in gasps about paper patterns for blouses. But the Vicar forgot and came in that way.

They saw the Vicar’s hat above the rhododendrons, and a bare curly head beside him. “I must ask him about Susan Wiggin,” said the Curate’s wife. She was about to serve, and stood with a racket in one hand and a ball between the fingers of the other. “*He* really ought to have gone to see her﻿—being the Vicar. Not George. I﻿—*Ah!*”

For the two figures suddenly turned the corner and were visible. The Vicar, arm in arm with﻿—

===Chinese===

副牧师太太带着她的两个女儿，还有杰霍拉姆太太，仍在教区牧师书房后面的草坪上打网球，打得起劲，说话直喘，谈的是女衬衫的纸样。偏偏教区牧师忘了这茬，从那边进来了。

她们先看见杜鹃花丛上露出教区牧师的帽子，旁边还有一颗赤裸裸的鬈发脑袋。“我得问问苏珊·威金的事，”副牧师太太说。她正要发球，一手握着球拍，另一手的指间夹着球。“*他*才是教区牧师，实在该他去看看。不该乔治去。我——*啊！*”

因为那两个人影忽然一拐弯，就露在了眼前。教区牧师，同——挽着臂，同——

===Original===

You see, it came on the Curate’s wife suddenly. The Angel’s face being towards her she saw nothing of the wings. Only a face of unearthly beauty in a halo of chestnut hair, and a graceful figure clothed in a saffron garment that barely reached the knees. The thought of those knees flashed upon the Vicar at once. He too was horrorstruck. So were the two girls and Mrs. Jehoram. All horrorstruck. The Angel stared in astonishment at the horrorstruck group. You see, he had never seen anyone horrorstruck before.

“*Mis*﻿—ter Hilyer!” said the Curate’s wife. “This is *too* much!” She stood speechless for a moment. “*Oh!*”

She swept round upon the rigid girls. “Come!” The Vicar opened and shut his voiceless mouth. The world hummed and spun about him. There was a whirling of zephyr skirts, four impassioned faces sweeping towards the open door of the passage that ran through the vicarage. He felt his position went with them.

===Chinese===

你看，这情景来得太突然。天使的脸正对着副牧师太太，翅膀她一点没瞧见。只见一张超凡脱俗的俊美脸庞，罩在一圈栗色头发的光环里，一副窈窕的身段，裹着一件刚过膝盖的藏红花色衣裳。那双膝盖的念头在教区牧师脑子里也电光一闪。他也是吓呆了。两个姑娘和杰霍拉姆太太也是。全都吓呆了。天使诧异地瞪着这群吓呆的人。你要知道，他从没见过吓呆了的人。

“希——利尔*先生*！”副牧师太太说。“这*太*不像话了！”她一时语塞。“*哦！*”

她霍地转向那两个僵住的姑娘。“走！”教区牧师张了张嘴，没出声。世界在他耳边嗡嗡打转。一阵和风般的裙子卷过去，四张激愤的脸，涌向穿堂那扇敞开的门。他觉得自己的体面也跟着她们去了。

===Original===

“Mrs. Mendham,” said the Vicar, stepping forward. “Mrs. Mendham. You don’t understand﻿—”

“*Oh!*” they all said again.

One, two, three, four skirts vanished in the doorway. The Vicar staggered halfway across the lawn and stopped, aghast. “This comes,” he heard the Curate’s wife say, out of the depth of the passage, “of having an unmarried vicar﻿—.” The umbrella stand wobbled. The front door of the vicarage slammed like a minute gun. There was silence for a space.

===Chinese===

“门德姆太太，”教区牧师抢上一步说。“门德姆太太。您不明白——”

“*哦！*”她们又齐声说。

一条、两条、三条、四条裙子消失在门洞里。教区牧师踉踉跄跄走到草坪当中，站住，惊骇万分。“这就是，”他听见副牧师太太在穿堂深处说，“用一个单身牧师的下场——。”伞架晃了一晃。牧师公馆的前门砰的一声关上，像一响小口径礼炮。接着静了好一阵。

===Original===

“I might have thought,” he said. “She is always so hasty.”

He put his hand to his chin﻿—a habit with him. Then turned his face to his companion. The Angel was evidently well bred. He was holding up Mrs. Jehoram’s sunshade﻿—she had left it on one of the cane chairs﻿—and examining it with extraordinary interest. He opened it. “What a curious little mechanism!” he said. “What can it be for?”

The Vicar did not answer. The angelic costume certainly was﻿—the Vicar knew it was a case for a French phrase﻿—but he could scarcely remember it. He so rarely used French. It was not *de trop*, he knew. Anything but *de trop*. The Angel was *de trop*, but certainly not his costume. Ah! *Sans culotte!*

===Chinese===

“我本该想到的，”他说，“她总是这么性急。”

他把手摸上下巴——这是他的习惯。然后转向他的同伴。天使显然是极有教养的。他正举起杰霍拉姆太太的阳伞——她把它落在一张藤椅上了——兴致勃勃地端详。他把它撑开。“多精巧的小机关！”他说。“这是做什么用的？”

教区牧师没有回答。天使这身行头确实是——教区牧师知道，该用一个法文短语来形容——可他想不起来了。他极少说法文。那不是*多余*（de trop），这个他知道。绝不是*多余*。天使这个人才是*多余*的，他的行头可绝不是。啊！*无裤汉（Sans culotte）！*

===Original===

The Vicar examined his visitor critically﻿—for the first time. “He *will* be difficult to explain,” he said to himself softly.

The Angel stuck the sunshade into the turf and went to smell the sweet briar. The sunshine fell upon his brown hair and gave it almost the appearance of a halo. He pricked his finger. “Odd!” he said. “Pain again.”

“Yes,” said the Vicar, thinking aloud. “He’s very beautiful and curious as he is. I should like him best so. But I am afraid I must.”

He approached the Angel with a nervous cough.

===Chinese===

教区牧师挑剔地打量起这位来客——这是头一回。“他*准会*很难解释，”他轻轻地自言自语。

天使把阳伞插进草皮，走去嗅那甜蔷薇。阳光落在他棕色的头发上，几乎给它罩上一圈光轮似的光泽。他的手指被扎了一下。“古怪！”他说，“又是痛。”

“是啊，”教区牧师出神地想道，“他这副模样已经够美、够稀奇了。我倒顶喜欢他这样。可是恐怕不能不那样办。”

他带着一声紧张的咳嗽，朝天使走去。

## Dedication / 献词

===Original===

To the 

 memory of my dear friend 

 Walter Low

===Chinese===

谨以此书

纪念我的挚友

沃尔特·洛（Walter Low）

## XL Delia / 四十 迪莉娅

===Original===

She was only four or five yards away from the Angel in the westward gable. The diamond-paned window of her little white room was open. She knelt on her box of japanned tin, and rested her chin on her hands, her elbows on the windowsill. The young moon hung over the pine trees, and its light, cool and colourless, lay softly upon the silent-sleeping world. Its light fell upon her white face, and discovered new depths in her dreaming eyes. Her soft lips fell apart and showed the little white teeth.

Delia was thinking, vaguely, wonderfully, as girls will think. It was feeling rather than thinking; clouds of beautiful translucent emotion drove across the clear sky of her mind, taking shape that changed and vanished. She had all that wonderful emotional tenderness, that subtle exquisite desire for self-sacrifice, which exists so inexplicably in a girl’s heart, exists it seems only to be presently trampled under foot by the grim and gross humours of daily life, to be ploughed in again roughly and remorselessly, as the farmer ploughs in the clover that has sprung up in the soil. She had been looking out at the tranquillity of the moonlight long before the Angel began to play﻿—waiting; then suddenly the quiet, motionless beauty of silver and shadow was suffused with tender music.

===Chinese===

她离西边山墙里的天使不过四五码远。她那间小白屋的菱格窗开着。她跪在她的黑漆铁皮箱上，双手托着下巴，胳膊肘支在窗台上。新月悬在松树梢上，那月光清清凉凉、不带颜色，轻柔地铺在这沉睡无声的世界上。月光落在她白皙的脸上，照出她那双梦幻眼睛里新的深处。她柔软的双唇微张，露出细小的白牙。

迪莉娅在想着什么，朦朦胧胧、如梦似幻，正像姑娘们想心事的样子。那与其说是想，不如说是感；一朵朵美丽透亮的情绪之云，掠过她心头晴朗的天空，幻出形状，又变了、散了。她满怀着那种奇妙的柔情，那种微妙而细腻的自我牺牲的渴望——它那样莫名地活在姑娘心里，仿佛生来就为了不久便被日常生活那冷酷粗鄙的脾气践踏在脚下，再被粗暴无情地翻犁进土里，就像庄稼人把地里长出的苜蓿翻犁回土里一样。早在天使开始演奏之前，她就这样望着月光下的宁静——等着；忽然，那银白与暗影之间静默不动的美，涨满了一片温柔的音乐。

===Original===

She did not move, but her lips closed and her eyes grew even softer. She had been thinking before of the strange glory that had suddenly flashed out about the stooping hunchback when he spoke to her in the sunset; of that and of a dozen other glances, chance turns, even once the touching of her hand. That afternoon he had spoken to her, asking strange questions. Now the music seemed to bring his very face before her, his look of half curious solicitude, peering into her face, into her eyes, into her and through her, deep down into her soul. He seemed now to be speaking directly to her, telling her of his solitude and trouble. Oh! that regret, that longing! For he was in trouble. And how could a servant-girl help him, this soft-spoken gentleman who carried himself so kindly, who played so sweetly. The music was so sweet and keen, it came so near to the thought of her heart, that presently one hand tightened on the other, and the tears came streaming down her face.

As Crump would tell you, people do not do that kind of thing unless there is something wrong with the nervous system. But then, from the scientific point of view, being in love is a pathological condition.

===Chinese===

她没有动，只是双唇合拢，眼睛变得更温柔了。她方才一直在想那骤然闪现在俯身驼背影上的奇异光辉——那时他在落日里同她说话；想着那个，还有另外十来次一瞥、无意的回眸，甚至有一回，他的手碰过她的手。那天下午，他同她说过话，问了些古怪的问题。此刻，那音乐仿佛把他整个人带到她眼前：他那种半是好奇、半是关切的神情，细细看她的脸，看她的眼睛，看进她、透过她，一直深深看进她的灵魂。他此刻仿佛在直接对她说话，向她诉说他的孤独和烦恼。哦！那份怅惘，那份渴望！他确实是身陷烦恼。可一个做仆人的姑娘，又能怎样帮上这位言语温柔、举止和善、琴声又那样甜美的先生呢。那音乐如此甜美、如此锥心，如此贴近她心底的念头，以致她一只手渐渐攥紧了另一只手，眼泪顺着面颊淌了下来。

正如克伦普会告诉你的：除非神经系统出了毛病，人是不会做这种事的。不过，从科学的观点看，恋爱本就是一种病态。

===Original===

---

I am painfully aware of the objectionable nature of my story here. I have even thought of wilfully perverting the truth to propitiate the Lady Reader. But I could not. The story has been too much for me. I do the thing with my eyes open. Delia must remain what she really was﻿—a servant girl. I know that to give a mere servant girl, or at least an English servant girl, the refined feelings of a human being, to present her as speaking with anything but an intolerable confusion of aspirates, places me outside the pale of respectable writers. Association with servants, even in thought, is dangerous in these days. I can only plead (pleading vainly, I know), that Delia was a very exceptional servant girl. Possibly, if one enquired, it might be found that her parentage was upper middle-class﻿—that she was made of the finer upper middle-class clay. And (this perhaps may avail me better) I will promise that in some future work I will redress the balance, and the patient reader shall have the recognised article, enormous feet and hands, systematic aspiration of vowels and elimination of aspirates, no figure (only middle-class girls have figures﻿—the thing is beyond a servant-girl’s means), a fringe (by agreement), and a cheerful readiness to dispose of her self-respect for half-a-crown. That is the accepted English servant, the typical English woman (when stripped of money and accomplishments) as she appears in the works of contemporary writers. But Delia somehow was different. I can only regret the circumstance﻿—it was altogether beyond my control.

===Chinese===

---

我痛切地知道，故事写到这里有多么犯忌。我甚至想过故意歪曲真相，以求平息女士读者们的怒气。可我做不到。这故事的力量大过了我。我是睁着眼睛做这件事的。迪莉娅必须保持她的本来面目——一个做仆人的姑娘。我知道，给一个区区仆人姑娘——至少是英国的女仆——配上一个人的细腻情感，让她说话不带那套令人无法忍受的气音大混乱，就等于把我自己划出了体面作家的圈子。同仆人厮混，哪怕只是在想象里，如今也是危险的。我只能说（我知道说了也白说）：迪莉娅是一个非常特别的仆人姑娘。或许，倘使认真查问一番，会查出她的出身是中上层阶级——她是由更细的中上层阶级的黏土捏成的。还有（这一条或许更能救我）我承诺在将来某部作品里挽回平衡，让耐心的读者领到公认的正牌货：天大的手脚、有系统的元音送气和气音吞咽、没有身段（只有中产阶级姑娘才有身段——这东西超出一个女仆的财力）、一绺刘海（约定俗成之），再加上乐呵呵肯为两个半先令出卖自尊的爽快。这便是公认的英国女仆、典型的英国女人（剥去金钱和才艺之后）在当代作家笔下的模样。可是迪莉娅不知怎么就是不一样。我只能为这一情况表示遗憾——这全然不在我掌握之中。

## XLI Doctor Crump Acts / 四十一 克伦普医生出手

===Original===

Early the next morning the Angel went down through the village, and climbing the fence, waded through the waist-high reeds that fringe the Sidder. He was going to Bandram Bay to take a nearer view of the sea, which one could just see on a clear day from the higher parts of Siddermorton Park. And suddenly he came upon Crump sitting on a log and smoking. (Crump always smoked exactly two ounces per week﻿—and he always smoked it in the open air.)

“Hullo!” said Crump, in his healthiest tone. “How’s the wing?”

“Very well,” said the Angel. “The pain’s gone.”

===Chinese===

第二天一大早，天使下山穿过村子，翻过栅栏，蹚着锡德河边齐腰深的芦苇往前走。他要去班德拉姆湾（Bandram Bay），就近看一看大海——天气晴朗的日子，从锡德莫顿园的高处刚能望见它。忽然，他撞见了克伦普，正坐在一根圆木上抽烟。（克伦普每星期不多不少恰好抽两盎司烟——而且总在露天里抽。）

“喂！”克伦普用他最健康的声调说。“翅膀怎么样了？”

“很好，”天使说，“不痛了。”

===Original===

“I suppose you know you are trespassing?”

“Trespassing!” said the Angel.

“I suppose you don’t know what that means,” said Crump.

===Chinese===

“想必你知道，你这叫擅闯私地吧？”

“擅闯私地！”天使说。

“我想你不知道这是什么意思，”克伦普说。

===Original===

“I don’t,” said the Angel.

“I must congratulate you. I don’t know how long you will last, but you are keeping it up remarkably well. I thought at first you were a mattoid, but you’re so amazingly consistent. Your attitude of entire ignorance of the elementary facts of life is really a very amusing pose. You make slips of course, but very few. But surely we two understand one another.”

He smiled at the Angel. “You would beat Sherlock Holmes. I wonder who you really are.”

===Chinese===

“确实不知道。”天使说。

“我必须恭喜你。不知道你还能撑多久，不过你撑得实在漂亮。起初我以为你是个狂质者，可你前后一致得惊人。你这副对人生基本常识一无所知的姿态，实在是个非常有趣的做派。你当然也有破绽，但极少。不过咱们俩总是彼此心照的吧。”

他朝天使笑笑。“连夏洛克·福尔摩斯也得败给你。我倒想知道，你究竟是谁。”

===Original===

The Angel smiled back, with eyebrows raised and hands extended. “It’s impossible for you to know who I am. Your eyes are blind, your ears deaf, your soul dark, to all that is wonderful about me. It’s no good my telling that I fell into your world.”

The Doctor waved his pipe. “Not that, please. I don’t want to pry if you have your reasons for keeping quiet. Only I would like you to think of Hilyer’s mental health. He really believes this story.”

The Angel shrugged his dwindling wings.

===Chinese===

天使也报以微笑，扬起眉毛，摊开双手。“你们不可能知道我是谁。对我身上一切奇妙之处，你们的眼睛是瞎的，耳朵是聋的，灵魂是暗的。我告诉你我是跌进你们这世界里来的，也无济于事。”

医生挥了挥烟斗。“那个就不必了。你要是有缄默的理由，我也不想刺探。只是希望你替希利尔的精神健康想一想。他可是真信了这套话。”

天使耸了耸他那对日渐萎缩的翅膀。

===Original===

“You did not know him before this affair. He’s changed tremendously. He used to be neat and comfortable. For the last fortnight he’s been hazy, with a faraway look in his eyes. He preached last Sunday without his cuff links, and something wrong with his tie, and he took for his text, ‘Eye hath not seen nor ear heard.’ He really believes all this nonsense about the Angel-land. The man is verging on monomania!”

“You *will* see things from your own standpoint,” said the Angel.

“Everyone must. At any rate, I think it jolly regrettable to see this poor old fellow hypnotized, as you certainly have hypnotized him. I don’t know where you come from nor who you are, but I warn you I’m not going to see the old boy made a fool of much longer.”

===Chinese===

“这件事之前你不认识他。他变得厉害极了。他从前是个整洁舒泰的人。这两个星期来他恍恍惚惚，眼里总带着一种茫远的神色。上个礼拜天他讲道，袖口没扣袖扣，领带也系得不对劲，选的经文还是‘眼睛未曾看见，耳朵未曾听见’。关于天使之国的这套胡话，他竟真信了。这人快要得偏执狂了！”

“你*非要*从你自己的立场看事情，”天使说。

“人人都是。不管怎么说，眼看这可怜的老家伙被催眠——你无疑把他催眠了——我觉得非常遗憾。我不知道你从哪儿来，也不知道你是谁，但我警告你，我不能眼看这老家伙再被人愚弄多久了。”

===Original===

“But he’s not being made a fool of. He’s simply beginning to dream of a world outside his knowledge﻿—”

“It won’t do,” said Crump. “I’m not one of the dupe class. You are either of two things﻿—a lunatic at large (which I don’t believe), or a knave. Nothing else is possible. I think I know a little of this world, whatever I do of yours. Very well. If you don’t leave Hilyer alone I shall communicate with the police, and either clap you into a prison, if you go back on your story, or into a madhouse if you don’t. It’s stretching a point, but I swear I’d certify you insane tomorrow to get you out of the village. It’s not only the Vicar. As you know. I hope that’s plain. Now what have you to say?”

With an affectation of great calm, the Doctor took out his penknife and began to dig the blade into his pipe bowl. His pipe had gone out during this last speech.

===Chinese===

“可他没有被人愚弄。他不过是开始梦想一个他所不知道的世界——”

“那可不行，”克伦普说。“我不是易受骗的那类人。你不是这个就是那个——要么是个逍遥法外的疯子（这个我不信），要么是个骗子。再没有第三种可能。这个世界我好歹略知一二，你们那个世界我懂多少另说。很好。你要是再缠着希利尔不放，我就去报警：你要是改口翻供，就把你送进监狱；你要是咬定不改，就把你送进疯人院。这未免过分，可我发誓，只要能把你弄出这个村子，我明天就能签字证明你精神失常。不光是教区牧师的事。这你是知道的。我希望我把话说清楚了。现在，你还有什么可说的？”

医生故作极度的镇定，掏出铅笔刀，把刀尖往烟斗钵里挖。刚才这番话的当儿，他的烟斗已经灭了。

===Original===

For a moment neither spoke. The Angel looked about him with a face that grew pale. The Doctor extracted a plug of tobacco from his pipe and flung it away, shut his penknife and put it in his waistcoat pocket. He had not meant to speak quite so emphatically, but speech always warmed him.

“Prison,” said the Angel. “Madhouse! Let me see.” Then he remembered the Vicar’s explanation. “Not that!” he said. He approached Crump with eyes dilated and hands outstretched.

“I knew *you* would know what those things meant﻿—at any rate. Sit down,” said Crump, indicating the tree trunk beside him by a movement of the head.

===Chinese===

两人有一会儿都没说话。天使环顾四周，脸色渐渐发白。医生从烟斗里挖出一小团烟油，甩掉，合上铅笔刀，放回背心口袋。他本来没打算把话说得那么重，可一开口，话总叫他热起来。

“监狱，”天使说。“疯人院！让我想想。”随即他记起了教区牧师的解说。“可不要那样！”他说。他瞳孔放大、双手前伸，朝克伦普走近。

“我早知道*你*至少是懂得这些字眼分量的。坐下吧，”克伦普说，头一动，指了指身旁的树干。

===Original===

The Angel, shivering, sat down on the tree trunk and stared at the Doctor.

Crump was getting out his pouch. “You are a strange man,” said the Angel. “Your beliefs are like﻿—a steel trap.”

“They are,” said Crump﻿—flattered.

===Chinese===

天使打着颤，在树干上坐下，直望着医生。

克伦普正掏他的烟袋。“你是个奇怪的人，”天使说，“你的信念就像——一只钢制捕兽夹。”

“正是，”克伦普说——颇受用。

===Original===

“But I tell you﻿—I assure you the thing is so﻿—I know nothing, or at least remember nothing of anything I knew of this world before I found myself in the darkness of night on the moorland above Sidderford.”

“Where did you learn the language then?”

“I don’t know. Only I tell you﻿—But I haven’t an atom of the sort of proof that would convince you.”

===Chinese===

“可我告诉你——我向你保证事情就是这样——在一个月夜里发觉自己身在锡德福德上面的高沼之前，我对这个世界一无所知，或者至少，什么也不记得。”

“那你的话是在哪儿学的？”

“我不知道。我只是告诉你——可我拿不出一星半点能叫你信服的证据。”

===Original===

“And you really,” said Crump, suddenly coming round upon him and looking into his eyes; “You really believe you were eternally in a kind of glorious heaven before then?”

“I do,” said the Angel.

“Pshaw!” said Crump, and lit his pipe. He sat smoking, elbow on knee, for some time, and the Angel sat and watched him. Then his face grew less troubled.

===Chinese===

“那么你当真，”克伦普突然转过身来，直盯着他的眼睛说，“你当真相信，在那以前你一直待在某种荣耀的天堂里？”

“当真。”天使说。

“呸！”克伦普说着，点着了烟斗。他肘撑着膝坐着抽烟，抽了好一阵，天使坐在一旁望着他。后来他的脸色没那么凝重了。

===Original===

“It is just possible,” he said to himself rather than to the Angel, and began another piece of silence.

“You see;” he said, when that was finished. “There is such a thing as double personality.﻿ ﻿… A man sometimes forgets who he is and thinks he is someone else. Leaves home, friends, and everything, and leads a double life. There was a case in *Nature* only a month or so ago. The man was sometimes English and right-handed, and sometimes Welsh and left-handed. When he was English he knew no Welsh, when he was Welsh he knew no English.﻿ ﻿… H’m.”

He turned suddenly on the Angel and said “Home!” He fancied he might revive in the Angel some latent memory of his lost youth. He went on “Dadda, Pappa, Daddy, Mammy, Pappy, Father, Dad, Governor, Old Boy, Mother, dear Mother, Ma, Mumsy.﻿ ﻿… No good? What are you laughing at?”

===Chinese===

“那也并非全无可能，”这话与其说是对天使说的，不如说是自言自语，说完又开始了另一段沉默。

“你要知道；”那段沉默结束之后他说。“世上有所谓双重人格。……人有时会忘掉自己是谁，以为自己另是一人。离家，别友，撇下一切，过起双重生活。就在一个月前，*《自然》*（Nature）上还有过一个病例。那人有时是英国人、用右手，有时是威尔士人、用左手。他是英国人时不懂威尔士语，是威尔士人时不懂英语。……嗯。”

他猛地转向天使，说了一声“家！”他盘算着，这兴许能在天使心里唤醒某种对他失去的少年时代的潜在记忆。他接着念：“爸爸、爹爹、老爸、妈妈、妈咪、父亲、爸、老爷子、老伙计、母亲、亲爱的母亲、妈、姆妈。……没用？你笑什么？”

===Original===

“Nothing,” said the Angel. “You surprised me a little﻿—that is all. A week ago I should have been puzzled by that vocabulary.”

For a minute Crump rebuked the Angel silently out of the corner of his eye.

“You have such an ingenuous face. You almost force me to believe you. You are certainly not an ordinary lunatic. Your mind﻿—except for your isolation from the past﻿—seems balanced enough. I wish Nordau or Lombroso or some of these *Saltpetriere* men could have a look at you. Down here one gets no practice worth speaking about in mental cases. There’s one idiot﻿—and he’s just a damned idiot of an idiot﻿—; all the rest are thoroughly sane people.”

===Chinese===

“没什么，”天使说，“你只是让我小吃了一惊——如此而已。换了一个星期以前，这份词汇准会把我难住。”

有一分钟，克伦普用眼角默默地责备着天使。

“你长了这么一张老实脸。你简直逼得我要相信你了。你肯定不是寻常的疯子。你的头脑——除了同过去隔绝这一点——似乎相当平衡。我真希望诺尔道（Nordau）或者隆布罗索（Lombroso），或者*萨尔佩蒂耶*（Saltpetriere）的那班人，能来瞧瞧你。在这乡下，精神病例少得不值一提。只有一个傻子——而且他也不过是个傻透了的傻子——；其余的人全都理智得彻底。”

===Original===

“Possibly that accounts for their behaviour,” said the Angel thoughtfully.

“But to consider your general position here,” said Crump, ignoring his comment, “I really regard you as a bad influence here. These fancies are contagious. It is not simply the Vicar. There is a man named Shine has caught the fad, and he has been in the drink for a week, off and on, and offering to fight anyone who says you are not an Angel. Then a man over at Sidderford is, I hear, affected with a kind of religious mania on the same tack. These things spread. There ought to be a quarantine in mischievous ideas. And I have heard another story.﻿ ﻿…”

“But what can I do?” said the Angel. “Suppose I am (quite unintentionally) doing mischief.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“他们的行止或许正是这么来的，”天使若有所思地说。

“可要论你在此地的总处境，”克伦普不理会他的评语，接着说，“我确实把你看作此地的祸害。这些妄想会传染。不光是教区牧师。有个叫夏因（Shine）的也染上了这个时髦，断断续续灌了一个星期的酒，谁说你不是天使就要跟谁干架。还有，听说锡德福德那边有个人，也顺着同一条道染上了一种宗教狂。这种事会蔓延。对害人的念头，实在该设个检疫。我还听了另一桩事。……”

“可我能做什么呢？”天使说。“就算我（全然无心地）在闯祸。……”

===Original===

“You can leave the village,” said Crump.

“Then I shall only go into another village.”

“That’s not my affair,” said Crump. “Go where you like. Only go. Leave these three people, the Vicar, Shine, the little servant girl, whose heads are all spinning with galaxies of Angels.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“你可以离开这个村子，”克伦普说。

“那我不过是进了另一个村子。”

“那就不关我的事了，”克伦普说。“爱去哪儿去哪儿。只管走。离开这三个人：教区牧师、夏因、还有那个小女仆，他们的脑袋里如今全转着满天星宿般的天使。……”

===Original===

“But,” said the Angel. “Face your world! I tell you I can’t. And leave Delia! I don’t understand.﻿ ﻿… I do not know how to set about getting Work and Food and Shelter. And I am growing afraid of human beings.﻿ ﻿…”

“Fancies, fancies,” said Crump, watching him, “mania.”

“It’s no good my persisting in worrying you,” he said suddenly, “but certainly the situation is impossible as it stands.” He stood up with a jerk.

“Good morning, Mr.﻿—Angel,” he said, “the long and the short of it is﻿—I say it as the medical adviser of this parish﻿—you are an unhealthy influence. We can’t have you. You must go.”

He turned, and went striding through the grass towards the roadway, leaving the Angel sitting disconsolately on the tree trunk. “An unhealthy influence,” said the Angel slowly, staring blankly in front of him, and trying to realise what it meant.

===Chinese===

“可是，”天使说，“去面对你们的世界！我告诉你，我做不到。还要离开迪莉娅！我不明白。……我不知道该怎样着手去弄到工作、食物和栖身之所。而且我越来越怕人类了。……”

“妄想，妄想，”克伦普注视着他说，“躁狂。”

“我再这样缠着你也无益，”他忽然说，“不过眼下这个局面，实在是行不通的。”他霍地站起身。

“早安，天——使先生，”他说，“总而言之——我以本教区医药顾问的身份说这句话——你是个不健康的影响。我们容不下你。你必须走。”

他转过身，大步穿过草地向大路走去，撇下天使怏怏地坐在树干上。“不健康的影响，”天使缓缓地说，茫然望着前方，努力体会这话的意思。

## XXX Further Adventures of the Angel in the Village (Continued) / 三十 天使在村里的进一步奇遇（续）

===Original===

After the Angel had left Crump’s house he went up the hill again towards the Vicarage. But﻿—possibly moved by the desire to avoid Mrs. Gustick﻿—he turned aside at the stile and made a detour by the Lark’s Field and Bradley’s Farm.

He came upon the Respectable Tramp slumbering peacefully among the wildflowers. He stopped to look, struck by the celestial tranquillity of that individual’s face. And even as he did so the Respectable Tramp awoke with a start and sat up. He was a pallid creature, dressed in rusty black, with a broken-spirited crush hat cocked over one eye. “Good afternoon,” he said affably. “How are you?”

“Very well, thank you,” said the Angel, who had mastered the phrase.

===Chinese===

天使离开克伦普家后，又上坡朝牧师公馆走去。不过——多半是想躲开古斯蒂克太太——他在踏阶那儿拐了个弯，绕道云雀地和布拉德利农场（Bradley's Farm）走。

他撞见了那位体面流浪汉，正安睡在野花丛中。他停下来端详，被那人脸上一种天上般的安详打动了。就在这时，体面流浪汉猛地惊醒，坐了起来。他是个面色苍白的家伙，穿一身锈黑色的衣裳，一顶垂头丧气的软帽歪遮着一只眼。“午后好，”他和和气气地说。“您好？”

“很好，谢谢您，”天使说，这句话他已经掌握了。

===Original===

The Respectable Tramp eyed the Angel critically. “Padding the Hoof, matey?” he said. “Like me.”

The Angel was puzzled by him. “Why,” asked the Angel, “do you sleep like this instead of sleeping up in the air on a bed?”

“Well I’m blowed!” said the Respectable Tramp. “Why don’t I sleep in a bed? Well, it’s like this. Sandringham’s got the painters in, there’s the drains up in Windsor Castle, and I ’aven’t no other ’ouse to go to. You ’aven’t the price of a arf pint in your pocket, ’ave yer?”

===Chinese===

体面流浪汉挑剔地打量着天使。“也在靠脚板跑江湖吗，伙计？”他说。“跟我一样。”

天使让他闹糊涂了。“请问，”天使问，“你为什么这样睡，而不睡到半空中的床上？”

“嚯，活见鬼！”体面流浪汉说。“我怎么不睡床上？呃，是这么回事。桑德林汉姆府上正在油漆，温莎堡的下水道又刨开了，我呢，又没别的宅子可去。您口袋里总不至于连半品脱的钱都没有吧，有吗？”

===Original===

“I have nothing in my pocket,” said the Angel.

“Is this here village called Siddermorton?” said the Tramp, rising creakily to his feet and pointing to the clustering roofs down the hill.

“Yes,” said the Angel, “they call it Siddermorton.”

===Chinese===

“我口袋里什么也没有。”天使说。

“下边这个村子叫锡德莫顿吗？”流浪汉说着，浑身咯吱咯吱地站起身来，指着山下成片的屋顶。

“是的，”天使说，“他们管它叫锡德莫顿。”

===Original===

“I know it, I know it,” said the Tramp. “And a very pretty little village it is too.” He stretched and yawned, and stood regarding the place. “ ’Ouses,” he said reflectively; “Projuce”﻿—waving his hand at the cornfields and orchards. “Looks cosy, don’t it?”

“It has a quaint beauty of its own,” said the Angel.

“It *’as* a quaint beauty of its own﻿—yes.﻿ ﻿… Lord! I’d like to sack the blooming place.﻿ ﻿… I was born there.”

===Chinese===

“我知道，我知道，”流浪汉说。“还是个挺俊的小村子呢。”他伸了个懒腰，打个哈欠，站在那儿端详这地方。“房子，”他若有所思地说；“出产”——朝麦田和果园挥了挥手。“瞧着挺安逸，是不？”

“它自有一种古雅之美。”天使说。

“它*是*自有一种古雅之美——不错。……老天爷！我真想把这个烂村子洗劫一空。……我就生在那儿。”

===Original===

“Dear me,” said the Angel.

“Yes, I was born there. Ever heard of a pithed frog?”

“Pithed frog,” said the Angel. “No!”

===Chinese===

“天哪。”天使说。

“是啊，我就生在那儿。听说过‘去髓蛙’吗？”

“去髓蛙，”天使说。“没听说过！”

===Original===

“It’s a thing these here vivisectionists do. They takes a frog and they cuts out his brains and they shoves a bit of pith in the place of ’em. That’s a pithed frog. Well﻿—that there village is full of pithed human beings.”

The Angel took it quite seriously. “Is that so?” he said.

“That’s so﻿—you take my word for it. Every one of them ’as ’ad their brains cut out and chunks of rotten touchwood put in the place of it. And you see that little red place there?”

===Chinese===

“就是那帮活体解剖的家伙干的事儿。他们逮一只青蛙，挖掉它的脑子，往那地方塞一小截髓。那就是去髓蛙。呃——那个村子里，就满是去了髓的人。”

天使把这话当了真。“是吗？”他说。

“就是——您信我的话没错。他们个个都叫人挖了脑子，原处填上一块块烂了的火绒。您瞧见那边那座小红房子了吗？”

===Original===

“That’s called the national school,” said the Angel.

“Yes﻿—that’s where they piths ’em,” said the Tramp, quite in love with his conceit.

“Really! That’s very interesting.”

===Chinese===

“那叫国民学校。”天使说。

“不错——他们就在那儿给人们去髓，”流浪汉说，对自个儿的高见得意极了。

“真的！那可真有意思。”

===Original===

“It stands to reason,” said the Tramp. “If they ’ad brains they’d ’ave ideas, and if they ’ad ideas they’d think for themselves. And you can go through that village from end to end and never meet anybody doing as much. Pithed human beings they are. I know that village. I was born there, and I might be there now, a toilin’ for my betters, if I ’adnt struck against the pithin’.”

“Is it a painful operation?” asked the Angel.

“In parts. Though it aint the heads gets hurt. And it lasts a long time. They take ’em young into that school, and they says to them, ‘come in ’ere and we’ll improve your minds,’ they says, and in the little kiddies go as good as gold. And they begins shovin’ it into them. Bit by bit and ’ard and dry, shovin’ out the nice juicy brains. Dates and lists and things. Out they comes, no brains in their ’eads, and wound up nice and tight, ready to touch their ’ats to anyone who looks at them. Why! One touched ’is ’at to me yesterday. And they runs about spry and does all the dirty work, and feels thankful they’re allowed to live. They take a positive pride in ’ard work for its own sake. Arter they bin pithed. See that chap ploughin’?”

===Chinese===

“这明摆着的理儿，”流浪汉说。“他们要有脑子，就会有想法；有想法，就会自己动脑子。可您把那村子从头到尾走一遍，也碰不上一个自己动脑子的。全是去了髓的人。那村子我熟。我就生在那儿，要不是我当年抗了那去髓的手术，这会儿没准还在那儿，替我的上等人卖命呢。”

“那手术疼吗？”天使问。

“有的地方疼。不过疼的不是脑袋。这手术还做得长远。他们趁小把人弄进那学校，跟他们说：‘到这儿来，我们把你脑子弄好，’他们说，小娃娃们便乖乖地进去了。跟着他们就往里塞。又干又硬，一点一点，把那好端端水灵的脑子挤出去。填的全是年代啊、名目单子啊这些玩意儿。出来的时候，脑袋里没了脑子，上得紧紧的发条，谁瞧上一眼就赶忙摘帽。嘿！昨天就有一个冲我摘了帽。他们腿脚麻利地跑来跑去，干尽脏活，还感恩戴德，谢天谢地准他们活着。他们干起苦活来，竟还为苦活本身感到体面。去髓之后。瞧见那个犁地的家伙了吗？”

===Original===

“Yes,” said the Angel; “is *he* pithed?”

“Rather. Else he’d be paddin’ the hoof this pleasant weather﻿—like me and the blessed Apostles.”

“I begin to understand,” said the Angel, rather dubiously.

===Chinese===

“瞧见了，”天使说，“*他*也去了髓吗？”

“可不。不然这么好的天气，他也该出来靠脚板跑江湖了——跟我还有那些有福的使徒们一样。”

“我有点明白了，”天使说，语气将信将疑。

===Original===

“I knew you would,” said the Philosophical Tramp. “I thought you was the right sort. But speaking serious, aint it ridiculous?﻿—centuries and centuries of civilization, and look at that poor swine there, sweatin’ ’isself empty and trudging up that ’ill-side. ’E’s English, ’e is. ’E belongs to the top race in creation, ’e does. ’E’s one of the rulers of Indjer. It’s enough to make a nigger laugh. The flag that’s braved a thousand years the battle an’ the breeze﻿—that’s *’is* flag. There never was a country was as great and glorious as this. Never. And that’s wot it makes of us. I’ll tell you a little story about them parts as you seems to be a bit of a stranger. There’s a chap called Gotch, Sir John Gotch they calls ’im, and when *’e* was a young gent from Oxford, I was a little chap of eight and my sister was a girl of seventeen. Their servant she was. But Lord! everybody’s ’eard that story﻿—it’s common enough, of ’im or the likes of ’im.”

“I haven’t,” said the Angel.

“All that’s pretty and lively of the gals they chucks into the gutters, and all the men with a pennorth of spunk or adventure, all who won’t drink what the Curate’s wife sends ’em instead of beer, and touch their hats promiscous, and leave the rabbits and birds alone for their betters, gets drove out of the villages as rough characters. Patriotism! Talk about improvin’ the race! Wot’s left aint fit to look a nigger in the face, a Chinaman ’ud be ashamed of ’em.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“我早知道您会明白，”哲人流浪汉说。“我一瞧您就是对路的人。可说正经的，这不荒唐吗？——文明了一千年又一千年，再瞧瞧那边那头可怜的猪，汗流空了身子，一步一步往那山坡上挣。他是英国人，他是。他属于造化里顶等的种族，他是。他是统治印度的人物之一。这景象能让黑鬼都笑掉牙。那面在战场与风中傲然飘扬了千年的旗——那是*他的*旗。从来没有哪个国家这样伟大、这样光荣。从来没有。可它就把咱们弄成这副模样。我给您讲个那一带的小故事吧，瞧您像个外乡人。有个家伙叫戈奇，他们叫他约翰·戈奇爵士，*他*还是牛津来的年轻少爷那会儿，我是个八岁的小子，我姐姐是个十七岁的姑娘。就是他们家的女仆。可是老天！那故事谁没听过——要么是他，要么是他那号人，多的是。”

“我就没听过。”天使说。

“凡是标致鲜活的姑娘，都被他们扔进了阴沟；凡是还有一分血气、一分闯劲的男人，凡是不肯拿副牧师太太送来的玩意儿当啤酒喝的、不肯见人就乱摘帽的、不肯把兔子和飞鸟留给上等人的，全被当作粗坯轰出了村子。爱国！还谈什么改良人种！剩下那些货色，见了黑鬼都不配照面，连中国人都替他们害臊。……”

===Original===

“But I don’t understand,” said the Angel. “I don’t follow you.”

At that the Philosophic Tramp became more explicit, and told the Angel the simple story of Sir John Gotch and the kitchen-maid. It’s scarcely necessary to repeat it. You may understand that it left the Angel puzzled. It was full of words he did not understand, for the only vehicle of emotion the Tramp possessed was blasphemy. Yet, though their tongues differed so, he could still convey to the Angel some of his own (probably unfounded) persuasion of the injustice and cruelty of life, and of the utter detestableness of Sir John Gotch.

The last the Angel saw of him was his dusty black back receding down the lane towards Iping Hanger. A pheasant appeared by the roadside, and the Philosophical Tramp immediately caught up a stone and sent the bird clucking with a viciously accurate shot. Then he disappeared round the corner.

===Chinese===

“可我不明白，”天使说，“我听不懂你的话。”

这下哲人流浪汉讲得更露骨了，把约翰·戈奇爵士和厨房女仆那段简简单单的故事讲给了天使听。那故事不必在此重述。你们可以想见，天使听得一头雾水。故事里满是他不认识的字眼，因为这位流浪汉抒发感情的唯一工具便是渎神的话。然而，尽管两人的语言如此不同，他仍能让天使多少领会到他自己（多半并无根据）的那种信念：人生如何不公、如何残忍，还有约翰·戈奇爵士如何可恶到了极点。

天使最后望见他的一幕，是他那落满尘土的黑背影顺着小径往伊平亨格方向远去。一只雉鸡在路边出现，哲人流浪汉立刻抓起一块石头，又狠又准地一掷，打得那鸟咯咯惊飞。随后他就消失在拐角后面了。

## XXIX Further Adventures of the Angel in the Village / 二十九 天使在村里的进一步奇遇

===Original===

“That’s all right,” said Crump when the bandaging was replaced. “It’s a trick of memory, no doubt, but these excrescences of yours don’t seem nearly so large as they did yesterday. I suppose they struck me rather forcibly. Stop and have lunch with me now you’re down here. Midday meal, you know. The youngsters will be swallowed up by school again in the afternoon.”

“I never saw anything heal so well in my life,” he said, as they walked into the dining-room. “Your blood and flesh must be as clean and free from bacteria as they make ’em. Whatever stuff there is in your head,” he added *sotto voce*.

At lunch he watched the Angel narrowly, and talked to draw him out.

===Chinese===

“这就对了，”绷带重新缠好之后，克伦普说。“无疑是记忆在作怪，不过你这两处赘生物，瞧着可远没有昨天那么大了。想是我昨天受的震动太深。既然到了这儿，就留下同我共进午餐吧。正午的那顿饭，你晓得。下午孩子们又要被学校吞回去。”

“我这辈子没见过愈合得这么好的，”他们走进饭厅时他说。“你的血肉想必干净得无以复加，一点儿细菌也没有。就是不知道你脑袋里装的是什么货色，”他*低声*补了一句。

午餐时他细细观察天使，东拉西扯引他说话。

===Original===

“Journey tire you yesterday?” he said suddenly.

“Journey!” said the Angel. “Oh! my wings felt a little stiff.”

(“Not to be had,”) said Crump to himself. (“Suppose I must enter into it.”)

===Chinese===

“昨天旅途累着了吧？”他忽然说。

“旅途！”天使说。“哦！我的翅膀有点发僵。”

（“不上钩，”克伦普自忖。（“看来只好入戏了。”）

===Original===

“So you flew all the way, eigh? No conveyance?”

“There wasn’t any way,” explained the Angel, taking mustard. “I was flying up a symphony with some Griffins and Fiery Cherubim, and suddenly everything went dark and I was in this world of yours.”

“Dear me!” said Crump. “And that’s why you haven’t any luggage.” He drew his serviette across his mouth, and a smile flickered in his eyes.

===Chinese===

“这么说，你一路都是飞来的，嗯？没坐什么车？”

“本来也没有路，”天使解释道，一边拿起芥末。“我方才正同几位狮鹫和火炽基路伯一起，把一部交响曲往上飞，忽然间一切都黑了，我就到了你们这个世界。”

“天哪！”克伦普说。“所以你才没带行李。”他拿餐巾抹了抹嘴，眼里闪过一丝笑意。

===Original===

“I suppose you know this world of ours pretty well? Watching us over the adamantine walls and all that kind of thing. Eigh?”

“Not very well. We dream of it sometimes. In the moonlight, when the Nightmares have fanned us to sleep with their wings.”

“Ah, yes﻿—of course,” said Crump. “Very poetical way of putting it. Won’t you take some burgundy? It’s just beside you.”

===Chinese===

“想必你对咱们这个世界相当熟悉吧？隔着金刚石墙看我们，还有那一套。嗯？”

“不怎么熟。我们有时梦见它。在月光里，梦魇们用翅膀扇着我们入睡的时候。”

“啊，是——当然，”克伦普说。“说法很有诗意。不来点红葡萄酒吗？就在你手边。”

===Original===

“There’s a persuasion in this world, you know, that Angels’ Visits are by no means infrequent. Perhaps some of your﻿—friends have travelled? They are supposed to come down to deserving persons in prisons, and do refined Nautches and that kind of thing. Faust business, you know.”

“I’ve never heard of anything of the kind,” said the Angel.

“Only the other day a lady whose baby was my patient for the time being﻿—indigestion﻿—assured me that certain facial contortions the little creature made indicated that it was Dreaming of Angels. In the novels of Mrs. Henry Wood that is spoken of as an infallible symptom of an early departure. I suppose you can’t throw any light on that obscure pathological manifestation?”

===Chinese===

“你要知道，这世上流传一种说法，说天使的探访绝不稀罕。或许你的某些——朋友也出游过？据说他们会降临到监狱里那些应得福报的人身边，跳些文雅的纳奇舞，诸如此类。浮士德那一套，你晓得。”

“这类事我从没听说过。”天使说。

“就在前几天，有位太太——她的小宝宝当时正由我诊治——消化不良——一口咬定说，那小东西做的某些鬼脸，表明它正在梦见天使。在亨利·伍德太太（Mrs. Henry Wood）的小说里，这被称为夭折的万无一失的征兆。这种费解的病理表现，想必你也解说不了一二吧？”

===Original===

“I don’t understand it at all,” said the Angel, puzzled, and not clearly apprehending the Doctor’s drift.

(“Getting huffy,”) said Crump to himself. (“Sees I’m poking fun at him.”) “There’s one thing I’m curious about. Do the new arrivals complain much about their medical attendants? I’ve always fancied there must be a good deal of hydropathic talk just at first. I was looking at that picture in the Academy only this June.﻿ ﻿…”

“New arrivals!” said the Angel. “I really don’t follow you.”

===Chinese===

“我全然不明白，”天使说，一脸困惑，也没听清医生的用意何在。

（“恼了，”克伦普自忖。（“看出我在拿他打趣了。”）“有件事我很好奇。新来报到的那些人，对他们的医护抱怨得多吗？我一向猜想，刚到的时候，水疗话题必定不少。就在今年六月，我还在皇家美术院看过那样一幅画。……”

“新来报到！”天使说。“我真跟不上你了。”

===Original===

The Doctor stared. “Don’t they come?”

“Come!” said the Angel. “Who?”

“The people who die here.”

===Chinese===

医生瞪着眼。“他们不来吗？”

“来！”天使说。“谁来？”

“在这里死掉的那些人。”

===Original===

“After they’ve gone to pieces here?”

“That’s the general belief, you know.”

“People, like the woman who screamed out of the door, and the blackfaced man and his volutations and the horrible little things that threw husks!﻿—certainly not. *I* never saw such creatures before I fell into this world.”

===Chinese===

“在这儿散完架之后？”

“你要知道，大家普遍是这么信的。”

“那些人——像从门里尖叫出来的那个女人，还有那个黑脸汉和他那套歪理，还有扔果壳的那些可恶的小东西！——当然不来。跌进这个世界之前，*我*可从没见过这样的生物。”

===Original===

“Oh! but come!” said the Doctor. “You’ll tell me next your official robes are not white and that you can’t play the harp.”

“There’s no such thing as white in the Angelic Land,” said the Angel. “It’s that queer blank colour you get by mixing up all the others.”

“Why, my dear Sir!” said the doctor, suddenly altering his tone, “you positively know nothing about the Land you come from. White’s the very essence of it.”

===Chinese===

“哦！可得了吧！”医生说。“下一步你就要告诉我，你的官袍不是白的，你不会弹竖琴了。”

“天使之国里根本没有白这种东西，”天使说。“白是你们把别的颜色统统搅和在一起得到的那种古怪的、空白的颜色。”

“哎呀，我亲爱的先生！”医生忽然变了口气说，“你对自己来自的那个国度实在一无所知。白正是它的精髓。”

===Original===

The Angel stared at him. Was the man jesting? He looked perfectly serious.

“Look here,” said Crump, and getting up, he went to the sideboard on which a copy of the Parish Magazine was lying. He brought it round to the Angel and opened it at the coloured supplement. “Here’s some *real* angels,” he said. “You see it’s not simply the wings make the Angel. White you see, with a curly whisp of robe, sailing up into the sky with their wings furled. Those are angels on the best authority. Hydroxyl kind of hair. One has a bit of a harp, you see, and the other is helping this wingless lady﻿—kind of larval Angel, you know﻿—upward.”

“Oh! but really!” said the Angel, “those are not angels at all.”

===Chinese===

天使盯着他瞧。这人是在说笑吗？他看上去一本正经。

“你瞧，”克伦普说着站起身，走到餐具柜前，那儿摊着一本教区杂志。他把它拿到天使跟前，翻开彩图附页。“这才是*真正的*天使，”他说。“你瞧，天使并不仅仅是靠翅膀算数的。瞧这一身白，衣袂卷成一缕，翅膀收拢着朝天上飞去。这才是权威认证的天使。氢氧化物似的一头头发。这一个还抱着架小竖琴，另一个正搀着这位没有翅膀的太太——一种幼虫期的天使，你晓得——往上飞呢。”

“哦！可真是的！”天使说，“那些根本不是天使。”

===Original===

“But they *are*,” said Crump, putting the magazine back on the sideboard and resuming his seat with an air of intense satisfaction. “I can assure you I have the *best* authority.﻿ ﻿…”

“I can assure you.﻿ ﻿…”

Crump tucked in the corners of his mouth and shook his head from side to side even as he had done to the Vicar. “No good,” he said, “can’t alter our ideas just because an irresponsible visitor.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“可他们*就是*，”克伦普说着把杂志放回餐具柜，带着大为满足的神气坐回座位。“我向你担保，我这可是*最权威*的依据。……”

“我向你担保。……”

克伦普把两边嘴角往里一抿，脑袋左右摇了摇，同他当初对教区牧师做的一模一样。“没用的，”他说，“总不能因为来了一位不负责任的客人，就把咱们的观念改掉。……”

===Original===

“If these are angels,” said the Angel, “then I have never been in the Angelic Land.”

“Precisely,” said Crump, ineffably self-satisfied; “that was just what I was getting at.”

The Angel stared at him for a minute round-eyed, and then was seized for the second time by the human disorder of laughter.

“Ha, ha, ha!” said Crump, joining in. “I *thought* you were not quite so mad as you seemed. Ha, ha, ha!”

And for the rest of the lunch they were both very merry, for entirely different reasons, and Crump insisted upon treating the Angel as a “dorg” of the highest degree.

===Chinese===

“如果这些是天使，”天使说，“那我就从没到过天使之国。”

“正是，”克伦普说，得意得无以言表；“我要说的正是这个。”

天使瞪圆眼睛望了他一分钟，随即第二次被笑这种人类的失调攫住了。

“哈，哈，哈！”克伦普也跟着笑起来。“我*就想到*你并不像看上去那么疯。哈，哈，哈！”

午餐剩下的时间里，两人都很高兴——高兴的缘由全然不同——克伦普一口咬定，要把天使当作一条最高级的“汪儿”看待。

## XXVIII Lady Hammergallow’s View / 二十八 哈默加洛夫人的看法

===Original===

In Siddermorton Park is Siddermorton House, where old Lady Hammergallow lives, chiefly upon burgundy and the little scandals of the village, a dear old lady with a ropy neck, a ruddled countenance and spasmodic gusts of odd temper, whose three remedies for all human trouble among her dependents are, a bottle of gin, a pair of charity blankets, or a new crown piece. The house is a mile-and-a-half out of Siddermorton. Almost all the village is hers, saving a fringe to the south which belongs to Sir John Gotch, and she rules it with an autocratic rule, refreshing in these days of divided government. She orders and forbids marriages, drives objectionable people out of the village by the simple expedient of raising their rent, dismisses labourers, obliges heretics to go to church, and made Susan Dangett, who wanted to call her little girl “Euphemia,” have the infant christened “Mary-Anne.” She is a sturdy Broad Protestant and disapproves of the Vicar’s going bald like a tonsure. She is on the Village Council, which obsequiously trudges up the hill and over the moor to her, and (as she is a trifle deaf) speaks all its speeches into her speaking trumpet instead of a rostrum. She takes no interest now in politics, but until last year she was an active enemy of “that Gladstone.” She has parlour maids instead of footmen to do her waiting, because of Hockley, the American stockbroker, and his four Titans in plush.

She exercises what is almost a fascination upon the village. If in the bar-parlour of the Cat and Cornucopia you swear by God no one would be shocked, but if you swore by Lady Hammergallow they would probably be shocked enough to turn you out of the room. When she drives through Siddermorton she always calls upon Bessy Flump, the postmistress, to hear all that has happened, and then upon Miss Finch, the dressmaker, to check back Bessy Flump. Sometimes she calls upon the Vicar, sometimes upon Mrs. Mendham whom she snubs, and even sometimes on Crump. Her sparkling pair of greys almost ran over the Angel as he was walking down to the village.

“So *that’s* the genius!” said Lady Hammergallow, and turned and looked at him through the gilt glasses on a stick that she always carried in her shrivelled and shaky hand. “Lunatic indeed! The poor creature has rather a pretty face. I’m sorry I’ve missed him.”

===Chinese===

锡德莫顿园里有座锡德莫顿宅，老哈默加洛夫人就住在那里，主要靠红葡萄酒和村里的小道传闻过日子。她是位亲热的老太太，脖子上的皮松成一绺一绺，脸蛋搽得红扑扑的，脾气一阵一阵地古怪发作；对她底下人的一切人间苦难，她有三样法宝：一瓶杜松子酒，一条慈善毯子，或者一枚崭新的五先令克朗币。宅子离锡德莫顿村一英里半。全村几乎都归她所有，只有南边一线地属约翰·戈奇爵士；她实行的是专制统治，在这分权自治的年代里倒令人耳目一新。她下令或禁止婚嫁，用涨房租这一简单手腕把碍眼的人赶出村子，辞退庄稼汉，逼异端分子上教堂，还叫苏珊·丹格特（Susan Dangett）给婴儿改了名字行洗礼——她本想给小女儿取名“尤菲米娅”，夫人硬让她叫“玛丽-安妮”。她是位坚定的广教派新教徒，不赞成教区牧师秃成一副削发僧的模样。她是村议会的成员，议会毕恭毕敬地跋涉上山、越过荒野去见她，而且（因为她耳朵有点背）所有发言都得对着她的号筒讲，不设讲坛。她如今对政治不再上心，可直到去年，她还是“那个格莱斯顿”的死对头。她用客厅侍女而不用男仆伺候端盘，这是因为霍克利（Hockley）——那位美国股票经纪人——和他的四个穿长毛绒的巨汉。

她对这村子施展着一种近乎魔力的影响。在“猫与丰饶角”的酒厅里，你要指上帝起誓，没人会吃惊；可你要指哈默加洛夫人起誓，人家准会吃惊得把你轰出屋去。她乘车穿过锡德莫顿时，总要去看邮局女局长贝西·弗拉姆普（Bessy Flump），听听发生了的一切，然后再去看裁缝芬奇小姐（Miss Finch），核对贝西·弗拉姆普说的对不对。她有时去看教区牧师，有时去看门德姆太太——顺带敲打她两句——偶尔也看看克伦普。她那对神气的灰马差点撞上天使，当时他正步行下山进村。

“原来*那就是*那位天才！”哈默加洛夫人说着，转过身来，用那柄带柄的金丝眼镜打量他——那眼镜她总攥在她干瘪发颤的手里。“还说是什么疯子！这可怜人倒有张挺俊的脸。错过了他，真遗憾。”

===Original===

But she went on to the vicarage nevertheless, and demanded news of it all. The conflicting accounts of Miss Flump, Miss Finch, Mrs. Mendham, Crump, and Mrs. Jehoram had puzzled her immensely. The Vicar, hard pressed, did all he could to say into her speaking trumpet what had really happened. He toned down the wings and the saffron robe. But he felt the case was hopeless. He spoke of his protégé as “Mr.” Angel. He addressed pathetic asides to the kingfisher. The old lady noticed his confusion. Her queer old head went jerking backwards and forwards, now the speaking trumpet in his face when he had nothing to say, then the shrunken eyes peering at him, oblivious of the explanation that was coming from his lips. A great many Ohs! and Ahs! She caught some fragments certainly.

“You have asked him to stop with you﻿—indefinitely?” said Lady Hammergallow with a Great Idea taking shape rapidly in her mind.

“I did﻿—perhaps inadvertently﻿—make such﻿—”

===Chinese===

可她还是径直到了牧师公馆，要求把事情原原本本讲一遍。弗拉姆普小姐、芬奇小姐、门德姆太太、克伦普和杰霍拉姆太太各执一词，把她搅得稀里糊涂。教区牧师被逼得紧了，只得对着她的号筒，把事情原委拣能说的都说了一遍。他把翅膀和藏红花色袍子的情节淡化了些。可他觉得这案子全无希望。他称他的被保护人为“天使先生”。他还对着那只翠鸟标本说了几句可怜的旁白。老太太注意到了他的慌乱。她那颗古怪的老脑袋一冲一冲地前后点动，一会儿把号筒杵在他脸上——正当他无话可说的时候——一会儿又眯起萎缩的眼睛端详他，全不管他嘴上正在作解释。好一大串“哦！”和“啊！”她确实听进去了一些片段。

“你请他住在你这儿——无限期？”哈默加洛夫人说，一个伟大的主意在她心里迅速成形。

“我确实——或许是一时失察——作过这样的——”

===Original===

“And you don’t know where he comes from?”

“Not at all.”

“Nor who his father is, I suppose?” said Lady Hammergallow mysteriously.

===Chinese===

“你也不知道他从哪儿来？”

“完全不知道。”

“我猜，也不知道他父亲是谁？”哈默加洛夫人神秘兮兮地说。

===Original===

“No,” said the Vicar.

“*Now!*” said Lady Hammergallow archly, and keeping her glasses to her eye, she suddenly dug at his ribs with her trumpet.

“My *dear* Lady Hammergallow!”

===Chinese===

“不知道。”教区牧师说。

“*这就对了！*”哈默加洛夫人狡黠地说，眼镜仍贴在眼上，突然用号筒戳了戳他的肋骨。

“我*亲爱的*哈默加洛夫人！”

===Original===

“I thought so. Don’t think *I* would blame you, Mr. Hilyer.” She gave a corrupt laugh that she delighted in. “The world is the world, and men are men. And the poor boy’s a cripple, eh? A kind of judgment. In mourning, I noticed. It reminds me of the *Scarlet Letter*. The mother’s dead, I suppose. It’s just as well. Really﻿—I’m not a *narrow* woman﻿—I *respect* you for having him. Really I do.”

“But, *Lady* Hammergallow!”

“Don’t spoil everything by denying it. It is so very, very plain, to a woman of the world. That Mrs. Mendham! She amuses me with her suspicions. Such odd ideas! In a Curate’s wife. But I hope it didn’t happen when you were in orders.”

===Chinese===

“我早料到了。别以为*我*会怪您，希利尔先生。”她发出一阵心照不宣的坏笑，那是她的得意之作。“世界就是这个世界，男人就是男人。可怜的孩子是个残废，嗯？算是一种报应吧。我还注意到他穿着丧服。这叫我想起*《红字》*来。母亲想必死了。那样倒也好。真的——我可不是个*心胸狭窄*的女人——您肯收留他，我倒*敬佩*您。真的，我敬佩。”

“可是，哈默加洛*夫人*！”

“别什么都否认，那样反而全糟蹋了。在一个见过世面的女人眼里，这事明得不能再明了。那位门德姆太太！她那些疑神疑鬼真叫我好笑。多古怪的念头！出在一个副牧师太太身上。不过我希望，事情不是您已经领了圣职之后出的。”

===Original===

“Lady Hammergallow, I protest. Upon my word.”

“Mr. Hilyer, I protest. I *know*. Not anything you can say will alter my opinion one jot. Don’t try. I never suspected you were nearly such an interesting man.”

“But this suspicion is unendurable!”

===Chinese===

“哈默加洛夫人，我抗议。我以人格担保。”

“希利尔先生，我也抗议。我*知道*。您说什么也改动不了我的看法一丝一毫。别费劲了。我从没想到，您竟是个这么有趣的人。”

“可这种猜疑让人没法忍受！”

===Original===

“We will help him together, Mr. Hilyer. You may rely upon me. It is most romantic.” She beamed benevolence.

“But, Lady Hammergallow, I *must* speak!”

She gripped her ear-trumpet resolutely, and held it before her and shook her head.

===Chinese===

“咱们一起帮帮他，希利尔先生。您尽管放心。这真是太罗曼蒂克了。”她满脸慈祥地放着光。

“可是，哈默加洛夫人，我*必须*说话！”

她毅然攥紧助听号筒，举在身前，摇了摇头。

===Original===

“He has quite a genius for music, Vicar, so I hear?”

“I can assure you most solemnly﻿—”

“I thought so. And being a cripple﻿—”

===Chinese===

“听说他在音乐上相当有天才，牧师？”

“我可以向您郑重保证——”

“我就知道。再说又是个残废——”

===Original===

“You are under a most cruel﻿—”

“I thought that if his gift is really what that Jehoram woman says.”

“An unjustifiable suspicion that ever a man﻿—”

===Chinese===

“您这是抱着一个最残忍的——”

“我想，倘若他的天分真像那位杰霍拉姆太太说的那样。”

“一个毫无根据的猜疑，天下哪有男人——”

===Original===

(“I don’t think much of her judgment, of course.”)

“Consider my position. Have I gained *no* character?”

“It might be possible to do something for him as a performer.”

===Chinese===

（“她的眼光嘛，我当然不大信得过。”）

“想想我的身份。我这点清誉就一文不值吗？”

“以一个演奏家的身份，兴许还能为他谋点出路。”

===Original===

“Have I﻿—(*Bother! It’s no good!*)”

“And so, dear Vicar, I propose to give him an opportunity of showing us what he can do. I have been thinking it all over as I drove here. On Tuesday next, I will invite just a few people of taste, and he shall bring his violin. Eigh? And if that goes well, I will see if I can get some introductions and really *push* him.”

“But *Lady*, Lady Hammergallow.”

===Chinese===

“我难道——（*糟了！白费劲！*）”

“所以呢，亲爱的牧师，我打算给他一个机会，让咱们瞧瞧他的本事。来的路上我一路都在盘算。下星期二，我只请几位有品味的人，让他带上他的小提琴来。嗯？倘若反响不错，我再想想能不能寻几封引荐，好好地*捧*他一捧。”

“可是*夫人*，哈默加洛夫人。”

===Original===

“Not another word!” said Lady Hammergallow, still resolutely holding her speaking trumpet before her and clutching her eyeglasses. “I really must not leave those horses. Cutler is so annoyed if I keep them too long. He finds waiting tedious, poor man, unless there is a public-house near.” She made for the door.

“*Damn!*” said the Vicar, under his breath. He had never used the word since he had taken orders. It shows you how an Angel’s visit may disorganize a man.

He stood under the verandah watching the carriage drive away. The world seemed coming to pieces about him. Had he lived a virtuous celibate life for thirty odd years in vain? The things of which these people thought him capable! He stood and stared at the green cornfield opposite, and down at the straggling village. It seemed real enough. And yet for the first time in his life there was a queer doubt of its reality. He rubbed his chin, then turned and went slowly upstairs to his dressing-room, and sat for a long time staring at a garment of some yellow texture. “Know his father!” he said. “And he is immortal, and was fluttering about his heaven when my ancestors were marsupials.﻿ ﻿… I wish he was there now.”

He got up and began to feel the robe.

“I wonder how they get such things,” said the Vicar. Then he went and stared out of the window. “I suppose everything is wonderful, even the rising and setting of the sun. I suppose there is no adamantine ground for any belief. But one gets into a regular way of taking things. This disturbs it. I seem to be waking up to the Invisible. It is the strangest of uncertainties. I have not felt so stirred and unsettled since my adolescence.”

===Chinese===

“不必再说了！”哈默加洛夫人说，仍旧毅然把号筒举在身前，一手攥着眼镜。“我实在不能把那两匹马再撂着了。让它们久等，卡特勒（Cutler）要恼火的。他觉得干等乏味，可怜的人——除非附近有酒馆。”她朝门口走去。

“*该死！*”教区牧师压着嗓子骂道。自领圣职以来，他还从没用过这个词。由此可见，天使的一次来访能搅乱一个人到什么地步。

他站在游廊下，望着马车驶远。世界仿佛正在他四周崩解。他过了三十几年清心的独身生活，难道全白过了？这些人竟以为他干得出那种事！他站着，呆望对面绿色的麦田，又低头望那摊开的村落。这一切似乎够实在的了。然而有生以来第一次，他对这实在生出一丝古怪的怀疑。他揉了揉下巴，转身上楼，慢慢走进更衣室，久久坐着，呆望着一件黄色质料的衣裳。“知道他父亲是谁！”他说。“他是不朽的，当年我的祖先还是揣着育儿袋的袋鼠辈的时候，他就在他的天堂里飞来飞去了。……我倒巴望他现在就在那儿。”

他站起身，动手摸那件袍子。

“真不知他们是怎样弄到这些东西的，”教区牧师说。随后他走到窗前，望着外面出神。“我想，样样东西都是奇迹，就连太阳的东升西落也是。我想，任何信仰都没有金刚不坏的根基。只是人习惯了按部就班地看待事物。这件事把它搅乱了。我仿佛正在醒来看见那无形之物。这是一种再奇怪不过的没着没落。打从少年时代以来，我还没有这样心潮翻涌、坐立不安过。”

## XXII Morning / 二十二 早晨

===Original===

The Vicar going to call the Angel, found him dressed and leaning out of his window. It was a glorious morning, still dewy, and the rising sunlight slanting round the corner of the house, struck warm and yellow upon the hillside. The birds were astir in the hedges and shrubbery. Up the hillside﻿—for it was late in August﻿—a plough drove slowly. The Angel’s chin rested upon his hands and he did not turn as the Vicar came up to him.

“How’s the wing?” said the Vicar.

“I’d forgotten it,” said the Angel. “Is that yonder a man?”

===Chinese===

教区牧师去叫天使，发现他已经穿戴整齐，正探身窗外。那是个晴朗的早晨，露水未晞，初升的阳光斜斜地绕过屋角，暖融融、金灿灿地照在山坡上。树篱和灌木林里鸟儿正闹。山坡上——那时已是八月末——一张犁缓缓地走着。天使的下巴搁在双手上，教区牧师走近时，他也没有回头。

“翅膀怎么样了？”教区牧师问。

“我都忘了它了，”天使说。“那边那个是人吗？”

===Original===

The Vicar looked. “That’s a ploughman.”

“Why does he go to and fro like that? Does it amuse him?”

“He’s ploughing. That’s his work.”

===Chinese===

教区牧师望了望。“那是个犁地的。”

“他为什么那样走来走去？他觉得好玩吗？”

“他在犁地。那是他的活儿。”

===Original===

“Work! Why does he do it? It seems a monotonous thing to do.”

“It is,” admitted the Vicar. “But he has to do it to get a living, you know. To get food to eat and all that kind of thing.”

“How curious!” said the Angel. “Do all men have to do that? Do you?”

===Chinese===

“活儿！他为什么要干这个？瞧着是件顶单调的事。”

“确实单调，”教区牧师承认说，“可他得靠这个谋生，你晓得。挣口饭吃，还有那一类的事。”

“真古怪！”天使说。“所有人都得干这个吗？你呢？”

===Original===

“Oh, no. He does it for me; does my share.”

“Why?” asked the Angel.

“Oh! in return for things I do for him, you know. We go in for division of labour in this world. Exchange is no robbery.”

===Chinese===

“哦，不。他替我干；把我的那份也干了。”

“为什么？”天使问。

“哦！作为回报，我也替他做事，你晓得。我们这个世界讲究分工。交易可不是抢劫。”

===Original===

“I see,” said the Angel, with his eyes still on the ploughman’s heavy movements.

“What do you do for him?”

“That seems an easy question to you,” said the Vicar, “but really!﻿—it’s difficult. Our social arrangements are rather complicated. It’s impossible to explain these things all at once, before breakfast. Don’t you feel hungry?”

===Chinese===

“我明白了，”天使说，眼睛仍盯着犁田人那迟重的动作。

“你替他做什么呢？”

“这问题听着容易，”教区牧师说，“可是说真的！——难答得很。我们的社会安排相当复杂。这些事没法一下子讲清，何况还没吃早饭。你不觉得饿吗？”

===Original===

“I think I do,” said the Angel slowly, still at the window; and then abruptly, “Somehow I can’t help thinking that ploughing must be far from enjoyable.”

“Possibly,” said the Vicar, “very possibly. But breakfast is ready. Won’t you come down?”

The Angel left the window reluctantly.

===Chinese===

“我想是饿了，”天使缓缓地说，人还在窗前；随即又冒出一句，“不知怎么，我总忍不住想，犁地这营生准没什么乐趣。”

“也许，”教区牧师说，“很可能。可早饭已经好了。不下来吗？”

天使恋恋不舍地离开了窗口。

===Original===

“Our society,” explained the Vicar on the staircase, “is a complicated organisation.”

“Yes?”

“And it is so arranged that some do one thing and some another.”

===Chinese===

“我们的社会，”教区牧师在楼梯上解说，“是个复杂的组织。”

“是吗？”

“它的安排是：一些人干这一样，另一些人干那一样。”

===Original===

“And that lean, bent old man trudges after that heavy blade of iron pulled by a couple of horses while we go down to eat?”

“Yes. You will find it is perfectly just. Ah! mushrooms and poached eggs! It’s the social system. Pray be seated. Possibly it strikes you as unfair?”

“I’m puzzled,” said the Angel.

“The drink I’m sending you is called coffee,” said the Vicar. “I daresay you are. When I was a young man I was puzzled in the same way. But afterwards comes a broader view of things. (These black things are called mushrooms; they look beautiful.) Other considerations. All men are brothers, of course, but some are younger brothers, so to speak. There is work that requires culture and refinement, and work in which culture and refinement would be an impediment. And the rights of property must not be forgotten. One must render unto Caesar.﻿ ﻿… Do you know, instead of explaining this matter now (this is yours), I think I will lend you a little book to read (*chum*, *chum*, *chum*﻿—these mushrooms are well up to their appearance), which sets the whole thing out very clearly.”

===Chinese===

“于是那个精瘦佝偻的老人，就跟着两匹马拉的那块沉重的铁刃一步步跋涉，咱们却下楼去吃饭？”

“正是。你会发觉这再公道不过。啊！蘑菇和水波蛋！这就是社会制度。请坐吧。你兴许觉得这不公平？”

“我是给弄糊涂了。”天使说。

“我给你喝的这东西叫咖啡，”教区牧师说。“你准是糊涂了。我年轻时也这样糊涂过。可往后眼界就会放宽。（这些黑东西叫蘑菇；长得漂亮，味道也配得上。）还有别的考虑。人当然都是兄弟，可有的，这么说吧，是弟弟。有些活计需要教养和文雅，也有些活计，教养和文雅反倒碍事。还有财产权也不容忘记。该撒的物当归给该撒。……这样吧，与其现在就讲解这个（这是你的），我看不如借一本小书给你读读（*嗑*、*嗑*、*嗑*——这蘑菇果然名不虚传），书里把整套事情讲得清清楚楚。”

## XLIV Mrs. Hinijer Acts / 四十四 希尼杰太太出手

===Original===

Mrs. Hinijer surprised the Vicar by tapping at his study door after tea. “Begging your pardon, Sir,” said Mrs. Hinijer. “But might I make so bold as to speak to you for a moment?”

“Certainly, Mrs. Hinijer,” said the Vicar, little dreaming of the blow that was coming. He held a letter in his hand, a very strange and disagreeable letter from his bishop, a letter that irritated and distressed him, criticising in the strongest language the guests he chose to entertain in his own house. Only a popular bishop living in a democratic age, a bishop who was still half a pedagogue, could have written such a letter.

Mrs. Hinijer coughed behind her hand and struggled with some respiratory disorganisation. The Vicar felt apprehensive. Usually in their interviews he was the most disconcerted. Invariably so when the interview ended.

===Chinese===

喝过下午茶，希尼杰太太来敲书房的门，让教区牧师吃了一惊。“请老爷恕罪，”希尼杰太太说，“小的能不能斗胆跟您说两句话？”

“当然可以，希尼杰太太，”教区牧师说，全然想不到即将临头的一击。他手里拿着一封信，一封来自主教的非常古怪、非常败兴的信；信里用最激烈的言辞，指责他竟在自己家中款待那样的客人，激得他又恼又痛。只有一位生活在民主时代的得人心主教，一位身上还留着半个教书匠影子的主教，才写得出这样的信。

希尼杰太太掩着手咳了一声，同某种呼吸系统的紊乱搏斗着。教区牧师心里发紧。往常两人面谈，最狼狈的总是他。到谈话结束时尤其如此，无一例外。

===Original===

“Well?” he said.

“May I make so bold, sir, as to arst when Mr. Angel is a-going?” (Cough.)

The Vicar started. “To ask when Mr. Angel is going?” he repeated slowly to gain time. “*Another!*”

===Chinese===

“什么事？”他说。

“小的斗胆问一句，老爷，天使先生什么时候走？”（咳。）

教区牧师一惊。“问天使先生什么时候走？”他慢慢重复了一遍，好争取时间。“*又来一个！*”

===Original===

“I’m sorry, sir. But I’ve been used to waitin’ on gentlefolks, sir; and you’d hardly imagine how it feels quite to wait on such as ’im.”

“Such as﻿ ﻿… *’im*! Do I understand you, Mrs. Hinijer, that you don’t like Mr. Angel?”

“You see, sir, before I came to you, sir, I was at Lord Dundoller’s seventeen years, and you, sir﻿—if you will excuse me﻿—are a perfect gentleman yourself, sir﻿—though in the Church. And then.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“对不住，老爷。可小的伺候惯了体面人，老爷；您简直想不出，伺候他这样一位是什么滋味。”

“这样一位……*他*！我没听错吧，希尼杰太太，你不喜欢天使先生？”

“您瞧，老爷，没上您这儿来以前，小的在邓多勒勋爵（Lord Dundoller）府上做了十七年；而您呢，老爷——恕小的直言——您自个儿就是一位十全十美的绅士，老爷——虽说在教会里当差。可是后来。……”

===Original===

“Dear, dear!” said the Vicar. “And don’t you regard Mr. Angel as a gentleman?”

“I’m sorry to ’ave to say it, sir.”

“But what﻿ ﻿… ? Dear me! Surely!”

===Chinese===

“哎呀呀！”教区牧师说。“你不把天使先生当绅士看待？”

“小的不得已才这么说，老爷。”

“可是……？天哪！不至于吧！”

===Original===

“I’m sorry to ’ave to say it, sir. But when a party goes turning vegetarian suddenly and putting out all the cooking, and hasn’t no proper luggage of his own, and borry’s shirts and socks from his ’ost, and don’t know no better than to try his knife at peas (as I seed my very self), and goes talking in odd corners to the housemaids, and folds up his napkin after meals, and eats with his fingers at minced veal, and plays the fiddle in the middle of the night keeping everybody awake, and stares and grins at his elders a-getting upstairs, and generally misconducts himself with things that I can scarcely tell you all, one can’t help thinking, sir. Thought is free, sir, and one can’t help coming to one’s own conclusions. Besides which, there is talk all over the village about him﻿—what with one thing and another. I know a gentleman when I sees a gentleman, and I know a gentleman when I don’t see a gentleman, and me, and Susan, and George, we’ve talked it over, being the upper servants, so to speak, and experienced, and leaving out that girl Delia, who I only hope won’t come to any harm through him, and depend upon it, sir, that Mr. Angel ain’t what you think he is, sir, and the sooner he leaves this house the better.”

Mrs. Hinijer ceased abruptly and stood panting but stern, and with her eyes grimly fixed on the Vicar’s face.

“*Really*, Mrs. Hinijer!” said the Vicar, and then, “Oh *Lord*!”

===Chinese===

“小的不得已才这么说，老爷。可一位客人，突然吃起了素，把满灶的烹调都撂了；自个儿连件像样的行李都没有，衬衣袜子全朝房东借；拿刀子去叉豌豆，懂得还不如个娃娃（小的亲眼瞧见的）；躲在犄角旮旯里同女仆们搭话；吃完了饭还要把餐巾叠起来；吃肉末小牛肉直接下手指头；半夜里拉琴，闹得全家睡不成；上楼梯时冲着长辈又盯又龇牙；还有种种行止失当的事，小的一时半会儿跟您说不全——老爷，人就不能不多想了。思想是自由的，老爷，各人免不了要得出各人的结论。再说，村里上下也都在议论他——这事那事地凑一块儿了。小的一眼认得出绅士，也一眼认得出不是绅士的。小的，还有苏珊、乔治，我们仨合计过——我们算是上等仆人，这么说吧，又是见过世面的；不算那位迪莉娅姑娘——但愿她别在他身上惹出祸来——老爷，您放心，天使先生绝不是您以为的那种人，他越早离开这宅子越好。”

希尼杰太太戛然而止，站在那儿喘着气，神情严厉，一双眼睛冷冷地钉在教区牧师脸上。

“*真是的*，希尼杰太太！”教区牧师说，接着又道，“哦，*主啊*！”

===Original===

“What *have* I done?” said the Vicar, suddenly starting up and appealing to the inexorable fates. “What *have* I done?”

“There’s no knowing,” said Mrs. Hinijer. “Though a deal of talk in the village.”

“*Bother!*” said the Vicar, going and staring out of the window. Then he turned. “Look here, Mrs. Hinijer! Mr. Angel will be leaving this house in the course of a week. Is that enough?”

“Quite,” said Mrs. Hinijer. “And I feel sure, sir.﻿ ﻿…”

The Vicar’s eyes fell with unwonted eloquence upon the door.

===Chinese===

“我*到底*做了什么？”教区牧师霍地站起来，向那无情的命运呼告。“我*到底*做了什么？”

“这谁说得准呢，”希尼杰太太说，“不过村里的闲话倒是不少。”

“*烦死了！*”教区牧师说着，走到窗前望着外面出神。随后他转过身。“听着，希尼杰太太！天使先生不出一个星期就会离开这宅子。这总够了吧？”

“足够了，”希尼杰太太说。“而且小的相信，老爷。……”

教区牧师的目光落在门上，那一落不同寻常地意味深长。

## XXXI Mrs. Jehoram’s Breadth of View / 三十一 杰霍拉姆太太的胸襟

===Original===

“I heard someone playing the fiddle in the Vicarage, as I came by,” said Mrs. Jehoram, taking her cup of tea from Mrs. Mendham.

“The Vicar plays,” said Mrs. Mendham. “I have spoken to George about it, but it’s no good. I do not think a Vicar should be allowed to do such things. It’s so foreign. But there, *he*.﻿ ﻿…”

“I know, dear,” said Mrs. Jehoram. “But I heard the Vicar once at the schoolroom. I don’t think this *was* the Vicar. It was quite clever, some of it, quite smart, you know. And new. I was telling dear Lady Hammergallow this morning. I fancy﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我路过的时候，听见牧师公馆里有人在拉琴，”杰霍拉姆太太说着，从门德姆太太手里接过茶。

“是教区牧师在拉，”门德姆太太说。“我跟乔治提过，可没用。我看就不该容许一位教区牧师干这种事。太洋派了。可偏偏，*他*。……”

“我晓得，亲爱的，”杰霍拉姆太太说。“不过教区牧师拉琴我听过一回，是在学校教室里。我看这回*不是*教区牧师。拉得相当聪明，有几段，相当漂亮，你晓得。而且新。今天早上我跟亲爱的哈默加洛夫人就是这么说的。我琢磨着——”

===Original===

“The lunatic! Very likely. These half-witted people.﻿ ﻿… My dear, I don’t think I shall ever forget that dreadful encounter. Yesterday.”

“Nor I.”

“My poor girls! They are too shocked to say a word about it. I was telling dear Lady Ham﻿—”

===Chinese===

“那个疯子！多半是。这种半痴不慧的人。……亲爱的，那场可怕的遭遇，我怕是一辈子忘不了。就在昨天。”

“我也忘不了。”

“我那几个可怜的女儿！她们吓得好些天说不出话来。我正跟亲爱的哈默——”

===Original===

“Quite proper of them. It was *dreadful*, dear. For them.”

“And now, dear, I want you to tell me frankly﻿—Do you really believe that creature was a man?”

“You should have heard the violin.”

===Chinese===

“她们这样才对。那*太可怕*了，亲爱的。对她们而言。”

“那么，亲爱的，我要你坦白告诉我——你当真相信那家伙是个男人？”

“你该听听那小提琴。”

===Original===

“I still more than half suspect, Jessie﻿—” Mrs. Mendham leant forward as if to whisper.

Mrs. Jehoram helped herself to cake. “I’m sure no woman could play the violin quite like I heard it played this morning.”

“Of course, if you say so that settles the matter,” said Mrs. Mendham. Mrs. Jehoram was the autocratic authority in Siddermorton upon all questions of art, music and belles-lettres. Her late husband had been a minor poet. Then Mrs. Mendham added a judicial “Still﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我到现在还有一大半疑心，杰西——”门德姆太太身子前倾，像要交头接耳。

杰霍拉姆太太自己动手拿了块蛋糕。“我敢说，没有哪个女人能把我今天早上听到的那琴拉成那样。”

“当然，你既然这么说，事情就定了，”门德姆太太说。在锡德莫顿，凡属艺术、音乐和纯文学的问题，杰霍拉姆太太便是不容分说的权威。她已故的丈夫是位小诗人。随后门德姆太太又裁决似的补了一句“不过——”

===Original===

“Do you know,” said Mrs. Jehoram, “I’m half inclined to believe the dear Vicar’s story.”

“How *good* of you, Jessie,” said Mrs. Mendham.

“But really, I don’t think he *could* have had anyone in the Vicarage before that afternoon. I feel sure we should have heard of it. I don’t see how a strange cat could come within four miles of Siddermorton without the report coming round to us. The people here gossip so.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“你可知道，”杰霍拉姆太太说，“我倒有几分倾向于相信亲爱的教区牧师讲的故事了。”

“你*真*宽宏大量，杰西，”门德姆太太说。

“不过说真的，我想那天下午以前，牧师公馆里*不可能*藏过什么人。要不然我们准会听到风声。一只生猫走近锡德莫顿四英里之内，消息不传到咱们耳朵里，我看都不可能。这地方的人就是会嚼舌头。……”

===Original===

“I always distrust the Vicar,” said Mrs. Mendham. “I know him.”

“Yes. But the story is plausible. If this Mr. Angel were someone very clever and eccentric﻿—”

“He would have to be *very* eccentric to dress as he did. There are degrees and limits, dear.”

===Chinese===

“我一向信不过教区牧师，”门德姆太太说。“我了解他。”

“是。可这故事讲得通。倘若这位天使先生是位非常聪明、非常乖僻的人物——”

“那他也得乖僻到*家*了，才会穿成那副样子。凡事有分寸、有界限，亲爱的。”

===Original===

“But kilts,” said Mrs. Jehoram.

“Are all very well in the Highlands.﻿ ﻿…”

Mrs. Jehoram’s eyes had rested upon a black speck creeping slowly across a patch of yellowish-green up the hill.

===Chinese===

“可苏格兰短裙呢，”杰霍拉姆太太说。

“在高地随便穿。……”

杰霍拉姆太太的眼睛一直停在山坡上一小片黄绿色之间缓缓爬行的一个黑点上。

===Original===

“There he goes,” said Mrs. Jehoram, rising, “across the cornfield. I’m sure that’s him. I can see the hump. Unless it’s a man with a sack. Bless me, Minnie! here’s an opera glass. How convenient for peeping at the Vicarage!﻿ ﻿… Yes, it’s the man. He is a man. With *such* a sweet face.”

Very unselfishly she allowed her hostess to share the opera glass. For a minute there was a rustling silence.

“His dress,” said Mrs. Mendham, “is *quite* respectable now.”

===Chinese===

“他来了，”杰霍拉姆太太站起身说，“正穿过麦田。准是他。我看得见那个驼背。除非是个背麻袋的人。哎呀，明妮！这儿有一副观剧镜。窥探牧师公馆可真方便！……没错，就是那个人。他是个人。一张脸*那么*甜。”

她极无私地让女主人也分享了观剧镜。有一分钟，只听得衣裳窸窣，一片肃静。

“他的衣裳，”门德姆太太说，“如今*十分*体面了。”

===Original===

“Quite,” said Mrs. Jehoram.

Pause.

“He looks cross!”

===Chinese===

“十分体面。”杰霍拉姆太太说。

停顿。

“他像是在生气！”

===Original===

“And his coat is dusty.”

“He walks steadily enough,” said Mrs. Mendham, “or one might think.﻿ ﻿… This hot weather.﻿ ﻿…”

Another pause.

===Chinese===

“他的上衣也脏了。”

“他步子倒挺稳，”门德姆太太说，“不然人家还当。……这么热的天。……”

又一阵停顿。

===Original===

“You see, dear,” said Mrs. Jehoram, putting down the lorgnette. “What I was going to say was, that possibly he might be a genius in disguise.”

“If you can call next door to nothing a disguise.”

“No doubt it was eccentric. But I’ve seen children in little blouses, not at all unlike him. So many clever people *are* peculiar in their dress and manners. A genius may steal a horse where a bank-clerk may not look over the hedge. Very possibly he’s quite well known and laughing at our Arcadian simplicity. And really it wasn’t so improper as some of these New Women bicycling costumes. I saw one in one of the Illustrated Papers only a few days ago﻿—the *New Budget* I think﻿—quite tights, you know, dear. No﻿—I cling to the genius theory. Especially after the playing. I’m sure the creature is original. Perhaps very amusing. In fact, I intend to ask the Vicar to introduce me.”

===Chinese===

“你瞧，亲爱的，”杰霍拉姆太太放下观剧镜说。“我方才想说的是，他没准是位隐姓埋名的天才。”

“要是那身打扮也能叫隐姓埋名的话。”

“确实乖僻。可我见过穿小罩衫的孩子，同他那身一点不像的地方也没有。多少聪明人，衣着做派*偏偏*都与众不同。天才偷得马，银行职员连往树篱外看一眼也不行。他很可能大有名气，正笑话咱们田园牧歌式的天真呢。再说，那身打扮比起有些新女性骑自行车的行头，实在算不得有伤风化。前几天我就在画报上见过一位——我想是*《新预算》*——浑身上下紧身，你晓得，亲爱的。不——我坚持天才论。尤其听过那琴之后。我敢说此人确有独创。兴许非常有趣。说真的，我打算请教区牧师给我引见。”

===Original===

“My dear!” cried Mrs. Mendham.

“I’m resolute,” said Mrs. Jehoram.

“I’m afraid you’re rash,” said Mrs. Mendham. “Geniuses and people of that kind are all very well in London. But here﻿—at the Vicarage.”

===Chinese===

“亲爱的！”门德姆太太叫道。

“我主意已定，”杰霍拉姆太太说。

“我怕你太莽撞，”门德姆太太说。“天才和那一类人物，在伦敦随便怎样都好。可在这儿——在牧师公馆。”

===Original===

“We are going to educate the folks. I love originality. At any rate I mean to see him.”

“Take care you don’t see too much of him,” said Mrs. Mendham. “I’ve heard the fashion is quite changing. I understand that some of the very best people have decided that genius is not to be encouraged any more. These recent scandals.﻿ ﻿…”

“Only in literature, I can assure you, dear. In music.﻿ ﻿…”

“Nothing you can say, my dear,” said Mrs. Mendham, going off at a tangent, “will convince me that that person’s costume was not extremely suggestive and improper.”

===Chinese===

“咱们就是要开化开化这些人。我爱独创性。无论如何我要见见他。”

“小心别见得太多，”门德姆太太说。“听说风气变得厉害。我了解，有些头等要人已经议定，天才再不可纵容了。近来那些丑闻。……”

“只是在文学界，我向你担保，亲爱的。音乐界。……”

“无论你说什么，亲爱的，”门德姆太太岔开去说，“都休想叫我相信，那个人的行头不是极尽挑逗、极不体面。”

## IX Parenthesis on Angels / 九 关于天使的插叙

===Original===

Let us be plain. The Angel of this story is the Angel of Art, not the Angel that one must be irreverent to touch﻿—neither the Angel of religious feeling nor the Angel of popular belief. The last we all know. She is alone among the angelic hosts in being distinctly feminine: she wears a robe of immaculate, unmitigated white with sleeves, is fair, with long golden tresses, and has eyes of the blue of Heaven. Just a pure woman she is, pure maiden or pure matron, in her *robe de nuit*, and with wings attached to her shoulder blades. Her callings are domestic and sympathetic, she watches over a cradle or assists a sister soul heavenward. Often she bears a palm leaf, but one would not be surprised if one met her carrying a warming-pan softly to some poor chilly sinner. She it was who came down in a bevy to Marguerite in prison, in the amended last scene in *Faust* at the Lyceum, and the interesting and improving little children that are to die young, have visions of such angels in the novels of Mrs. Henry Wood. This white womanliness with her indescribable charm of lavender-like holiness, her aroma of clean, methodical lives, is, it would seem after all, a purely Teutonic invention. Latin thought knows her not; the old masters have none of her. She is of a piece with that gentle innocent ladylike school of art whereof the greatest triumph is “a lump in one’s throat,” and where wit and passion, scorn and pomp, have no place. The white angel was made in Germany, in the land of blonde women and the domestic sentiments. She comes to us cool and worshipful, pure and tranquil, as silently soothing as the breadth and calmness of the starlit sky, which also is so unspeakably dear to the Teutonic soul.﻿ ﻿… We do her reverence. And to the angels of the Hebrews, those spirits of power and mystery, to Raphael, Zadkiel, and Michael, of whom only Watts has caught the shadow, of whom only Blake has seen the splendour, to them too, do we do reverence.

But this Angel the Vicar shot is, we say, no such angel at all, but the Angel of Italian art, polychromatic and gay. He comes from the land of beautiful dreams and not from any holier place. At best he is a popish creature. Bear patiently, therefore, with his scattered remiges, and be not hasty with your charge of irreverence before the story is read.

===Chinese===

让我们把话说透。本故事里的天使，是艺术的天使，不是那一位须待不敬方能触及的天使——既不是宗教感情的天使，也不是民间信仰的天使。后一位我们谁都认得。她在整队天使中独一无二，是明确的女性：她穿一件洁白无瑕、不打折扣、带袖的白袍，皮肤白皙，长长的金色鬈发，一双眼睛是天国的蓝。她就是一位纯粹的女子——纯粹的少女或纯粹的妇人，穿着她的*睡袍*（robe de nuit），肩胛上安着一对翅膀。她的职分是家务的和慈爱的：她守护摇篮，或搀扶一个姊妹的灵魂升天。她常持一枝棕榈叶，可倘若你撞见她提着暖床炉，轻手轻脚地走向某个又冷又可怜的罪人，你也不会吃惊。在兰心剧院（Lyceum）改订版*《浮士德》*的末场里，成群结队下到狱中探望玛格丽特的，正是她；在亨利·伍德太太的小说里，那些早早夭折、有趣又长进的小孩子望见的，也是这样的天使。这一身白色的女性气质，带着她那薰衣草般圣洁的不可言传的魅力，那清洁而井井有条的生活的芬芳，说到底，看来纯属条顿人的发明。拉丁的思维方式不认识她；古代大师们笔下没有她。她同那门温柔、天真、贵妇气的艺术学派一脉相承——那一派的最高成就是“喉头一哽”，机智与激情、轻蔑与堂皇，在那里全无立足之地。这位白衣天使是德国造的，出产自金发女人和家庭情感的国土。她来到我们面前，清凉而可敬，纯洁而安宁，像星光满天的广袤与沉静一样无声地抚慰人——那片星空，在条顿人的灵魂里也正是说不出的亲切。……我们对她致敬。对希伯来人的那些天使——那些权能与奥秘的精灵，对拉斐尔、扎德基尔和米迦勒，唯有瓦茨（Watts）捕捉到他们的影，唯有布莱克（Blake）见到过他们的辉光——对他们，我们也一样致敬。

可是教区牧师打下的这位天使，我们说，根本不是那样一位天使，而是意大利艺术的天使，五彩而欢快。他来自美梦的国度，不是来自什么更神圣的地方。充其量，他是个天主教味的造物。所以，请耐心容忍他四散的飞羽；在读完这个故事之前，也不要急着指控我们轻慢。

## XLII Sir John Gotch Acts / 四十二 约翰·戈奇爵士出手

===Original===

Sir John Gotch was a little man with scrubby hair, a small, thin nose sticking out of a face crackled with wrinkles, tight brown gaiters, and a riding whip. “I’ve come, you see,” he said, as Mrs. Hinijer closed the door.

“Thank you,” said the Vicar, “I’m obliged to you. I’m really obliged to you.”

“Glad to be of any service to you,” said Sir John Gotch. (Angular attitude.)

===Chinese===

约翰·戈奇爵士是个小个子男人，头发粗硬蓬乱，一张皴满皱纹的脸上戳出一个小而尖的鼻子，绑着紧邦邦的棕色绑腿，手里一根马鞭。“你瞧，我来了，”希尼杰太太关门时，他说。

“谢谢你，”教区牧师说，“我很感激你。实在感激。”

“乐意为您效劳，”约翰·戈奇爵士说。（一副棱角分明的姿态。）

===Original===

“This business,” said the Vicar, “this unfortunate business of the barbed wire﻿—is really, you know, a most unfortunate business.”

Sir John Gotch became decidedly more angular in his attitude. “It is,” he said.

“This Mr. Angel being my guest﻿—”

===Chinese===

“这件事，”教区牧师说，“这件铁蒺藜的不幸事件——实在，你晓得，是件极其不幸的事。”

约翰·戈奇爵士的姿态分明更加棱角分明了。“正是。”他说。

“这位天使先生是我的客人——”

===Original===

“No reason why he should cut my wire,” said Sir John Gotch, briefly.

“None whatever.”

“May I ask *who* this Mr. Angel is?” asked Sir John Gotch with the abruptness of long premeditation.

===Chinese===

“那也不能成为他剪我铁丝的理由，”约翰·戈奇爵士言简意赅地说。

“确实不能。”

“我可以问一句，这位天使先生*究竟*是谁吗？”约翰·戈奇爵士问道，突兀得像蓄谋已久。

===Original===

The Vicar’s fingers jumped to his chin. What *was* the good of talking to a man like Sir John Gotch about Angels?

“To tell you the exact truth,” said the Vicar, “there is a little secret﻿—”

“Lady Hammergallow told me as much.”

===Chinese===

教区牧师的手指一下子摸上下巴。同约翰·戈奇爵士这样的人谈天使，究竟*有什么*用处？

“跟您说实话，”教区牧师说，“这里头有桩小小的隐情——”

“哈默加洛夫人也这么说了。”

===Original===

The Vicar’s face suddenly became bright red.

“Do you know,” said Sir John, with scarcely a pause, “he’s been going about this village preaching Socialism?”

“Good heavens!” said the Vicar, “*No!*”

===Chinese===

教区牧师的脸霎时涨得通红。

“您可知道，”约翰爵士几乎不容停顿地说，“他一直在这个村子里到处宣讲社会主义？”

“天哪！”教区牧师说，“*不会吧！*”

===Original===

“He has. He has been buttonholing every yokel he came across, and asking them why they had to work, while we﻿—I and you, you know﻿—did nothing. He has been saying we ought to educate every man up to your level and mine﻿—out of the rates, I suppose, as usual. He has been suggesting that we﻿—I and you, you know﻿—keep these people down﻿—pith ’em.”

“*Dear* me!” said the Vicar, “I had no idea.”

“He has done this wire-cutting as a demonstration, I tell you, as a Socialistic demonstration. If we don’t come down on him pretty sharply, I tell you, we shall have the palings down in Flinders Lane next, and the next thing will be ricks afire, and every damned (I beg your pardon, Vicar. I know I’m too fond of that word), every blessed pheasant’s egg in the parish smashed. I know these﻿—”

===Chinese===

“他干了。他见一个庄稼汉就拽住一个，问他们凭什么他们非干活不可，而咱们——我和您，您晓得——什么都不干。他一直说，咱们应该把每个人都教育到您我这样的水平——经费嘛，我猜，照例从地方税里出。他还暗示说，咱们——我和您，您晓得——把这些老百姓压在底下——给他们去髓。”

“*天*哪！”教区牧师说，“我一点都不知道。”

“这次剪铁丝，我告诉您，就是一场示威，一场社会主义的示威。咱们要是下不了狠手收拾他，我告诉您，下一步弗林德斯巷的栅栏就得倒，再下一步就是草垛着火，教区里每一颗该死的（请您原谅，牧师。我知道我太爱用这个词）每一颗宝贝雉鸡蛋都得被砸个稀烂。这种人我见得多了——”

===Original===

“A Socialist,” said the Vicar, quite put out, “I had *no* idea.”

“You see why I am inclined to push matters against our gentleman though he *is* your guest. It seems to me he has been taking advantage of your paternal﻿—”

“Oh, *not* paternal!” said the Vicar. “Really﻿—”

===Chinese===

“一个社会主义者，”教区牧师说，心烦意乱，“我*一点*没想到。”

“您瞧，这就是为什么我倾向于对咱们这位绅士采取措施，尽管他*确实是*您的客人。依我看，他一直在利用您的慈父般的——”

“哦，*不是*慈父般的！”教区牧师说。“真的——”

===Original===

“(I beg your pardon, Vicar﻿—it was a slip.) Of your kindness, to go mischief-making everywhere, setting class against class, and the poor man against his bread and butter.”

The Vicar’s fingers were at his chin again.

“So there’s one of two things,” said Sir John Gotch. “Either that guest of yours leaves the parish, or﻿—I take proceedings. That’s final.”

===Chinese===

“（请您原谅，牧师——说滑了嘴。）利用您的好心，到处闯祸，挑动阶级斗阶级，还挑动穷人跟自己的生计过不去。”

教区牧师的手指又摸上了下巴。

“所以就这么两条路，”约翰·戈奇爵士说。“要么您那位客人离开教区，要么——我起诉。就这么定了。”

===Original===

The Vicar’s mouth was all askew.

“That’s the position,” said Sir John, jumping to his feet, “if it were not for you, I should take proceedings at once. As it is﻿—am I to take proceedings or no?”

“You see,” said the Vicar in horrible perplexity.

===Chinese===

教区牧师的嘴都歪了。

“形势就是这样，”约翰爵士说着跳起身，“要不是看在您的份上，我当场就起诉了。眼下的情形——我到底起诉不起诉？”

“您瞧，”教区牧师说，狼狈到了极点。

===Original===

“Well?”

“Arrangements have to be made.”

“He’s a mischief-making idler.﻿ ﻿… I know the breed. But I’ll give you a week﻿—”

===Chinese===

“怎么样？”

“总得安排安排。”

“他是个专会闯祸的闲汉。……这种货色我认得。不过我给您一个星期——”

===Original===

“Thank you,” said the Vicar. “I understand your position. I perceive the situation is getting intolerable.﻿ ﻿…”

“Sorry to give you this bother, of course,” said Sir John.

“A week,” said the Vicar.

===Chinese===

“谢谢您，”教区牧师说。“我明白您的处境。我看得出来，局面正变得叫人没法容忍。……”

“给您添了这桩烦心事，自然抱歉，”约翰爵士说。

“一个星期。”教区牧师说。

===Original===

“A week,” said Sir John, leaving.

---

The Vicar returned, after accompanying Gotch out, and for a long time he remained sitting before the desk in his study, plunged in thought. “A week!” he said, after an immense silence. “Here is an Angel, a glorious Angel, who has quickened my soul to beauty and delight, who has opened my eyes to Wonderland, and something more than Wonderland,﻿ ﻿… and I have promised to get rid of him in a week! What are we men made of?﻿ ﻿… How *can* I tell him?”

He began to walk up and down the room, then he went into the dining-room, and stood staring blankly out at the cornfield. The table was already laid for lunch. Presently he turned, still dreaming, and almost mechanically helped himself to a glass of sherry.

===Chinese===

“一个星期。”约翰爵士说着，告辞而去。

---

送走戈奇之后，教区牧师回到屋里，在书房的书桌前久坐，沉入深思。“一个星期！”良久的沉默之后，他说。“这儿有一位天使，一位光辉的天使，他唤醒了我的灵魂去感受美与欢乐，他让我睁眼看见了仙境，还不止仙境，……而我竟答应了一个星期内打发他走！我们这些人究竟是什么做的？……这话教我*怎么*开口？”

他在屋里踱来踱去，随后走进饭厅，呆呆地望着外面的麦田出神。午餐的桌子已经摆好。过了一会儿，他回过身来，仍旧如梦，几乎是机械地给自己斟了一杯雪利酒。

## XXV The Angel Explores the Village (Continued) / 二十五 天使探访村庄（续）

===Original===

Then the Angel came in sight of the forge, where Sandy Bright’s brother was shoeing a horse for the carter from Upmorton. Two hobbledehoys were standing by the forge staring in a bovine way at the proceedings. As the Angel approached these two and then the carter turned slowly through an angle of thirty degrees and watched his approach, staring quietly and steadily at him. The expression on their faces was one of abstract interest.

The Angel became self-conscious for the first time in his life. He drew nearer, trying to maintain an amiable expression on his face, an expression that beat in vain against their granitic stare. His hands were behind him. He smiled pleasantly, looking curiously at the (to him) incomprehensible employment of the smith. But the battery of eyes seemed to angle for his regard. Trying to meet the three pairs at once, the Angel lost his alertness and stumbled over a stone. One of the yokels gave a sarcastic cough, and was immediately covered with confusion at the Angel’s enquiring gaze, nudging his companion with his elbow to cover his disorder. None spoke, and the Angel did not speak.

===Chinese===

随后，天使望见了铁匠铺，桑迪·布莱特的兄弟正在给阿普莫顿（Upmorton）来的车把式钉马掌。两个半大小子站在铺子边，呆头呆脑地盯着这一套活计。天使走近时，这两个人，还有那车把式，缓缓转过三十度的角，望着他过来，不声不响、直直地盯着他瞧。他们脸上的神情，是一种超然的兴致。

天使有生以来第一次觉出了羞怯。他再走近些，竭力在脸上维持一副和气的表情，那表情撞在他们花岗岩般的凝视上，徒劳地碎了。他的手背在身后。他愉快地微笑着，好奇地望着铁匠那门（在他看来）无法理解的营生。可那几双眼睛组成的炮阵，似乎总在斜着瞄他。天使想同时应付这三双眼睛，一失神，绊在一块石头上。一个乡下小子讥诮地咳了一声，被天使探询的目光一扫，顿时慌作一团，用胳膊肘捅了捅同伴，好遮掩自己的失态。谁都不说话，天使也没有开口。

===Original===

So soon as the Angel had passed, one of the three hummed this tune in an aggressive tone.

Then all three of them laughed. One tried to sing something and found his throat contained phlegm. The Angel proceeded on his way.

“Who’s *e* then?” said the second hobbledehoy.

===Chinese===

天使刚一走过，三个人里就有一个挑衅地哼起了这个调子。

接着三个人都笑了。有一个想唱点什么，发觉喉咙里全是痰。天使继续赶他的路。

“那*人*是谁呀？”第二个半大小子说。

===Original===

“Ping, ping, ping,” went the blacksmith’s hammer.

“Spose he’s one of these here foweners,” said the carter from Upmorton. “Daamned silly fool he do look to be sure.”

“Tas the way with them foweners,” said the first hobbledehoy sagely.

===Chinese===

“叮，叮，叮，”铁锤响着。

“保准是那些个外乡佬里的一个，”阿普莫顿来的车把式说。“瞧他那副傻样儿，真真是天字第一号的傻瓜。”

“外乡佬都这德行，”第一个半大小子煞有见识地说。

===Original===

“Got something very like the ’ump,” said the carter from Upmorton. “Daa-a-amned if ’e ent.”

Then the silence healed again, and they resumed their quiet expressionless consideration of the Angel’s retreating figure.

“Very like the ’ump et is,” said the carter after an enormous pause.

===Chinese===

“背上那玩意儿活脱是个驼包，”阿普莫顿来的车把式说。“要不是才怪，真——真怪了去了。”

随后沉寂重新合拢，他们又继续安安静静、面无表情地端详天使远去的背影。

“那背上准是个驼包没错，”隔了老大一阵，车把式说。

## XXIV The Angel Explores the Village / 二十四 天使探访村庄

===Original===

Very unwisely, as I think, the Vicar allowed the Angel to go down into the village by himself, to enlarge his ideas of humanity. Unwisely, because how was he to imagine the reception the Angel would receive? Not thoughtlessly, I am afraid. He had always carried himself with decorum in the village, and the idea of a slow procession through the little street with all the inevitable curious remarks, explanations, pointings, was too much for him. The Angel might do the strangest things, the village was certain to think them. Peering faces. “Who’s *he* got now?” Besides, was it not his duty to prepare his sermon in good time?
