The Angel, duly directed, went down cheerfully by himself﻿—still innocent of most of the peculiarities of the human as distinguished from the angelic turn of mind.

The Angel walked slowly, his white hands folded behind his hunched back, his sweet face looking this way and that. He peered curiously into the eyes of the people he met. A little child picking a bunch of vetch and honeysuckle looked in his face, and forthwith came and put them in his hand. It was about the only kindness he had from a human being (saving only the Vicar and one other). He heard Mother Gustick scolding that granddaughter of hers as he passed the door. “You *Brazen* Faggit﻿—you!” said Mother Gustick. “You Trumpery Baggage!”

The Angel stopped, startled at the strange sounds of Mother Gustick’s voice. “Put yer best clo’es on, and yer feather in yer ’at, and off you goes to meet en, fal lal, and me at ’ome slaving for ye. ’Tis a Fancy Lady you’ll be wantin’ to be, my gal, a walkin’ Touch and Go, with yer idleness and finery﻿—”

===Chinese===

依我看，教区牧师实在欠考虑，竟放任天使独自下村，去开阔他对人类的见识。欠考虑，因为他哪里想象得到天使会受到怎样的接待？恐怕也不是无心，而是有意。他在这村子里向来举止端方，一想到要领着天使在小街上慢慢招摇而过，听着那一准躲不掉的闲话、盘问和指指点点，他就吃不消。天使尽可以做出最离奇的事，村里人反正铁定了心往那上头想。一张张窥探的脸。“这回他*又*捡了个什么人？”再说了，及早安顿他的讲道稿，难道不是他的本分么？天使得了妥帖的指点，便独自欢欢喜喜地下山去了——对人类有别于天使心智的种种怪癖，仍旧一无所知。

天使慢慢走着，一双白净的手交叠在隆起的后背上，那张甜美的脸左顾右盼。他好奇地细看每一个迎面而来的人的眼睛。一个小娃娃正掐一束野豌豆和金银花，抬头望了望他的脸，随即走上前来，把花塞进了他的手里。这差不多是他从一个人那里得到的唯一的善意（除了教区牧师和另外一位）。走过一户门口时，他听见古斯蒂克大妈正在骂她那孙女。“你这个*不要脸的*贱蹄子——就是你！”古斯蒂克大妈说。“你这贱货小骚货！”

天使停住脚，被古斯蒂克大妈嗓音里那陌生的声响吓了一跳。“穿上你最好的衣裳，帽子上插根羽毛，就颠儿颠儿去会他，花哩胡哨，把老娘一个人撂在家给你当牛做马。你想当阔太太呢，我的姑娘，一个招摇过市的轻浮货，凭着你的懒和你的俏——”

===Original===

The voice ceased abruptly, and a great peace came upon the battered air. “Most grotesque and strange!” said the Angel, still surveying this wonderful box of discords. “Walking Touch and Go!” He did not know that Mrs. Gustick had suddenly become aware of his existence, and was scrutinizing his appearance through the window-blind. Abruptly the door flew open, and she stared out into the Angel’s face. A strange apparition, grey and dusty hair, and the dirty pink dress unhooked to show the stringy throat, a discoloured gargoyle, presently to begin spouting incomprehensible abuse.

“Now, then, Mister,” began Mrs. Gustick. “Have ye nothin’ better to do than listen at people’s doors for what you can pick up?”

The Angel stared at her in astonishment.

===Chinese===

声音戛然而止，一片大的安宁降临到这被搅坏的空气上。“真是再古怪没有！”天使说，仍旧端详着这只装满不谐和音的奇妙的盒子。“招摇过市的轻浮货！”他不知道古斯蒂克太太已然察觉了他的存在，正从窗帘后头细审他的模样。门猛地开了，她瞪眼直望进天使的脸。一个怪异的形影：灰扑扑乱蓬蓬的头发，脏兮兮的粉红衣裳敞着钩扣，露出筋条条脖子，一尊变了色的石像鬼，眼看就要开始喷出叫人听不懂的臭骂。

“喂，我说，先生，”古斯蒂克太太开了腔。“你没事干，专在人家门口偷听，好捡些话柄是不是？”

天使惊讶地望着她。

===Original===

“D’year!” said Mrs. Gustick, evidently very angry indeed. “Listenin’.”

“Have you any objection to my hearing.﻿ ﻿…”

“Object to my hearing! Course I have! Whad yer think? You aint such a Ninny.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“听见没！”古斯蒂克太太说，显然当真气极了。“偷听。”

“您反对我听见。……”

“反对我听见！当然反对！你当怎样？你又不痴不傻。……”

===Original===

“But if you didn’t want me to hear, why did you cry out so loud? I thought.﻿ ﻿…”

“*You thought!* Softie﻿—that’s what *you* are! You silly girt staring Gaby, what don’t know any better than to come holding yer girt mouth wide open for all that you can catch holt on? And then off up there to tell! You great Fat-Faced, Tale-Bearin’ Silly-Billy! I’d be ashamed to come poking and peering round quiet people’s houses.﻿ ﻿…”

The Angel was surprised to find that some inexplicable quality in her voice excited the most disagreeable sensations in him and a strong desire to withdraw. But, resisting this, he stood listening politely (as the custom is in the Angelic Land, so long as anyone is speaking). The entire eruption was beyond his comprehension. He could not perceive any reason for the sudden projection of this vituperative head, out of infinity, so to speak. And questions without a break for an answer were outside his experience altogether.

===Chinese===

“可您要是不想让我听见，干吗喊那么大声？我原以为。……”

“*你原以为*！软蛋——*你*就是这么个东西！你这个傻大个瞪眼猴，什么都不懂，就知道张着那张大河马嘴，逮着什么吞什么？回头再跑上头去学舌！你这个肥脸蛋、搬是非的大傻蛋！我可没脸皮去人家安生人家门口探头探脑。……”

天使惊讶地发觉，她嗓音里某种说不清的品质，激起他一身极不痛快的感觉，还有一股强烈的抽身走开的欲望。可他忍住了，站在那里礼貌地听着（这是天使之国的规矩，只要有人开口，就得听完）。这场全面的喷发远非他所能理解。他实在看不出，这颗破口大骂的脑袋，怎么忽然间——这么说吧——从无穷里探了出来。而连珠炮一样不给回答余地的问话，更是他闻所未闻。

===Original===

Mrs. Gustick proceeded with her characteristic fluency, assured him he was no gentleman, enquired if he called himself one, remarked that every tramp did as much nowadays, compared him to a Stuck Pig, marvelled at his impudence, asked him if he wasn’t ashamed of himself standing there, enquired if he was rooted to the ground, was curious to be told what he meant by it, wanted to know whether he robbed a scarecrow for his clothes, suggested that an abnormal vanity prompted his behaviour, enquired if his mother knew he was out, and finally remarking, “I got somethin’ll move you, my gentleman,” disappeared with a ferocious slamming of the door.

The interval struck the Angel as singularly peaceful. His whirling mind had time to analyse his sensations. He ceased bowing and smiling, and stood merely astonished.

“This is a curious painful feeling,” said the Angel. “Almost worse than Hungry, and quite different. When one is hungry one wants to eat. I suppose she was a woman. Here one wants to get away. I suppose I might just as well go.”

===Chinese===

古斯蒂克太太拿出她特有的口才，滔滔不绝：一口咬定他算不得绅士，问他自己配不配称绅士，又说如今连个流浪汉也做得像他这样，把他比作一头钉住的猪，惊叹他脸皮之厚，问他站在那里害不害臊，问他是生了根长在地里么，很想知道他这究竟是什么意思，想知道他那身衣裳是不是抢了稻草人，点破说是一种反常的虚荣心指使他这般行事，又问他妈知不知道他跑出来了，末了撂下一句“老娘这儿有样东西能叫你挪窝，我的好先生”，砰的一声狠狠摔上门，不见了。

这段间歇在天使看来分外安宁。他晕乎乎的头脑有了工夫来理一理自己的感觉。他不再鞠躬微笑，只是站在那里发怔。

“这是一种古怪的痛苦感觉，”天使说。“比‘饿’还难受些，又全然不同。人饿了想吃东西。我猜她是个女人。在这儿，人只想逃开。我看我不如就走吧。”

===Original===

He turned slowly and went down the road meditating. He heard the cottage door reopen, and turning his head, saw through intervening scarlet runners Mrs. Gustick with a steaming saucepan full of boiling cabbage water in her hand.

“ ’Tis well you went, Mister Stolen Breeches,” came the voice of Mrs. Gustick floating down through the vermilion blossoms. “Don’t you come peeping and prying round this yer cottage again or I’ll learn ye manners, I will!”

The Angel stood in a state of considerable perplexity. He had no desire to come within earshot of the cottage again﻿—ever. He did not understand the precise import of the black pot, but his general impression was entirely disagreeable. There was no explaining it.

===Chinese===

他缓缓转身，一路思索着往坡下走去。他听见小屋的门重新开了，一回头，透过夹道的红花菜豆，望见古斯蒂克太太手里端着一只冒气的长柄锅，满满一锅滚开的煮菜水。

“你走了倒好，偷人马裤的先生，”古斯蒂克太太的声音穿过那片朱红的花串飘下来。“再敢来这小屋跟前探头探脑，老娘就教你学个规矩，一定教！”

天使站在那里，惶惑得很。他再也不想走近那小屋的听力所及之处——永远不想。那口黑锅的确切用意他不明白，可他总的印象是彻头彻尾的不痛快。没法解释。

===Original===

“I *mean* it!” said Mrs. Gustick, crescendo. “Drat it!﻿—I *mean* it.”

The Angel turned and went on, a dazzled look in his eyes.

“She was very grotesque!” said the Angel. “*Very.* Much more than the little man in black. And she means it.﻿—But what she means I don’t know!﻿ ﻿…” He became silent. “I suppose they all mean something,” he said, presently, still perplexed.

===Chinese===

“我*是*当真的！”古斯蒂克太太说，嗓门渐高。“该死的！——我*就是*当真。”

天使转过身，继续赶路，眼里带着一层恍惚。

“她可真够古怪的！”天使说。“*太*古怪了。比那个黑衣裳的小个子厉害多了。而且她是当真的。——可她当真的是什么，我不知道！……”他不作声了。“我猜他们个个都当真指着什么，”过了一会儿他说，仍旧满心困惑。

## XLVI The Angel in Trouble (Continued) / 四十六 天使落难（续）

===Original===

Delia sat with her window open, hoping to hear the Angel play. But that night there was to be no playing. The sky was overcast, yet not so thickly but that the moon was visible. High up a broken cloud-lace drove across the sky, and now the moon was a hazy patch of light, and now it was darkened, and now rode clear and bright and sharply outlined against the blue gulf of night. And presently she heard the door into the garden opening, and a figure came out under the drifting pallor of the moonlight.

It was the Angel. But he wore once more the saffron robe in the place of his formless overcoat. In the uncertain light this garment had only a colourless shimmer, and his wings behind him seemed a leaden grey. He began taking short runs, flapping his wings and leaping, going to and fro amidst the drifting patches of light and the shadows of the trees. Delia watched him in amazement. He gave a despondent cry, leaping higher. His shrivelled wings flashed and fell. A thicker patch in the cloud-film made everything obscure. He seemed to spring five or six feet from the ground and fall clumsily. She saw him in the dimness crouching on the ground and then she heard him sobbing.

“He’s hurt!” said Delia, pressing her lips together hard and staring. “I ought to help him.”

===Chinese===

迪莉娅敞着窗坐在屋里，盼着听见天使演奏。可那晚注定没有琴声。天阴着，却不算厚，月亮还看得见。高天上，一绺破碎的云纱掠过天空，月亮时而化作一团朦胧的光，时而被遮暗，时而又清清爽爽地亮着，轮廓分明地悬在夜的蓝色深渊上。不一会儿，她听见通花园的门开了，一个人影走出来，立在月光漂移的苍白里。

是天使。可他身上又换回了那件藏红花色袍子，不再穿那件没款没形的旧大衣。在明暗不定的光里，这件衣裳只剩一层无色的微光，他背后的翅膀看上去是铅灰色。他开始一段段助跑，扑着翅膀跳起来，在漂移的光斑和树影之间来来回回。迪莉娅惊讶地望着他。他发出一声沮丧的喊叫，跳得更高了。萎缩的翅膀闪了一下，坠了下去。云翳厚了一层，一切暗了下来。他仿佛从地上腾起五六英尺，又笨拙地跌落。她看见昏暗里他蜷伏在地上，跟着听见他在啜泣。

“他受伤了！”迪莉娅说，紧紧抿住嘴唇，直望着。“我该去帮他。”

===Original===

She hesitated, then stood up and flitted swiftly towards the door, went slipping quietly downstairs and out into the moonlight. The Angel still lay upon the lawn, and sobbed for utter wretchedness.

“Oh! what is the matter?” said Delia, stooping over him and touching his head timidly.

The Angel ceased sobbing, sat up abruptly, and stared at her. He saw her face, moonlit, and soft with pity. “What is the matter?” she whispered. “Are you hurt?”

===Chinese===

她迟疑了一下，随即站起身，快步飘到门边，悄悄溜下楼，走进月光里。天使还躺在草地上，伤心到了极处，抽抽噎噎地哭。

“哦！怎么了？”迪莉娅俯下身，怯怯地碰了碰他的头。

天使止住哭，猛地坐起来，瞪着她。他看见她的脸，月光照着，因怜惜而柔和。“怎么了？”她低声问。“你受伤了吗？”

===Original===

The Angel stared about him, and his eyes came to rest on her face. “Delia!” he whispered.

“Are you hurt?” said Delia.

“My wings,” said the Angel. “I cannot use my wings.”

===Chinese===

天使朝四下里望，目光落在她脸上。“迪莉娅！”他低唤。

“你受伤了吗？”迪莉娅说。

“我的翅膀，”天使说，“我用不了我的翅膀了。”

===Original===

Delia did not understand, but she realised that it was something very dreadful. “It is dark, it is cold,” whispered the Angel; “I cannot use my wings.”

It hurt her unaccountably to see the tears on his face. She did not know what to do.

“Pity me, Delia,” said the Angel, suddenly extending his arms towards her; “pity me.”

===Chinese===

迪莉娅不懂，可她明白这是非常可怕的事。“天黑，天冷，”天使低声说；“我用不了我的翅膀了。”

看见他脸上的眼泪，她没来由地心疼。她不知道该怎么办。

“怜惜我吧，迪莉娅，”天使忽然朝她张开双臂；“怜惜我吧。”

===Original===

Impulsively she knelt down and took his face between her hands. “I do not know,” she said; “but I am sorry. I am sorry for you, with all my heart.”

The Angel said not a word. He was looking at her little face in the bright moonlight, with an expression of uncomprehending wonder in his eyes. “This strange world!” he said.

She suddenly withdrew her hands. A cloud drove over the moon. “What can I do to help you?” she whispered. “I would do anything to help you.”

===Chinese===

她一时冲动，跪下来，双手捧住他的脸。“我不知道，”她说，“可是我难过。我为你难过，全心全意地难过。”

天使一句话也没说。他望着明月清辉里她那张小小的脸，眼里带着不解的惊奇。“这个奇怪的世界！”他说。

她忽然缩回手。一片云掠过月亮。“我能做什么帮你呢？”她低声问。“只要能帮你，我什么都肯做。”

===Original===

He still held her at arm’s length, perplexity replacing misery in his face. “This strange world!” he repeated.

Both whispered, she kneeling, he sitting, in the fluctuating moonlight and darkness of the lawn.

---

===Chinese===

他仍旧隔着一臂之远扶着她，脸上的凄苦换成了困惑。“这个奇怪的世界！”他又说了一遍。

两人都低声说着，她跪着，他坐着，在草地上明灭变幻的月光和暗影里。

---

===Original===

“Delia!” said Mrs. Hinijer, suddenly projecting from her window; “Delia, is that you?”

They both looked up at her in consternation.

“Come in at once, Delia,” said Mrs. Hinijer. “If that Mr. Angel was a gentleman (which he isn’t), he’d feel ashamed of hisself. And you an orphan too!”

===Chinese===

“迪莉娅！”希尼杰太太忽然从窗口探出身来；“迪莉娅，是你吗？”

两人都惊慌地抬头望她。

“马上进来，迪莉娅，”希尼杰太太说。“那位天使先生要真是个绅士（他偏不是），他也该害臊。你爹妈还都不在了呢！”

## XLV The Angel in Trouble / 四十五 天使落难

===Original===

“The fact is,” said the Vicar, “this is no world for Angels.”

The blinds had not been drawn, and the twilight outer world under an overcast sky seemed unspeakably grey and cold. The Angel sat at table in dejected silence. His inevitable departure had been proclaimed. Since his presence hurt people and made the Vicar wretched he acquiesced in the justice of the decision, but what would happen to him after his plunge he could not imagine. Something very disagreeable certainly.

“There is the violin,” said the Vicar. “Only after our experience﻿—”

===Chinese===

“实情是，”教区牧师说，“这世界不是天使待的。”

百叶帘没有放下，阴天底下的暮色世界，隔着窗望出去，灰得、冷得说不出。天使坐在桌旁，郁郁地沉默着。他非走不可这事已经宣布了。既然他的存在伤人，又让教区牧师这样难过，他默认这决定是公道的；可一纵身跳下去之后会落到什么田地，他想象不出。必定是非常不痛快的什么事。

“还有那把小提琴，”教区牧师说，“只是照咱们那段经历看来——”

===Original===

“I must get you clothes﻿—a general outfit.﻿—Dear me! you don’t understand railway travelling! And coinage! Taking lodgings! Eating-houses!﻿—I must come up at least and see you settled. Get work for you. But an Angel in London! Working for his living! That grey cold wilderness of people! What *will* become of you?﻿—If I had one friend in the world I could trust to believe me!”

“I ought not to be sending you away﻿—”

“Do not trouble overmuch for me, my friend,” said the Angel. “At least this life of yours ends. And there are things in it. There is something in this life of yours﻿—Your care for me! I thought there was nothing beautiful at all in life﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我还得给你置办衣裳——一整套行头。——天哪！你连坐火车都不懂！还有钱币！租房！下馆子！——至少我得陪你上伦敦，看你安顿下来。给你找活儿。可是一位天使在伦敦！自食其力！那样一个灰冷的荒漠人海！你*会*落个什么下场？——世上要是有一个人肯信我，哪怕一个朋友也好！”

“我本不该送你走的——”

“不要为我过分操心，我的朋友，”天使说。“至少你们这个生命有个完。而且这里面有东西。你们这个生命里有种东西——你对我的这番挂心！我原先以为生命里根本没有美的东西——”

===Original===

“And I have betrayed you!” said the Vicar, with a sudden wave of remorse. “Why did I not face them all﻿—say, ‘This is the best of life’? What do these everyday things matter?”

He stopped suddenly. “What *do* they matter?” he said.

“I have only come into your life to trouble it,” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“而我背叛了你！”教区牧师说，一阵悔恨猛地涌上来。“我为什么不顶着他们所有人——说，‘这才是生命中最宝贵的’？这些日常琐碎，算得了什么？”

他忽然停住。“它们究竟*算*得了什么？”他说。

“我闯进你的生活，只是来搅扰它的，”天使说。

===Original===

“Don’t say that,” said the Vicar. “You have come into my life to awaken me. I have been dreaming﻿—dreaming. Dreaming this was necessary and that. Dreaming that this narrow prison was the world. And the dream still hangs about me and troubles me. That is all. Even your departure﻿—. Am I not dreaming that you must go?”

When he was in bed that night the mystical aspect of the case came still more forcibly before the Vicar. He lay awake and had the most horrible visions of his sweet and delicate visitor drifting through this unsympathetic world and happening upon the cruellest misadventures. His guest *was* an Angel assuredly. He tried to go over the whole story of the past eight days again. He thought of the hot afternoon, the shot fired out of sheer surprise, the fluttering iridescent wings, the beautiful saffron-robed figure upon the ground. How wonderful that had seemed to him! Then his mind turned to the things he had heard of the other world, to the dreams the violin had conjured up, to the vague, fluctuating, wonderful cities of the Angelic Land. He tried to recall the forms of the buildings, the shapes of the fruits upon the trees, the aspect of the winged shapes that traversed its ways. They grew from a memory into a present reality, grew every moment just a little more vivid and his troubles a little less immediate; and so, softly and quietly, the Vicar slipped out of his troubles and perplexities into the Land of Dreams.

===Chinese===

“别这么说，”教区牧师说。“你来到我的生活里，是为了唤醒我。我一直在做梦——做梦啊。梦见这个不可少、那个不可少。梦见这座窄狱就是全世界。如今那梦还缠着我、搅扰我。仅此而已。就连你的离开——。我如今梦见你非走不可，难道不也是一场梦？”

那天夜里上了床，这桩事的神秘一面愈发有力地摆到教区牧师眼前。他醒着躺在那里，眼前闪过一幅幅最可怕的幻象：他那位娇美脆弱的客人，漂过这个冷硬无情的世界，撞上最残忍的厄运。他的客人*确确实实*是位天使。他把过去八天的事从头到尾又捋了一遍。他想起那个炎热的午后，那枪纯属惊讶之下的误发，那对扑打的虹彩翅膀，地上那个藏红花色袍子的美丽身影。在他看来，那一切曾经多么奇妙！随后他的心思转向他听来的那个世界的种种，转向小提琴变出来的那些梦，转向天使之国那些隐约、变幻、奇妙的城市。他竭力回想那些楼宇的形状，树上果子的模样，来往其间的带翼身影的姿容。它们从一段记忆长成眼下的实在，时时刻刻都更鲜活一分，他的烦恼也就更远一分；就这样，轻轻地、悄悄地，教区牧师从他的烦恼与惶惑里滑了出去，滑进了梦之国。

## XXXV The Angel’s Debut (Continued) / 三十五 天使的初次亮相（续）

===Original===

Mrs. Jehoram and the Angel (apart)﻿—Mr. Wilmerdings playing.

“I have so longed for a quiet word with you,” said Mrs. Jehoram in a low tone. “To tell you how delightful I found your playing.”

“I am glad it pleased you,” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

杰霍拉姆太太与天使（一旁）——威尔默丁斯先生正在弹琴。

“我一直盼着能同您静静说句话，”杰霍拉姆太太低声说。“告诉您，您的演奏让我觉得多么美好。”

“它让您高兴，我也高兴，”天使说。

===Original===

“Pleased is scarcely the word,” said Mrs. Jehoram. “I was moved﻿—profoundly. These others did not understand.﻿ ﻿… I was glad you did not play with him.”

The Angel looked at the mechanism called Wilmerdings, and felt glad too. (The Angelic conception of duets is a kind of conversation upon violins.) But he said nothing.

“I worship music,” said Mrs. Jehoram. “I know nothing about it technically, but there is something in it﻿—a longing, a wish.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“‘高兴’二字远远不够，”杰霍拉姆太太说。“我被打动了——深深地。这些别人不懂。……您没有同他合奏，我很高兴。”

天使望着那台叫作威尔默丁斯的机关，也觉得高兴。（天使国里对二重奏的构想，是两把提琴之间的一种谈话。）可他什么也没说。

“我崇拜音乐，”杰霍拉姆太太说。“技巧上的事我一窍不通，可音乐里有一种东西——一种渴望，一种祈愿。……”

===Original===

The Angel stared at her face. She met his eyes.

“You understand,” she said. “I see you understand.” He was certainly a very nice boy, sentimentally precocious perhaps, and with deliciously liquid eyes.

There was an interval of Chopin (Op. 40) played with immense precision.

===Chinese===

天使凝视她的脸。她迎着他的目光。

“您懂，”她说。“我看得出来您懂。”他实在是个很讨人喜欢的男孩子，情感上或许早熟，一双眼睛水汪汪的，甜得很。

中间是一段肖邦（作品 40 号），弹得分毫不差。

===Original===

Mrs. Jehoram had a sweet face still, in shadow, with the light falling round her golden hair, and a curious theory flashed across the Angel’s mind. The perceptible powder only supported his view of something infinitely bright and lovable caught, tarnished, coarsened, coated over.

“Do you,” said the Angel in a low tone. “Are you﻿ ﻿… separated from﻿ ﻿… *your* world?”

“As you are?” whispered Mrs. Jehoram.

===Chinese===

杰霍拉姆太太的脸依然甜美，罩在阴影里，灯光洒在她金色的头发四周；一个奇妙的理论闪过天使的心头。那看得见的一层敷粉，恰恰坐实了他的看法：有一样无限明亮、无限可爱的东西，被逮住了，蒙了尘，变粗了，裹了一层壳。

“您可，”天使低声说，“您是不是……同……*您的*世界分离了？”

“像您一样？”杰霍拉姆太太低声问。

===Original===

“This is so﻿—cold,” said the Angel. “So harsh!” He meant the whole world.

“I feel it too,” said Mrs. Jehoram, referring to Siddermorton Home.

“There are those who cannot live without sympathy,” she said after a sympathetic pause. “And times when one feels alone in the world. Fighting a battle against it all. Laughing, flirting, hiding the pain of it.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“这里是这么——冷，”天使说。“这么硬！”他指的是整个世界。

“我也有同感，”杰霍拉姆太太说，指的却是锡德莫顿宅。

“有的人，没有同情就活不下去，”怜惜地顿了一顿之后，她说。“也有些时候，人觉得天地间只剩自己一个。独自同这一切苦斗。笑啊，调情啊，把这份痛藏起来。……”

===Original===

“And hoping,” said the Angel with a wonderful glance.﻿—“Yes.”

Mrs. Jehoram (who was an epicure of flirtations) felt the Angel was more than redeeming the promise of his appearance. (Indisputably he worshipped her.) “Do *you* look for sympathy?” she said. “Or have you found it?”

“I think,” said the Angel, very softly, leaning forward, “I think I have found it.”

===Chinese===

“并且盼着，”天使说着，投来美妙的一瞥。——“是的。”

杰霍拉姆太太（她是调情这门学问的行家）觉得，天使大大挽回了他这副相貌许下的诺言。（无可争辩，他崇拜她。）“*您*可是在寻觅同情？”她说，“还是已经寻着了？”

“我想，”天使说着，身子前倾，声音极轻，“我想我已经寻着了。”

===Original===

Interval of Chopin Op. 40. The very eldest Miss Papaver and Mrs. Pirbright whispering. Lady Hammergallow (glasses up) looking down the saloon with an unfriendly expression at the Angel. Mrs. Jehoram and the Angel exchanging deep and significant glances.

“Her name,” said the Angel (Mrs. Jehoram made a movement) “is Delia. She is.﻿ ﻿…”

“Delia!” said Mrs. Jehoram sharply, slowly realising a terrible misunderstanding. “A fanciful name.﻿ ﻿… Why!﻿ ﻿… No! Not that little housemaid at the Vicarage﻿—?﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

又是肖邦作品 40 号的间隙。帕帕弗家最年长的小姐同皮尔布赖特太太窃窃私语。哈默加洛夫人（举着眼镜）顺着厅堂朝天使望下来，一脸不友善。杰霍拉姆太太同天使交换着深沉而意味深长的目光。

“她的名字，”天使说（杰霍拉姆太太动了一下）“是迪莉娅。她是。……”

“迪莉娅！”杰霍拉姆太太尖声说，慢慢意识到一场可怕的误会。“一个想入非非的名字。……哎呀！……不会吧！不是牧师公馆那个小女仆吧——？……”

===Original===

The Polonaise terminated with a flourish. The Angel was quite surprised at the change in Mrs. Jehoram’s expression.

“*I never* did!” said Mrs. Jehoram recovering. “To make me your confidant in an intrigue with a servant. Really Mr. Angel it’s possible to be too original.﻿ ﻿…”

Then suddenly their colloquy was interrupted.

===Chinese===

波兰舞曲以一记华丽的收束告终。杰霍拉姆太太神色的骤变，让天使好生诧异。

“*我从没有过*！”杰霍拉姆太太定下神来说。“竟拿我当您同一个小女仆的风流账的心腹。说真的，天使先生，太标新立异也未免过头了。……”

就在这时，他们的密谈被打断了。

## XXXIV The Angel’s Debut / 三十四 天使的初次亮相

===Original===

When Lady Hammergallow made up her mind, things happened as she resolved. And though the Vicar made a spasmodic protest, she carried out her purpose and got audience, Angel, and violin together, at Siddermorton House before the week was out. “A genius the Vicar has discovered,” she said; so with eminent foresight putting any possibility of blame for a failure on the Vicar’s shoulders. “The dear Vicar tells me,” she would say, and proceed to marvellous anecdotes of the Angel’s cleverness with his instrument. But she was quite in love with her idea﻿—she had always had a secret desire to play the patroness to obscure talent. Hitherto it had not turned out to be talent when it came to the test.

“It would be such a good thing for him,” she said. “His hair is long already, and with that high colour he would be beautiful, simply beautiful on a platform. The Vicar’s clothes fitting him so badly makes him look quite like a fashionable pianist already. And the scandal of his birth﻿—not told, of course, but whispered﻿—would be﻿—quite an inducement﻿—when he gets to London, that is.”

The Vicar had the most horrible sensations as the day approached. He spent hours trying to explain the situation to the Angel, other hours trying to imagine what people would think, still worse hours trying to anticipate the Angel’s behaviour. Hitherto the Angel had always played for his own satisfaction. The Vicar would startle him every now and then by rushing upon him with some new point of etiquette that had just occurred to him. As for instance: “It’s very important where you put your hat, you know. Don’t put it on a chair, whatever you do. Hold it until you get your tea, you know, and then﻿—let me see﻿—then put it down somewhere, you know.” The journey to Siddermorton House was accomplished without misadventure, but at the moment of introduction the Vicar had a spasm of horrible misgivings. He had forgotten to explain introductions. The Angel’s naive amusement was evident, but nothing very terrible happened.

===Chinese===

哈默加洛夫人一旦拿定主意，事情就会照她决断的那样发生。尽管教区牧师抽风似的抗议了一通，她还是贯彻了她的意图：不出一个星期，听众、天使和小提琴便在锡德莫顿宅齐聚一堂。“教区牧师发掘的一位天才，”她说；凭着这份高明的先见，把失败的一切可能责任都预先安放到教区牧师肩上。“亲爱的牧师告诉我，”她总这么说，接着便讲起天使摆弄那件乐器的种种奇闻。她着实爱上了自己这个主意——她一直暗暗渴望给无名的才俊当一回恩主。只可惜迄今为止，那些才俊一经考验，全都不是才俊。

“这对他是件大好事，”她说。“他头发已经够长了，配上那副红扑扑的脸色，往台上一站准漂亮，简直漂亮。教区牧师的衣裳他又穿不合身，倒让他看着已经很像一位时髦的钢琴家了。还有他出身那段丑闻——当然不明说，只悄悄耳语——将来会是一大卖点——等他到了伦敦，那就是。”

日子越近，教区牧师心里的滋味越发可怕。他花了几个钟头向天使解说形势，又花几个钟头揣想众人会作何感想，再花更可怕的几个钟头预想天使的举止。迄今为止，天使拉琴从来只图自己快意。教区牧师时不时想起一条新的礼数，便猛扑到他跟前。比如：“帽子搁在哪儿，这很要紧，你晓得。千万千万别搁在椅子上。拿到茶之前一直捧着，你晓得，然后——让我想想——然后找个地方放下，你晓得。”去锡德莫顿宅的一路倒没出岔子，可到引见的当口，教区牧师一阵可怕的疑惧直透心口。他忘了解说引见的规矩。天使那种天真的好奇明明白白挂在脸上，好在没出什么了不得的事。

===Original===

“Rummy looking greaser,” said Mr. Rathbone Slater, who devoted considerable attention to costume. “Wants grooming. No manners. Grinned when he saw me shaking hands. Did it *chic* enough, I thought.”

One trivial misadventure occurred. When Lady Hammergallow welcomed the Angel she looked at him through her glasses. The apparent size of her eyes startled him. His surprise and his quick attempt to peer over the brims was only too evident. But the Vicar had warned him of the ear trumpet.

The Angel’s incapacity to sit on anything but a music stool appeared to excite some interest among the ladies, but led to no remarks. They regarded it perhaps as the affectation of a budding professional. He was remiss with the teacups and scattered the crumbs of his cake abroad. (You must remember he was quite an amateur at eating.) He crossed his legs. He fumbled over the hat business after vainly trying to catch the Vicar’s eye. The eldest Miss Papaver tried to talk to him about continental watering places and cigarettes, and formed a low opinion of his intelligence.

===Chinese===

“模样邪门的外国佬，”拉思本-斯莱特先生说，此公在衣着上颇下功夫。“欠拾掇。没规矩。见我握手居然咧嘴笑。不过那一下做得*够潇洒*，我承认。”

也出了一桩小小的意外。哈默加洛夫人迎接天使时，用眼镜端详他。她眼睛显出来的个头吓了他一跳。他那惊讶、急忙从镜框上边偷觑的样子，实在再明显不过。好在教区牧师预先给他打过助听号筒的招呼。

天使除了琴凳之外坐不了别的椅子，这似乎引起了太太们的一些兴趣，倒也没人议论。她们大概把这看作一位初出茅庐的行家的做派。他接茶杯时手忙脚乱，蛋糕屑撒了一身。（你们得记着，在吃这件事上他完全是个生手。）他跷起了二郎腿。摆弄帽子那一套，他先是无望地想捕捉教区牧师的目光，末了还是出了洋相。帕帕弗家最年长的小姐想同他聊聊欧洲大陆的疗养地和香烟，末了对他智力大摇其头。

===Original===

The Angel was surprised by the production of an easel and several books of music, and a little unnerved at first by the sight of Lady Hammergallow sitting with her head on one side, watching him with those magnified eyes through her gilt glasses.

Mrs. Jehoram came up to him before he began to play and asked him the name of the charming piece he was playing the other afternoon. The Angel said it had no name, and Mrs. Jehoram thought music ought never to have any names and wanted to know who it was by, and when the Angel told her he played it out of his head, she said he must be “quite a genius” and looked open (and indisputably fascinating) admiration at him. The Curate from Iping Hanger (who was professionally a Celt and who played the piano and talked colour and music with an air of racial superiority) watched him jealously.

The Vicar, who was presently captured and set down next to Lady Hammergallow, kept an anxious eye ever Angelward while she told him particulars of the incomes made by violinists﻿—particulars which, for the most part, she invented as she went along. She had been a little ruffled by the incident of the glasses, but had decided that it came within the limits of permissible originality.

===Chinese===

抬出谱架和几册乐谱来，天使吃了一惊；再看见哈默加洛夫人歪着头坐下，用金丝眼镜后那双放大了的眼睛盯着他，起先还真有点发慌。

开演之前，杰霍拉姆太太凑上前，问他前几天下午拉的那支迷人曲子叫什么名字。天使说它没有名字；杰霍拉姆太太认为音乐本来就不该有名字，又问是谁作的；天使告诉她说，是他从自己脑袋里拉出来的。她说他准是“十足的天才”，向他投去坦率的（无疑迷人的）倾慕。伊平亨格来的那位副牧师（他在职业上是位凯尔特人，弹得一手钢琴，谈论色彩与音乐时总带着一种种族优越的神气）嫉妒地望着他。

教区牧师不久被截住，安置在哈默加洛夫人身边；她对他大讲小提琴手们的进账——那些细账，多半是她随讲随编的。眼镜那一幕让她有点光火，可她已经裁定，那尚在“可容许的别致”的限度之内。

===Original===

So figure to yourself the Green Saloon at Siddermorton Park; an Angel thinly disguised in clerical vestments and with a violin in his hands, standing by the grand piano, and a respectable gathering of quiet nice people, nicely dressed, grouped about the room. Anticipatory gabble﻿—one hears scattered fragments of conversation.

“He is *incog.*”; said the very eldest Miss Papaver to Mrs. Pirbright. “Isn’t it quaint and delicious. Jessica Jehoram says she saw him at Vienna, but she can’t remember the name. The Vicar knows all about him, but he is so close﻿—”

“How hot and uncomfortable the dear Vicar is looking,” said Mrs. Pirbright. “I’ve noticed it before when he sits next to Lady Hammergallow. She simply will *not* respect his cloth. She goes on﻿—”

===Chinese===

那么请你想一想锡德莫顿园的绿厅：一位天使，薄薄罩着一层教士的黑衣，手执小提琴，立在大钢琴旁；满屋子一群体面的、安安静静的好人，衣冠楚楚，环厅而坐。开演前的嗡嗡闲谈——零零碎碎飘来几句。

“他是*隐姓埋名*（incog.）来的，”帕帕弗家最年长的小姐对皮尔布赖特太太说。“多别致，多有趣。杰西卡·杰霍拉姆说她在维也纳见过他，就是想不起名字。教区牧师全知道底细，可他嘴真严——”

“亲爱的牧师瞧着多热、多不自在呀，”皮尔布赖特太太说。“从前他挨着哈默加洛夫人坐，我就注意到了。她就是*不肯*尊重他的圣职。她一个劲儿——”

===Original===

“His tie is all askew,” said the very eldest Miss Papaver, “and his hair! It really hardly looks as though he had brushed it all day.”

“Seems a foreign sort of chap. Affected. All very well in a drawing-room,” said George Harringay, sitting apart with the younger Miss Pirbright. “But for my part give me a masculine man and a feminine woman. What do you think?”

“Oh!﻿—I think so too,” said the younger Miss Pirbright.

===Chinese===

“他的领带全歪了，”帕帕弗家最年长的小姐说，“还有他的头发！简直像一整天没梳过。”

“像是外国种的家伙。矫揉造作。在客厅里玩玩倒也罢了，”乔治·哈林盖同皮尔布赖特家的小姐坐在一旁说。“但要依我说，还是给我一个爷们儿气的男人、一个娘们儿气的女人。你说呢？”

“哦！——我也这么想，”皮尔布赖特家的小姐说。

===Original===

“Guineas and guineas,” said Lady Hammergallow. “I’ve heard that some of them keep quite stylish establishments. You would scarcely credit it﻿—”

“I love music, Mr. Angel, I adore it. It stirs something in me. I can scarcely describe it,” said Mrs. Jehoram. “Who is it says that delicious antithesis: Life without music is brutality; music without life is﻿—Dear me! perhaps you remember? Music without life﻿—it’s Ruskin I think?”

“I’m sorry that I do not,” said the Angel. “I have read very few books.”

===Chinese===

“几尼接着几尼，”哈默加洛夫人说。“听说他们有些人宅子都过得相当阔气。你简直不敢信——”

“我爱音乐，天使先生，我崇拜它。它在我心里搅动起什么东西。我简直形容不出，”杰霍拉姆太太说。“不知是谁说的那句妙对：没有音乐的人生是野蛮；没有人生的音乐是——哎呀！您兴许记得？没有人生的音乐——我想是罗斯金说的？”

“很抱歉，我不记得，”天使说。“我读过的书极少。”

===Original===

“How charming of you!” said Mrs. Jehoram. “I wish I didn’t. I sympathise with you profoundly. I would do the same, only we poor women﻿—I suppose it’s originality we lack﻿—And down here one is driven to the most desperate proceedings﻿—”

“He’s certainly very *pretty*. But the ultimate test of a man is his strength,” said George Harringay. “What do you think?”

“Oh!﻿—I think so too,” said the younger Miss Pirbright.

===Chinese===

“您这人真可爱！”杰霍拉姆太太说。“我倒希望自己也没读过。我深深地同情您。我也想这样，只可惜我们这些可怜的女人——大概是缺了那份独创——再加上在这种地方，人就被逼到铤而走险的地步了——”

“他确实非常*漂亮*。可检验一个男人的终极标准是力气，”乔治·哈林盖说。“你说呢？”

“哦！——我也这么想，”皮尔布赖特家的小姐说。

===Original===

“It’s the effeminate man who makes the masculine woman. When the glory of a man is his hair, what’s a woman to do? And when men go running about with beautiful hectic dabs﻿—”

“Oh George! You are so dreadfully satirical today,” said the younger Miss Pirbright. “I’m *sure* it isn’t paint.”

“I’m really not his guardian, my dear Lady Hammergallow. Of course it’s very kind indeed of you to take such an interest﻿—”

===Chinese===

“就是这些娘们儿气的男人，才造出了爷们儿气的女人。男人的荣耀都剩下头发了，女人可怎么办？男人们还满世界跑，脸上搽着漂亮的红膏子——”

“哦，乔治！你今天刻薄得吓人，”皮尔布赖特家的小姐说。“我*敢说*那不是胭脂。”

“我实在不是他的监护人，亲爱的哈默加洛夫人。您这样关照他，自然好意可感——”

===Original===

“Are you really going to improvise?” said Mrs. Jehoram in a state of cooing delight.

“*Sssh!*” said the curate from Iping Hanger.

Then the Angel began to play, looking straight before him as he did so, thinking of the wonderful things of the Angelic Land, and yet insensibly letting the sadness he was beginning to feel, steal over the fantasia he was playing. When he forgot his company the music was strange and sweet; when the sense of his surroundings floated into his mind the music grew capricious and grotesque. But so great was the hold of the Angelic music upon the Vicar that his anxieties fell from him at once, so soon as the Angel began to play. Mrs. Jehoram sat and looked rapt and sympathetic as hard as she could (though the music was puzzling at times) and tried to catch the Angel’s eye. He really had a wonderfully mobile face, and the tenderest shades of expression! And Mrs. Jehoram was a judge. George Harringay looked bored, until the younger Miss Pirbright, who adored him, put out her mousy little shoe to touch his manly boot, and then he turned his face to catch the feminine delicacy of her coquettish eye, and was comforted. The very eldest Miss Papaver and Mrs. Pirbright sat quite still and looked churchy for nearly four minutes.

===Chinese===

“您真要即兴演奏？”杰霍拉姆太太说，欢喜得咕咕直响。

“*嘘！*”伊平亨格的副牧师说。

于是天使开始演奏，眼睛直望着前方，心里想着天使之国种种奇妙的事物，却又不知不觉地，让他初初感到的那份忧伤，悄悄渗进他指下的幻想曲里。忘了身畔的人群时，那音乐便奇异而甜美；周围世界的感知浮上心头时，音乐便变得任性和古怪。可天使的音乐对教区牧师的威力实在太大，弓子一响，他的忧虑顿时尽消。杰霍拉姆太太坐在那里，竭尽全力做出陶醉而动情的模样（虽然那音乐有时也叫她犯迷糊），还想捕捉天使的目光。他的脸真是灵动得出奇，表情的层次又最细腻不过！而杰霍拉姆太太是位行家。乔治·哈林盖显出无聊的样子，直到崇拜他的皮尔布赖特家小姐悄悄伸出她那双老鼠似的小鞋，去碰他男子气的靴子；他这才回过脸来，接住她秋波里那份女性的娇俏，得了安慰。帕帕弗家最年长的小姐同皮尔布赖特太太端坐不动，做出教堂里的肃穆模样，足有将近四分钟。

===Original===

Then said the eldest Miss Papaver in a whisper, “I always enjoy violin music so much.” And Mrs. Pirbright answered, “We get so little nice music down here.” And Miss Papaver said, “He plays very nicely.” And Mrs. Pirbright, “Such a delicate touch!” And Miss Papaver, “Does Willie keep up his lessons?” and so to a whispered conversation.

The Curate from Iping Hanger sat (he felt) in full view of the company. He had one hand curled round his ear, and his eyes hard and staring fixedly at the pedestal of the Hammergallow Sèvres vase. He supplied, by the movements of his mouth, a kind of critical guide to any of the company who were disposed to avail themselves of it. It was a generous way he had. His aspect was severely judicial, tempered by starts of evident disapproval and guarded appreciation. The Vicar leaned back in his chair and stared at the Angel’s face, and was presently rapt away in a wonderful dream. Lady Hammergallow, with quick jerky movements of the head and a low but insistent rustling, surveyed and tried to judge of the effect of the Angelic playing. Mr. Rathbone-Slater stared very solemnly into his hat and looked very miserable, and Mrs. Rathbone-Slater made mental memoranda of Mrs. Jehoram’s sleeves. And the air about them all was heavy with exquisite music﻿—for all that had ears to hear.

“Scarcely affected enough,” whispered Lady Hammergallow hoarsely, suddenly poking the Vicar in the ribs. The Vicar came out of Dreamland suddenly. “Eigh?” shouted the Vicar, startled, coming up with a jump. “Sssh!” said the Curate from Iping Hanger, and everyone looked shocked at the brutal insensibility of Hilyer. “So unusual of the Vicar,” said the very eldest Miss Papaver, “to do things like that!” The Angel went on playing.

===Chinese===

随后帕帕弗家最年长的小姐悄声说：“小提琴曲我向来这样喜欢。”皮尔布赖特太太答：“咱们这地方好听的音乐太少了。”帕帕弗小姐说：“他拉得很好。”皮尔布赖特太太说：“触弦多细！”帕帕弗小姐说：“威利的功课还坚持吗？”就这样窃窃私语开了。

伊平亨格来的副牧师坐在（他自认为）满座瞩目的位置。他一只手蜷在耳边，眼睛硬硬地死盯着哈默加洛家塞夫勒瓷瓶的座子。他用嘴部的动作，给座中凡有雅兴的人提供一份批评指南。这是他的一大美德。他的神情严厉如法官，间或迸出几分明明可见的不以为然，又夹着有所保留的赏识。教区牧师仰在椅中，凝望着天使的脸，不久便被卷进一场奇妙的梦里去了。哈默加洛夫人脑袋一冲一冲，发出低而执拗的窸窣声，环视四周，掂量天使演奏的效果。拉思本-斯莱特先生庄重地盯着自己的帽子里面，一副愁苦相；拉思本-斯莱特太太则在心里记下杰霍拉姆太太的袖子样式。他们所有人四周的空气里，弥漫着精美的音乐——凡有耳朵可听的，都听见了。

“还不够做作，”哈默加洛夫人沙哑地低语，忽然捅了捅教区牧师的肋骨。教区牧师猛地从梦土惊醒。“嗯？”教区牧师吓了一跳，跳着喊了出来。“嘘！”伊平亨格的副牧师说；人人都对希利尔这番粗野的无动于衷表示震惊。“教区牧师向来不这样，”帕帕弗家最年长的小姐说，“竟会干出这种事！”天使只管拉他的琴。

===Original===

The Curate from Iping Hanger began making mesmeric movements with his index finger, and as the thing proceeded Mr. Rathbone-Slater got amazingly limp. He solemnly turned his hat round and altered his view. The Vicar lapsed from an uneasy discomfort into dreamland again. Lady Hammergallow rustled a great deal, and presently found a way of making her chair creak. And at last the thing came to an end. Lady Hammergallow exclaimed “De﻿—licious!” though she had never heard a note, and began clapping her hands. At that everyone clapped except Mr. Rathbone-Slater, who rapped his hat brim instead. The Curate from Iping Hanger clapped with a judicial air.

“So I said (*clap, clap, clap*), if you cannot cook the food my way (*clap, clap, clap*) you must *go*,” said Mrs. Pirbright, clapping vigorously. “(This music is a delightful treat.)”

“(It is. I always *revel* in music,)” said the very eldest Miss Papaver. “And did she improve after that?”

===Chinese===

伊平亨格的副牧师开始用食指做催眠的动作；一曲行进中，拉思本-斯莱特先生软得不成样子。他郑重其事地把帽子转了半圈，改变了看法。教区牧师从一阵不安的不适里，重新沉入梦土。哈默加洛夫人窸窸窣窣闹了好一阵，后来又找到了让椅子吱嘎作响的法子。曲子终于到了头。哈默加洛夫人喊了一声“妙——极了！”——其实她一个音符也没听——跟着鼓起掌来。于是人人鼓掌，只有拉思本-斯莱特先生改敲帽檐。伊平亨格的副牧师带着法官的气派鼓了掌。

“所以我就说了（*啪，啪，啪*），你要是不能照我的法子做饭（*啪，啪，啪*），你就*走人*，”皮尔布赖特太太说，鼓得起劲。“（这音乐真是一大享受。）”

“（可不是。我在音乐里向来*如鱼得水*。）后来她改了吗？”帕帕弗家最年长的小姐说。

===Original===

“Not a bit of it,” said Mrs. Pirbright.

The Vicar woke up again and stared round the saloon. Did other people see these visions, or were they confined to him alone? Surely they must all see﻿ ﻿… and have a wonderful command of their feelings. It was incredible that such music should not affect them. “He’s a trifle *gauche*,” said Lady Hammergallow, jumping upon the Vicar’s attention. “He neither bows nor smiles. He must cultivate oddities like that. Every successful executant is more or less *gauche*.”

“Did you really make that up yourself?” said Mrs. Jehoram, sparkling her eyes at him, “as you went along. Really, it is *wonderful*! Nothing less than wonderful.”

===Chinese===

“一点儿也没改，”皮尔布赖特太太说。

教区牧师又醒过来，环视大厅。这些幻象别人也看得见么，还是只他独享？他们想必一定看得见……而且对自己感情的控制竟然如此了得。这样的音乐居然打动不了他们，简直叫人不能相信。“他有点*笨拙*，”哈默加洛夫人说，猛然跃上教区牧师的注意力。“既不鞠躬也不微笑。这种怪癖他得好好培养。凡是成功的演奏家，多少都有点*笨拙*。”

“那真是您自己即兴编的？”杰霍拉姆太太眼睛亮亮地望着他说，“边拉边编出来的。真的，*了不起*！只能是了不起。”

===Original===

“A little amateurish,” said the Curate from Iping Hanger to Mr. Rathbone-Slater. “A great gift, undoubtedly, but a certain lack of sustained training. There were one or two little things﻿ ﻿… I would like to talk to him.”

“His trousers look like concertinas,” said Mr. Rathbone-Slater. “He ought to be told *that*. It’s scarcely decent.”

“Can you do imitations, Mr. Angel?” said Lady Hammergallow.

===Chinese===

“有点业余，”伊平亨格的副牧师对拉思本-斯莱特先生说。“无疑很有天分，可是缺少一点持之以恒的训练。有一两处小地方……我倒想同他谈谈。”

“他那裤子活像两架六角手风琴，”拉思本-斯莱特先生说。“*这一点*总该有人告诉他。简直有伤体面。”

“您会模仿吗，天使先生？”哈默加洛夫人说。

===Original===

“Oh *do*, do some imitations!” said Mrs. Jehoram. “I adore imitations.”

“It was a fantastic thing,” said the Curate of Iping Hanger to the Vicar of Siddermorton, waving his long indisputably musical hands as he spoke; “a little involved, to my mind. I have heard it before somewhere﻿—I forget where. He has genius undoubtedly, but occasionally he is﻿—loose. There is a certain deadly precision wanting. There are years of discipline yet.”

“I *don’t* admire these complicated pieces of music,” said George Harringay. “I have simple tastes, I’m afraid. There seems to me no *tune* in it. There’s nothing I like so much as simple music. Tune, simplicity is the need of the age, in my opinion. We are so over subtle. Everything is far-fetched. Home grown thoughts and ‘Home, Sweet Home’ for me. What do you think?”

===Chinese===

“哦*快*，快学几段！”杰霍拉姆太太说。“我最崇拜模仿了。”

“那是一件奇幻之作，”伊平亨格的副牧师对锡德莫顿的教区牧师说，说话时挥动着他那双无疑富于乐感的修长之手；“依我看，有点缠夹。我从前在哪儿听过——忘了在哪儿。他无疑有天才，只是偶尔——松散。缺少一种致命的精确。还得下几年苦功。”

“这种繁复的曲子我*不*欣赏，”乔治·哈林盖说。“恐怕我的口味简单。我听这里头就没有*调子*。我最喜欢的莫过于简单的音乐。调子、简单，依我看正是时代的需要。我们都太拐弯抹角了。样样都牵强。我要的是本乡本土的思想，还有《家，甜蜜的家》。你说呢？”

===Original===

“Oh! I think so﻿—*quite*,” said the younger Miss Pirbright.

“Well, Amy, chattering to George as usual?” said Mrs. Pirbright, across the room.

“As usual, Ma!” said the younger Miss Pirbright, glancing round with a bright smile at Miss Papaver, and turning again so as not to lose the next utterance from George.

===Chinese===

“哦！我也这么想——*十分*同意，”皮尔布赖特家的小姐说。

“怎么，艾米，照旧跟乔治唠个没完？”皮尔布赖特太太隔着屋子喊。

“照旧，妈！”皮尔布赖特家的小姐说，朝帕帕弗小姐嫣然一笑，随即又转回去，惟恐漏掉乔治的下一句高论。

===Original===

“I wonder if you and Mr. Angel could manage a duet?” said Lady Hammergallow to the Curate from Iping Hanger, who was looking preternaturally gloomy.

“I’m sure I should be delighted,” said the Curate from Iping Hanger, brightening up.

“Duets!” said the Angel; “the two of us. Then he can play. I understood﻿—the Vicar told me﻿—”

===Chinese===

“您同天使先生能不能来个二重奏？”哈默加洛夫人对伊平亨格来的副牧师说，后者正阴沉得超乎自然。

“我肯定非常乐意，”伊平亨格的副牧师说，登时开朗起来。

“二重奏！”天使说；“我们两个。那么他也会拉琴。我还以为——教区牧师告诉我的是——”

===Original===

“Mr. Wilmerdings is an accomplished pianist,” interrupted the Vicar.

“But the imitations?” said Mrs. Jehoram, who detested Wilmerdings.

“Imitations!” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“威尔默丁斯先生是位造诣精湛的钢琴家，”教区牧师插话说。

“可模仿呢？”杰霍拉姆太太说，她最烦威尔默丁斯。

“模仿！”天使说。

===Original===

“A pig squeaking, a cock crowing, you know,” said Mr. Rathbone-Slater, and added lower, “Best fun you can get out of a fiddle﻿—*my* opinion.”

“I really don’t understand,” said the Angel. “A pig crowing!”

“You don’t like imitations,” said Mrs. Jehoram. “Nor do I﻿—really. I accept the snub. I think they degrade.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“学猪叫，学鸡打鸣，你晓得，”拉思本-斯莱特先生说，又压低嗓子补了一句，“一把提琴能榨出的最大乐子——*我*的见解。”

“我真不明白，”天使说。“猪打鸣！”

“您不喜欢模仿，”杰霍拉姆太太说，“我也不喜欢——真的。这记冷遇我领了。我觉得那玩意儿有失身份。……”

===Original===

“Perhaps afterwards Mr. Angel will relent,” said Lady Hammergallow, when Mrs. Pirbright had explained the matter to her. She could scarcely credit her ear-trumpet. When she asked for imitations she was accustomed to get imitations.

Mr. Wilmerdings had seated himself at the piano, and had turned to a familiar pile of music in the recess. “What do you think of that Barcarole thing of Spohr’s?” he said over his shoulder. “I suppose you know it?” The Angel looked bewildered.

He opened the folio before the Angel.

===Chinese===

“也许过一会儿天使先生就肯赏脸了，”等皮尔布赖特太太把事情对着她的号筒讲明白之后，哈默加洛夫人说。她几乎信不过自己的助听号筒。她要模仿，向来是有人给模仿的。

威尔默丁斯先生在钢琴前坐定，转身朝壁龛里那摞熟悉的乐谱翻去。“斯波尔那首船歌您觉得如何？”他回头说。“您想必熟悉？”天使一脸茫然。

他把对开本乐谱在天使面前翻开。

===Original===

“What an odd kind of book!” said the Angel. “What do all those crazy dots mean?” (At that the Vicar’s blood ran cold.)

“What dots?” said the Curate.

“There!” said the Angel with incriminating finger.

===Chinese===

“多古怪的一本书！”天使说。“这些疯疯癫癫的点子都是什么意思？”（一听这话，教区牧师的血都凉了。）

“什么点子？”副牧师说。

“那儿！”天使说，指头点得罪证确凿。

===Original===

“Oh *come*!” said the Curate.

There was one of those swift, short silences that mean so much in a social gathering.

Then the eldest Miss Papaver turned upon the Vicar. “Does not Mr. Angel play from ordinary.﻿ ﻿… Music﻿—from the ordinary notation?”

===Chinese===

“哦，*得了吧*！”副牧师说。

席间掠过一阵急促而短促的沉默——社交场合里，这种沉默最意味深长。

随后帕帕弗家最年长的小姐转向教区牧师。“天使先生难道不照寻常的……乐谱演奏——不识寻常的记谱法？”

===Original===

“I have never heard,” said the Vicar, getting red now after the first shock of horror. “I have really never seen.﻿ ﻿…”

The Angel felt the situation was strained, though what was straining it he could not understand. He became aware of a doubtful, an unfriendly look upon the faces that regarded him. “Impossible!” he heard Mrs. Pirbright say; “after that *beautiful* music.” The eldest Miss Papaver went to Lady Hammergallow at once, and began to explain into her ear-trumpet that Mr. Angel did not wish to play with Mr. Wilmerdings, and alleged an ignorance of written music.

“He cannot play from notes!” said Lady Hammergallow in a voice of measured horror. “Non﻿—sense!”

===Chinese===

“我从未听说过，”教区牧师说，骇然的第一冲击过后，脸红了上来。“我实在从未见过。……”

天使只觉得局面绷紧了，虽然绷紧的缘由他弄不懂。他察觉到一张张望着他的脸上带着疑忌的、不友好的神色。“不可能！”他听见皮尔布赖特太太说；“出了那样*美妙*的音乐之后。”帕帕弗家最年长的小姐立刻走到哈默加洛夫人跟前，对着号筒解释起来：天使先生不愿同威尔默丁斯先生合奏，还自称不识谱。

“他不识谱！”哈默加洛夫人说，声音里的惊骇一板一眼。“胡——说！”

===Original===

“Notes!” said the Angel perplexed. “Are these notes?”

“It’s carrying the joke too far﻿—simply because he doesn’t want to play with Wilmerdings,” said Mr. Rathbone-Slater to George Harringay.

There was an expectant pause. The Angel perceived he had to be ashamed of himself. He was ashamed of himself.

===Chinese===

“谱！”天使困惑地说。“这些就是谱？”

“这玩笑开过头了——分明就是不想同威尔默丁斯同台，”拉思本-斯莱特先生对乔治·哈林盖说。

一阵引颈以待的停顿。天使察觉到自己该害臊了。他就害了臊。

===Original===

“Then,” said Lady Hammergallow, throwing her head back and speaking with deliberate indignation, as she rustled forward, “if you cannot play with Mr. Wilmerdings I am afraid I cannot ask you to play again.” She made it sound like an ultimatum. Her glasses in her hand quivered violently with indignation. The Angel was now human enough to appreciate the fact that he was crushed.

“What is it?” said little Lucy Rustchuck in the further bay.

“He’s refused to play with old Wilmerdings,” said Tommy Rathbone-Slater. “What a lark! The old girl’s purple. She thinks heaps of that ass, Wilmerdings.”

===Chinese===

“那么，”哈默加洛夫人头往后一仰，窸窸窣窣地逼上前来，字字含怒地说，“既然您不能同威尔默丁斯先生合奏，恐怕我也不能再请您演奏了。”她把这话说得像一纸最后通牒。她手中的眼镜气得直哆嗦。天使如今已经人性够足，完全领会到自己被碾得粉碎。

“出什么事了？”远处凸窗里小露西·拉斯丘克（Lucy Rustchuck）问。

“他不肯同老威尔默丁斯合奏，”汤米·拉思本-斯莱特说。“多有意思！老太太都气紫了。她把威尔默丁斯那头蠢驴宝贝得不行。”

===Original===

“Perhaps, Mr. Wilmerdings, you will favour us with that delicious Polonaise of Chopin’s,” said Lady Hammergallow. Everybody else was hushed. The indignation of Lady Hammergallow inspired much the same silence as a coming earthquake or an eclipse. Mr. Wilmerdings perceived he would be doing a real social service to begin at once, and (be it entered to his credit now that his account draws near its settlement) he did.

“If a man pretend to practise an art,” said George Harringay, “he ought at least to have the conscience to study the elements of it. What do you.﻿ ﻿…”

“Oh! I think so too,” said the younger Miss Pirbright.

The Vicar felt that the heavens had fallen. He sat crumpled up in his chair, a shattered man. Lady Hammergallow sat down next to him without appearing to see him. She was breathing heavily, but her face was terribly calm. Everyone sat down. Was the Angel grossly ignorant or only grossly impertinent? The Angel was vaguely aware of some frightful offence, aware that in some mysterious way he had ceased to be the centre of the gathering. He saw reproachful despair in the Vicar’s eye. He drifted slowly towards the window in the recess and sat down on the little octagonal Moorish stool by the side of Mrs. Jehoram. And under the circumstances he appreciated at more than its proper value Mrs. Jehoram’s kindly smile. He put down the violin in the window seat.

===Chinese===

“威尔默丁斯先生，也许您肯赏光，给我们来那支肖邦美妙的波兰舞曲，”哈默加洛夫人说。其余的人鸦雀无声。哈默加洛夫人的义愤所激发的肃静，同将至的地震或日食相差无几。威尔默丁斯先生领会到立刻开弹乃是一桩真正的社会贡献，而且（趁他的账目临近清算，这一点应记入他的功劳簿）他确实这么做了。

“一个人要是假装操弄一门艺术，”乔治·哈林盖说，“至少也该有点良心，先把这门艺术的基本功学一学。你说呢。……”

“哦！我也这么想，”皮尔布赖特家的小姐说。

教区牧师觉得天塌下来了。他瘫坐椅中，成了一个垮掉的人。哈默加洛夫人在他身边坐下，仿佛没看见他。她呼吸沉重，脸上却是可怕的平静。人人落座。天使究竟是蠢得离谱，还是无礼得离谱？天使隐约察觉自己犯下了某种可怕的过失，察觉自己已以某种神秘的方式不再是这场聚会的中心。他看见教区牧师眼里责备的绝望。他缓缓飘到壁龛的窗前，在杰霍拉姆太太身旁那只八边形摩尔小凳上坐下。处此境地，杰霍拉姆太太那记和善的微笑，在他心里的分量被高估了许多。他把小提琴搁在了窗座上。

## II The Coming of the Strange Bird / 二 怪鸟来临

===Original===

Sandy Bright was coming down the road from Spinner’s carrying a side of bacon he had taken in exchange for a clock. He saw nothing of the light but he heard and saw the Strange Bird. He suddenly heard a flapping and a voice like a woman wailing, and being a nervous man and all alone, he was alarmed forthwith, and turning (all a-tremble) saw something large and black against the dim darkness of the cedars up the hill. It seemed to be coming right down upon him, and incontinently he dropped his bacon and set off running, only to fall headlong.

He tried in vain﻿—such was his state of mind﻿—to remember the beginning of the Lord’s Prayer. The strange bird flapped over him, something larger than himself, with a vast spread of wings, and, as he thought, black. He screamed and gave himself up for lost. Then it went past him, sailing down the hill, and, soaring over the vicarage, vanished into the hazy valley towards Sidderford.

===Chinese===

桑迪·布莱特正从斯平纳铺子那条路上走下来，扛着一片腌肉——那是他拿一座钟换来的。那道光他一点没瞧见，可怪鸟他又听见又看见了。他忽然听见一阵扑翅声，还有一个像女人哀哭的声音；他本就是个胆小的人，又孤身一个，当即吓坏了，浑身发抖地一回头，看见山坡上雪松朦胧的黑影前，一个又大又黑的东西。那东西似乎直朝他扑下来，他登时撂下腌肉，撒腿就跑，偏偏一个跟头栽倒在地。

他心慌到那步田地，连主祷文的开头都想不起来了。怪鸟从他头顶扑过，那东西比他还大，双翅张开老大一片，而且——照他想——是黑的。他尖叫一声，只当自己死定了。随后它掠过他，飘下山坡，翱过牧师公馆，消失在朝锡德福德方向的雾蒙蒙的山谷里。

===Original===

And Sandy Bright lay upon his stomach there, for ever so long, staring into the darkness after the strange bird. At last he got upon his knees and began to thank Heaven for his merciful deliverance, with his eyes downhill. He went on down into the village, talking aloud and confessing his sins as he went, lest the strange bird should come back. All who heard him thought him drunk. But from that night he was a changed man, and had done with drunkenness and defrauding the revenue by selling silver ornaments without a licence. And the side of bacon lay upon the hillside until the tallyman from Portburdock found it in the morning.

The next who saw the Strange Bird was a solicitor’s clerk at Iping Hanger, who was climbing the hill before breakfast, to see the sunrise. Save for a few dissolving wisps of cloud the sky had been blown clear in the night. At first he thought it was an eagle he saw. It was near the zenith, and incredibly remote, a mere bright speck above the pink cirri, and it seemed as if it fluttered and beat itself against the sky, as an imprisoned swallow might do against a window pane. Then down it came into the shadow of the earth, sweeping in a great curve towards Portburdock and round over the Hanger, and so vanishing behind the woods of Siddermorton Park. It seemed larger than a man. Just before it was hidden, the light of the rising sun smote over the edge of the downs and touched its wings, and they flashed with the brightness of flames and the colour of precious stones, and so passed, leaving the witness agape.

===Chinese===

桑迪·布莱特就那样趴在地上，趴了好半天，朝怪鸟消失的黑暗里张望。末了他爬起来跪着，眼睛望着坡下，开始感谢上天的慈悲搭救。他一路下山进村，一路大声说话、一路认罪，惟恐怪鸟去而复返。听见的人个个当他喝醉了。可打那天夜里起，他换了一个人，戒了酒，也不再无照贩卖银器、坑骗国税了。那片腌肉在山坡上一直躺到第二天早上，才叫波特伯多克来的赊销员捡着。

第二个看见怪鸟的，是伊平亨格一个律师的书记员，他早饭前爬山去看日出。夜风把天吹得干干净净，只剩几绺正在消散的云丝。起初他以为自己望见的是一只鹰。它就在天顶附近，远得难以置信，只是粉色卷云上方的一个亮点，仿佛在扑腾，仿佛在撞击天空，像一只囚禁的燕子撞击窗玻璃。随后它降下来，进入大地的阴影，划出一道大弧线扫向波特伯多克，绕过亨格上空，消失在锡德莫顿园的树林后面。它看上去比人还大。就在它隐没之前，初升的太阳越过丘陵的边缘照到它的翅膀，那双翅顿时亮得像火焰、彩得像宝石，一闪而过，留下那位目击者张着嘴发怔。

===Original===

A ploughman going to his work, along under the stone wall of Siddermorton Park, saw the Strange Bird flash over him for a moment and vanish among the hazy interstices of the beech trees. But he saw little of the colour of the wings, witnessing only that its legs, which were long, seemed pink and bare like naked flesh, and its body mottled white. It smote like an arrow through the air and was gone.

These were the first three eyewitnesses of the Strange Bird.

===Chinese===

一个犁田的汉子沿锡德莫顿园的石墙下去上工，看见怪鸟一闪而过，消失在山毛榉朦胧的枝隙间。可翅膀的颜色他没大看清，只作证说，那鸟的一双腿很长，粉红而光秃，像赤裸的皮肉，身子是斑驳的白色。它像一支箭破空而过，没了。

这就是怪鸟最早的三个目击者。

===Original===

Now in these days one does not cower before the devil and one’s own sinfulness, or see strange iridiscent wings in the light of dawn, and say nothing of it afterwards. The young solicitor’s clerk told his mother and sisters at breakfast, and, afterwards, on his way to the office at Portburdock, spoke of it to the blacksmith of Hammerpond, and spent the morning with his fellow clerks marvelling instead of copying deeds. And Sandy Bright went to talk the matter over with Mr. Jekyll, the “Primitive” minister, and the ploughman told old Hugh and afterwards the vicar of Siddermorton.

“They are not an imaginative race about here,” said the Vicar of Siddermorton, “I wonder how much of that was true. Barring that he thinks the wings were brown it sounds uncommonly like a Flamingo.”

===Chinese===

如今这个年头，人不再在魔鬼和自己的罪面前发抖，也不再在黎明的光里望见五彩的翅膀、事后绝口不提。年轻的法律书记员在早饭桌上讲给了母亲和妹妹们听，随后在去波特伯多克事务所的路上，又讲给哈默庞德（Hammerpond）的铁匠听，一个上午都同书记员同僚们一起惊叹，正事文书倒一个字没抄。桑迪·布莱特去找“原始”教会的杰基尔先生（Mr. Jekyll）商量此事；犁田汉讲给了老休听，随后又讲给锡德莫顿的教区牧师。

“这一带的人种缺少想象力，”锡德莫顿的教区牧师说，“不知这话里有几分真。除了他把翅膀认成棕色这一点，听上去倒极像一只红鹳。”

## XVI The Curate (Continued) / 十六 副牧师（续）

===Original===

The Angel had finished his tea and was standing looking pensively out of the window. He thought the old church down the valley lit by the light of the setting sun was very beautiful, but he could not understand the serried ranks of tombstones that lay up the hillside beyond. He turned as Mendham and the Vicar came in.

Now Mendham could bully his Vicar cheerfully enough, just as he could bully his congregation; but he was not the sort of man to bully a stranger. He looked at the Angel, and the “strange woman” theory was disposed of. The Angel’s beauty was too clearly the beauty of the youth.

“Mr. Hilyer tells me,” Mendham began, in an almost apologetic tone, “that you﻿—ah﻿—it’s so curious﻿—claim to be an Angel.”

===Chinese===

天使用完了茶，站在窗前若有所思地朝外望。他觉得谷底那座被落日映亮的老教堂很美，可他弄不懂教堂后山坡上密密匝匝排开的那一片墓碑。门德姆同教区牧师进来时，他转过身来。

门德姆欺压他的教区牧师可以欺压得兴高采烈，正如他欺压他的会众；可他不是那种欺压生人的人。他看了看天使，“陌生女子”之说便告吹了。天使的美，分明是少年的美。

“希利尔先生告诉我，”门德姆开口说，语气近乎抱歉，“您——啊——实在太稀奇了——自称是一位天使。”

===Original===

“*Are* an Angel,” said the Vicar.

The Angel bowed.

“Naturally,” said Mendham, “we are curious.”

===Chinese===

“*是*一位天使，”教区牧师说。

天使鞠了一躬。

“自然，”门德姆说，“我们很好奇。”

===Original===

“Very,” said the Angel. “The blackness and the shape.”

“I beg your pardon?” said Mendham.

“The blackness and the flaps,” repeated the Angel; “and no wings.”

===Chinese===

“非常好奇，”天使说。“这黑颜色，还有这形状。”

“对不起，您说什么？”门德姆说。

“黑颜色，还有这些垂片，”天使又说了一遍；“还有，没有翅膀。”

===Original===

“Precisely,” said Mendham, who was altogether at a loss. “We are, of course, curious to know something of how you came into the village in such a peculiar costume.”

The Angel looked at the Vicar. The Vicar touched his chin.

“You see,” began the Vicar.

===Chinese===

“正是，”门德姆说，全然摸不着头脑。“我们当然好奇，想知道您穿着那么一身古怪的行头，是怎么进的村子。”

天使看了看教区牧师。教区牧师摸了摸下巴。

“你要知道，”教区牧师开口说。

===Original===

“Let *him* explain,” said Mendham; “I beg.”

“I wanted to suggest,” began the Vicar.

“And I don’t want you to suggest.”

===Chinese===

“让*他*自己解释，”门德姆说；“求您了。”

“我想提个建议，”教区牧师开口说。

“我不想听您提建议。”

===Original===

“*Bother!*” said the Vicar.

The Angel looked from one to the other. “Such rugose expressions flit across your faces!” he said.

“You see, Mr.﻿—Mr.﻿—I don’t know your name,” said Mendham, with a certain diminution of suavity. “The case stands thus: My wife﻿—four ladies, I might say﻿—are playing lawn tennis, when you suddenly rush out on them, sir; you rush out on them from among the rhododendra in a very defective costume. You and Mr. Hilyer.”

===Chinese===

“*烦人！*”教区牧师说。

天使望望这个，又望望那个。“多少皱巴巴的表情，从你们脸上一掠而过！”他说。

“您瞧，先生——先生——我还不知道您的名字，”门德姆说，客气劲儿打了折扣。“事情是这样：我的太太——不妨说四位女士——正在打草地网球，您突然朝她们冲过去，先生；从杜鹃花丛里冲出去，穿着一身大有欠缺的衣裳。您，还有希利尔先生。”

===Original===

“But I﻿—” said the Vicar.

“I know. It was this gentleman’s costume was defective. Naturally﻿—it is my place in fact﻿—to demand an explanation.” His voice was growing in volume. “And I *must* demand an explanation.”

The Angel smiled faintly at his note of anger and his sudden attitude of determination﻿—arms tightly folded.

===Chinese===

“可是我——”教区牧师说。

“我知道。衣裳大有欠缺的是这位先生。我自然要——按规矩这本就是我的职分——要求一个解释。”他的嗓门渐渐放大。“而且我*必须*得到一个解释。”

天使听到他话里的怒气，看到他突然摆出的决绝架势——双臂紧紧抱在胸前——只淡淡一笑。

===Original===

“I am rather new to the world,” the Angel began.

“Nineteen at least,” said Mendham. “Old enough to know better. That’s a poor excuse.”

“May I ask one question first?” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“我到这世界来，还不久，”天使开口说。

“至少十九岁了，”门德姆说。“到了懂事的年纪了。这个借口不怎么样。”

“我可以先问一个问题吗？”天使说。

===Original===

“Well?”

“Do you think I am a Man﻿—like yourself? As the chequered man did.”

“If you are not a man﻿—”

===Chinese===

“问吧？”

“您以为我是一个人——像您一样？像那个格子花纹的人那样。”

“倘若您不是人——”

===Original===

“One other question. Have you *never* heard of an Angel?”

“I warn you not to try that story upon me,” said Mendham, now back at his familiar crescendo.

The Vicar interrupted: “But Mendham﻿—he has wings!”

===Chinese===

“还有一个问题。您*从来没有*听说过天使吗？”

“我警告您，别拿这套故事到我这儿来试，”门德姆说，又回到了他惯常的渐强音上。

教区牧师插进来：“可是门德姆——他有翅膀！”

===Original===

“*Please* let me talk to him,” said Mendham.

“You are so quaint,” said the Angel; “you interrupt everything I have to say.”

“But what *have* you to say?” said Mendham.

===Chinese===

“*请*让我来同他谈，”门德姆说。

“你们真古怪，”天使说；“我每说一句话你们都要打断。”

“可你究竟*有什么*要说的？”门德姆说。

===Original===

“That I really *am* an Angel.﻿ ﻿…”

“Pshaw!”

“There you go!”

===Chinese===

“说我确实*是*一位天使。……”

“呸！”

“又来了！”

===Original===

“But tell me, honestly, how you came to be in the shrubbery of Siddermorton Vicarage﻿—in the state in which you were. And in the Vicar’s company. Cannot you abandon this ridiculous story of yours?﻿ ﻿…”

The Angel shrugged his wings. “What is the matter with this man?” he said to the Vicar.

“My dear Mendham,” said the Vicar, “a few words from me.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“可是老实告诉我，你怎么会待在锡德莫顿牧师公馆的灌木林里——还处在那样一种状态。还同教区牧师在一起。你就不能丢开这套荒唐故事吗？……”

天使耸了耸他的翅膀。“这个人怎么了？”他对教区牧师说。

“亲爱的门德姆，”教区牧师说，“容我说几句。……”

===Original===

“Surely my question is straightforward enough!”

“But you won’t tell me the answer you want, and it’s no good my telling you any other.”

“*Pshaw!*” said the Curate again. And then turning suddenly on the Vicar, “Where does he come from?”

===Chinese===

“我的问题总够直截了当了吧！”

“可你们不肯说我要的答案，我说什么别的也白搭。”

“*呸！*”副牧师又说了一遍。随即猛地转向教区牧师：“他是从哪儿来的？”

===Original===

The Vicar was in a dreadful state of doubt by this time.

“He *says* he is an Angel!” said the Vicar. “Why don’t you listen to him?”

“No angel would alarm four ladies.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

这时候教区牧师已经疑虑重重，狼狈不堪。

“他*说他*是位天使！”教区牧师说。“你为什么不听他说？”

“任何天使也不会惊吓四位女士。……”

===Original===

“Is *that* what it is all about?” said the Angel.

“Enough cause too, I should think!” said the Curate.

“But I really did not know,” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“*就*为这个吗？”天使说。

“我看这理由也尽够了！”副牧师说。

“可我实在不知道啊，”天使说。

===Original===

“This is altogether too much!”

“I am sincerely sorry I alarmed these ladies.”

“You ought to be. But I see I shall get nothing out of you two.” Mendham went towards the door. “I am convinced there is something discreditable at the bottom of this business. Or why not tell a simple straightforward story? I will confess you puzzle me. Why, in this enlightened age, you should tell this fantastic, this far-fetched story of an Angel, altogether beats me. What good *can* it do?﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“这实在太过分了！”

“我诚心诚意地抱歉，惊吓了那些女士。”

“你该抱歉。不过我看，从你们俩嘴里是问不出什么了。”门德姆朝门口走去。“我认定这桩事底下准有什么见不得人的东西。不然为什么不老老实实讲个明明白白的故事？我承认你们把我难住了。偏偏在这个开明的时代，你们偏要讲这么个荒唐、这么个牵强的天使故事，我实在想不通。这能有什么*好处*？……”

===Original===

“But stop and look at his wings!” said the Vicar. “I can assure you he has wings!”

Mendham had his fingers on the door-handle. “I have seen quite enough,” he said. “It may be this is simply a foolish attempt at a hoax, Hilyer.”

“But Mendham!” said the Vicar.

===Chinese===

“可你留步看看他的翅膀呀！”教区牧师说。“我向你担保，他有翅膀！”

门德姆的手指已经搭上门把。“我看够多了，”他说。“这多半只是一场愚蠢的骗局，希利尔。”

“可是门德姆！”教区牧师说。

===Original===

The Curate halted in the doorway and looked at the Vicar over his shoulder. The accumulating judgment of months found vent. “I cannot understand, Hilyer, why you are in the Church. For the life of me I cannot. The air is full of Social Movements, of Economic change, the Woman Movement, Rational Dress, The Reunion of Christendom, Socialism, Individualism﻿—all the great and moving questions of the hour! Surely, we who follow the Great Reformer.﻿ ﻿… And here you are stuffing birds, and startling ladies with your callous disregard.﻿ ﻿…”

“But Mendham,” began the Vicar.

The Curate would not hear him. “You shame the Apostles with your levity.﻿ ﻿… But this is only a preliminary enquiry,” he said, with a threatening note in his sonorous voice, and so vanished abruptly (with a violent slam) from the room.

===Chinese===

副牧师在门口停住，回头望着教区牧师。积攒了几个月的判词，这一下找到了出口。“我不明白，希利尔，你为什么待在教会里。我死活想不明白。如今空气里满满的都是社会运动、经济变革、妇女运动、理性服装、基督徒合一、社会主义、个人主义——全是当下伟大而激动人心的问题！我们这些追随那位伟大改革者的人，理当……而你呢，忙着制作鸟类标本、拿你那份无心无肺惊吓女士。……”

“可是门德姆，”教区牧师开口说。

副牧师不肯听他说完。“你这份轻浮，叫使徒们蒙羞。……不过这只是一场预审，”他说，洪亮的嗓音里带着威胁的调子，说罢猛然（砰的一声大响）出了屋子。

## XV The Curate / 十五 副牧师

===Original===

The Vicar opened the door half mechanically to let out Crump, and saw Mendham, his curate, coming up the pathway by the hedge of purple vetch and meadowsweet. At that his hand went up to his chin and his eyes grew perplexed. Suppose he *was* deceived. The Doctor passed the Curate with a sweep of his hand from his hat brim. Crump was an extraordinarily clever fellow, the Vicar thought, and knew far more of anyone’s brain than one did oneself. The Vicar felt that so acutely. It made the coming explanation difficult. Suppose he were to go back into the drawing-room, and find just a tramp asleep on the hearthrug.

Mendham was a cadaverous man with a magnificent beard. He looked, indeed, as though he had run to beard as a mustard plant does to seed. But when he spoke you found he had a voice as well.

“My wife came home in a dreadful state,” he brayed out at long range.

===Chinese===

教区牧师半机械地开门送克伦普出去，一眼看见门德姆——他的副牧师——正沿紫色野豌豆和绣线菊的树篱间的小径走来。他的手立刻摸上下巴，眼里露出困惑。万一自己*果真*受了骗呢。医生同副牧师擦肩而过，手从帽檐上潇洒地一挥。克伦普是个出奇聪明的人，教区牧师想，对任何人的脑子，他都比你本人知道得多。这一点教区牧师感同身受。这就使即将到来的解释难办了。万一他回到客厅，发现炉前地毯上睡着的不过是个流浪汉呢。

门德姆是个面如死灰的人，一部威风凛凛的大胡子。瞧那样子，他这一身仿佛全跑到胡子上去了，就像芥菜一咕嘟全跑了籽。可等他一开口，你才发现他原来还有嗓门。

“我太太回家时吓得不轻，”他人还没进门就嗥了开来。

===Original===

“Come in,” said the Vicar; “come in. Most remarkable occurrence. Please come in. Come into the study. I’m really dreadfully sorry. But when I explain.﻿ ﻿…”

“And apologise, I hope,” brayed the Curate.

“And apologise. No, not that way. This way. The study.”

===Chinese===

“进来，”教区牧师说，“快进来。出了顶了不得的事。请进。到书房来。我实在抱歉极了。且等我解释。……”

“还要道歉吧，我希望，”副牧师嗥道。

“还要道歉。不，不走那边。这边。书房。”

===Original===

“Now what *was* that woman?” said the Curate, turning on the Vicar as the latter closed the study door.

“What woman?”

“Pah!”

===Chinese===

“方才那个女人*到底*是谁？”副牧师一进书房门，等门一关就朝教区牧师转过身来。

“什么女人？”

“呸！”

===Original===

“But really!”

“The painted creature in light attire﻿—disgustingly light attire, to speak freely﻿—with whom you were promenading the garden.”

“My dear Mendham﻿—that was an Angel!”

===Chinese===

“真的！”

“那个涂脂抹粉、衣衫轻薄的东西——轻薄得叫人恶心，恕我直言——你同她在花园里挽着手散步。”

“亲爱的门德姆——那是一位天使！”

===Original===

“A *very pretty* Angel?”

“The world is getting so matter-of-fact,” said the Vicar.

“The world,” roared the Curate, “grows blacker every day. But to find a man in your position, shamelessly, openly.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“一位*很标致的*天使？”

“这世界越来越实际了，”教区牧师说。

“这个世界，”副牧师吼道，“一天黑似一天。可没想到，一个处在你这样位置上的人，居然寡廉鲜耻、明目张胆。……”

===Original===

“*Bother!*” said the Vicar aside. He rarely swore. “Look here, Mendham, you really misunderstand. I can assure you.﻿ ﻿…”

“Very well,” said the Curate. “Explain!” He stood with his lank legs apart, his arms folded, scowling at his Vicar over his big beard.

(Explanations, I repeat, I have always considered the peculiar fallacy of this scientific age.)

===Chinese===

“*烦人！*”教区牧师低声嘀咕了一句。他难得骂人。“你听着，门德姆，你实在是误会了。我可以向你担保。……”

“很好，”副牧师说。“那就解释吧！”他两条细腿岔开站着，双臂抱在胸前，从大胡子上头恶狠狠地瞪着他的教区牧师。

（解释这回事，我再说一遍，我一向认为是这个科学时代特有的谬见。）

===Original===

The Vicar looked about him helplessly. The world had all gone dull and dead. Had he been dreaming all the afternoon? Was there really an angel in the drawing-room? Or was he the sport of a complicated hallucination?

“Well?” said Mendham, at the end of a minute.

The Vicar’s hand fluttered about his chin. “It’s such a roundabout story,” he said.

===Chinese===

教区牧师无助地环顾四周。整个世界都变得灰暗死寂。难道一下午他都在做梦？客厅里真有一位天使么？还是他成了一场复杂幻觉的玩物？

“怎么样？”过了一分钟，门德姆说。

教区牧师的手在下巴边扑棱。“这事说来话长，七拐八绕，”他说。

===Original===

“No doubt it will be,” said Mendham harshly.

The Vicar restrained a movement of impatience.

“I went out to look for a strange bird this afternoon.﻿ ﻿… Do you believe in angels, Mendham, real angels?”

===Chinese===

“想必就要拐弯抹角，”门德姆刻薄地说。

教区牧师忍住了一阵不耐烦。

“今天下午我出门去找一只怪鸟。……你信天使吗，门德姆，真正的天使？”

===Original===

“I’m not here to discuss theology. I am the husband of an insulted woman.”

“But I tell you it’s not a figure of speech; this *is* an angel, a real angel with wings. He’s in the next room now. You do misunderstand me, so.﻿ ﻿…”

“Really, Hilyer﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我不是来讨论神学的。我是一个受了辱的女人的丈夫。”

“可我告诉你，这不是修辞；这*就是*一位天使，一位真正长着翅膀的天使。他这会儿就在隔壁屋里。你实在是误会我了，所以。……”

“说真的，希利尔——”

===Original===

“It is true I tell you, Mendham. I swear it is true.” The Vicar’s voice grew impassioned. “What sin I have done that I should entertain and clothe angelic visitants, I don’t know. I only know that﻿—inconvenient as it undoubtedly will be﻿—I have an angel now in the drawing-room, wearing my new suit and finishing his tea. And he’s stopping with me, indefinitely, at my invitation. No doubt it was rash of me. But I can’t turn him out, you know, because Mrs. Mendham﻿—I may be a weakling, but I am still a gentleman.”

“Really, Hilyer﻿—”

“I can assure you it is true.” There was a note of hysterical desperation in the Vicar’s voice. “I fired at him, taking him for a flamingo, and hit him in the wing.”

===Chinese===

“我说的全是真的，门德姆。我发誓是真的。”教区牧师的声音激昂起来。“我造了什么孽，竟要我接待、供我这般天上来客穿衣吃饭，我不知道。我只知道——尽管这毫无疑问会是个麻烦——我的客厅里现在就有一位天使，穿着我的新衣裳，用着他的茶。而且他要在我家住下，无限期地住下，是我请的。无疑是我鲁莽了。可我不能把他撵出去，你晓得，因为门德姆太太——我也许软弱，可我到底还是个绅士。”

“说真的，希利尔——”

“我向你担保这是真的。”教区牧师的嗓音里带上了歇斯底里的绝望。“我朝他开的枪，把他当成了红鹳，打中了他的翅膀。”

===Original===

“I thought this was a case for the Bishop. I find it is a case for the Lunacy Commissioners.”

“Come and see him, Mendham!”

“But there *are* no angels.”

===Chinese===

“我原想这案子该交给主教。现在看，该交给精神病事务委员会。”

“来看他一眼吧，门德姆！”

“可天上根本*没有*天使。”

===Original===

“We teach the people differently,” said the Vicar.

“Not as material bodies,” said the Curate.

“Anyhow, come and see him.”

===Chinese===

“我们教给众人的可不是这话，”教区牧师说。

“没说他们是血肉之躯，”副牧师说。

“不管怎么说，来看看他。”

===Original===

“I don’t want to see your hallucinations,” began the Curate.

“I can’t explain anything unless you come and see him,” said the Vicar. “A man who’s more like an angel than anything else in heaven or earth. You simply must see if you wish to understand.”

“I don’t wish to understand,” said the Curate. “I don’t wish to lend myself to any imposture. Surely, Hilyer, if this is not an imposition, you can tell me yourself.﻿ ﻿… Flamingo, indeed!”

===Chinese===

“我不想看你的幻觉，”副牧师开口说。

“你不来看他，我什么也解释不清，”教区牧师说。“一个天上地下比什么都更像天使的人。你要想明白，就非看不可。”

“我不想明白，”副牧师说。“我也不想给任何骗局当托儿。说真的，希利尔，倘若这里头没有猫腻，你自己就能给我讲清楚。……还红鹳呢！”

## LIII The Epilogue / 五十三 尾声

===Original===

And there the story of the Wonderful Visit ends. The Epilogue is in the mouth of Mrs. Mendham. There stand two little white crosses in the Siddermorton churchyard, near together, where the brambles come clambering over the stone wall. One is inscribed Thomas Angel and the other Delia Hardy, and the dates of the deaths are the same. Really there is nothing beneath them but the ashes of the Vicar’s stuffed ostrich. (You will remember the Vicar had his ornithological side.) I noticed them when Mrs. Mendham was showing me the new De la Beche monument. (Mendham has been Vicar since Hilyer died.) “The granite came from somewhere in Scotland,” said Mrs. Mendham, “and cost ever so much﻿—I forget how much﻿—but a wonderful lot! It’s quite the talk of the village.”

“Mother,” said Cissie Mendham, “you are stepping on a grave.”

===Chinese===

奇异的来访的故事，到这里就结束了。尾声出自门德姆太太之口。锡德莫顿的教堂墓地里立着两个小小的白色十字架，紧挨在一起，荆棘从石墙上爬过来掩着它们。一个上面刻着托马斯·安吉尔（Thomas Angel），另一个刻着迪莉娅·哈代（Delia Hardy），忌辰是同一天。其实底下什么也没有，只有教区牧师那只鸵鸟标本的灰。（你们总还记得，教区牧师有他的鸟类学的一面。）这两座碑是我陪门德姆太太看德拉贝什家（De la Beche）的新纪念碑时留意到的。（希利尔去世后，门德姆接任了教区牧师。）“这花岗岩是从苏格兰什么地方运来的，”门德姆太太说，“花了好大一笔钱——多少我忘了——反正多得了不得！全村都拿它当谈资呢。”

“妈，”西茜·门德姆说，“您踩到一座坟了。”

===Original===

“Dear me!” said Mrs. Mendham, “How heedless of me! And the cripple’s grave too. But really you’ve no idea how much this monument cost them.”

“These two people, by the by,” said Mrs. Mendham, “were killed when the old Vicarage was burnt. It’s rather a strange story. He was a curious person, a hunchbacked fiddler, who came from nobody knows where, and imposed upon the late Vicar to a frightful extent. He played in a pretentious way by ear, and we found out afterwards that he did not know a note of music﻿—not a note. He was exposed before quite a lot of people. Among other things, he seems to have been ‘carrying on,’ as people say, with one of the servants, a sly little drab.﻿ ﻿… But Mendham had better tell you all about it. The man was half-witted and curiously deformed. It’s strange the fancies girls have.”

===Chinese===

“哎呀！”门德姆太太说，“我多不小心！还是那瘸子的坟呢。不过这纪念碑花了他们多少钱，您真没法想象。”

“顺带说说，这两个人，”门德姆太太说，“是老牧师公馆失火那年烧死的。说来是桩离奇的故事。那男的是个古怪人物，一个驼背的提琴手，来历无人知晓，把已故的教区牧师蒙骗得好苦。他拉琴全凭耳朵，装腔作势，我们后来才发现，他连一个音符都不识——一个都不识。还是当着好多人揭穿的。此外呢，他还同一个小女仆‘有一手’，照人们的说法——一个贼眉鼠眼的小骚货。……不过这些还是让门德姆讲给你们听吧。那人脑子缺根弦，身子又畸得古怪。姑娘家的心思真是奇怪。”

===Original===

She looked sharply at Cissie, and Cissie blushed to the eyes.

“She was left in the house and he rushed into the flames in an attempt to save her. Quite romantic﻿—isn’t it? He was rather clever with the fiddle in his uneducated way.

“All the poor Vicar’s stuffed skins were burned at the same time. It was almost all he cared for. He never really got over the blow. He came to stop with us﻿—for there wasn’t another house available in the village. But he never seemed happy. He seemed all shaken. I never saw a man so changed. I tried to stir him up, but it was no good﻿—no good at all. He had the queerest delusions about angels and that kind of thing. It made him odd company at times. He would say he heard music, and stare quite stupidly at nothing for hours together. He got quite careless about his dress.﻿ ﻿… He died within a twelvemonth of the fire.”

===Chinese===

她锐利地瞟了西茜一眼，西茜一直红到了眼根。

“她当时被落在了屋里，他冲进火里想去救她。够罗曼蒂克的——是不是？他那没受过教育的拉法，倒还算有两下子。”

“可怜的教区牧师那些鸟类标本，也一并烧了个精光。那几乎是他全部的心头爱。这个打击他到底没有真正缓过来。他来我们家住了一段——村里再寻不出别的空房。可他总不快活，整个人像散了架。我从没见一个男人变得这样厉害。我想提提他的精神，可没用——全没用。他对天使之类的事抱着最古怪的妄想，有时弄得人同他做伴都觉得别扭。他总说听见音乐，能对着空无一物的地方呆望好几个钟头。穿衣裳也马虎起来了。……火灾过后不到一年，他就去世了。”

## IV The Hunting of the Strange Bird (Continued) / 四 猎捕怪鸟（续）

===Original===

If it were not for collectors England would be full, so to speak, of rare birds and wonderful butterflies, strange flowers and a thousand interesting things. But happily the collector prevents all that, either killing with his own hands or, by buying extravagantly, procuring people of the lower classes to kill such eccentricities as appear. It makes work for people, even though Acts of Parliament interfere. In this way, for instance, he is killing off the chough in Cornwall, the Bath white butterfly, the Queen of Spain Fritillary; and can plume himself upon the extermination of the Great Auk, and a hundred other rare birds and plants and insects. All that is the work of the collector and his glory alone. In the name of Science. And this is right and as it should be; eccentricity, in fact, is immorality﻿—think over it again if you do not think so now﻿—just as eccentricity in one’s way of thinking is madness (I defy you to find another definition that will fit all the cases of either); and if a species is rare it follows that it is not Fitted to Survive. The collector is after all merely like the foot soldier in the days of heavy armour﻿—he leaves the combatants alone and cuts the throats of those who are overthrown. So one may go through England from end to end in the summer time and see only eight or ten commonplace wild flowers, and the commoner butterflies, and a dozen or so common birds, and never be offended by any breach of the monotony, any splash of strange blossom or flutter of unknown wing. All the rest have been “collected” years ago. For which cause we should all love Collectors, and bear in mind what we owe them when their little collections are displayed. These camphorated little drawers of theirs, their glass cases and blotting-paper books, are the graves of the Rare and the Beautiful, the symbols of the Triumph of Leisure (morally spent) over the Delights of Life. (All of which, as you very properly remark, has nothing whatever to do with the Strange Bird.)

===Chinese===

若不是有收藏家，英格兰——可以这么说——早就遍地都是珍禽、奇蝶、异花和千百样有趣的东西了。所幸收藏家杜绝了这一切：或亲手动刀，或一掷千金，雇下等阶级的人把冒出来的种种怪东西宰掉。这也给人创造了活计，尽管议会立法会来添乱。就这样，比方说，他正在消灭康沃尔的红嘴山鸦、巴斯白粉蝶、西班牙女王蛱蝶；还能凭着大海雀的灭绝自鸣得意，外加上百种别的珍禽异卉奇虫。这一切全是收藏家的功劳，荣耀也全归他一人。以科学之名。而这正当其宜、理该如此；说到底，怪，即是恶——你若此刻不以为然，不妨再想想——正如思想之怪即是疯（我倒要请你另找一条定义，把两种情形都套得上的）；一个物种既然稀少，可见它不适于生存。收藏家到底就像重甲时代步兵的角色——他不去碰交战双方，专割倒地者的喉咙。于是人们在夏天把英格兰从头走到尾，看见的无非八种十种平庸的野花、几样常见的蝴蝶、一打上下寻常的鸟，再不会叫任何破坏单调的东西、任何异花的泼溅、任何陌生的翅膀冒犯眼睛。其余的，多年前就统统“收藏”完了。为着这个缘故，我们都该爱收藏家，他们的小小收藏一亮相，我们更该铭记自己的感激。他们那些樟脑味的小抽屉、玻璃匣子和吸水纸册子，是珍异与美之物的坟墓，是有闲阶级（在道德上花掉了的闲）战胜人生之乐的凯旋标志。（这一切，正像你十分得体地指出的，同怪鸟毫无干系。）

## III The Hunting of the Strange Bird / 三 猎捕怪鸟

===Original===

The Vicar of Siddermorton (which is nine miles inland from Siddermouth as the crow flies) was an ornithologist. Some such pursuit, botany, antiquity, folklore, is almost inevitable for a single man in his position. He was given to geometry also, propounding occasionally impossible problems in the *Educational Times*, but ornithology was his forte. He had already added two visitors to the list of occasional British birds. His name was well-known in the columns of the *Zoologist* (I am afraid it may be forgotten by now, for the world moves apace). And on the day after the coming of the Strange Bird, came first one and then another to confirm the ploughman’s story and tell him, not that it had any connection, of the Glare upon Sidderford moor.

Now, the Vicar of Siddermorton had two rivals in his scientific pursuits; Gully of Sidderton, who had actually seen the Glare, and who it was sent the drawing to *Nature*, and Borland the natural history dealer, who kept the marine laboratory at Portburdock. Borland, the Vicar thought, should have stuck to his copepods, but instead he kept a taxidermist, and took advantage of his littoral position to pick up rare sea birds. It was evident to anyone who knew anything of collecting that both these men would be scouring the country after the strange visitant, before twenty-four hours were out.

===Chinese===

锡德莫顿的教区牧师（那地方离锡德茅斯（Siddermouth）取直线往内陆九英里）是位鸟类学家。处在他那种位置的单身汉，总要有个这类的营生——植物学也好，古物也好，民俗也好，几乎是免不了的。他还好几何，偶尔在*《教育时报》*（Educational Times）上出几道无解的题目，但鸟类学才是他的专长。他已经在偶见英国鸟类名录上添过两位新客。他的名字在*《动物学家》*（Zoologist）的专栏里颇有声名（只怕如今已被遗忘，这世界跑得太快）。怪鸟来临的第二天，先后来了一拨人又一拨人，证实了犁田汉的说法，还——虽说彼此并不相干——讲了锡德福德荒野上的那道光。

再说，锡德莫顿的教区牧师在科学营生上有两个对头：一个是锡德顿的加利，那道光他亲眼见过，寄画图给*《自然》*的就是他；另一个是博物商博兰（Borland），在波特伯多克开着一间海洋生物实验室。在教区牧师看来，博兰本该守着他的桡足虫过日子，可他偏偏养着一名标本师，又借着滨海的地利，专捡珍稀海鸟。稍懂收藏的人一看便知，不出二十四个钟头，这两人准会把乡野梳个底朝天，追那 位不速之客。

===Original===

The Vicar’s eye rested on the back of Saunders’ *British Birds*, for he was in his study at the time. Already in two places there was entered: “the only known British specimen was secured by the Rev. K. Hilyer, Vicar of Siddermorton.” A third such entry. He doubted if any other collector had that.

He looked at his watch﻿—*two*. He had just lunched, and usually he “rested” in the afternoon. He knew it would make him feel very disagreeable if he went out into the hot sunshine﻿—both on the top of his head and generally. Yet Gully perhaps was out, prowling observant. Suppose it was something very good and Gully got it!

===Chinese===

教区牧师的目光落在桑德斯《英国鸟类》（Saunders' *British Birds*）的书脊上——他此刻正在书房里。书里已有两处写着：“唯一已知的英国标本，由锡德莫顿教区牧师 K. 希利尔牧师采得。”再来上第三处。他怀疑还有哪个收藏家有过这样的纪录。

他看了看表——*两点*。他刚吃过午饭，下午照例要“歇一歇”。他知道，顶着烈日出门准会叫他很不痛快——头顶上和浑身都不痛快。可加利说不定已经出门，正滴溜溜地巡着呢。万一那是顶好的东西，又叫加利得了手呢！

===Original===

His gun stood in the corner. (The thing had iridiscent wings and pink legs! The chromatic conflict was certainly exceedingly stimulating). He took his gun.

He would have gone out by the glass doors and verandah, and down the garden into the hill road, in order to avoid his housekeeper’s eye. He knew his gun expeditions were not approved of. But advancing towards him up the garden, he saw the curate’s wife and her two daughters, carrying tennis rackets. His curate’s wife was a young woman of immense will, who used to play tennis on his lawn, and cut his roses, differ from him on doctrinal points, and criticise his personal behaviour all over the parish. He went in abject fear of her, was always trying to propitiate her. But so far he had clung to his ornithology.﻿ ﻿…

However, he went out by the front door.

===Chinese===

他的枪立在墙角。（那东西有一对虹彩的翅膀、粉红的双腿！这色彩的冲突着实刺激得很。）他取了枪。

他本想从玻璃门和游廊出去，穿过花园上山路，好躲开管家的眼睛。他知道他的持枪远征是不被赞成的。可他一眼望见副牧师太太带着两个女儿，拎着网球拍，正从花园那头走上来。他这位副牧师太太是个意志极盛的年轻妇人，惯于在他的草坪上打网球、剪他的玫瑰、同他争教义上的分歧，还要满教区数落他的私德。他对她怕得五体投地，总想方设法讨好她。但直到如今，他还死抱着他的鸟类学不放。……

不过，这一次他走的前门。

## XLVIII The Last Day of the Visit (Continued) / 四十八 来访的最后一天（续）

===Original===

Sir John Gotch (still smarting over the business of the barbed wire) was riding along one of the grassy ways through the preserves by the Sidder, when he saw, strolling slowly through the trees beyond the undergrowth, the one particular human being he did not want to see.

“I’m damned,” said Sir John Gotch, with immense emphasis; “if this isn’t altogether too much.”

He raised himself in the stirrups. “Hi!” he shouted. “You there!”

===Chinese===

约翰·戈奇爵士（还为铁蒺藜那档子事窝着火）正沿着锡德河边猎苑里一条草径骑马走着，忽然看见矮树林那边，一个身影在树间缓缓踱步——正是他最不想看见的那一个人。

“见鬼，”约翰·戈奇爵士说，字字千钧，“这真是欺人太甚了。”

他踩着马镫直起身子。“喂！”他喊道。“说你呢！”

===Original===

The Angel turned smiling.

“Get out of this wood!” said Sir John Gotch.

“*Why?*” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

天使微笑着转过身来。

“滚出这片林子！”约翰·戈奇爵士说。

“*为什么？*”天使说。

===Original===

“I’m﻿—,” said Sir John Gotch, meditating some cataclysmal expletive. But he could think of nothing more than “damned.” “Get out of this wood,” he said.

The Angel’s smile vanished. “Why should I get out of this wood?” he said, and stood still.

Neither spoke for a full half minute perhaps, and then Sir John Gotch dropped out of his saddle and stood by the horse.

===Chinese===

“我——，”约翰·戈奇爵士说，寻思着一句天崩地裂的咒骂。可想来想去，只想起一个“见鬼”。“滚出这片林子，”他说。

天使脸上的笑消失了。“我为什么该滚出这片林子？”他说，站着不动。

两人足足有半分钟谁也不说话，随后约翰·戈奇爵士翻身下马，立在马旁。

===Original===

(Now you must remember﻿—lest the Angelic Hosts be discredited hereby﻿—that this Angel had been breathing the poisonous air of this struggle for existence of ours for more than a week. It was not only his wings and the brightness of his face that suffered. He had eaten and slept and learnt the lesson of pain﻿—had travelled so far on the road to humanity. All the length of his visit he had been meeting more and more of the harshness and conflict of this world, and losing touch with the glorious altitudes of his own.)

“You won’t go, eigh!” said Gotch, and began to lead his horse through the bushes towards the Angel. The Angel stood, all his muscles tight and his nerves quivering, watching his antagonist approach.

“Get out of this wood,” said Gotch, stopping three yards away, his face white with rage, his bridle in one hand and his riding whip in the other.

===Chinese===

（这里你们务必记着——免得天军因此蒙羞——这位天使呼吸我们这生存斗争的毒气，已经一个多星期了。受损的不单是他的翅膀和脸上的光彩。他吃过，睡过，领教过痛的功课——在通往人的路上已走出这么远。来访的这一整段日子里，他碰到的这个世界的冷酷与冲突越来越多，同他自己那个光荣的高天，也越来越隔膜了。）

“你不走，嗯！”戈奇说，开始牵着马穿过灌木朝天使走去。天使站着，浑身肌肉绷紧，神经震颤，望着他的对头逼近。

“滚出这片林子，”戈奇说，在三码开外站住，脸气得发白，一手攥着缰绳，一手攥着马鞭。

===Original===

Strange floods of emotion were running through the Angel. “Who are you,” he said, in a low quivering voice; “who am I﻿—that you should order me out of this place? What has the world done that men like you.﻿ ﻿…”

“You’re the fool who cut my barbed wire,” said Gotch, threatening, “If you want to know!”

“*Your* barbed wire,” said the Angel. “Was that your barbed wire? Are you the man who put down that barbed wire? What right have you.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

一股股奇异的情绪的洪流在天使体内奔涌。“你是谁，”他用低而发颤的声音说，“我又是谁——轮得到你把我从这里撵出去？这个世界究竟做了什么，竟养出你们这样的人。……”

“你就是剪我铁蒺藜的那个蠢货，”戈奇威吓道，“你要知道的话！”

“*你的*铁蒺藜，”天使说。“那是你的铁蒺藜？安那铁蒺藜的就是你？你有什么权利。……”

===Original===

“Don’t you go talking Socialist rot,” said Gotch in short gasps. “This wood’s mine, and I’ve a right to protect it how I can. I know your kind of muck. Talking rot and stirring up discontent. And if you don’t get out of it jolly sharp.﻿ ﻿…”

“*Well!*” said the Angel, a brimming reservoir of unaccountable energy.

“Get out of this damned wood!” said Gotch, flashing into the bully out of sheer alarm at the light in the Angel’s face.

===Chinese===

“少跟我扯社会主义的屁话，”戈奇喘着粗气说。“这林子是我的，我有权随便怎么护着它。你们那套乌七八糟我见得多了。满嘴屁话，专会煽风点火。你要是不赶紧滚。……”

“*好啊！*”天使说，成了一座无从名之的精力的满溢的水库。

“滚出这片该死的林子！”戈奇说，一见天使脸上的光，心里发怵，索性耍起横来。

===Original===

He made one step towards him, with the whip raised, and then something happened that neither he nor the Angel properly understood. The Angel seemed to leap into the air, a pair of grey wings flashed out at the Squire, he saw a face bearing down upon him, full of the wild beauty of passionate anger. His riding whip was torn out of his hand. His horse reared behind him, pulled him over, gained his bridle and fled.

The whip cut across his face as he fell back, stung across his face again as he sat on the ground. He saw the Angel, radiant with anger, in the act to strike again. Gotch flung up his hands, pitched himself forward to save his eyes, and rolled on the ground under the pitiless fury of the blows that rained down upon him.

“You brute,” cried the Angel, striking wherever he saw flesh to feel. “You bestial thing of pride and lies! You who have overshadowed the souls of other men. You shallow fool with your horses and dogs! To lift your face against any living thing! Learn! Learn! Learn!”

===Chinese===

他朝天使跨出一步，扬起了鞭子，跟着便发生了一桩连他和天使都没弄明白的事。天使仿佛腾空跃起，一对灰色的翅膀朝乡绅直闪过来，他看见一张脸直逼下来，满盛着狂怒的野性之美。他的马鞭被从手里夺去。他的马在身后人立而起，把他拽翻，挣脱缰绳逃了。

他仰面倒下时，鞭子横抽过他的脸；他坐起身，鞭子又狠狠掹上他的脸。他看见天使怒容焕发，正挥鞭再打。戈奇双手乱挡，一头前扑护住眼睛，在那劈头盖脸、毫不留情的痛打下满地打滚。

“你这畜生，”天使喊着，见肉就抽。“你这骄傲与谎言堆成的兽物！你这遮蔽旁人灵魂的家伙！你这只知道马呀狗呀的浅薄蠢货！竟敢对任何活物扬起你的脸！学着点！学着点！学着点！”

===Original===

Gotch began screaming for help. Twice he tried to clamber to his feet, got to his knees, and went headlong again under the ferocious anger of the Angel. Presently he made a strange noise in his throat, and ceased even to writhe under his punishment.

Then suddenly the Angel awakened from his wrath, and found himself standing, panting and trembling, one foot on a motionless figure, under the green stillness of the sunlit woods.

He stared about him, then down at his feet where, among the tangled dead leaves, the hair was matted with blood. The whip dropped from his hands, the hot colour fled from his face. “*Pain!*” he said. “Why does he lie so still?”

===Chinese===

戈奇开始尖叫呼救。他两番挣扎着想爬起来，刚跪起身，又在天使的狂怒之下一头栽倒。不一会儿，他喉咙里发出一声怪响，连挨打都不抽搐了。

随后天使忽然从震怒中惊醒，发觉自己站在那里，喘着，抖着，一只脚踩在一个一动不动的人形上，四下是阳光照绿的、静静的树林。

他环顾四周，又低头看脚下：乱糟糟的枯叶中间，头发已被血粘成了一团。鞭子从他手里掉了下去，脸上的热色褪得干干净净。“*痛！*”他说。“他怎么躺得这么死？”

===Original===
