He took his foot off Gotch’s shoulder, bent down towards the prostrate figure, stood listening, knelt﻿—shook him. “Awake!” said the Angel. Then still more softly, “*Awake!*”

He remained listening some minutes or more, stood up sharply, and looked round him at the silent trees. A feeling of profound horror descended upon him, wrapped him round about. With an abrupt gesture he turned. “What has happened to me?” he said, in an awestricken whisper.

He started back from the motionless figure. “*Dead!*” he said suddenly, and turning, panic stricken, fled headlong through the wood.

===Chinese===

他把脚从戈奇肩上挪开，朝那俯卧的人形弯下腰，站着听了听，跪下——摇他。“醒来！”天使说。随后更轻地，“*醒来！*”

他又听了好几分钟，猛地站起身，环望四周沉寂的树。一种深深的恐怖降临到他身上，把他层层裹住。他猛一转身。“我这是怎么了？”他用敬畏交加的耳语说。

他从那一动不动的人形跟前直退出来。“*死了！*”他忽然说，转身没命地穿过树林逃了。

## XLVII The Last Day of the Visit / 四十七 来访的最后一天

===Original===

On the morning of the next day the Angel, after he had breakfasted, went out towards the moor, and Mrs. Hinijer had an interview with the Vicar. What happened need not concern us now. The Vicar was visibly disconcerted. “He *must* go,” he said; “certainly he must go,” and straightway he forgot the particular accusation in the general trouble. He spent the morning in hazy meditation, interspersed by a spasmodic study of Skiff and Waterlow’s price list, and the catalogue of the Medical, Scholastic, and Clerical Stores. A schedule grew slowly on a sheet of paper that lay on the desk before him. He cut out a self-measurement form from the tailoring department of the Stores and pinned it to the study curtains. This was the kind of document he was making:

> “1 Black Melton Frock Coat, patts? £3, 10s.
> “? Trousers. 2 pairs or one.
> “1 Cheviot Tweed Suit (write for patterns. Self-meas.?)”

The Vicar spent some time studying a pleasing array of model gentlemen. They were all very nice-looking, but he found it hard to imagine the Angel so transfigured. For, although six days had passed, the Angel remained without any suit of his own. The Vicar had vacillated between a project of driving the Angel into Portbroddock and getting him measured for a suit, and his absolute horror of the insinuating manners of the tailor he employed. He knew that tailor would demand an exhaustive explanation. Besides which, one never knew when the Angel might leave. So the six days had passed, and the Angel had grown steadily in the wisdom of this world and shrouded his brightness still in the ample retirement of the Vicar’s newest clothes.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，天使用了早饭，出门往荒野去了；希尼杰太太则同教区牧师进行了一场面谈。谈了些什么，眼下不必烦扰我们。教区牧师明显地心慌意乱。“他*必须*走，”他说，“当然必须走，”随即便把这桩具体的控诉忘在了一片笼统的烦恼里。一整个上午，他都在恍恍惚惚的沉思中度过，其间断断续续地翻看斯基夫与沃特洛（Skiff and Waterlow）的价目表，还有医学、学界与教士用品商店的货册。一张单子在他面前书桌上的纸上慢慢成形。他从商店裁缝部的广告上剪下一张自助量体表，钉在书房的窗帘上。他拟的正是这样一份文书：

> “黑色麦尔登长礼服一件，要料样？3 镑 10 先令。
> “长裤？两条还是一条。
> “切维奥特粗花呢套装一套（函索料样。自助量体？）”

教区牧师花了不少工夫，研究一列讨人喜欢的模特绅士。他们个个仪表堂堂，可他很难想象天使也能如此改头换面。原来，尽管六天过去了，天使仍旧没有一套自己的衣裳。教区牧师一直在两个念头之间摇摆：一是驾车载天使去波特伯多克量体做衣，一是他一想起自己那位裁缝那种刨根问底的做派就深恶痛绝。他知道那裁缝准会逼他作一番详尽无遗的解释。再说，谁说得准天使哪天就走了呢。就这样过了六天，天使在这个世界的世事上稳步长进，那身光彩也仍旧裹在教区牧师几件最新衣裳的宽大退隐里。

===Original===

> “1 Soft Felt Hat, No. G. 7 (say), 8s. 6d.
> “1 Silk Hat, 14s. 6d. Hatbox?”

(“I suppose he ought to have a silk hat,” said the Vicar; “it’s the correct thing up there. Shape No. 3 seems best suited to his style. But it’s dreadful to think of him all alone in that great city. Everyone will misunderstand him, and he will misunderstand everybody. However, I suppose it *must* be. Where was I?)”

> “1 Toothbrush. 1 Brush and Comb. Razor?
> “½ doz. Shirts (? measure his neck), 6s ea.
> “Socks? Pants?
> “2 suits Pyjamas. Price? Say 15s.
> “1 doz. Collars (‘The Life Guardsman’), 8s.
> “Braces. Oxon Patent Versatile, 1s 11½d.”

===Chinese===

> “软呢帽一顶，G. 7 号（姑且这么说），8 先令 6 便士。
> “大礼帽一顶，14 先令 6 便士。帽盒？”

（“我想他该有顶大礼帽，”教区牧师说；“在那边这才是正派行头。3 号款式似乎最配他的风度。可是一想到他孤零零一个人待在那座大城市里，就可怕。人人都会误解他，他也会误解人人。不过，我想这*总归*免不了。我方才写到哪儿了？）”

> “牙刷一把。毛刷与梳子一套。剃刀？
> “衬衫半打（量他的领围？），每件 6 先令。
> “袜子？衬裤？
> “睡衣两套。价格？姑且算 15 先令。
> “领子一打（‘近卫骑兵’式），8 先令。
> “背带。牛津专利多用式，1 先令 11 便士半。”

===Original===

(“But how will he get them on?” said the Vicar.)

> “1 Rubber Stamp, T. Angel, and Marking Ink in box complete, 9d.

(“Those washerwomen are certain to steal all his things.”)

===Chinese===

（“可这些东西他怎么穿得上身呢？”教区牧师说。）

> “印章一枚，T. 安吉尔，配盒装记号墨水，9 便士。”

（“那些洗衣婆准会把他的东西偷个精光。”）

===Original===

> “1 Single-bladed Penknife with Corkscrew, say 1s. 6d.
> “N.B.﻿—Don’t forget Cuff Links, Collar Stud, etc.”

(The Vicar loved “etc.,” it gave things such a precise and businesslike air.)

> “1 Leather Portmanteau (had better see these).”

And so forth﻿—meanderingly. It kept the Vicar busy until lunch time, though his heart ached.
The Angel did not return to lunch. This was not so very remarkable﻿—once before he had missed the midday meal. Yet, considering how short was the time they would have together now, he might perhaps have come back. Doubtless he had excellent reasons, though, for his absence. The Vicar made an indifferent lunch. In the afternoon he rested in his usual manner, and did a little more to the list of requirements. He did not begin to feel nervous about the Angel till teatime. He waited, perhaps, half an hour before he took tea. “Odd,” said the Vicar, feeling still more lonely as he drank his tea.
As the time for dinner crept on and no Angel appeared the Vicar’s imagination began to trouble him. “He will come in to dinner, surely,” said the Vicar, caressing his chin, and beginning to fret about the house upon inconsiderable errands, as his habit was when anything occurred to break his routine. The sun set, a gorgeous spectacle, amidst tumbled masses of purple cloud. The gold and red faded into twilight; the evening star gathered her robe of light together from out the brightness of the sky in the West. Breaking the silence of evening that crept over the outer world, a corncrake began his whirring chant. The Vicar’s face grew troubled; twice he went and stared at the darkening hillside, and then fretted back to the house again. Mrs. Hinijer served dinner. “Your dinner’s ready,” she announced for the second time, with a reproachful intonation. “Yes, yes,” said the Vicar, fussing off upstairs.
He came down and went into his study and lit his reading lamp, a patent affair with an incandescent wick, dropping the match into his wastepaper basket without stopping to see if it was extinguished. Then he fretted into the dining-room and began a desultory attack on the cooling dinner.﻿ ﻿…
(Dear Reader, the time is almost ripe to say farewell to this little Vicar of ours.)

===Chinese===

> “单刃小刀一把带开塞钻，姑且算 1 先令 6 便士。
> “又及——袖扣、领扣等物勿忘。”

（教区牧师最爱“等物”二字，它给一切平添一种精确干练的气象。）

> “皮旅行箱一只（此类最好亲看）。”

诸如此类——信马由缰。这桩事让教区牧师一直忙到午饭时分，尽管他心里作痛。天使没有回来吃午饭。这不算太稀奇——先前他也误过一顿正餐。可是，想想如今他们能在一起的日子已这么短，他总该回来的。不过，他的缺席想必有十足的理由。教区牧师马马虎虎吃了午饭。下午他照例歇了歇，又给那份需用清单添了几笔。直到喝下午茶的时辰，他才开始为天使悬心。他等了大概半个钟头才用茶。“古怪，”教区牧师说，喝茶时愈发觉得冷清。晚饭的时辰一步步挨近，天使还是不见踪影，教区牧师的想象力开始作祟了。“他总会回来吃晚饭的吧，”教区牧师说，一边摩挲着下巴，一边为些不要紧的差事在屋里团团打转——每逢日常的轨道被什么事打断，他总要这样的。太阳落山了，紫色的乱云堆里，一派壮丽的景象。金红褪成暮色；长庚星从西天的明亮里拢起她一身的光袍。外间的世界渐渐爬上夜的沉寂，一只秧鸡打破了它，开始它嗡嗡的吟唱。教区牧师的脸色不安起来；他两度走出去，望着渐渐暗下去的山坡发怔，又心事重重地折回屋里。希尼杰太太端上晚饭。“晚饭得了，”她第二次通报，语调里带着责备。“就来，就来，”教区牧师说，慌慌张张地上了楼。他下楼进了书房，点亮阅读灯——那是一盏白炽灯芯的新式玩意儿——随手把火柴丢进废纸篓，也没停下来看看它熄了没有。随后他又心神不定地踱进饭厅，对那渐渐凉下去的晚饭发起一场零星的攻势。……（亲爱的读者，同我们这位小个子牧师道别的时辰，差不多熟了。）

## XIV The Man of Science (Continued) / 十四 科学家（续）

===Original===

“That *is* an Angel,” whispered the Vicar. “You don’t understand.”

“*What?*” said the Doctor in a quick, sharp voice. His eyebrows went up and he smiled.

“But the wings?”

===Chinese===

“那*是*一位天使，”教区牧师低声说。“你不明白。”

“*什么？*”医生说，声音急促尖利。他眉毛扬了起来，笑了。

“可那翅膀呢？”

===Original===

“Quite natural, quite﻿ ﻿… if a little abnormal.”

“Are you sure they are natural?”

“My dear fellow, everything that is, is natural. There is nothing unnatural in the world. If I thought there was I should give up practice and go into *Le Grand Chartreuse*. There are abnormal phenomena, of course. And﻿—”

===Chinese===

“再自然不过，再自然……只是有点反常。”

“你肯定它们是自然的？”

“亲爱的伙计，凡是存在的，都是自然的。世上没有不自然的东西。倘使我认为有，我就该弃了行医，进*大沙特勒斯修道院（Le Grand Chartreuse）*去。当然，反常的现象是有的。还有——”

===Original===

“But the way I came upon him,” said the Vicar.

“Yes, tell me where you picked him up,” said the Doctor. He sat down on the hall table.

The Vicar began rather hesitatingly﻿—he was not very good at story telling﻿—with the rumours of a strange great bird. He told the story in clumsy sentences﻿—for, knowing the Bishop as he did, with that awful example always before him he dreaded getting his pulpit style into his daily conversation﻿—and at every third sentence or so, the Doctor made a downward movement of his head﻿—the corners of his mouth tucked away, so to speak﻿—as though he ticked off the phases of the story and so far found it just as it ought to be. “Self-hypnotism,” he murmured once.

===Chinese===

“可我撞见他的那个经过，”教区牧师说。

“是啊，说说你在哪儿捡到他的，”医生说。他在门厅的桌上坐了下来。

教区牧师讲得颇为迟疑——他本不擅讲故事——从一只巨大怪鸟的传闻讲起。他讲得句子笨拙——因为他深知主教其人，那可怕的前车之鉴总悬在眼前，他生怕把讲道坛的腔调带进日常谈话——大约每隔三句，医生就把头往下一沉——嘴角那么往里一收——仿佛给故事的各段落一个个打钩，到眼下为止还算中规中矩。“自我催眠，”有一回他喃喃道。

===Original===

“I beg your pardon?” said the Vicar.

“Nothing,” said the Doctor. “Nothing, I assure you. Go on. This is extremely interesting.”

The Vicar told him he went out with his gun.

===Chinese===

“你说什么？”教区牧师问。

“没什么，”医生说。“没什么，我担保。接着讲。这有趣极了。”

教区牧师讲到他带着枪出门。

===Original===

“*After* lunch, I think you said?” interrupted the Doctor.

“Immediately after,” said the Vicar.

“You should not do such things, you know. But go on, please.”

===Chinese===

“*午饭后*，我记得你是这么说的？”医生插话。

“一吃完就去了，”教区牧师说。

“这种事你可不该干，你晓得。不过请继续。”

===Original===

He came to the glimpse of the Angel from the gate.

“In the full glare,” said the Doctor, in parenthesis. “It was seventy-nine in the shade.”

When the Vicar had finished, the Doctor pressed his lips together tighter than ever, smiled faintly, and looked significantly into the Vicar’s eyes.

===Chinese===

他讲到在大门口瞥见天使的那一幕。

“正是烈日当头，”医生插了句旁白。“背阴处都七十九度。”

教区牧师讲完，医生把双唇抿得紧上加紧，淡淡一笑，意味深长地直望着教区牧师的眼睛。

===Original===

“You don’t﻿ ﻿…” began the Vicar, falteringly.

The Doctor shook his head. “Forgive me,” he said, putting his hand on the Vicar’s arm.

“You go out,” he said, “on a hot lunch and on a hot afternoon. Probably over eighty. Your mind, what there is of it, is whirling with avian expectations. I say, ‘what there is of it,’ because most of your nervous energy is down there, digesting your dinner. A man who has been lying in the bracken stands up before you and you blaze away. Over he goes﻿—and as it happens﻿—as it happens﻿—he has reduplicate forelimbs, one pair being not unlike wings. It’s a coincidence certainly. And as for his iridescent colours and so forth﻿—. Have you never had patches of colour swim before your eyes before, on a brilliant sunlight day?﻿ ﻿… Are you sure they were confined to the wings? Think.”

===Chinese===

“你并不……”教区牧师结结巴巴地开口。

医生摇了摇头。“原谅我，”他说，一只手搭上教区牧师的手臂。

“你出门，”他说，“揣着一顿热乎乎的午饭，又赶上一个大热天的下午。多半八十度开外。你的脑子——剩下能用的那一部分——正被满脑子的鸟类憧憬搅得团团转。我说‘剩下能用的那一部分’，是因为你大半的神经能量都沉在底下消化午饭呢。一个一直在蕨丛里躺着的人在你面前站起来，你就一枪打过去。他倒下了——而碰巧——碰巧——他长着重复的前肢，其中一对颇像翅膀。这当然是个巧合。至于他身上虹彩的颜色等等——。晴空万里的日子里，你眼前难道从没漂过一片片色斑吗？……你肯定那些颜色只限于翅膀上吗？想想。”

===Original===

“But he says he *is* an Angel!” said the Vicar, staring out of his little round eyes, his plump hands in his pockets.

“*Ah!*” said the Doctor with his eye on the Vicar. “I expected as much.” He paused.

“But don’t you think﻿ ﻿…” began the Vicar.

===Chinese===

“可他说他*是*一位天使！”教区牧师说，一双小圆眼睛直瞪着，胖胖的手插在口袋里。

“*啊！*”医生说着，眼睛盯着教区牧师。“我早料到这一层。”他顿了顿。

“可你不觉得……”教区牧师开口说。

===Original===

“That man,” said the Doctor in a low, earnest voice, “is a mattoid.”

“A what?” said the Vicar.

“A mattoid. An abnormal man. Did you notice the effeminate delicacy of his face? His tendency to quite unmeaning laughter? His neglected hair? Then consider his singular dress.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“那个人，”医生低声而恳切地说，“是个狂质者。”

“一个什么？”教区牧师说。

“狂质者。一个反常的人。你可留意到他脸上那种女子气的娇弱？他那毫无来由的发笑的倾向？他那蓬乱的头发？再想想他那身古怪的打扮。……”

===Original===

The Vicar’s hand went up to his chin.

“Marks of mental weakness,” said the Doctor. “Many of this type of degenerate show this same disposition to assume some vast mysterious credentials. One will call himself the Prince of Wales, another the Archangel Gabriel, another the Deity even. Ibsen thinks he is a Great Teacher, and Maeterlink a new Shakespeare. I’ve just been reading all about it﻿—in Nordau. No doubt his odd deformity gave him an idea.﻿ ﻿…”

“But really,” began the Vicar.

===Chinese===

教区牧师的手摸上了下巴。

“心智薄弱的标记，”医生说。“这类退化者当中，许多人都有同一种倾向，要给自己安上一份庞大而神秘的来历。这个自称威尔士亲王，那个自称天使长加百列，还有的自称神明。易卜生自以为是位大导师，梅特林克自以为是新莎士比亚。我最近正读这方面的书——诺尔道写的。毫无疑问，他那身古怪的畸形给了他一个灵感。……”

“可是说真的，”教区牧师开口说。

===Original===

“No doubt he’s slipped away from confinement.”

“I do not altogether accept.﻿ ﻿…”

“You will. If not, there’s the police, and failing that, advertisement; but, of course, his people may want to hush it up. It’s a sad thing in a family.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“毫无疑问，他是从什么看管的地方溜出来的。”

“我不能完全接受。……”

“你会接受的。不然，还有警察；再不行，还有登报招领。当然，他的家里人也许想把事情压下去。这种事，于一门人家总是不幸。……”

===Original===

“He seems so altogether.﻿ ﻿…”

“Probably you’ll hear from his friends in a day or so,” said the Doctor, feeling for his watch. “He can’t live far from here, I should think. He seems harmless enough. I must come along and see that wing again tomorrow.” He slid off the hall table and stood up.

“Those old wives’ tales still have their hold on you,” he said, patting the Vicar on the shoulder. “But an angel, you know﻿—Ha, ha!”

===Chinese===

“他看上去这么完全。……”

“多半一两天内他家里人就会来消息，”医生说着，摸索着掏出怀表。“我想他住得离这儿不会远。他瞧着没什么害处。明天我一定再来看看那只翅膀。”他从门厅桌上滑下来，站起身。

“那些老婆子们的老故事，在你身上还有余威呢，”他说，拍了拍教区牧师的肩膀。“可一位天使，你晓得——哈，哈！”

===Original===

“I certainly *did* think.﻿ ﻿…” said the Vicar dubiously.

“Weigh the evidence,” said the Doctor, still fumbling at his watch. “Weigh the evidence with our instruments of precision. What does it leave you? Splashes of colour, spots of fancy﻿—*muscae volantes*.”

“And yet,” said the Vicar, “I could almost swear to the glory on his wings.﻿ ﻿…”

“Think it over,” said the Doctor (watch out); “hot afternoon﻿—brilliant sunshine﻿—boiling down on your head.﻿ ﻿… But really I *must* be going. It is a quarter to five. I’ll see your﻿—angel (ha, ha!) tomorrow again, if no one has been to fetch him in the meanwhile. Your bandaging was really very good. I flatter *myself* on that score. Our ambulance classes *were* a success you see.﻿ ﻿… Good afternoon.”

===Chinese===

“我方才*确实*觉得……”教区牧师迟疑地说。

“掂一掂证据，”医生说，仍在摆弄怀表。“用我们精密的仪器掂一掂证据。剩下什么？几摊颜色，几点幻影——*飞蚊症（muscae volitantes）*。”

“可是，”教区牧师说，“他翅膀上那圈光晕，我几乎可以起誓。……”

“回去好好想想，”医生说（表掏出来了）；“大热的天——明晃晃的日头——当头煮着。……可我实在*必须*走了。现在是五点差一刻。要是这期间没有人来领他，明天我再来看你的——天使（哈，哈！）。你上的那绷带实在高明。这一点上*我*很得意。瞧见没，咱们的急救训练班*没白办*。……再会。”

## XIII The Man of Science / 十三 科学家

===Original===

Then Doctor Crump arrived. Grummet had met him not a hundred yards from the vicarage gate. He was a large, rather heavy-looking man, with a clean-shaven face and a double chin. He was dressed in a grey morning coat (he always affected grey), with a chequered black and white tie. “What’s the trouble?” he said, entering and staring without a shadow of surprise at the Angel’s radiant face.

“This﻿—*ahem*﻿—gentleman,” said the Vicar, “or﻿—*ah*﻿—Angel”﻿—the Angel bowed﻿—“is suffering from a gunshot wound.”

“Gunshot wound!” said Doctor Crump. “In July! May I look at it, Mr.﻿—Angel, I think you said?”

===Chinese===

接着，克伦普医生到了。格拉米特在离牧师公馆大门不到一百码的地方迎着了他。他是个高大、模样颇沉重的男人，脸刮得干干净净，长着双下巴。他穿一件灰色常礼服（他素来偏爱灰色），系一条黑白格纹领带。“出什么毛病了？”他说着走进来，对天使那张光彩焕发的脸没有一丝惊讶。

“这位——*咳咳*——先生，”教区牧师说，“或者——*啊*——天使”——天使鞠了一躬——“正受着枪伤的苦。”

“枪伤！”克伦普医生说。“七月里！我可以看看吗，天——使先生，我记得你是这么称呼的？”

===Original===

“He will probably be able to assuage your pain,” said the Vicar. “Let me assist you to remove your coat?”

The Angel turned obediently.

“Spinal curvature?” muttered Doctor Crump quite audibly, walking round behind the Angel. “No! abnormal growth. Hullo! This is odd!” He clutched the left wing. “Curious,” he said. “Reduplication of the anterior limb﻿—bifid coracoid. Possible, of course, but I’ve never seen it before.” The angel winced under his hands. “Humerus. Radius and Ulna. All there. Congenital, of course. Humerus broken. Curious integumentary simulation of feathers. Dear me. Almost avian. Probably of considerable interest in comparative anatomy. I never did!﻿—How did this gunshot happen, Mr. Angel?”

===Chinese===

“他或许能平息您的疼痛，”教区牧师说。“让我帮您把上衣脱下来？”

天使顺从地转过身去。

“脊柱弯曲？”克伦普医生绕到天使背后，声音不小地咕哝道。“不对！异常增生。嚯！这可稀奇了！”他一把攥住左边那只翅膀。“有趣，”他说。“前肢重复——叉状喙骨。当然可能，可我从前没见过。”天使在他的手下直缩。“肱骨。桡骨和尺骨。一应俱全。先天性的，自然。肱骨骨折。有趣的羽状皮膜拟态。天哪。几乎就是鸟了。在比较解剖学上多半大有意思。我从没见过！——这枪伤是怎么来的，天使先生？”

===Original===

The Vicar was amazed at the Doctor’s matter-of-fact manner.

“Our friend,” said the Angel, moving his head at the Vicar.

“Unhappily it is my doing,” said the Vicar, stepping forward, explanatory. “I mistook the gentleman﻿—the Angel (*ahem*)﻿—for a large bird﻿—”

===Chinese===

医生这种就事论事的态度，让教区牧师大为惊异。

“我们的朋友，”天使说，朝教区牧师那边偏了偏头。

“不幸正是我的手笔，”教区牧师上前提解释说。“我把这位先生——这位天使（*咳咳*）——误当成了一只大鸟——”

===Original===

“Mistook him for a large bird! What next? Your eyes want seeing to,” said Doctor Crump. “I’ve told you so before.” He went on patting and feeling, keeping time with a series of grunts and inarticulate mutterings.﻿ ﻿… “But this is really a very good bit of amateur bandaging,” said he. “I think I shall leave it. Curious malformation this is! Don’t you find it inconvenient, Mr. Angel?”

He suddenly walked round so as to look in the Angel’s face.

The Angel thought he referred to the wound. “It is rather,” he said.

===Chinese===

“把他当成大鸟！还有什么是干不出来的？您这双眼睛该查一查了，”克伦普医生说。“我早跟您说过。”他继续又拍又摸，用一串哼哼和含混的咕哝打着拍子。……“不过这业余的绷带缠得实在高明，”他说。“我看就留着吧。这畸形真有趣！您不觉得碍事吗，天使先生？”

他忽然绕到天使面前，直望他的脸。

天使以为他说的是伤口。“是有点儿，”他说。

===Original===

“If it wasn’t for the bones I should say paint with iodine night and morning. Nothing like iodine. You could paint your face flat with it. But the osseous outgrowth, the bones, you know, complicate things. I could saw them off, of course. It’s not a thing one should have done in a hurry﻿—”

“Do you mean my wings?” said the Angel in alarm.

“Wings!” said the Doctor. “Eigh? Call ’em wings! Yes﻿—what else should I mean?”

===Chinese===

“要不是碍着这些骨头，我就叫您早晚上拿碘酒涂。没有比碘酒更好的了。您都可以拿它把脸涂平了。可这骨性的增生，这些骨头，您晓得，把事情弄复杂了。当然，我可以把它们锯掉。这可不是个该着急上手的事——”

“您是说我的翅膀？”天使惊慌地说。

“翅膀！”医生说。“嗯？管它们叫翅膀！是啊——不然我还能指什么？”

===Original===

“Saw them off!” said the Angel.

“Don’t you think so? It’s of course your affair. I am only advising﻿—”

“Saw them off! What a funny creature you are!” said the Angel, beginning to laugh.

===Chinese===

“锯掉它们！”天使说。

“您不这么想吗？这自然是您的事。我不过是从旁建议——”

“锯掉它们！你可真是个有趣的家伙！”天使说着，笑了起来。

===Original===

“As you will,” said the Doctor. He detested people who laughed. “The things are curious,” he said, turning to the Vicar. “If inconvenient”﻿—to the Angel. “I never heard of such complete reduplication before﻿—at least among animals. In plants it’s common enough. Were you the only one in your family?” He did not wait for a reply. “Partial cases of the fission of limbs are not at all uncommon, of course, Vicar﻿—six-fingered children, calves with six feet, and cats with double toes, you know. May I assist you?” he said, turning to the Angel who was struggling with the coat. “But such a complete reduplication, and so avian, too! It would be much less remarkable if it was simply another pair of arms.”

The coat was got on and he and the Angel stared at one another.

“Really,” said the Doctor, “one begins to understand how that beautiful myth of the angels arose. You look a little hectic, Mr. Angel﻿—feverish. Excessive brilliance is almost worse as a symptom than excessive pallor. Curious your name should be Angel. I must send you a cooling draught, if you should feel thirsty in the night.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“随您的便，”医生说。他最烦人笑。“这两样东西确属罕见，”他转向教区牧师说。“要说碍事的话”——又转向天使——“这么完全的重复，我从未听说见过——至少在动物里。在植物里倒常见得很。您是家里独一个吧？”他不等人回答。“肢体的部分分裂病例，其实并不算少见，您晓得，牧师——六指的小孩啊，六条腿的牛犊啊，双趾的猫啊。要我帮忙吗？”他转向正同上衣较劲的天使说。“可这么完全的重复，而且还是鸟形的！倘若只是寻常多出的一对手臂，倒远不至于这么稀奇。”

上衣总算穿上了，他和天使面面相觑。

“说真的，”医生说，“人们这才明白，天使那个美丽的神话是怎么起来的。您脸色有点过艳了，天使先生——像发烧。光彩过甚，作为一种症状，几乎比苍白过甚还要糟。您偏偏姓天使，真是有趣。夜里若觉着渴，我一定给您送一副清凉合剂来。……”

===Original===

He made a memorandum on his shirt cuff. The Angel watched him thoughtfully, with the dawn of a smile in his eyes.

“One minute, Crump,” said the Vicar, taking the Doctor’s arm and leading him towards the door.

The Angel’s smile grew brighter. He looked down at his black-clad legs. “He positively thinks I am a man!” said the Angel. “What he makes of the wings beats me altogether. What a queer creature he must be! This is really a most extraordinary Dream!”

===Chinese===

他在衬衫袖口上记了一笔。天使若有所思地望着他，眼里绽出一点笑意。

“等一下，克伦普，”教区牧师说着，挽起医生的胳膊，把他往门口引。

天使的笑意更亮了。他低头望了望自己一身黑衣的两条腿。“他真真把我当成一个人了！”天使说。“他把这对翅膀看成什么，我全然猜不透。他准是个顶古怪的家伙！这真是一场再离奇不过的梦！”

## I The Night of the Strange Bird / 一 怪鸟之夜

===Original===

On the Night of the Strange Bird, many people at Sidderton (and some nearer) saw a Glare on the Sidderford moor. But no one in Sidderford saw it, for most of Sidderford was abed.

All day the wind had been rising, so that the larks on the moor chirruped fitfully near the ground, or rose only to be driven like leaves before the wind. The sun set in a bloody welter of clouds, and the moon was hidden. The Glare, they say, was golden like a beam shining out of the sky, not a uniform blaze, but broken all over by curving flashes like the waving of swords. It lasted but a moment and left the night dark and obscure. There were letters about it in *Nature*, and a rough drawing that no one thought very like. (You may see it for yourself﻿—the drawing that was unlike the Glare﻿—on page 42 of Vol. CCLX of that publication.)

===Chinese===

怪鸟降临的那一夜，锡德顿有许多人（还有住得更近的一些人）看见锡德福德荒野上有一道光。可锡德福德本地反倒没人看见，因为锡德福德的人多半已经上了床。

一整天风都在越刮越紧，荒野上的云雀只贴着地面断断续续地啁啾，飞起来也像叶子似的被风赶着走。太阳沉入一团血污般的乱云，月亮也掩了。据人说，那道光是金色的，像一道从天上射下的光柱，并不均匀，而是满布着蜿蜒的闪光，宛如剑影翻飞。它只持续了一瞬，留下黑沉沉、模糊糊的夜。*《自然》*上登过几封关于它的信，还有一幅谁都说画得不像的草图。（那幅不像的图，诸位可以亲自去看看——在该刊第 260 卷（CCLX）第 42 页上。）

===Original===

None in Sidderford saw the light, but Annie, Hooker Durgan’s wife, was lying awake, and she saw the reflection of it﻿—a flickering tongue of gold﻿—dancing on the wall.

She, too, was one of those who heard the sound. The others who heard the sound were Lumpy Durgan, the half-wit, and Amory’s mother. They said it was a sound like children singing and a throbbing of harp strings, carried on a rush of notes like that which sometimes comes from an organ. It began and ended like the opening and shutting of a door, and before and after they heard nothing but the night wind howling over the moor and the noise of the caves under Sidderford cliff. Amory’s mother said she wanted to cry when she heard it, but Lumpy was only sorry he could hear no more.

===Chinese===

锡德福德没有人看见那光，可是胡克·德根（Hooker Durgan）的妻子安妮醒着躺在床上，看见了它的反光——一条闪动的金色火舌——在墙上舞。

她也是听见那声音的人之一。另外听见声音的，还有半傻子“块头”德根，和阿莫里的母亲。她们说，那声音像孩童歌唱，像琴弦震颤，随一阵奔涌的音符涌来，同管风琴里偶尔涌出的那种一样。它开始和结束，像一扇门开了又关上；在那之前和之后，她们听见的只有刮过荒野的夜风，和锡德福德崖下岩洞的轰鸣。阿莫里的母亲说，听见它时直想哭；“块头”却只遗憾没能再多听一会儿。

===Original===

That is as much as anyone can tell you of the Glare upon Sidderford Moor and the alleged music therewith. And whether these had any real connection with the Strange Bird whose history follows, is more than I can say. But I set it down here for reasons that will be more apparent as the story proceeds.

===Chinese===

关于锡德福德荒野上的那道光，还有传闻中伴随的音乐，人们所能告诉你的，也就这么多。这些同下面要讲的怪鸟究竟有无干系，我无从说起。不过我把它们记在这里，理由随着故事的展开自会分明。

## XLIII The Sea Cliff / 四十三 海崖

===Original===

The Angel lay upon the summit of the cliff above Bandram Bay, and stared out at the glittering sea. Sheer from under his elbows fell the cliff, five hundred and seven feet of it down to the datum line, and the seabirds eddied and soared below him. The upper part of the cliff was a greenish chalky rock, the lower two-thirds a warm red, marbled with gypsum bands, and from half-a-dozen places spurted jets of water, to fall in long cascades down its face. The swell frothed white on the flinty beach, and the water beyond where the shadows of an outstanding rock lay, was green and purple in a thousand tints and marked with streaks and flakes of foam. The air was full of sunlight and the tinkling of the little waterfalls and the slow soughing of the seas below. Now and then a butterfly flickered over the face of the cliff, and a multitude of sea birds perched and flew hither and thither.

The Angel lay with his crippled, shrivelled wings humped upon his back, watching the gulls and jackdaws and rooks, circling in the sunlight, soaring, eddying, sweeping down to the water or upward into the dazzling blue of the sky. Long the Angel lay there and watched them going to and fro on outspread wings. He watched, and as he watched them he remembered with infinite longing the rivers of starlight and the sweetness of the land from which he came. And a gull came gliding overhead, swiftly and easily, with its broad wings spreading white and fair against the blue. And suddenly a shadow came into the Angel’s eyes, the sunlight left them, he thought of his own crippled pinions, and put his face upon his arm and wept.

===Chinese===

天使卧在班德拉姆湾上方崖顶，凝望着波光粼粼的大海。悬崖从他肘下笔直坠落，直到基准线足有五百零七英尺，海鸟在他下方盘旋翱翔。崖的上段是一种泛绿的白垩岩，下段三分之二却是暖暖的红色，石膏的纹层染成大理石般的花纹，有五六处喷出股股泉水，顺着崖面泻成一道道长长的瀑布。涌浪在燧石海滩上翻起白沫，往外，一座凸礁的阴影躺卧之处，海水绿紫千变，缀着一绺绺、一片片的浪花。空气里满是阳光、小瀑布的叮咚，和底下海水的缓缓太息。时不时有一只蝴蝶掠过崖面，无数海鸟栖的栖、飞的飞，来来去去。

天使趴在那里，残废萎缩的翅膀拱在背上，望着海鸥、寒鸦和白嘴鸦在阳光里打旋，翱翔，回环，或俯冲下水，或直上炫目的蓝天。天使久久卧在那里，望着它们展翅来去。他望着，望着，心里怀着无尽的渴念，想起星光的河流，想起他来处那个国度的甜美。一只海鸥掠过头顶，轻捷自在，宽阔的翅膀衬着蓝天，白得好看。忽然，一片阴影落进天使的眼睛，阳光从那里退了出去；他想起自己那双残废的翅膀，把脸埋进臂弯，哭了。

===Original===

A woman who was walking along the footpath across the Cliff Field saw only a twisted hunchback dressed in the Vicar of Siddermorton’s cast-off clothes, sprawling foolishly at the edge of the cliff and with his forehead on his arm. She looked at him and looked again. “The silly creature has gone to sleep,” she said, and though she had a heavy basket to carry, came towards him with an idea of waking him up. But as she drew near she saw his shoulders heave and heard the sound of his sobbing.

She stood still a minute, and her features twitched into a kind of grin. Then treading softly she turned and went back towards the pathway. “ ’Tis so hard to think of anything to say,” she said. “Poor afflicted soul!”

===Chinese===

一个沿着崖顶田间小径走来的女人，看见的只是一个穿锡德莫顿教区牧师旧衣裳的歪扭驼子，傻摊在悬崖边上，额头枕着胳膊。她看了看，又回头看了一眼。“这傻家伙睡着了，”她说；虽然拎着沉甸甸的篮子，还是朝他走过去，想叫醒他。可走近了，她看见他的肩膀在耸动，听见了他的啜泣声。

她呆立了一分钟，五官抽动成一个近似龇牙的表情。随后她放轻脚步，转身回到小径上去了。“就是想不出说什么话好，”她说。“可怜的受苦人！”

===Original===

Presently the Angel ceased sobbing, and stared with a tear-stained face at the beach below him.

“This world,” he said, “wraps me round and swallows me up. My wings grow shrivelled and useless. Soon I shall be nothing more than a crippled man, and I shall age, and bow myself to pain, and die.﻿ ﻿… I am miserable. And I am alone.”

===Chinese===

后来天使止住了哭，用一张泪痕斑斑的脸呆望着脚下的海滩。

“这个世界，”他说，“把我层层裹住，把我吞没。我的翅膀日渐干缩、无用。要不了多久，我就不过是个残废的人了，我会衰老，会向痛苦低头，会死。……我苦。而且我孤单。”

===Original===

Then he rested his chin on his hands upon the edge of the cliff, and began to think of Delia’s face with the light in her eyes. The Angel felt a curious desire to go to her and tell her of his withered wings. To place his arms about her and weep for the land he had lost. “Delia!” he said to himself very softly. And presently a cloud drove in front of the sun.

===Chinese===

随后他把下巴搁在崖边的手背上，开始想迪莉娅的脸，和她眼睛里的光。天使生出一股古怪的愿望，想去她跟前，对她说说他这对枯萎的翅膀。想搂住她，为他失去的国度一哭。“迪莉娅！”他极轻地对自己说。不一会儿，一片云赶到了太阳前面。

## XXXIX The Trouble of the Barbed Wire (Continued) / 三十九 铁蒺藜之患（续）

===Original===

The Vicar’s table-talk at dinner that night, after the Angel had stated his case, was full of grim explanations, prisons, madness.

“It’s too late to tell the truth about you now,” said the Vicar. “Besides, that’s impossible. I really do not know what to say. We must face our circumstances, I suppose. I am so undecided﻿—so torn. It’s the two worlds. If your Angelic world were only a dream, or if *this* world were only a dream﻿—or if I could believe either or both dreams, it would be all right with me. But here is a real Angel and a real summons﻿—how to reconcile them I do not know. I must talk to Gotch.﻿ ﻿… But he won’t understand. Nobody will understand.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

那天晚饭桌上，天使说明了自己的事由之后，教区牧师的席间谈话里，满是冷冰冰的解说、监狱和疯狂。

“如今再对人说你的真相，已经太迟了，”教区牧师说。“再说，那也办不到。我实在不知道该怎么说才好。我想，咱们只好面对眼下的处境。我这样举棋不定——这样撕扯。这是两个世界啊。倘若你的天使世界只是一场梦，或者*这个*世界只是一场梦——或者我能信其中一个是梦、或者两个都是梦，我倒好办了。可偏偏是一位真实的天使，和一张真实的传票——怎样调和它们，我不知道。我得同戈奇谈一谈。……可他不会懂的。谁也不会懂。……”

===Original===

“I am putting you to terrible inconvenience, I am afraid. My appalling unworldliness﻿—”

“It’s not you,” said the Vicar. “It’s not you. I perceive you have brought something strange and beautiful into my life. It’s not you. It’s myself. If I had more faith either way. If I could believe entirely in this world, and call you an Abnormal Phenomenon, as Crump does. But no. Terrestrial Angelic, Angelic Terrestrial.﻿ ﻿… Seesaw.”

===Chinese===

“我怕是给你添了可怕的麻烦。我这骇人的不谙世事——”

“不是你，”教区牧师说。“不是你。我看得出，你给我生命里带来的是某种奇特而美好的东西。不是你。是我自己。假如我在这两头里多有一点信。假如我能全然信这个世界，像克伦普那样，管你叫一个反常现象。可是不。属地的属天的，属天的属地的。……跷跷板。”

===Original===

“Still, Gotch is certain to be disagreeable, *most* disagreeable. He always is. It puts me into his hands. He is a bad moral influence, I know. Drinking. Gambling. Worse. Still, one must render unto Caesar the things that are Caesar’s. And he is against Disestablishment.﻿ ﻿…”

Then the Vicar would revert to the social collapse of the afternoon. “You are so very fundamental, you know,” he said﻿—several times.

===Chinese===

“话说回来，戈奇准要闹得不愉快，*极其*不愉快。他一向如此。这把我交到他手心里了。我知道他是个坏道德影响。酗酒。赌钱。还有更糟的。可该撒的物总当归给该撒。再说他反对政教分离。……”

说着教区牧师又回到下午那场社会性崩塌上来。“你实在是太根本了，你晓得，”这话他说了好几遍。

===Original===

The Angel went to his own room puzzled but very depressed. Every day the world had frowned darker upon him and his angelic ways. He could see how the trouble affected the Vicar, yet he could not imagine how he could avert it. It was all so strange and unreasonable. Twice again, too, he had been pelted out of the village.

He found the violin lying on his bed where he had laid it before dinner. And taking it up he began to play to comfort himself. But now he played no delicious vision of the Angelic Land. The iron of the world was entering into his soul. For a week now he had known pain and rejection, suspicion and hatred; a strange new spirit of revolt was growing up in his heart. He played a melody, still sweet and tender as those of the Angelic Land, but charged with a new note, the note of human sorrow and effort, now swelling into something like defiance, dying now into a plaintive sadness. He played softly, playing to himself to comfort himself, but the Vicar heard, and all his finite bothers were swallowed up in a hazy melancholy, a melancholy that was quite remote from sorrow. And besides the Vicar, the Angel had another hearer of whom neither Angel nor Vicar was thinking.

===Chinese===

天使回自己房里去，满心困惑，又万分消沉。这个世界对他的天使做派，一天比一天皱紧眉头。他看得出这桩烦恼怎样折磨着教区牧师，却想不出怎样才能避开它。这一切太古怪、太没道理了。而且，他又两次叫人从村子里轰了出来。

他发现小提琴躺在晚饭前搁下的床上。他拿起来，开始拉琴宽慰自己。可这一回，他拉的不再是天使之国那种甜美的幻景。这个世界的铁，正渗进他的灵魂。一个星期以来，他尝遍了疼痛与排斥、猜疑与仇恨；一种陌生的、新的反叛精神，正在他心里生长起来。他奏出一支旋律，依旧像天使之国的曲调那样甜美温柔，却载着一个新的音符——人间悲苦与挣扎的音符，时而涨成近乎挑战的东西，时而落成哀婉的忧伤。他轻轻地拉着，拉给自己听，好宽慰自己；可教区牧师听见了，他那点有限的烦恼，被一片朦胧的忧郁吞没了——那忧郁，又同悲伤相去很远。而在教区牧师之外，天使还有一位听者，天使和牧师谁都没有想到的听者。

## XXXVIII The Trouble of the Barbed Wire / 三十八 铁蒺藜之患

===Original===

So, ingloriously, ended the Angel’s first and last appearance in Society. Vicar and Angel returned to the Vicarage; crestfallen black figures in the bright sunlight, going dejectedly. The Angel, deeply pained that the Vicar was pained. The Vicar, dishevelled and desperate, intercalating spasmodic remorse and apprehension with broken explanations of the Theory of Etiquette. “They do *not* understand,” said the Vicar over and over again. “They will all be so very much aggrieved. I do not know what to say to them. It is all so confused, so perplexing.” And at the gate of the Vicarage, at the very spot where Delia had first seemed beautiful, stood Horrocks the village constable, awaiting them. He held coiled up about his hand certain short lengths of barbed wire.

“Good evening, Horrocks,” said the Vicar as the constable held the gate open.

“Evenin’, Sir,” said Horrocks, and added in a kind of mysterious undertone, “*Could* I speak to you a minute, Sir?”

===Chinese===

就这样，不光彩地结束了天使在社交界的头一次也是最后一次露面。教区牧师同天使往牧师公馆走回；明晃晃的太阳底下两个垂头丧气的黑影，怏怏地走着。天使，为教区牧师的痛而深深作痛。教区牧师，蓬头垢面、绝望已极，一阵阵的懊悔与忧惧之间，夹着断断续续的礼规理论讲解。“他们*实在*不明白，”教区牧师翻来覆去地说。“他们全会大大地见怪。我不知道该怎么向他们交代。一切这么乱，这么叫人糊涂。”而牧师公馆门口，正在迪莉娅当初头一回显得美丽的那个地点，站着村里的巡警霍罗克斯（Horrocks），正等着他们。他手里盘着几小卷铁蒺藜。

“晚上好，霍罗克斯，”巡警替他们拉开大门时，教区牧师说。

“晚上好，牧师，”霍罗克斯说，又用一种神秘的低声补了一句，“*能不能*借一步说话，牧师？”

===Original===

“Certainly,” said the Vicar. The Angel walked on thoughtfully to the house, and meeting Delia in the hall stopped her and cross-examined her at length over differences between servants and ladies.

“You’ll excuse my taking the liberty, Sir,” said Horrocks, “but there’s trouble brewin’ for that crippled gent you got stayin’ here.”

“Bless me!” said the Vicar. “You don’t say so!”

===Chinese===

“当然可以，”教区牧师说。天使若有所思地径自朝宅子走去，在门厅里迎面碰上迪莉娅，拦住她细细盘问仆人和女士之间的种种区别。

“恕我斗胆，牧师，”霍罗克斯说，“您留宿的那位瘸腿先生，要有麻烦上身了。”

“天哪！”教区牧师说。“不会吧！”

===Original===

“Sir John Gotch, Sir. He’s very angry indeed, Sir. His language, Sir﻿—. But I felt bound to tell you, Sir. He’s certain set on taking out a summons on account of that there barbed wire. Certain set, Sir, he is.”

“Sir John Gotch!” said the Vicar. “Wire! I don’t understand.”

“He asked me to find out who did it. Course I’ve had to do my duty, Sir. Naturally a disagreeable one.”

===Chinese===

“是约翰·戈奇爵士，牧师。他实在气坏了，牧师。他那个话呀，牧师——。可我觉得不能不告诉您。为着那段铁蒺藜的事，他一门心思要递传票了。铁了心了，牧师，铁了心。”

“约翰·戈奇爵士！”教区牧师说。“铁丝！我不明白。”

“他要我查出是谁干的。我自然只好例行公事，牧师。这种差事自然不讨喜。”

===Original===

“Barbed wire! Duty! I don’t understand you, Horrocks.”

“I’m afraid, Sir, there’s no denying the evidence. I’ve made careful enquiries, Sir.” And forthwith the constable began telling the Vicar of a new and terrible outrage committed by the Angelic visitor.

But we need not follow that explanation in detail﻿—or the subsequent confession. (For my own part I think there is nothing more tedious than dialogue). It gave the Vicar a new view of the Angelic character, a vignette of the Angelic indignation. A shady lane, sun-mottled, sweet hedges full of honeysuckle and vetch on either side, and a little girl gathering flowers, forgetful of the barbed wire which, all along the Sidderford Road, fenced in the dignity of Sir John Gotch from “bounders” and the detested “million.” Then suddenly a gashed hand, a bitter outcry, and the Angel sympathetic, comforting, inquisitive. Explanations sob-set, and then﻿—altogether novel phenomenon in the Angelic career﻿—*passion*. A furious onslaught upon the barbed wire of Sir John Gotch, barbed wire recklessly handled, slashed, bent and broken. Yet the Angel acted without personal malice﻿—saw in the thing only an ugly and vicious plant that trailed insidiously among its fellows. Finally the Angel’s explanations gave the Vicar a picture of the Angel alone amidst his destruction, trembling and amazed at the sudden force, not himself, that had sprung up within him, and set him striking and cutting. Amazed, too, at the crimson blood that trickled down his fingers.

===Chinese===

“铁蒺藜！公事！我听不懂你的话，霍罗克斯。”

“恐怕，牧师，证据赖不掉了。我作了仔细的查访，牧师。”于是巡警开始对教区牧师讲起这位天上来客新犯下的一桩可怕暴行。

这段解说——还有随后的招认——我们不必细追。（就我个人而言，我想不出比对话更乏味的东西了。）这事让教区牧师对天使的品性有了新的认识，得窥天使之怒的一幅小像。一条背阴的小巷，日影斑驳，两旁甜香的树篱开满金银花和野豌豆，一个小女孩采着花，全然忘了那道铁蒺藜——它一路沿着锡德福德大道，护卫着约翰·戈奇爵士的尊严，防着“暴发户”和可恨的“平民”。随后忽然一只割破的手，一声凄厉的哭喊，天使又同情、又抚慰、又追问。泣不成声的解释，然后是——天使生涯中全新的一种现象——*激愤*。对着约翰·戈奇爵士的铁蒺藜一场狂怒的猛攻：铁蒺藜遭了殃，被乱撕、乱掰、扭断折断。然而天使的举动并无私人恶意——在他眼里，那不过是一株丑恶歹毒的藤蔓，阴险地缠绕在同类中间。末了，天使的解释给了教区牧师一幅图景：天使独自站在他的破坏物中间，颤抖着，惊异于那股不是他自己的、蓦然从心里蹿起来逼他劈砍的力量。也惊异于顺着他手指淌下来的、绯红的血。

===Original===

“It is still more horrible,” said the Angel when the Vicar explained the artificial nature of the thing. “If I had seen the man who put this silly-cruel stuff there to hurt little children, I know I should have tried to inflict pain upon him. I have never felt like this before. I am indeed becoming tainted and coloured altogether by the wickedness of this world.”

“To think, too, that you men should be so foolish as to uphold the laws that let a man do such spiteful things. Yes﻿—I know; you will say it has to be so. For some remoter reason. That is a thing that only makes me angrier. Why cannot an act rest on its own merits?﻿ ﻿… As it does in the Angelic Land.”

That was the incident the history of which the Vicar now gradually learnt, getting the bare outline from Horrocks, the colour and emotion subsequently from the Angel. The thing had happened the day before the musical festival at Siddermorton House.

===Chinese===

“这就更加可怕了，”等教区牧师解释了这东西的人造性质之后，天使说。“倘若叫我看见那个把这蠢毒玩意儿安上去伤害小孩子的人，我知道我准会想法叫他也尝尝痛。我从前从来没有过这样的感觉。我当真是在被这个世界的邪恶玷污、浸染了。”

“再想到你们这些人竟这样糊涂，维护那种听任一个人干这种恶毒事的法律。是——我知道；你会说，不得不如此。为了某个更远的道理。这种话只会叫我更怒。为什么一件事不能就凭它本身的是非立身？……在天使之国，事情就是这样的。”

教区牧师如今渐渐知悉的这段事故始末，便是这么一回事：骨架得自霍罗克斯，血色与情感随后得自天使。事情出在锡德莫顿宅音乐盛会的前一天。

===Original===

“Have you told Sir John who did it?” asked the Vicar. “And are you sure?”

“Quite sure, Sir. There can be no doubting it was your gentleman, Sir. I’ve not told Sir John yet, Sir. But I shall have to tell Sir John this evening. Meaning no offence to you, Sir, as I hopes you’ll see. It’s my duty, Sir. Besides which﻿—”

“Of course,” said the Vicar, hastily. “Certainly it’s your duty. And what will Sir John do?”

===Chinese===

“你告诉约翰爵士是谁干的了吗？”教区牧师问。“你拿得准吗？”

“拿得死准，牧师。错不了，是您那位先生干的，牧师。我还没告诉约翰爵士，牧师。可今晚我就不得不告诉了。望您体谅，牧师，我无意冒犯。这是我的职责，牧师。再说了——”

“当然，”教区牧师忙说。“这当然是你的职责。约翰爵士会怎么样呢？”

===Original===

“He’s dreadful set against the person who did it﻿—destroying property like that﻿—and sort of slapping his arrangements in the face.”

Pause. Horrocks made a movement. The Vicar, tie almost at the back of his neck now, a most unusual thing for him, stared blankly at his toes.

“I thought I’d tell you, Sir,” said Horrocks.

===Chinese===

“他对干这事的人恨透了——这样毁人财物——简直是扇他安排的脸面。”

停顿。霍罗克斯动了一下。教区牧师——领带这会儿几乎跑到后脖颈去了，这在他可是破天荒——呆呆地盯着自己的脚尖。

“我想着还是先告诉您一声，牧师，”霍罗克斯说。

===Original===

“Yes,” said the Vicar. “Thanks, Horrocks, thanks!” He scratched the back of his head. “You might perhaps﻿ ﻿… I think it’s the best way﻿ ﻿… Quite sure Mr. Angel did it?”

“Sherlock ’Omes, Sir, couldn’t be cocksurer.”

“Then I’d better give you a little note to the Squire.”

===Chinese===

“是啊，”教区牧师说。“多谢，霍罗克斯，多谢！”他挠了挠后脑勺。“你兴许可以……我想这是最好的办法……天使先生干的事，十拿九稳？”

“歇洛克·福尔摩斯来，牧师，也不能更有把握了。”

“那我最好给乡绅写个便条让你带去。”

## VII The Vicar and the Angel (Continued) / 七 教区牧师与天使（续）

===Original===

Now in the land of the Angels, so the Vicar learnt in the course of many conversations, there is neither pain nor trouble nor death, marrying nor giving in marriage, birth nor forgetting. Only at times new things begin. It is a land without hill or dale, a wonderfully level land, glittering with strange buildings, with incessant sunlight or full moon, and with incessant breezes blowing through the Aeolian traceries of the trees. It is Wonderland, with glittering seas hanging in the sky, across which strange fleets go sailing, none know whither. There the flowers glow in Heaven and the stars shine about one’s feet and the breath of life is a delight. The land goes on forever﻿—there is no solar system nor interstellar space such as there is in our universe﻿—and the air goes upward past the sun into the uttermost abyss of their sky. And there is nothing but Beauty there﻿—all the beauty in our art is but feeble rendering of faint glimpses of that wonderful world, and our composers, our original composers, are those who hear, however faintly, the dust of melody that drives before its winds. And the Angels, and wonderful monsters of bronze and marble and living fire, go to and fro therein.

It is a land of law﻿—for whatever is, is under the law﻿—but its laws all, in some strange way, differ from ours. Their geometry is different because their space has a curve in it so that all their planes are cylinders; and their law of gravitation is not according to the law of inverse squares, and there are four-and-twenty primary colours instead of only three. Most of the fantastic things of our science are commonplaces there, and all our earthly science would seem to them the maddest dreaming. There are no flowers upon their plants, for instance, but jets of coloured fire. That, of course, will seem mere nonsense to you because you do not understand most of what the Angel told the Vicar, indeed the Vicar could not realise, because his own experiences, being only of this world of matter, warred against his understanding. It was too strange to imagine.

===Chinese===

天使的国度里——教区牧师在多次长谈中陆续得知——没有痛，没有烦恼，没有死，没有嫁娶，没有出生，也没有遗忘。只是不时地，有新的事物开始。那是一片无丘无谷的国土，一片奇异地平坦的国土，闪烁着种种奇异的建筑，悬着不落的太阳或满月，阵阵清风不断吹过树木的风神花纹。那是仙境，天上悬着波光闪闪的海，一支支古怪的船队扬帆驶过，驶向无人知晓的远方。在那里，花在天上放光，星在脚边闪耀，生命的呼吸本身就是欢悦。那片国土绵延无尽——没有我们宇宙里的太阳系，也没有星际空间——空气一路向上，越过太阳，升进他们天空的最深的深渊。那里只有美——我们艺术中一切的美，不过是那个奇妙世界惊鸿一瞥的微弱摹写；我们的作曲家，我们真正的作曲家，就是那些隐约听得见随风扬起的旋律之尘的人。而天使们，还有青铜、大理石和活火铸成的奇异巨兽，就在其中来来往往。

那是一片有法则的国土——因为凡存在的，皆受法则辖制——只是它的法则，都以某种奇异的方式，同我们的不同。他们的几何不同，因为他们的空间里带着曲率，所以他们所有的平面都是圆柱面；他们的引力定律不遵从平方反比；他们的原色有二十四种，而不是三种。我们科学里种种稀奇的东西，在那里多半是家常便饭，而我们全部尘世的科学，在他们看来准是最疯癫的梦呓。比如，他们的植物不开花，喷的是彩色的火。这在你听来当然纯属胡话，因为天使讲给教区牧师听的话，你多半并不明白；说真的，连教区牧师也无法领会，因为他自己的经验纯是这一个物质世界的，同他的理解力打架。那太奇怪了，无从想象。

===Original===

What had jolted these twin universes together so that the Angel had fallen suddenly into Sidderford, neither the Angel nor the Vicar could tell. Nor for the matter of that could the author of this story. The author is concerned with the facts of the case, and has neither the desire nor the confidence to explain them. Explanations are the fallacy of a scientific age. And the cardinal fact of the case is this, that out in Siddermorton Park, with the glory of some wonderful world where there is neither sorrow nor sighing, still clinging to him, on the 4th of August 1895, stood an Angel, bright and beautiful, talking to the Vicar of Siddermorton about the plurality of worlds. The author will swear to the Angel, if need be; and there he draws the line.

===Chinese===

究竟是什么把这一对孪生的宇宙撞到了一处，叫天使骤然跌进了锡德福德，天使说不上，教区牧师也说不上。说来，本故事的作者也说不上。作者只管这桩案子的事实，既无意、也没有把握去解释它们。解释，本是科学时代的谬见。而这桩案子的首要事实是：在锡德莫顿园外，1895 年 8 月 4 日，站着一位天使，身上还披着某个既无忧愁也无叹息的奇妙世界的光辉，光艳照人，正同锡德莫顿的教区牧师谈论着世界的多元问题。必要的话，作者可以为这位天使起誓；也仅止于此。

## VI The Vicar and the Angel / 六 教区牧师与天使

===Original===

Now there are some things frankly impossible. The weakest intellect will admit this situation is impossible. The *Athenaeum* will probably say as much should it venture to review this. Sunbespattered ferns, spreading beech trees, the Vicar and the gun are acceptable enough. But this Angel is a different matter. Plain sensible people will scarcely go on with such an extravagant book. And the Vicar fully appreciated this impossibility. But he lacked decision. Consequently he went on with it, as you shall immediately hear. He was hot, it was after dinner, he was in no mood for mental subtleties. The Angel had him at a disadvantage, and further distracted him from the main issue by irrelevant iridescence and a violent fluttering. For the moment it never occurred to the Vicar to ask whether the Angel was possible or not. He accepted him in the confusion of the moment, and the mischief was done. Put yourself in his place, my dear *Athenaeum*. You go out shooting. You hit something. That alone would disconcert you. You find you have hit an Angel, and he writhes about for a minute and then sits up and addresses you. He makes no apology for his own impossibility. Indeed, he carries the charge clean into your camp. “A man!” he says, pointing. “A man in the maddest black clothes and without a feather upon him. Then I was not deceived. I am indeed in the Land of Dreams!” You *must* answer him. Unless you take to your heels. Or blow his brains out with your second barrel as an escape from the controversy.

“The Land of Dreams! Pardon me if I suggest you have just come out of it,” was the Vicar’s remark.

“How can that be?” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

世上有种种事情，坦白说是不可能的。最不济的智力也会承认眼下这个局面不可能。*《雅典娜神殿》*（Athenaeum）倘使斗胆评论本书，多半也会这么说。洒满阳光的蕨丛、铺展的山毛榉、教区牧师和那支枪，都还说得过去。可这位天使就是另一回事了。明白事理的正派读者，恐怕不会把这样一部铺张的书读下去。而教区牧师对这种不可能也深有领会。偏偏他缺少决断。于是他照样把这事儿进行下去了，你们马上就会听到。他浑身燥热，又是晚饭之后，全无心思琢磨什么微妙的哲理。天使占了他的上风，又用不相干的虹彩和一阵猛烈的扑腾，岔开了他对正题的注意。一时间，教区牧师压根儿没想到要问一问这位天使究竟可不可能。他在慌乱中把他照单全收了，祸事就此铸成。设身处地想一想吧，我亲爱的*《雅典娜神殿》*。你出门打猎。你打中了什么东西。单这一条就够你狼狈的。你发觉自己打中的是一位天使，他翻扭了一阵，随即坐起来同你搭话。他对自己这件不可能的事没有半句歉意。说真的，他倒把这记炮弹干干净净直轰进你的大营。“一个人！”他指着你说，“一个穿着最疯癫的黑衣裳、身上连一根羽毛也没有的人。这么说，我没有受骗。我当真是在梦之国了！”你*必须*回答他。除非你掉头就跑。要么拿第二管枪轰掉他的脑袋，好从这场论争里脱身。

“梦之国！恕我直言，您倒是刚从那里头出来的，”教区牧师这样应答。

“这怎么可能？”天使说。

===Original===

“Your wing,” said the Vicar, “is bleeding. Before we talk, may I have the pleasure﻿—the melancholy pleasure﻿—of tying it up? I am really most sincerely sorry.﻿ ﻿…” The Angel put his hand behind his back and winced.

The Vicar assisted his victim to stand up. The Angel turned gravely and the Vicar, with numberless insignificant panting parentheses, carefully examined the injured wings. (They articulated, he observed with interest, to a kind of second glenoid on the outer and upper edge of the shoulder blade. The left wing had suffered little except the loss of some of the primary wing-quills, and a shot or so in the *ala spuria*, but the humerus bone of the right was evidently smashed.) The Vicar stanched the bleeding as well as he could and tied up the bone with his pocket handkerchief and the neck wrap his housekeeper made him carry in all weathers.

“I’m afraid you will not be able to fly for some time,” said he, feeling the bone.

===Chinese===

“您的翅膀，”教区牧师说，“在流血。谈话之前，可否赏光——赏这悲哀的光——让我替您把它包上？我实在万分抱歉。……”天使把手伸到背后，缩了一下。

教区牧师扶他的伤者站起来。天使庄重地转过身，教区牧师一面喘着气插入无数无关紧要的旁白，一面仔细检查那对受伤的翅膀。（它们铰接在肩胛外上缘的一种第二肩臼上——这一点他看得颇有兴味。左翅伤得轻，只掉了些初级拨风羽，翼状小翼上中了一两粒霰弹，右翅的肱骨却分明是打碎了。）教区牧师尽力止住血，用他的手帕、加上管家嘱咐他晴雨不离身的那条围脖，把骨头捆扎了起来。

“恐怕有一阵子您飞不成了，”他摸着骨头说。

===Original===

“I don’t like this new sensation,” said the Angel.

“The pain when I feel your bone?”

“The *what*?” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“我不喜欢这个新感觉，”天使说。

“我摸您骨头时的痛吗？”

“*什么*？”天使说。

===Original===

“The pain.”

“ ‘Pain’﻿—you call it. No, I certainly don’t like the Pain. Do you have much of this Pain in the Land of Dreams?”

“A very fair share,” said the Vicar. “Is it new to you?”

===Chinese===

“痛。”

“‘痛’——你们这么叫它。不，我自然不喜欢这个痛。在梦之国里，你们有很多这种痛吗？”

“份额不小，”教区牧师说。“您从前没有过？”

===Original===

“Quite,” said the Angel. “I don’t like it.”

“How curious!” said the Vicar, and bit at the end of a strip of linen to tie a knot. “I think this bandaging must serve for the present,” he said. “I’ve studied ambulance work before, but never the bandaging up of wing wounds. Is your pain any better?”

“It glows now instead of flashing,” said the Angel.

===Chinese===

“从没有，”天使说。“我不喜欢它。”

“真稀奇！”教区牧师说，咬住一条绷带布的头好打结。“我看这包扎暂且只能这样了，”他说。“急救这门功课我从前学过，只是从没包扎过翅膀上的伤。您的痛好些了吗？”

“现在是发着热，不再是打着闪了，”天使说。

===Original===

“I am afraid you will find it glow for some time,” said the Vicar, still intent on the wound.

The Angel gave a shrug of the wing and turned round to look at the Vicar again. He had been trying to keep an eye on the Vicar over his shoulder during all their interview. He looked at him from top to toe with raised eyebrows and a growing smile on his beautiful soft-featured face. “It seems so odd,” he said with a sweet little laugh, “to be talking to a Man!”

“Do you know,” said the Vicar, “now that I come to think of it, it is equally odd to me that I should be talking to an Angel. I am a somewhat matter-of-fact person. A Vicar has to be. Angels I have always regarded as﻿—artistic conceptions﻿—”

===Chinese===

“恐怕它还要发好一阵热呢，”教区牧师说，仍旧专心对着伤口。

天使耸了耸翅膀，回过头来再打量教区牧师。方才会谈的整个过程中，他一直想从肩膀上头用眼角瞄住教区牧师。此刻他扬着眉毛，那张俊美温柔的脸上一笑渐浓，把他从头到脚看了一遍。“同一个男人说话，”他甜甜地轻笑一声说，“感觉真是说不出的古怪！”

“您可知道，”教区牧师说，“细想起来，同一个天使说话，于我同样古怪。我这人多少是讲究实际的。做教区牧师的不得不如此。天使嘛，我一向看作——艺术上的构想——”

===Original===

“Exactly what we think of men.”

“But surely you have seen so many men﻿—”

“Never before today. In pictures and books, times enough of course. But I have seen several since the sunrise, solid real men, besides a horse or so﻿—those Unicorn things you know, without horns﻿—and quite a number of those grotesque knobby things called ‘cows.’ I was naturally a little frightened at so many mythical monsters, and came to hide here until it was dark. I suppose it will be dark again presently like it was at first. *Phew!* This Pain of yours is poor fun. I hope I shall wake up directly.”

===Chinese===

“我们对男人正是这么想的。”

“可您想必见过许许多多男人——”

“今天以前从没见过。图画里、书本里，自然见得多了。可自日出以来，我见了几个活生生、实打实的男人，外加一两匹马——就是你们那种独角兽，没长角的——还有好些叫作‘母牛’的疙疙瘩瘩的怪物。一下子见着这么多神话里的怪兽，我自然有点害怕，就躲到这里来，等天黑。想必过一会儿天又会像起初那样黑下去。*嚯！*你们这个痛可真没趣味。但愿我马上就醒。”

===Original===

“I don’t understand quite,” said the Vicar, knitting his brows and tapping his forehead with his flat hand. “Mythical monster!” The worst thing he had been called for years hitherto was a “medieval anachronism” (by an advocate of Disestablishment). “Do I understand that you consider me as﻿—as something in a dream?”

“Of course,” said the Angel smiling.

“And this world about me, these rugged trees and spreading fronds﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我不大明白，”教区牧师皱起眉，用手掌拍着脑门说。“神话里的怪兽！”多少年来，人家加给他最难听的字眼，不过是一句“中世纪的错时代人物”（出自一位政教分离的鼓吹者）。“我没理解错吧，您把我看作——看作一场梦里的什么东西？”

“当然，”天使微笑着说。

“那我四周的这个世界，这些虬曲的树、铺展的蕨叶——”

===Original===

“Is all so *very* dreamlike,” said the Angel. “Just exactly what one dreams of﻿—or artists imagine.”

“You have artists then among the Angels?”

“All kinds of artists, Angels with wonderful imaginations, who invent men and cows and eagles and a thousand impossible creatures.”

===Chinese===

“全都*非常*像梦，”天使说。“恰恰是人梦见的那些——或者画家们想象的那些。”

“这么说，天使们中间也有画家？”

“什么样的艺术家都有，天使里有想象力奇妙得很的，专门发明男人、母牛、鹰，还有千百种不可能的生物。”

===Original===

“Impossible creatures!” said the Vicar.

“Impossible creatures,” said the Angel. “Myths.”

“But I’m real!” said the Vicar. “I assure you I’m real.”

===Chinese===

“不可能的生物！”教区牧师说。

“不可能的生物，”天使说。“神话。”

“可我是真实的！”教区牧师说。“我向您担保，我是真实的。”

===Original===

The Angel shrugged his wings and winced and smiled. “I can always tell when I am dreaming,” he said.

“*You*﻿—dreaming,” said the Vicar. He looked round him.

“*You* dreaming!” he repeated. His mind worked diffusely.

===Chinese===

天使耸耸翅膀，缩了一下，又笑了。“我做梦的时候，向来分辨得出，”他说。

“*您*——在做梦，”教区牧师说。他环顾四周。

“*您*在做梦！”他又说了一遍。他的脑筋四下漫散地转着。

===Original===

He held out his hand with all his fingers moving. “I have it!” he said. “I begin to see.” A really brilliant idea was dawning upon his mind. He had not studied mathematics at Cambridge for nothing, after all. “Tell me please. Some animals of *your* world﻿ ﻿… of the Real World, real animals you know.”

“Real animals!” said the Angel smiling. “Why﻿—there’s Griffins and Dragons﻿—and Jabberwocks﻿—and Cherubim﻿—and Sphinxes﻿—and the Hippogriff﻿—and Mermaids﻿—and Satyrs﻿—and.﻿ ﻿…”

“Thank you,” said the Vicar as the Angel appeared to be warming to his work; “thank you. That is *quite* enough. I begin to understand.”

===Chinese===

他伸出手来，手指个个动着。“明白了！”他说。“我开始懂了。”一个真正灿烂的念头正在他脑海里升起。他在剑桥的数学终究没有白学。“请告诉我。*您*那个世界的几种动物……真实世界的，您晓得，真实的动物。”

“真实的动物！”天使笑着说。“这个——有狮鹫，有龙——有炸脖龙——有基路伯——有斯芬克斯——有鹰马——有美人鱼——有萨提——还有。……”

“多谢，”眼看天使越数越来劲，教区牧师赶忙说，“多谢。*尽*够了。我开始明白了。”

===Original===

He paused for a moment, his face pursed up. “Yes﻿ ﻿… I begin to see it.”

“See what?” asked the Angel.

“The Griffins and Satyrs and so forth. It’s as clear.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

他停了片刻，噘起脸。“是啊……我开始看出门道了。”

“看出什么？”天使问。

“狮鹫、萨提这一类。再清楚不过。……”

===Original===

“I don’t see them,” said the Angel.

“No, the whole point is they are not to be seen in this world. But our men with imaginations have told us all about them, you know. And even I at times﻿ ﻿… there are places in this village where you must simply take what they set before you, or give offence﻿—I, I say, have seen in my dreams Jabberwocks, Bogle brutes, Mandrakes.﻿ ﻿… From our point of view, you know, they are Dream Creatures.﻿ ﻿…”

“Dream Creatures!” said the Angel. “How singular! This is a very curious dream. A kind of topsy-turvey one. You call men real and angels a myth. It almost makes one think that in some odd way there must be two worlds as it were.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“我看不见它们，”天使说。

“对，妙就妙在它们在这个世界里是看不见的。可我们有想象力的人，把它们的一切都讲给我们听了，你晓得。连我有时也……这村里有些地方，人家端上来什么你就得用什么，不然就是失礼——我，我说的是我，就在梦里见过炸脖龙、妖怪、曼德拉草。……从我们的观点看，您晓得，它们是梦之生物。……”

“梦之生物！”天使说。“真稀奇！这是一场非常古怪的梦。一种颠颠倒倒的梦。你们管男人叫真实，管天使叫神话。这几乎让人想，冥冥之中，必定是有两个世界的。……”

===Original===

“At least two,” said the Vicar.

“Lying somewhere close together, and yet scarcely suspecting.﻿ ﻿…”

“As near as page to page of a book.”

===Chinese===

“至少两个，”教区牧师说。

“紧挨着躺在一处，却彼此毫不疑心。……”

“近得就像一册书的前后两页。”

===Original===

“Penetrating each other, living each its own life. This is really a delicious dream!”

“And never dreaming of each other.”

“Except when people go a-dreaming!”

===Chinese===

“彼此穿透，各自过各自的日子。这真是一场妙不可言的梦！”

“而且彼此梦不见对方。”

“除非有人做起了清醒的梦！”

===Original===

“Yes,” said the Angel thoughtfully. “It must be something of the sort. And that reminds me. Sometimes when I have been dropping asleep, or drowsing under the noontide sun, I have seen strange corrugated faces just like yours, going by me, and trees with green leaves upon them, and such queer uneven ground as this.﻿ ﻿… It must be so. I have fallen into another world.”

“Sometimes,” began the Vicar, “at bedtime, when I have been just on the edge of consciousness, I have seen faces as beautiful as yours, and the strange dazzling vistas of a wonderful scene, that flowed past me, winged shapes soaring over it, and wonderful﻿—sometimes terrible﻿—forms going to and fro. I have even heard sweet music too in my ears.﻿ ﻿… It may be that as we withdraw our attention from the world of sense, the pressing world about us, as we pass into the twilight of repose, other worlds.﻿ ﻿… Just as we see the stars, those other worlds in space, when the glare of day recedes.﻿ ﻿… And the artistic dreamers who see such things most clearly.﻿ ﻿…”

They looked at one another.

===Chinese===

“是啊，”天使若有所思地说。“必定是这么一回事。这倒提醒了我。有时，在我将睡未睡的时候，在正午的太阳底下打盹的时候，我见过一些怪模怪样、起瓦楞的脸，同你的一模一样，从我跟前过去，还有长着绿叶子的树，还有像这样坑坑洼洼的怪地。……必定是这么回事。我跌进了另一个世界。”

“有时候，”教区牧师开口说，“在就寝时分，意识将失未失的当口，我见过一些同您一样美的脸，见过一片奇景炫目的远景，从我身边流过，带翅膀的身影翱翔其上，还有种种奇异的——有时是可怖的——形体来来往往。我耳朵里甚至还听过甜美的音乐。……也许，当我们把注意力从感官的世界、从这围逼着我们的世界撤回，当我们沉入安眠的暮色，另一些世界就。……正如白昼的强光退去，我们便望见星斗——空间里那些别的世界。……而那些看得最真切的艺术的做梦者们。……”

两人对望了一眼。

===Original===

“And in some incomprehensible manner I have fallen into this world of yours out of my own!” said the Angel, “into the world of my dreams, grown real.”

He looked about him. “Into the world of my dreams.”

“It is confusing,” said the Vicar. “It almost makes one think there may be (ahem) four dimensions after all. In which case, of course,” he went on hurriedly﻿—for he loved geometrical speculations and took a certain pride in his knowledge of them﻿—“there may be any number of three-dimensional universes packed side by side, and all dimly dreaming of one another. There may be world upon world, universe upon universe. It’s perfectly possible. There’s nothing so incredible as the absolutely possible. But I wonder how you came to fall out of your world into mine.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“而我竟以某种不可理解的方式，从自己的世界跌进了你们这个世界！”天使说，“跌进了我的梦，还成了真的。”

他环顾四周。“跌进了我的梦。”

“这真把人搅糊涂了，”教区牧师说。“这几乎让人想，没准真有（咳咳）第四维。果真如此，当然，”他赶忙接下去——他素爱几何学的玄想，也颇以此自矜——“那就可能有不知多少个三维宇宙肩并肩挤在一处，都隐隐约约地彼此做着梦。可能世界之上还有世界，宇宙之上还有宇宙。完全可能。再没有比绝对可能的事更不可思议的了。只是我不明白，您怎么会从您的世界里跌出来，落进我的世界。……”

===Original===

“Dear me!” said the Angel; “There’s deer and a stag! Just as they draw them on the coats of arms. How grotesque it all seems! Can I really be awake?”

He rubbed his knuckles into his eyes.

The half-dozen of dappled deer came in Indian file obliquely through the trees and halted, watching. “It’s no dream﻿—I am really a solid concrete Angel, in Dream Land,” said the Angel. He laughed. The Vicar stood surveying him. The Reverend gentleman was pulling his mouth askew after a habit he had, and slowly stroking his chin. He was asking himself whether he too was not in the Land of Dreams.

===Chinese===

“哎呀！”天使说；“有鹿，还有一头针鹿！跟纹章上画的一模一样。这一切瞧着多么古怪！我难道真是醒着的吗？”

他用指节揉了揉眼睛。

五六头斑鹿排成单行斜穿过树间，停下来张望。“这不是梦——我实实在在是一位结结实实的天使，待在梦之国里，”天使说。他笑了。教区牧师站在那里端详他。这位可敬的绅士正照着他的老习惯把嘴歪向一边，缓缓摩挲着下巴。他心里正问自己：他自己是不是也在梦之国里。

## XXIII The Violin / 二十三 小提琴

===Original===

After breakfast the Vicar went into the little room next his study to find a book on Political Economy for the Angel to read. For the Angel’s social ignorances were clearly beyond any verbal explanations. The door stood ajar.

“What is that?” said the Angel, following him. “A violin!” He took it down.

“You play?” said the Vicar.

===Chinese===

早饭之后，教区牧师走进书房隔壁的小屋，想找一本政治经济学的书给天使读。因为天使对社会常识的无知，显然不是言语讲解所能济事的。门虚掩着。

“那是什么？”跟过来的天使说。“一把小提琴！”他把它取了下来。

“您会拉？”教区牧师说。

===Original===

The Angel had the bow in his hand, and by way of answer drove it across the strings. The quality of the note made the Vicar turn suddenly.

The Angel’s hand tightened on the instrument. The bow flew back and flickered, and an air the Vicar had never heard before danced in his ears. The Angel shifted the fiddle under his dainty chin and went on playing, and as he played his eyes grew bright and his lips smiled. At first he looked at the Vicar, then his expression became abstracted. He seemed no longer to look at the Vicar, but through him, at something beyond, something in his memory or his imagination, something infinitely remote, undreamt of hitherto.﻿ ﻿…

The Vicar tried to follow the music. The air reminded him of a flame, it rushed up, shone, flickered and danced, passed and reappeared. No!﻿—it did not reappear! Another air﻿—like it and unlike it, shot up after it, wavered, vanished. Then another, the same and not the same. It reminded him of the flaring tongues that palpitate and change above a newly lit fire. There are two airs﻿—or motifs, which is it?﻿—thought the Vicar. He knew remarkably little of musical technique. They go dancing up, one pursuing the other, out of the fire of the incantation, pursuing, fluctuating, turning, up into the sky. There below was the fire burning, a flame without fuel upon a level space, and there two flirting butterflies of sound, dancing away from it, up, one over another, swift, abrupt, uncertain.

===Chinese===

天使已经把弓拿在手里，权作回答，往弦上一拉。那音色叫教区牧师猛地回过头来。

天使的手在琴上扣紧了。琴弓飞回，闪烁跳跃，一支教区牧师从未听过的曲调在他耳边舞了起来。天使把琴换到他秀气的下颏底下，继续拉着；拉的时候，他的眼睛亮起来，唇边带上了笑。起初他还望着教区牧师，随后神情渐渐入定。他仿佛不再看教区牧师，而是透过他，看着更远的什么，他记忆里或想象中的什么，无限遥远、迄今梦也未梦见的什么。……

教区牧师想跟上那音乐。那曲调叫他想起一道火焰，蹿起来，发亮，闪烁，舞蹈，过去了，又回来。不！——它没有回来！另一支曲调——像它又不像它——追着它蹿上来，摇曳，消逝。接着又一支，是它又不是它。它叫他想起新点的火堆上那种跳动变幻的火舌。是两支曲调——还是两个动机，究竟是哪个？——教区牧师想。音乐技巧他懂得极少。它们舞着往上去，一个追一个，从咒法的火里出来，追逐着，起伏着，回旋着，升上天空。底下是燃着的火，一片平地上一柱无薪的火焰，那边是两只声音的蝴蝶，调笑着舞开去，向上，一只越过一只，迅疾，突兀，飘忽。

===Original===

“Flirting butterflies were they!” What was the Vicar thinking of? Where was he? In the little room next to his study, of course! And the Angel standing in front of him smiling into his face, playing the violin, and looking through him as though he was only a window﻿—. That motif again, a yellow flare, spread fanlike by a gust, and now one, then with a swift eddying upward flight the other, the two things of fire and light pursuing one another again up into that clear immensity.

The study and the realities of life suddenly faded out of the Vicar’s eyes, grew thinner and thinner like a mist that dissolves into air, and he and the Angel stood together on a pinnacle of wrought music, about which glittering melodies circled, and vanished, and reappeared. He was in the land of Beauty, and once more the glory of heaven was upon the Angel’s face, and the glowing delights of colour pulsated in his wings. Himself the Vicar could not see. But I cannot tell you of the vision of that great and spacious land, of its incredible openness, and height, and nobility. For there is no space there like ours, no time as we know it; one must needs speak by bungling metaphors and own in bitterness after all that one has failed. And it was only a vision. The wonderful creatures flying through the aether saw them not as they stood there, flew through them as one might pass through a whisp of mist. The Vicar lost all sense of duration, all sense of necessity﻿—

“Ah!” said the Angel, suddenly putting down the fiddle.

===Chinese===

“声音的蝴蝶调笑着！”教区牧师想到哪儿去了？他这是在哪儿？自然是在书房隔壁的小屋里！天使立在他面前，笑着对他弹奏，目光却穿透他，仿佛他只是一扇窗——。又是那个动机，一朵黄色的火苗，被一阵风兜头扇开，如今是这一只，随即另一只一个急旋向上，那两个火与光的小东西又彼此追逐着，升进那澄澈的无限里去。

书房和人生的种种实在，忽然从教区牧师眼前褪去，越褪越薄，像一霭融进空气的雾；他同天使并肩立在一座音乐筑成的峰巅上，四周闪亮的旋律盘旋着，消逝了，又出现。他置身在美的国度里，天堂的荣光重又落上天使的脸，彩色的光华在他的翅膀里搏动。教区牧师自己却看不见自己。可那片伟大而空阔的国土的景象，它那难以置信的开朗、高旷与高贵，我无法向你们描摹。因为那里没有我们这样的空间，没有我们所知的时间；人只能靠笨拙的譬喻说话，末了还得苦涩地承认，终究是失败了。何况那只是一场幻见。那些翔过太空的奇妙造物，看不见站在那里的他们，径直从他们身中穿过，如同穿过一缕薄雾。教区牧师丢了一切时长之感、一切必然之感——

“啊！”天使忽然放下琴，说。

===Original===

The Vicar had forgotten the book on Political Economy, had forgotten everything until the Angel had done. For a minute he sat quite still. Then he woke up with a start. He was sitting on the old iron-bound chest.

“Really,” he said slowly, “you are very clever.”

He looked about him in a puzzled way. “I had a kind of vision while you were playing. I seemed to see﻿—. What did I see? It has gone.”

He stood up with a dazzled expression upon his face. “I shall never play the violin again,” he said. “I wish you would take it to your room﻿—and keep it﻿—. And play to me again. I did not know anything of music until I heard you play. I do not feel as though I had ever heard any music before.”

He stared at the Angel, then about him at the room. “I have never felt anything of this kind with music before,” he said. He shook his head. “I shall never play again.”

===Chinese===

教区牧师早忘了那本政治经济学，天使收弓之前，他把一切都忘了。有一分钟他一动不动地坐着。随后他一惊，醒了过来。他正坐在那口包铁的旧箱子上。

“说真的，”他缓缓地说，“您非常高明。”

他困惑地环顾四周。“您拉琴的时候，我有点儿像看见了幻象。我仿佛看见了——。我看见了什么？它没了。”

他站起来，脸上带着恍惚的神情。“我再也不拉小提琴了，”他说。“我想请您把它拿到您房里去——留着它——。再拉给我听。听见您拉琴以前，我对音乐一无所知。我觉得，我从前的那些音乐，简直等于没听过。”

他望着天使，又望望四周的屋子。“音乐从前从没有让我有过这样的感觉，”他说。他摇了摇头。“我再也不拉了。”

## XXXIII The Warp and the Woof of Things / 三十三 世事的经与纬

===Original===

At the dinner table the Angel told the Vicar the more striking of his day’s adventures.

“The strange thing,” said the Angel, “is the readiness of you Human Beings﻿—the zest, with which you inflict pain. Those boys pelting me this morning﻿—”

“Seemed to enjoy it,” said the Vicar. “I know.”

===Chinese===

晚饭桌上，天使把他这一天历险中最扎眼的几桩，讲给教区牧师听。

“顶古怪的是，”天使说，“你们人类施加痛苦的这份踊跃——这份兴头。今儿早上那些孩子拿果壳砸我——”

“瞧着确实乐在其中，”教区牧师说。“我知道。”

===Original===

“Yet they don’t like pain,” said the Angel.

“No,” said the Vicar; “*they* don’t like it.”

“Then,” said the Angel, “I saw some beautiful plants rising with a spike of leaves, two this way and two that, and when I caressed one it caused the most uncomfortable﻿—”

===Chinese===

“可他们自己并不喜欢痛，”天使说。

“不，”教区牧师说；“*他们*不喜欢。”

“后来，”天使说，“我见到一些漂亮的植物，抽出一穗叶子，两片朝这边，两片朝那边，我一抚摸其中一株，它就给了我一种顶不舒服的——”

===Original===

“Stinging nettle!” said the Vicar.

“At any rate a new sort of pain. And another plant with a head like a coronet, and richly decorated leaves, spiked and jagged﻿—”

“A thistle, possibly.”

===Chinese===

“荨麻！”教区牧师说。

“总之是一种新的痛。还有一种植物，头顶像个王冠，叶子打扮得富丽堂皇，带刺，锯齿——”

“多半是蓟。”

===Original===

“And in your garden, the beautiful, sweet-smelling plant﻿—”

“The sweet briar,” said the Vicar. “I remember.”

“And that pink flower that sprang out of the box﻿—”

===Chinese===

“还有你们花园里那种漂亮的、香喷喷的植物——”

“甜蔷薇，”教区牧师说。“我记得。”

“还有从盒子里蹦出来的那种粉红色的花——”

===Original===

“Out of the box?” said the Vicar.

“Last night,” said the Angel, “that went climbing up the curtains﻿—Flame!”

“Oh!﻿—the matches and the candles! Yes,” said the Vicar.

===Chinese===

“从盒子里？”教区牧师说。

“昨天夜里，”天使说，“窜上窗帘的那个——焰！”

“哦！——火柴和蜡烛！是了，”教区牧师说。

===Original===

“Then the animals. A dog today behaved most disagreeably﻿—. And these boys, and the way in which people speak﻿—. Everyone seems anxious﻿—willing at any rate﻿—to give this Pain. Everyone seems busy giving pain﻿—”

“Or avoiding it,” said the Vicar, pushing his dinner away before him. “Yes﻿—of course. It’s fighting everywhere. The whole living world is a battlefield﻿—the whole world. We are driven by pain. Here. How it lies on the surface! This Angel sees it in a day!”

“But why does everyone﻿—everything﻿—want to give pain?” asked the Angel.

===Chinese===

“再有就是动物。今天一条狗的表现实在恶劣——。还有这些孩子，还有人们说话的那副样子——。人人都在抢着——至少是肯着——把这个痛送人。人人都在忙着给痛——”

“或者躲着它，”教区牧师说着，把面前的晚饭推开了。“是啊——当然。到处都是战斗。整个活的世界就是一片战场——整个世界。我们被痛驱赶着。看看吧。这道理摆得多浅！这位天使一天就看透了！”

“可为什么人人——样样东西——都想给痛呢？”天使问。

===Original===

“It is not so in the Angelic Land?” said the Vicar.

“No,” said the Angel. “Why is it so here?”

The Vicar wiped his lips with his napkin slowly. “It *is* so,” he said. “Pain,” said he still more slowly, “is the warp and the woof of this life. Do you know,” he said, after a pause, “it is almost impossible for me to imagine﻿ ﻿… a world without pain.﻿ ﻿… And yet, as you played this morning﻿—

===Chinese===

“天使之国不是这样的？”教区牧师说。

“不是，”天使说。“这里为什么是这样？”

教区牧师用餐巾慢慢抹了抹嘴唇。“*就是这样*，”他说。“痛，”他说得更慢了，“就是这个生命的经和纬。你可知道，”他顿了一顿说，“我几乎无法想象……一个没有痛的世界。……然而，正如您今天早上拉琴的时候——”

===Original===

“But this world is different. It is the very reverse of an Angelic world. Indeed, a number of people﻿—excellent religious people﻿—have been so impressed by the universality of pain that they think, after death, things will be even worse for a great many of us. It seems to me an excessive view. But it’s a deep question. Almost beyond one’s power of discussion﻿—”

And incontinently the Vicar plumped into an impromptu dissertation upon “Necessity,” how things were so because they were so, how one *had* to do this and that. “Even our food,” said the Vicar. “What?” said the Angel. “Is not obtained without inflicting Pain,” said the Vicar.

The Angel’s face went so white that the Vicar checked himself suddenly. Or he was just on the very verge of a concise explanation of the antecedents of a leg of lamb. There was a pause.

“By the by,” said the Angel, suddenly. “Have you been pithed? Like the common people.”

===Chinese===

“可这个世界不一样。它恰恰是天使世界的反面。说真的，有许多人——顶虔敬的教徒——被痛的无所不在镇住了，以至于认为，死后，对我们当中的许多人来说，事情还会更糟。这在我看来未免过分。但这是个深奥的问题。几乎超出了人的讨论能力——”

说罢，教区牧师一头栽进一篇关于‘必然’的即席论文里：事情怎样因为如此所以如此，人怎样*不得不*做这做那。“连我们的食物，”教区牧师说。“什么？”天使问。“也不是不施痛苦就到得了嘴的，”教区牧师说。

天使的脸白得那样厉害，教区牧师猛地收住了话头。再不然，他正讲到一只羊腿的来龙去脉的简明解说的节骨眼上。一阵停顿。

“对了，”天使忽然说，“你被去过髓吗？像那些普通人一样。”
